“说得好，”朱夫说，“我只想提醒您注意，由于目前的线路抢修工程，过去两个月来所有的列车在进入隧道口前都必须定点停靠。如果凶手预先策划好通过这种方式脱身，他很可能早就对这种定点停车了如指掌。”
法官的自信被便衣警探这些层出不穷的新推论弄得有些动摇，但他又抛出了另一个异议。
“我们在城堡周围并没有搜出任何足迹。”
“严格来说，确实没有，”朱夫承认道，“但显而易见，如果凶手踩过草坪（他多半踩过），他是在夜间踩踏的，也就是说，是在清晨晨露降临之前。

===Original===

Now everybody knows that when the dew rises in the early morning, grass that has been bent down by any passing man or animal, stands up again in its original position, thereby destroying all traces; so if the murderer did walk on the lawn when he was getting away, nobody could tell that he had done so. Nevertheless, on the lawn in front of the window of the room where the murder was committed I have observed, not exactly footprints, but signs that the earth has been disturbed at that spot. I imagine that if I were to jump out of a first floor window on to the soft surface of a lawn, and wanted to efface the marks of my boots, I should smooth the earth and the grass around them in just the same way that the little piece of lawn I speak of seems to have been smoothed.”
“I should like to have a look at that,” said M. de Presles.
“Well, there’s no difficulty about it,” Juve replied. “Come along.”
The two men hurried down the staircase and out of the house. When they reached the patch of grass which the inspector said had been “made up,” they crouched down and scrutinised it closely. Just by the side of the grass, even overhanging it a little, a large rhubarb plant outspread its thick, dentelated leaves almost parallel with the soil. Juve happened to glance casually at the nearest leaf, and uttered an exclamation of surprise and gratification.
“Gad, here’s something interesting!” and he drew the magistrate’s attention to some little pilules of earth with which the plant was peppered.
“What is that?” enquired M. de Presles.
“Earth,” said Juve, who had swept the top of the leaf with the palm of his hand; “ordinary earth, like the rest ten inches below, on the grass.”
“Well, what about it?” said the puzzled magistrate.
“Well,” said Juve with a smile, “I imagine that ordinary earth, or any kind of earth, has no power to move of its own volition, much less to jump up ten inches into the air and settle on the top of a leaf, even a rhubarb leaf! So I conclude that since this earth did not get here by itself it was brought here. How? That is very simple! Somebody has jumped on to the grass there, M.

===Chinese===

众所周知，每当清晨露水升腾蒸发时，被路过的人或走兽踩伏压弯的青草便会重新挺直立起，恢复到原来的位置，从而彻底湮没所有的痕迹；因此，即使凶手在逃离时确实踩踏过草坪，谁也看不出他走过的迹象。然而，在发生命案的房间窗前的草坪上，我注意到的并非确凿的足迹，而是泥土在那个地点被人抹平翻动过的迹象。我想，如果我从二楼窗户跳到柔软的草坪上，想要擦掉自己皮靴留下的凹坑，我一定会把周围的泥土和青草抹平抚顺，就像我刚才说的那小块草坪所呈现出的被抚平过的状态一模一样。”
“我很想亲自去瞧瞧，”德·普雷勒先生说。
“哎呀，这毫无难度，”朱夫回答，“来吧。”
两个男人快步走下楼梯，来到了户外。当他们来到那片被便衣警探称为“修饰伪造过”的草地旁时，两人蹲下身来，贴近仔细勘查。就在草坪的一侧，甚至有些伸向草坪上方，一株硕大茂盛的大黄伸展着宽厚带齿的叶片，几乎平行于地面铺展开来。朱夫随手朝最近的一片叶子上漫不经心地扫了一眼，猛地发出一声欣喜若狂的惊叹。
“天哪，这可真有意思！”他说着，引起法官注意大黄叶片上撒落着的一颗颗细小的泥丸。
“那是什么？”德·普雷勒先生问。
“泥土，”朱夫用手掌轻轻抹过叶面说道，“普通的泥土，跟下方十英寸草地上的泥土一模一样。”
“好吧，这又能说明什么？”法官大惑不解。
“哎呀，”朱夫微笑着说，“我想普通的泥土，或者任何一种泥土，绝不可能具有自主移动的能力，更不可能自己跳起十英寸高蹦到半空中、落在一片叶子——哪怕是大黄叶子上！因此我断定，既然这些泥土不是自己飞上来的，就必定是被什么东西带上来的。怎么带上来的？再简单不过了！有人从上面跳到了那片草地上，

===Original===

de Presles; he has removed the marks of his feet by smoothing the earth with his hands; the earth soiled his hands, and he rubbed one against the other quite mechanically; the earth which was on his hands fell off in little balls on to the rhubarb leaf, and remained there for us to discover. And so it is certain﻿—this is one proof more﻿—that even if the murderer did not get in from outside, he did at any rate take to flight after he had committed the crime.”
“So it can’t be Charles Rambert after all,” said the magistrate.
“It ‘ought to be’ Charles Rambert!” was Juve’s baffling reply.
The magistrate waxed irritable.
“My dear sir, your everlasting contradictions end by being rather absurd! You have hardly finished building up one laborious theory before you start knocking it down again. I fail to understand you.”
Juve smiled at M. de Presles’ sudden irritability, but quickly became grave again.
“I am anxious not to be led away by any preconceived opinion. I put the hypothesis that so-and-so is guilty, and examine all the arguments in support of that theory; then I submit that the crime was committed by somebody else, and proceed in the same way. My method certainly has the objection that it confronts every argument with a diametrically opposite one, but we are not concerned with establishing any one case in preference to another﻿—it is the truth, and nothing else, that we have to discover.”
“And that is tantamount to saying that in spite of the overwhelming circumstantial evidence, and in spite of the fact that he has run away, Charles Rambert is innocent?”
“Charles Rambert is the culprit, sir,” Juve replied brightly. “If he were not, whom else could we possibly suspect?”
The detective’s placidity and his perpetual self-contradictions exasperated M. de Presles. He held his tongue, and was silently revolving the case in his mind when Juve made yet one more suggestion.
“There is one final hypothesis which I feel obliged to put before you. Do you realise, sir, that this is a typical Fantômas crime?”
M. de Presles shrugged his shoulders as the detective pronounced this half-mythical name.
“Upon my word, M. Juve, I should never have expected you to invoke Fantômas! Why, Fantômas is the too obvious subterfuge, the cheapest device for investing a case with mock honours.

===Chinese===

德·普雷勒先生；他用双手把泥土抹平，企图掩盖自己的落脚痕迹；泥土弄脏了他的双手，他极其自然地下意识互搓双手拍打尘土；粘在他手上的泥土便揉搓成小泥球，落在了这片大黄叶子上，留在这里等着我们去发现。因此，确凿无疑的事实——又多了一份铁证——即便凶手不是从外面潜入的，无论如何他在得手之后立刻畏罪潜逃了。”
“这么说，终究不可能是夏尔·朗贝尔干的了，”法官说。
“理该是夏尔·朗贝尔！”朱夫给出了令人捉摸不透的答复。
法官烦躁地发起火来。
“我亲爱的先生，您这没完没了的前后矛盾简直荒谬透顶！您刚煞费苦心地建立起一套假设，转眼间又亲手把它推翻。我实在听不懂您的意思。”
面对德·普雷勒先生突如其来的愠怒，朱夫微微一笑，但脸色旋即重新变得严峻起来。
“我急切希望避免被任何先入为主的偏见所误导。我先假设某某人有罪，并仔细审查支持这一假设的所有论据；随后我又提出罪案系他人所为，并按照同样的方法展开论证。我的方法固然有一个弊端，那就是让每一个论据都面临与其截然对立的反证，但我们的职责并不是主观偏袒某一种推论——我们要发掘的只有真相，纯粹客观的真相。”
“这岂不是等于说，尽管存在压倒性的情势证据，尽管那小子畏罪潜逃，夏尔·朗贝尔依然是无辜的？”
“大人，夏尔·朗贝尔正是案犯，”朱夫神采飞扬地答道，“要是他不是凶手，我们还能怀疑谁呢？”
便衣警探那玩世不恭的从容自若以及永无休止的前后矛盾，彻底激怒了德·普雷勒先生。他紧闭嘴唇一言不发，在心头默默盘算着案情，这时朱夫又抛出了一个崭新的推测。
“还有一个最后的推论，我认为必须呈报给您。大人，您难道没看出来，这是一桩典型的芳托马斯式罪案吗？”
当便衣警探吐出这个半带神话色彩的名字时，德·普雷勒先生不以为然地耸了耸肩。
“说实在的，朱夫先生，我绝没想到连您也会把芳托马斯抬出来！哎呀，芳托马斯不过是那种显而易见的遁词，是给毫无头绪的疑案披上一层虚张声势的体面外衣的最廉价招数。

===Original===

Between you and me, you know perfectly well that Fantômas is merely a legal fiction﻿—a lawyers’ joke. Fantômas has no existence in fact!”
Juve stopped in his stride. He paused a moment before replying; then spoke in a restrained voice, but with an emphasis on his words that always marked him when he spoke in all seriousness.
“You are wrong to laugh, sir; very wrong. You are a magistrate and I am only a humble detective inspector, but you have three or four years’ experience, perhaps less, while I have fifteen years’ work behind me. I know that Fantômas does exist, and I do anything but laugh when I suspect his intervention in a case.”
M. de Presles could hardly conceal his surprise, and Juve went on:
“No one has ever said of me, sir, that I was a coward. I have looked death in the eyes; I have often hunted and arrested criminals who would not have had the least hesitation in doing away with me. There are whole gangs of rascals who have vowed my death. All manner of horrible revenges threaten me today. For all that I have the most complete indifference! But when people talk to me of Fantômas, when I fancy that I can detect the intervention of that genius of crime in any case, then, M. de Presles, I am in a funk! I tell you frankly I am in a funk. I am frightened, because Fantômas is a being against whom it is idle to use ordinary weapons; because he has been able to hide his identity and elude all pursuit for years; because his daring is boundless and his power unmeasurable; because he is everywhere and nowhere at once and, if he has had a hand in this affair, I am not even sure that he is not listening to me now! And finally, M. de Presles, because everyone whom I have known to attack Fantômas, my friends, my colleagues, my superior officers, have one and all, one and all, sir, been beaten in the fight! Fantômas does exist, I know, but who is he? A man can brave a danger he can measure, but he trembles when confronted with a peril he suspects but cannot see.”
“But this Fantômas is not a devil,” the magistrate broke in testily; “he is a man like you and me!”
“You are right, sir, in saying he is a man; but I repeat, the man is a genius! I don’t know whether he works alone or whether he is the head of a gang of criminals; I know nothing of his life; I know nothing of his object.

===Chinese===

私下里说，你心里非常清楚芳托马斯纯粹是法律拟制——是法界人士开的玩笑。芳托马斯在现实中根本不存在！”
朱夫驻足停步。他顿了顿才回答；随后用一种克制的声音开口，但他字里行间那股特有的重音，唯有在他极其郑重其事时才会显露无遗。
“大人，您笑得不对；大错特错。您是法官，而我只是一个微不足道的刑侦警探，但您的办案资历顶多三四年，甚至更短，而我背后却有十五年的办案历练。我深知芳托马斯确有其人，当我在某桩案件中察觉到他的黑手时，我绝不可能笑得出来。”
德·普雷勒先生几乎难以掩饰脸上的惊愕，朱夫接着说道：
“大人，从来没有人敢说我是个懦夫。我曾与死神四目相对；我屡次追捕缉拿的凶犯，没有一个会在干掉我这件事上有一丝一毫的犹豫。有成群结队的歹徒发誓要置我于死地。如今有形形色色可怕的复仇阴云笼罩着我。对于所有这一切，我都毫不在意！但是，当人们向我提起芳托马斯，当我隐约察觉到那个罪恶天才插手了某桩罪案时，德·普雷勒大人，我心里害怕！我坦白告诉您，我怕极了。我之所以害怕，是因为芳托马斯是一个凡常手段根本无法对付的生灵；因为多年来他不仅能隐藏自己的真实身份，更能躲过警方的每一次追捕；因为他的胆大包天绝无止境，他的能量无法估量；因为他无处不在又无迹可寻，如果他在本案中插了一脚，我甚至不敢肯定他此刻是不是正在暗中偷听我的谈话！最后，德·普雷勒大人，还因为我所知道的每一个向芳托马斯发起挑战的人——我的好友、我的同僚、我的上级长官——无一例外，大人，无一例外全在这场较量中一败涂地！芳托马斯确实存在，我坚信这一点，可他究竟是谁？一个人可以坦然面对他能量度清浅的危险，但当面对一个他能感知却看不见的凶险时，谁都会不寒而栗。”
“可是这个芳托马斯并不是恶魔，”法官没好气地打断道，“他也是个跟你我一样的人！”
“大人，您说他是一个人，这话完全正确；但我重复一遍，这人是个旷世奇才！我不知道他是单打独斗，还是统领着一个犯罪团伙；我对他的身世一无所知；对他的企图同样一无所知。

===Original===

In no single case yet has it been possible to determine the exact part he has taken. He seems to possess the extraordinary gift of being able to slay and leave no trace. You don’t see him; you divine his presence: you don’t hear him; you have a presentiment of him. If Fantômas is mixed up in this present affair, I don’t know if we ever shall succeed in clearing it up!”
M. de Presles was impressed in spite of himself by the detective’s earnestness.
“But I suppose you are not recommending me to drop the enquiry, are you, Juve?”
The detective forced a laugh that did not ring quite true.
“Come, come, sir,” he answered, “I told you just now that I was frightened, but I never said I was a coward. You may be quite sure I shall do my duty, to the very end. When I first began﻿—and that was not yesterday, nor yet the day before﻿—to realise the importance and the power of this Fantômas, I took an oath, sir, that some day I would discover his identity and effect his arrest! Fantômas is an enemy of society, you say? I prefer to regard him first and foremost as my own personal enemy! I have declared war on him, and I am ready to lose my skin in the war if necessary, but by God I’ll have his!”
Juve ceased. M. de Presles also was silent. But the magistrate was still sceptical, despite the detective’s strange utterance, and presently he could not refrain from making a gentle protest and appeal.
“Do please bring in a verdict against someone, M. Juve, for really I would rather believe that your Fantômas is﻿—a creation of the imagination!”
Juve shrugged his shoulders, seemed to be arriving at a mighty decision, and began:
“You are quite right, sir, to require me to draw some definite conclusion, even if you are not right in denying the existence of Fantômas. So I make the assertion that the murderer is﻿—”
---
The sound of hurrying steps behind them made both men turn round. A postman, hot and perspiring, was hurrying to the château; he had a telegram in his hand.
“Does either of you gentlemen know M. Juve?” he asked.
“My name is Juve,” said the detective, and he took the telegram and tore the envelope open. He glanced through it and then handed it to the magistrate.
“Please read that, sir,” he said.
The telegram was from the Criminal Investigation Department, and ran as follows:

===Chinese===

迄今为止，在任何一起具体案件中，警方都没能查明他究竟在其中扮演了何种角色。他似乎拥有一种匪夷所思的惊人天赋：能够杀人不留丝毫蛛丝马迹。你看不见他，却能揣测到他的存在；你听不见他，冥冥中却能预感到他的降临。如果芳托马斯卷入了这起案子，我不知道咱们这辈子到底能不能把它查个水落石出！”
尽管心存疑虑，德·普雷勒先生还是不由自主地被这位便衣警探那严肃郑重的神态打动了。
“不过我想，你该不会是建议我放弃调查吧，朱夫？”
警探勉强挤出一声并不自然的笑声。
“得了吧，大人，”他回答，“我刚才确实说过我害怕，但我可从没说过我是个懦夫。您完全可以放心，我一定会恪尽职守，坚持到底。早在很久以前——那绝非昨天或前天的事——当我第一次领教到这个芳托马斯的重大利害与非凡手腕时，大人，我就立下了誓言：有朝一日我必将查明他的真实身份并将其逮捕归案！您说芳托马斯是社会的公敌？我倒宁愿首先将他视作我不共戴天的私仇！我已经向他正式宣战了，在战争中哪怕掉脑袋我也在所不惜，但我对天发誓，我非扒了他的皮不可！”
朱夫打住了话头。德·普雷勒先生同样默不作声。尽管警探这番慷慨陈词极具冲击力，但法官骨子里依旧将信将疑，过了一会儿，他忍不住温和地抗议与敦促道：
“朱夫先生，请您无论如何指控一个具体的人吧，因为说真的，我宁可相信您的芳托马斯纯粹是——凭空捏造出来的幻觉！”
朱夫耸了耸肩，仿佛下定了一个重大的决断，开口说道：
“大人，即便您否认芳托马斯的存在是错的，但您要求我给出一个确凿结论完全合情合理。因此，我断言杀人凶手正是——”
---
身后传来的急促脚步声让两个男人同时回过头来。一名邮差满头大汗、气喘吁吁地朝城堡跑来；他手里紧握着一封电报。
“请问二位大人哪位是朱夫先生？”他打听道。
“我就是朱夫，”便衣警探说着，接过电报一把撕开了信封。他飞快扫视了一眼，随后将电报递给了法官。
“大人，请看这个，”他说。
这封电报来自刑事调查处，原文如下：

===Original===

> Return immediately to Paris. Are convinced that extraordinary crime lies behind disappearance of Lord Beltham. Privately, suspect Fantômas’ work.

===Chinese===

> 速返巴黎。确信贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）失踪案背后隐藏非同寻常之重案。私下疑系芳托马斯（Fantômas）所为。

## VII The Criminal Investigation Department / 第七章 刑事调查处
===Original===

“Does M. Gurn live here, please?”
Mme. Doulenques, the concierge at No. 147 rue Lévert, looked at the enquirer and saw a tall, dark man with a heavy moustache, wearing a soft hat and a tightly buttoned overcoat, the collar of which was turned up to his ears.
“M. Gurn is away, sir,” she answered; “he has been away for some little time.”
“I know,” said the stranger, “but still I want to go up to his rooms if you will kindly go with me.”
“You want﻿—” the concierge began in surprise and doubt. “Oh, I know; of course you are the man from the what’s-its-name company, come for his luggage? Wait a bit; what is the name of that company? Something funny﻿—an English name, I fancy.”
The woman left the door, which she had been holding just ajar, and went to the back of her lodge; she looked through the pigeonholes where she kept the tenants’ letters ready sorted, and picked out a soiled printed circular addressed to M. Gurn. She was busy putting on her spectacles when the stranger drew near and from over her shoulder got a glimpse of the name for which she was looking. He drew back again noiselessly, and said quietly:
“I have come from the South Steamship Company.”
“Yes, that’s it,” said the concierge, laboriously spelling out the words: “the South﻿—what you said. I can never pronounce those names. Rue d’Hauteville, isn’t it?”
“That’s it,” replied the man in the soft hat in pleasant, measured tones.
“Well, it’s very plain that you don’t bustle much in your place,” the concierge remarked. “I’ve been expecting you to come for M. Gurn’s things for nearly three weeks; he told me you would come a few days after he had gone. However, that’s your business.”
Mme. Doulenques cast a mechanical glance through the window that looked on to the street, and then surveyed the stranger from top to toe; he seemed to be much too well dressed to be a mere porter.
“But you haven’t got any handcart or truck,” she exclaimed. “You’re not thinking of carrying the trunks on your shoulder, are you? Why, there are at least three or four of them﻿—and heavy!”
The stranger paused before answering, as though he found it necessary to weigh each word.
“As a matter of fact I merely wanted to get an idea of the size of the luggage,” he said quietly. “Will you show me the things?”
“If I must, I must,” said the concierge with a heavy sigh.

===Chinese===

“请问居尔恩先生（M. Gurn）住在这里吗？”
莱韦尔街（rue Lévert）147号的门房杜朗克太太（Mme. Doulenques）打量着眼前的问路人，只见他身材高大，皮肤微黑，留着浓密的八字胡，戴着一顶软呢帽，大衣扣子扣得严严实实，衣领竖起遮住了耳朵。
“居尔恩先生出远门了，先生，”她回答道，“他已经出门有一阵子了。”
“我知道，”陌生人说，“不过如果您肯陪我走一趟，我还是想上楼去他的住处看看。”
“你想——”门房带着几分惊讶与狐疑开口道。“噢，我知道了；你肯定是那家叫什么什么公司的伙计，来取他的行李的吧？等等；那家公司叫什么名字来着？挺古怪的——我想是个英国名字。”
妇人松开了刚刚一直半掩着的门，走回门房传达室深处；她翻检着平时用来将房客信件分门别类收纳的小格子，抽出了一张寄给居尔恩先生、有些弄脏了的印刷通告函。她正忙着戴老花镜，陌生人已经凑上前去，越过她的肩膀瞥见了她正在寻找的名字。他不声不响地退了回来，平静地说道：
“我是从南方轮船公司（South Steamship Company）来的。”
“对，就是这个，”门房艰难地拼读着上面的字词：“南方——就是你刚才说的那个。那些洋名儿我从来都念不利索。在奥特维尔街（rue d’Hauteville），对不对？”
“正是，”戴软呢帽的男人用悦耳、从容的语调回答道。
“哼，明摆着你们那儿办事一点儿都不利索，”门房抱怨道，“我盼着你们来取居尔恩先生的东西，都盼了快三个星期了；他出门前跟我讲，他走后没几天你们就会来。不过，那是你们自己的事。”
杜朗克太太机械地朝临街的窗外扫了一眼，随后从头到脚把陌生人打量了一番；比起一个普通脚夫，这人的穿着未免显得太体面了。
“可是你连辆手推车或者板车都没带，”她惊呼道，“你该不会是打算用肩膀把大箱子扛走吧？哎呀，那里至少有三四只箱子呢——沉着呢！”
陌生人顿了顿才回答，仿佛觉得每个字都有必要字斟句酌。
“事实上我只是想先来看看行李的尺寸大小，”他平静地说，“您能带我去看看那些东西吗？”
“非去不可的话，那就走吧，”门房长叹了一声。

===Original===

“Come up with me: it’s the fifth floor,” and as she climbed the stairs she grumbled: “It’s a pity you didn’t come when I was doing my work: I shouldn’t have had to climb a hundred stairs a second time then; it counts up at the end of the day, and I’m not so young as I was.”
The stranger followed her up the stairs, murmuring monosyllabic sympathy, and regulating his pace by hers. Arrived at the fifth floor, the concierge drew a key from her pocket and opened the door of the flat.
It was a small modest place, but quite prettily decorated. The door on the landing opened into a tiny sort of anteroom, from which one passed into a front room furnished with little but a round table and a few armchairs. Beyond this was a bedroom, almost filled by the large bed, which was the first thing one saw on entering, and on the right there was yet another room, probably a little office. Both the first room, which was a kind of general living room, and the bedroom had wide windows overlooking gardens as far as one could see. An advantage of the flat was that it had nothing opposite, so that the occupant could move about with the windows open if he liked, and yet have nothing to fear from the inquisitiveness of neighbours.
The rooms had been shut up for several days, since the tenant had gone away indeed, and there was a stuffy smell about them, mingled with a strong smell of chemicals.
“I must air the place,” the concierge muttered, “or else M. Gurn won’t be pleased when he comes back. He always says he is too hot and can’t breathe in Paris.”
“So he does not live here regularly?” said the stranger, scanning the place curiously as he spoke.
“Oh, no, sir,” the concierge answered. “M. Gurn is a kind of commercial traveller and is often away, sometimes for a month or six weeks together,” and the gossiping woman was beginning a long and incoherent story when the stranger interrupted her, pointing to a silver-framed photograph of a young woman he had noticed on the mantelpiece.
“Is that Mme. Gurn?”
“M. Gurn is a bachelor,” Mme. Doulenques replied. “I can’t fancy him married, with his roaming kind of life.”
“Just a little friend of his, eh?” said the man in the soft hat, with a wink and a meaning smile.
“Oh, no,” said the concierge, shaking her head. “That photograph is not a bit like her.”
“So you know her, then?”
“I do and I don’t. That’s to say, when M.

===Chinese===

“跟我上楼吧：在六楼呢，”一边爬楼梯，她一边絮絮叨叨地抱怨：“真可惜你没趁我打扫卫生的时候来：那样我就用不着大老远爬两次一百级台阶了；一天下来骨头都要散架，我可不像以前那么年轻了。”
陌生人跟在她身后上了楼，嘴里吐出简短的同情之词，脚步放慢配合着她的节奏。来到六楼，门房从口袋里掏出一把钥匙，打开了套间的房门。
这是一处面积不大、陈设简朴却布置得颇为雅致的寓所。楼梯平廊上的门通向一间小小的门厅，穿过门厅便是一间起居室，屋里除了一张圆桌和几把扶手椅外别无长物。再往里是一间卧室，几乎被一张大床占满，进门第一眼看到的就是这张床；在右侧还有另一个房间，大概是一间小书房。第一间起居室兼生活用房以及卧室内都开有大窗，放眼望去是一片望不到边际的花园绿荫。这套公寓的一大优势是正对面毫无遮挡，只要房客乐意，哪怕大敞着窗户走动，也丝毫不用担心邻人窥探的好奇目光。
自从房客离开后，这几间屋子已经紧闭了好几天，空气中弥漫着一股发霉沉闷的气味，其间还夹杂着浓烈的化学药剂气味。
“我得通通风，”门房咕哝道，“不然等居尔恩先生回来肯定会不高兴的。他老是嚷嚷说巴黎太热，闷得他喘不过气来。”
“这么说，他并不常住在这里？”陌生人一边好奇地打量着四周，一边漫不经心地探询道。
“噢，不住常，先生，”门房回答，“居尔恩先生算是个商务旅行推销员，经常出远门，有时一走就是一个月或者六个星期，”这个多嘴多舌的妇人刚要开始长篇大论讲起颠三倒四的故事，陌生人指着壁炉台上注意到的一个镶银框的年轻女子相片，打断了她的话。
“那是居尔恩太太吗？”
“居尔恩先生是个单身汉，”杜朗克太太回答，“以他那种四海为家的生活方式，我可想象不出他成家的样子。”
“只是他相好的小情人，嗯？”戴软呢帽的男人眨了眨眼，露出了意味深长的微笑。
“噢，不是，”门房摇了摇头，“那张照片跟她一点儿都不像。”
“这么说，你认得那位女士？”
“谈不上认得，也谈不上不认得。也就是说，每当居尔恩先生

===Original===

Gurn is in Paris, he often has visits from a lady in the afternoon: a very fashionable lady, I can tell you, not the sort that one often sees in this quarter. Why, the woman who comes is a society lady, I am sure: she always has her veil down and passes by my lodge ever so fast, and never has any conversation with me; free with her money, too: it’s very seldom she does not give me something when she comes.”
The stranger seemed to find the concierge’s communications very interesting, but they did not interrupt his mental inventory of the room.
“In other words, your tenant does not keep too sharp an eye on his money?” he suggested.
“No, indeed: the rent is always paid in advance, and sometimes M. Gurn even pays two terms in advance because he says he never can tell if his business won’t be keeping him away when the rent falls due.”
Just then a deep voice called up the staircase:
“Concierge: M. Gurn: have you anyone of that name in the house?”
“Come up to the fifth floor,” the concierge called back to the man. “I am in his rooms now,” and she went back into the flat. “Here’s somebody else for M. Gurn,” she exclaimed.
“Does he have many visitors?” the stranger enquired.
“Hardly any, sir: that’s why I’m so surprised.”
Two men appeared; their blue blouses and metal-peaked caps proclaimed them to be porters. The concierge turned to the man in the soft hat.
“I suppose these are your men, come to fetch the trunks?”
The stranger made a slight grimace, seemed to hesitate and finally made up his mind to remain silent.
Rather surprised to see that the three men did not seem to be acquainted with each other, the concierge was about to ask what it meant, when one of the porters addressed her curtly:
“We’ve come from the South Steamship Company for four boxes from M. Gurn’s place. Are those the ones?” and taking no notice of the visitor in the room, the man pointed to two large trunks and two small boxes which were placed in a corner of the room.
“But aren’t you three all together?” enquired Mme. Doulenques, visibly uneasy.
The stranger still remained silent, but the first porter replied at once.
“No; we have nothing to do with the gentleman. Get on to it, mate! We’ve no time to waste!”
Anticipating their action, the concierge got instinctively between the porters and the luggage: so too did the man in the soft hat.
“Pardon,” said he politely but peremptorily.

===Chinese===

人在巴黎的时候，下午经常会有一位贵妇来拜访他：我敢告诉您，是一位极其时髦的贵妇，绝不是平时在咱们这一带能见到的那种人。哎呀，来的那个女人肯定是个上流社会的贵妇，我敢打包票：她每次都压低面纱，从我的门房窗前飞快地走过，从来不跟我说半句话；手头阔绰得很：每次来几乎没有不赏我小费的时候。”
陌生人似乎对门房透露的这些信息颇感兴趣，但这丝毫没有打乱他暗中在脑海里对房间物件进行清点的思绪。
“换句话说，您的这位房客花起钱来并不怎么斤斤计较？”他启发道。
“确实如此：房租向来都是提前交的，有时居尔恩先生甚至提前付两个季度的房租，因为他说他从没法预料到期时生意会不会把他绊在外面回不来。”
恰在此时，楼梯口传来一声洪亮的喊声：
“门房：居尔恩先生：楼里有叫这名字的人吗？”
“上六楼来吧，”门房冲着那人高喊道，“我现在就在他的屋里呢，”说完她走回了套间里。“又有人来找居尔恩先生了，”她惊呼道。
“他的访客很多吗？”陌生人探问道。
“几乎没有，先生：所以我才这么奇怪。”
两名男子出现在门前；他们身上穿的蓝色罩衫和金属帽檐的制帽表明他们是搬运脚夫。门房转向戴软呢帽的男人。
“我想这两位是你的伙计，来抬大箱子的吧？”
陌生人嘴角微微抽搐了一下，似乎迟疑了片刻，最终决定保持沉默。
看到这三个男人彼此之间似乎素不相识，门房颇感纳闷，刚想打听是怎么回事，其中一名脚夫便粗声粗气地对她说：
“我们是从南方轮船公司来的，来取居尔恩先生这里的四个箱子。是墙角那些吗？”那人全然没有理会屋里的陌生访客，用手指向放在屋角里的两只大衣箱和两只小箱子。
“可你们三个难道不是一伙的吗？”杜朗克太太探问道，脸上显出明显的不安。
陌生人依然一言不发，但领头的脚夫立刻答道：
“不是；我们跟这位先生毫无干系。动手吧，伙计！咱们可没闲工夫瞎耽误！”
预料到他们的企图，门房本能地横身挡在了脚夫与行李箱之间：戴软呢帽的男人同样一步跨上前去。
“且慢，”他礼貌却威严地喝道。

===Original===

“Please take nothing away.”
One of the porters drew a crumpled and dirty memorandum book from his pocket and turned over the pages, wetting his thumb every time. He looked at it attentively and then spoke.
“There’s no mistake: this is where we were told to come,” and again he signed to his mate. “Let’s get on with it!”
The concierge was puzzled. She looked first at the mysterious stranger, who was as quiet and silent as ever, and then at the porters, who were beginning to be irritated by these incomprehensible complications.
Mme. Doulenques’ mistrust waxed greater, and she sincerely regretted being alone on the fifth floor with these strangers, for the other occupants of this floor had gone off to their daily work long ago. Suddenly she escaped from the room, and called shrilly down the stairs:
“Madame Aurore! Madame Aurore!”
The man in the soft hat rushed after her, seized her gently but firmly by the arm, and led her back into the room.
“I beg you, madame, make no noise: do not call out!” he said in a low tone. “Everything will be all right. I only ask you not to create a disturbance.”
But the concierge was thoroughly alarmed by the really odd behaviour of all these men, and again screamed at the top of her voice:
“Help! Police!”
The first porter was exasperated.
“It’s unfortunate to be taken for thieves,” he said with a shrug of his shoulders. “Look here, Auguste, just run down to the corner of the street and bring back a gendarme. The gentleman can explain to the concierge in his presence, and then we shall be at liberty to get on with our job.”
Auguste hastened to obey, and several tense moments passed, during which not a single word was exchanged between the three people who were left together.
Then heavy steps were heard, and Auguste reappeared with a gendarme. The latter came swaggering into the room with a would-be majestic air, and solemnly and pompously enquired:
“Now then, what’s all this about?”
At sight of the officer every countenance cleared. The concierge ceased to tremble; the porter lost his air of suspicion. Both were beginning to explain to the representative of authority, when the man in the soft hat waved them aside, stepped up to the guardian of the peace and looking him straight in the eyes, said:
“Criminal Investigation Department! Inspector Juve!”

===Chinese===

“请不要动任何东西。”
其中一名脚夫从口袋里掏出一个揉得脏兮兮的记事本，每翻一页都要蘸一下口水。他全神贯注地盯视着本子，随后开口道：
“绝对没错：上面交代的就是这个地址，”他再次向同伴示意：“动手吧！”
门房全给搞糊涂了。她先是看看那个神秘莫测的陌生人（那人依然一如既往地从容沉默），接着又看看脚夫们（面对这莫名其妙的阻挠，脚夫们开始烦躁起来）。
杜朗克太太的戒心愈发强烈，她真后悔自己独自一人同这几个陌生男人待在六楼，因为同楼层的其他房客早就出门去干各自的日常营生了。突然间，她夺门而出，顺着楼梯尖声呼救：
“奥罗尔太太！奥罗尔太太！”
戴软呢帽的男人快步追上前去，动作轻柔却极其有力地抓住了她的手臂，把她领回了房间。
“求您了，太太，千万别声张：别喊叫！”他压低嗓门说，“一切都会弄清楚的。我只求您别惊动四邻。”
然而门房被这些男人匪夷所思的举止彻底吓破了胆，再次扯开嗓门尖叫起来：
“救命啊！抓贼啊！”
领头的脚夫被惹火了。
“被人当成小偷可真倒霉，”他耸了耸肩说道，“听着，奥古斯特，赶紧跑到街角去叫个巡警过来。当着巡警的面，这位先生可以好好向门房解释个明白，然后咱们就能放开手脚干活了。”
奥古斯特连忙领命飞奔而去，几分钟令人窒息的漫长时间过去了，留下的三个人彼此之间没再说哪怕一个字。
紧接着传来了沉重的脚步声，奥古斯特领着一名巡警折返回来。巡警迈着自命威严的步伐大摇大摆地跨进屋里，神情严肃、装腔作势地盘问道：
“喂，这到底是怎么一回事？”
一见到警察，所有人脸上的紧绷神色顿时烟消云散。门房不再发抖；脚夫眼中的狐疑也消失了。两人正要争先恐后地向执法代表陈述原委，戴软呢帽的男人抬手将他们挡在一旁，径直走到治安警察面前，直视着他的双眼，沉声说道：
“刑事调查处！警探朱夫！”

===Original===

The gendarme, who was quite unprepared for this announcement, stepped back a pace and raised his eyes towards the man who addressed him: then suddenly raised his hand to his *képi* and came to attention.
“Beg pardon, Inspector, I didn’t recognise you! M. Juve! And you have been in this division a long time too!” He turned angrily to the foremost porter. “Step forward, please, and let’s have no nonsense!”
Juve, who had thus disclosed his identity as a detective, smiled, seeing that the gendarme assumed that the South Steamship Company’s porter was a thief.
“That’s all right,” he said. “Leave the man alone. He’s done no harm.”
“Then who am I to arrest?” the puzzled gendarme asked.
The concierge broke in to explain: she had been much impressed by the style and title of the stranger.
“If the gentleman had told me where he came from I would certainly never have allowed anyone to go for a gendarme.”
Inspector Juve smiled.
“If I had told you who I was just now, madame, when you were, quite naturally, so upset, you would not have believed me. You would have continued to call out. Now, I am particularly anxious to avoid any scandal or noise at the present moment. I rely on your discretion.” He turned to the two porters, who were dumb with amazement and could make nothing of the affair. “As for you, my good fellows, I must ask you to leave your other work and go back at once to your office in the rue d’Hauteville and tell your manager﻿—what is his name?”
“M. Wooland,” one of the men replied.
“Good: tell M. Wooland that I want to see him here at the earliest possible moment; and tell him to bring with him all the papers he has that refer to M. Gurn. And not a word to anyone about all this, please, especially in this neighbourhood. Take my message to your manager, and that’s all.”
---
The porters had left hurriedly for the rue d’Hauteville and a quarter of an hour went by. The detective had requested the concierge to ask the Madame Aurore to whom she had previously appealed so loudly for help, to take her place temporarily in the lodge. Juve kept Mme. Doulenques upstairs with him partly to get information from her, and partly to prevent her from gossiping downstairs.
While he was opening drawers and ransacking furniture, and plunging his hand into presses and cupboards, Juve asked the concierge to describe this tenant of hers, M.

===Chinese===

毫无防备的巡警冷不防听到这番通报，猛地后退了一步，抬眼打量着面前向他说话的男人；接着突然扬手扶住大檐帽敬礼，啪地立正。
“请原谅，警探先生，我没能认出您来！朱夫先生！而且您负责这个辖区也已经有段日子了！”他气势汹汹地转向领头的脚夫。“请上前一步，老实点，别耍花招！”
自亮便衣警探身份的朱夫见巡警误以为南方轮船公司的脚夫是小偷，不由得微微一笑。
“行了，”他说，“别为难人家。他没干犯法的事。”
“那我到底该抓谁呢？”巡警一头雾水地问。
门房连忙抢着解释；这位陌生人的官衔头衔给她留下了极深的震慑。
“要是这位先生早点向我说明他的来头，我断然绝不会让任何人去叫巡警的。”
朱夫警探微微一笑。
“太太，刚才您那么激动失控，实属人之常情，要是我那时向您表明身份，您断然不会相信我。您只会继续扯着嗓子大喊大叫。而眼下，我特别希望避免引起任何风波与喧嚷。我仰仗您的谨慎守秘。”他转向那两名目瞪口呆、对眼前的一切摸不着头脑的脚夫。“至于你们二位，我的好伙计，我得劳驾你们暂停手头的其他活计，立刻赶回奥特维尔街的办事处，告诉你们的经理——他叫什么名字来着？”
“乌兰先生（M. Wooland），”其中一名脚夫回答。
“很好：转告乌兰先生，我希望他以最快的速度赶到这里来见我；并嘱咐他务必随身带来与居尔恩先生相关的所有文件底单。还有，请对整件事严格守口如瓶，尤其是对这一带的街坊四邻。把我的话带给你们经理就行了。”
---
脚夫们急匆匆地赶往奥特维尔街，一刻钟过去了。便衣警探请门房吩咐她方才大声呼救的那位奥罗尔太太暂时到楼下门房传达室顶班替她看门。朱夫之所以把杜朗克太太留在楼上身边，一方面是为了从她嘴里探问线索，另一方面也是为了防止她下楼到处嚼舌根。
在拉开抽屉翻箱倒柜、将手伸进各个大衣柜和橱柜进行搜查的同时，朱夫让门房仔细描摹一番她这位房客居尔恩先生

===Original===

Gurn, in whom he appeared to be so deeply interested.
“He is a rather fair man,” the concierge told him, “medium height, stout build, and clean shaven like an Englishman; there is nothing particular about him: he is like lots of other people.”
This very vague description was hardly satisfactory. The detective told the policeman to unscrew the lock on a locked trunk, and gave him a small screwdriver which he had found in the kitchen. Then he turned again to Mme. Doulenques who was standing stiffly against the wall, severely silent.
“You told me that M. Gurn had a lady friend. When used he to see her?”
“Pretty often, when he was in Paris; and always in the afternoon. Sometimes they were together till six or seven o’clock, and once or twice the lady did not come down before half-past seven.”
“Used they to leave the house together?”
“No, sir.”
“Did the lady ever stay the night here?”
“Never, sir.”
“Yes: evidently a married woman,” murmured the detective as if speaking to himself.
Mme. Doulenques made a vague gesture to show her ignorance on the point.
“I can’t tell you anything about that, sir.”
“Very well,” said the detective; “kindly pass me that coat behind you.”
The concierge obediently took down a coat from a hook and handed it to Juve who searched it quickly, looked it all over and then found a label sewn on the inside of the collar: it bore the one word “Pretoria.”
“Good!” said he, in an undertone; “I thought as much.”
Then he looked at the buttons; these were stamped on the under side with the name “Smith.”
The gendarme understood what the detective was about, and he too examined the clothes in the first trunk which he had just opened.
“There is nothing to show where these things came from, sir,” he remarked. “The name of the maker is not on them.”
“That’s all right,” said Juve. “Open the other trunk.”
While the gendarme was busy forcing this second lock Juve went for a moment into the kitchen and came back holding a rather heavy copper mallet with an iron handle, which he had found there. He was looking at this mallet with some curiosity, balancing and weighing it in his hands, when a sudden exclamation of fright from the gendarme drew his eyes to the trunk, the lid of which had just been thrown back.

===Chinese===

的相貌，警探似乎对这人抱有极深厚的兴趣。
“他是个金发男子，”门房告诉他，“中等身材，体格结实，胡子刮得干干净净，像个英国绅士；长相没什么特殊的地方：跟满大街的普通人差不多。”
这种极其模糊的描述显然无法让人满意。便衣警探吩咐巡警用他在厨房里找到的一把小螺丝刀把一口上了锁的大衣箱上的锁具拧开。随后他再次转向笔直贴墙站立、神情凝重肃穆的杜朗克太太。
“你刚才告诉我，居尔恩先生有一位红颜知己。他平时通常什么时候见她？”
“只要他在巴黎，他们就挺常见面的；而且向来都是在下午。有时他们一直待到六七点钟，还有一两回那位贵妇直到七点半才下楼。”
“他们平时一起出门离开大宅吗？”
“不，先生，从来没有。”
“那位贵妇可曾在这里留宿过夜？”
“从来没有，先生。”
“不错，显然是个有夫之妇，”便衣警探仿佛在自言自语般喃喃低语。
杜朗克太太做出了一个含糊的手势，表明自己对内情一无所知。
“关于那个，先生，我可什么都说不上来。”
“很好，”便衣警探说，“劳驾把你身后挂着的那件大衣递给我。”
门房顺从地从衣钩上取下一件外套递给朱夫，朱夫手脚麻利地翻检了一遍，仔细端详了一番，随后在衣领内侧发现缝着一个标签，上面印着一个单词：“比勒陀利亚（Pretoria）”。
“很好！”他压低嗓门说，“正如我所料。”
接着他又端详起扣子；扣子的背面打着一行钢印名字：“史密斯（Smith）”。
巡警心领神会地明白了警探的意图，他也仔细检查起刚刚撬开的第一只大衣箱里的衣物。
“先生，这里头的东西看不出是从哪儿置办的，”他插话道，“上面没有生产厂商的名字。”
“不要紧，”朱夫说，“把另一只箱子撬开。”
当巡警正忙着强行撬开第二把锁时，朱夫快步走进厨房片刻，手里拎着一把带铁柄的颇为沉重的铜槌走了出来，这是他在厨房里找到的。他颇感好奇地端详着这把铜槌，拿在手里掂量着分量，突然间，巡警发出了一声惊恐万状的尖叫，将他的目光引向了那只刚刚被掀开箱盖的大箱子。

===Original===

Juve did not lose all his professional impassivity, but even he leaped forward like a flash, swept the gendarme to one side, and dropped on his knees beside the open box. A horrid spectacle met his eyes. For the trunk contained a corpse!
The moment Mme. Doulenques caught sight of the ghastly thing, she fell back into a chair half fainting, and there she remained, unable to move, with her body hunched forward, and haggard eyes fixed upon the corpse, of which she caught occasional glimpses as the movements of Juve and the gendarme every now and then left the shocking thing within the trunk exposed to her view.
Yet there was nothing especially gruesome or repellent about the corpse. It was the body of a man of about fifty years of age, with a pronounced brick-red complexion, and a lofty brow, the height of which was increased by premature baldness. Long, fair moustaches drooped from the upper lip almost to the top of the chest. The unfortunate creature was doubled up in the trunk, with knees bent and head forced down by the weight of the lid. The body was dressed with a certain fastidiousness, and it was obviously that of a man of fashion and distinction; there was no wound to be seen. The calm, quiet face suggested that the victim had been taken by surprise while in the full vigour of life and killed suddenly, and had not been subjected to the anguish of a fight for life or to any slow agony.
Juve half turned to the concierge.
“When did you see M. Gurn last? Exactly, please: it is important.”
Mme. Doulenques babbled something unintelligible and then, as the detective pressed her, made an effort to collect her scattered wits.
“Three weeks ago at least, sir: yes, three weeks exactly; no one has been here since, I will swear.”
Juve made a sign to the gendarme, who understood, and felt the body carefully.
“Quite stiff, and hard, sir,” he said; “yet there is no smell from it. Perhaps the cold﻿—”
Juve shook his head.
“Even severe cold could not preserve a body in that condition for three weeks, and it’s not cold now, but there is this:” and he showed his subordinate a small yellowish stain just at the opening of the collar, close to the Adam’s apple, which, in spite of the comparative thinness of the body, was very much developed.

===Chinese===

朱夫并没有完全丧失职业刑警的从容镇定，但也如闪电般一个箭步冲上前去，一把将巡警拨到一旁，扑通一声双膝跪在敞开的箱子旁。一幕骇人听闻的景象映入了他的眼帘。那只箱子里赫然装着一具尸体！
杜朗克太太一瞥见那可怕的惨状，顿时瘫软在椅子里半昏厥过去；她蜷缩着身子呆坐在那里动弹不得，惊恐绝望的双眼直勾勾地盯着尸体——随着朱夫和巡警的走动，箱子里那具惨绝人寰的遗体不时暴露在她的视线中。
然而，这具尸体本身并没有什么特别可怖或令人作呕的痕迹。死者是一名年约五十岁的男子，面色泛着显著的赭红色，天庭饱满高阔，由于过早秃顶，额头显得尤为宽广。两撇浓密的淡黄色长八字胡从上唇垂下，几乎垂到了胸口上方。这个不幸的死者蜷缩在箱子里，双膝弯曲，头颅被沉重的箱盖生生压低。遗体的着装颇为考究讲究，显然属于一位优雅体面的上流社会人士；身上看不到任何外伤。那安详沉静的面容表明，受害者是在精力充沛的壮年时期冷不防遭到猝然暗算、一击毙命的，既没有经历过殊死搏斗的剧烈挣扎，也没有经受过缓慢濒死的痛苦折磨。
朱夫半侧过身面对门房。
“你最后一次见到居尔恩先生是什么时候？请确切告诉我：这至关重要。”
杜朗克太太结结巴巴地吐出几句含混不清的话，随后在便衣警探的再三敦促下，好不容易定下心神收拢散乱的思绪。
“至少是三个星期以前了，先生：对，整整三个星期；从那以后绝对再也没人来过这里，我对天发誓。”
朱夫向巡警递了个眼神，巡警心领神会，伸手仔细触摸检查着尸体。
“全身邦邦硬，僵死了，先生，”他说，“不过尸体身上毫无异味。也许是天气寒冷的缘故——”
朱夫摇了摇头。
“哪怕是刺骨的严寒，也不可能把尸体保存得三星期完好如初，更何况现在天气并不算冷，但你看这里：”他指给下属看衣领开口处、靠近喉结的一处黄色小斑块，尽管死者身材相对瘦削，但那喉结却异常凸起。

===Original===

Juve took the corpse under the armpits and raised it gently, wishing to examine it closely, but anxious, also, not to alter its position. On the nape of the neck was a large stain of blood, like a black wen and as big as a five-shilling piece, just above the last vertebra of the spinal column.
“That’s the explanation,” the detective murmured, and carefully replacing the body he continued his investigation. With quick, clever hands he searched the coat pockets and found the watch in its proper place. Another pocket was full of money, chiefly small change, with a few louis. But Juve looked in vain for the pocketbook which the man had doubtless been in the habit of carrying about with him: the pocketbook probably containing some means of identification.
The inspector merely grunted, got up, began pacing the room, and questioned the concierge.
“Did M. Gurn have a motorcar?”
“No, sir,” she replied, looking surprised. “Why do you ask?”
“Oh, for no particular reason,” said the inspector with affected indifference, but at the same time he was contemplating a large nickel pump that lay on a whatnot, a syringe holding perhaps half a pint, like those that chauffeurs use. He looked at it steadfastly for several minutes. His next question was addressed to the gendarme who was still on his knees by the trunk.
“We have found one yellow stain on the neck; you will very likely find some more. Have a look at the wrists and the calves of the legs and the stomach. But do it carefully, so as not to disturb the body.” While the gendarme began to obey his chief’s order, carefully undoing the clothing on the corpse, Juve looked at the concierge again.
“Who did the work of this flat?”
“I did, sir.”
Juve pointed to the velvet curtain that screened the door between the little anteroom and the room in which they were.
“How did you come to leave that curtain unhooked at the top, without putting it to rights?”
Mme. Doulenques looked at it.
“It’s the first time I’ve seen it like that,” she said apologetically; “the curtain could not have been unhooked when I did the room last without my noticing it. Anyhow, it hasn’t been like that long. I ought to say that as M. Gurn was seldom here I didn’t do the place out thoroughly very often.”
“When did you do it out last?”
“Quite a month ago.”
“That is to say M. Gurn went away a week after you last cleaned the place up?”
“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===

朱夫托住尸体的腋下轻轻往上抬起，既想仔细勘查，又极力避免破坏其原有的姿势。在后颈处，恰在脊柱最后一节颈椎上方，赫然有一大块凝结的血印，宛如一颗黑色的肉瘤，足足有五先令硬币大小。
“谜底解开了，”便衣警探喃喃低语，小心翼翼地把尸体放回原处，继续他的勘查。他双手熟练利落地搜查着上衣口袋，在应有的位置找到了怀表。另一个口袋里塞满了钱币，大部分是零钱，还有几枚路易金币。然而朱夫四处搜寻，却未能找到这个男人平日里无疑习惯随身携带的皮夹：皮夹里通常必定装有能证明其身份的证件。
警探只是低哼了一声，站起身来，在屋里踱着步子，向门房发问。
“居尔恩先生有汽车吗？”
“没有，先生，”她面带诧异地回答，“您干吗打听这个？”
“噢，随口问问，”警探假装漫不经心地答道，但与此同时，他的目光却定格在搁在多层置物架上的一支大型镀镍打气筒上，那是一支容量约有半品脱的注油器，跟汽车司机平时用的那种一模一样。他目不转睛地凝视了它好几分钟。接下来他的问题是冲着依然跪在箱子旁的巡警发出的。
“我们在脖子上发现了一处黄色污斑；你很可能还会在别处找到类似的斑迹。仔细查查手腕、小腿肚子和腹部。但动作要轻，千万别破坏尸体的姿态。”当巡警遵照上司的指令、小心翼翼地解开尸体上的衣物时，朱夫再次看向门房。
“这套公寓平时是谁收拾打扫的？”
“是我打扫的，先生。”
朱夫指着遮挡在小前厅与他们所在房间之间门上的天鹅绒门帘。
“你打扫房间时，怎么会让门帘顶端脱了钩挂着、而不顺手把它挂好呢？”
杜朗克太太打量着门帘。
“我也是头一回见它变成这样，”她满怀歉意地分辩道，“上次我收拾房间时，门帘绝不可能脱了钩而我没发觉。不管怎么说，变成这样肯定没多久。我得说明一下，因为居尔恩先生平时难得在这里露面，我并不是经常彻底大扫除。”
“你上一次彻底大扫除是什么时候？”
“足足一个月前了。”
“也就是说，居尔恩先生是在你打扫完房间一个星期之后才离开的？”
“是的，先生。”

===Original===

Juve changed the subject, and pointed to the corpse.
“Tell me, madame, did you know that person?”
The concierge fought down her nervousness and for the first time looked at the unfortunate victim with a steady gaze.
“I have never seen him before,” she said, with a little shudder.
“And so, when that gentleman came up here, you did not notice him?” said the inspector gently.
“No, I did not notice him,” she declared, and then went on as if answering some question which occurred to her own mind. “And I wonder I didn’t, for people very seldom enquired for M. Gurn; of course when the lady was with him M. Gurn was not at home to anybody. This﻿—this dead man must have come straight up himself.”
Juve nodded, and was about to continue his questioning when the bell rang.
“Open the door,” said Juve to the concierge, and he followed her to the entrance of the flat, partly fearing to find some intruder there, partly hoping to see some unexpected person whose arrival might throw a little light upon the situation.
At the opened door Juve saw a young man of about twenty-five, an obvious Englishman with clear eyes and close-cropped hair. With an accent that further made his British origin unmistakable, the visitor introduced himself:
“I am Mr. Wooland, manager of the Paris branch of the South Steamship Company. It seems that I am wanted at M. Gurn’s flat on the fifth floor of this house, by desire of the police.”
Juve came forward.
“I am much obliged to you for putting yourself to this inconvenience, sir: allow me to introduce myself: M. Juve, an Inspector from the Criminal Investigation Department. Please come in.”
Solemn and impassive, Mr. Wooland entered the room; a side glance suddenly showed him the open trunk and the dead body, but not a muscle of his face moved. Mr. Wooland came of a good stock, and had all that admirable self-possession which is the strength of the powerful Anglo-Saxon race. He looked at the inspector in somewhat haughty silence, waiting for him to begin.
“Will you kindly let me know, sir, the instructions your firm had with regard to the forwarding of the baggage which you sent for to this flat of M. Gurn’s this morning?”

===Chinese===

朱夫转换了话题，指着尸体问道。
“告诉我，太太，你认识这个人吗？”
门房克制住内心的恐惧，第一次将目光定定地投向这位不幸的受害者。
“我以前从没见过他，”她打了个寒颤说道。
“这么说，当那位绅士上楼到这里来的时候，你并没有注意到他？”警探温和地问。
“是的，我没注意到他，”她坚决地答道，随后仿佛在回答自己心头冒出的疑问般接着说道：“我真纳闷自己怎么会没看见，因为平时很少有人找居尔恩先生；当然，当那位贵妇和他在一起的时候，居尔恩先生对任何来客都是闭门谢客的。这个——这个死人肯定是自己径直走上楼来的。”
朱夫点了点头，正打算继续讯问，门铃突然响了。
“去开门，”朱夫对门房说，并跟着她走到公寓门厅，他既担心是哪位不速之客闯入，又期盼能遇到某个意想不到的人物、其出现能为眼前的僵局带来一丝曙光。
门打开后，朱夫看到了一名年约二十五岁的年轻人，显然是个英国人，眼神清澈，留着利落的短发。来人操着一口让人绝不可能错认其英国血统的口音自我介绍道：
“我是南方轮船公司巴黎分部的经理乌兰先生。听说警方要我在本楼六层居尔恩先生的套间里配合办案。”
朱夫走上前去。
“劳驾您亲自跑一趟，不胜感激，先生：请允许我自我介绍一下：我是刑事调查处的便衣警探朱夫。请进。”
乌兰先生神情严肃、不苟言笑地走进了房间；他斜眼一瞥，蓦地看到了敞开的大箱子和里面的尸体，但他脸上的肌肉连一丝抽搐都没有。乌兰先生出身名门，具备强悍的盎格鲁-撒克逊民族所独有的那份令人赞叹的沉着与自制力。他神色微带傲慢地默然注视着警探，等候对方先开口。
“先生，能否请您见告，贵公司今天早晨派人来居尔恩先生这套公寓提取行李，究竟是接到了何种托运指令？”

===Original===

“Four days ago, Inspector,” said the young man, “on the 14th of December to be precise, the London mail brought us a letter in which Lord Beltham, who had been a client of ours for several years, instructed us to collect, on the 17th of December, that is, today, four articles marked H. W. K., 1, 2, 3 and 4, from M. Gurn’s apartments, 147 rue Lévert. He informed us that the concierge had orders to allow us to take them away.”
“To what address were you to despatch them?”
“Our client instructed us to forward the trunks by the first steamer to Johannesburg, where he would send for them; we were to send two invoices with the goods as usual; the third invoice was to be sent to London, Box 63, Charing Cross Post Office.”
Juve made a note of Box 63, Charing Cross in his pocketbook.
“Addressed to what name or initials?”
“Simply Beltham.”
“Good. There are no other documents relating to the matter?”
“No, I have nothing else,” said Mr. Wooland.
The young fellow relapsed into his usual impassive silence. Juve watched him for a minute or two and then said:
“You must have heard the various rumours current in Paris three weeks ago, sir, about Lord Beltham. He was a very well-known personage in society. Suddenly he disappeared; his wife left nothing undone to give the matter the widest publicity. Were you not rather surprised when you received a letter from Lord Beltham four days ago?”
Mr. Wooland was not disconcerted by the rather embarrassing question.
“Of course I had heard of Lord Beltham’s disappearance, but it was not for me to form any official opinion about it. I am a business man, sir, not a detective. Lord Beltham might have disappeared voluntarily or the reverse: I was not asked to say which. When I got his letter I simply decided to carry out the orders it contained. I should do the same again in similar circumstances.”
“Are you satisfied that the order was sent by Lord Beltham?”
“I have already told you, sir, that Lord Beltham had been a client of ours for several years; we have had many similar dealings with him. This last order which we received from him appeared to be entirely above suspicion: identical in form and in terms with the previous letters we had had from him.” He took a letter out of his pocketbook, and handed it to Juve.

===Chinese===

“四天前，警探先生，”年轻人开口道，“确切地说是在十二月十四日，伦敦邮递班次给我们送来了一封信；贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）是本公司多年的老主顾，他在信中指示我们于十二月十七日（也就是今天），前往莱韦尔街147号居尔恩先生的住所提取标有‘H. W. K.’以及1、2、3、4号标记的四件货物。他通知我们，门房已经接到指令准许我们提货。”
“你们原定把货物运往哪个地址？”
“我们的客户指示我们通过最近一班轮船将这些箱子发往约翰内斯堡（Johannesburg），他届时会派人去提取；按照惯例，我们需要随货附带两份发票单据；第三份发票单据则需寄往伦敦查令十字（Charing Cross）邮局63号信箱。”
朱夫在随身记事本上记下了查令十字63号信箱。
“收件人写的是什么姓名或缩写？”
“简简单单，就写‘贝尔特姆’。”
“很好。还有没有其他与此事相关的文件？”
“没有，别的我手头全没有，”乌兰先生说。
年轻人重新陷入了他那惯有的冷漠沉默之中。朱夫端详了他一两分钟，随后开口道：
“先生，三个星期前在巴黎流传的关于贝尔特姆勋爵的种种传闻，您想必有所耳闻吧。他在上流社交界是一位声名显赫的大人物。突然间他下落不明，他的夫人用尽一切手段让这起失踪案闹得满城风雨、人尽皆知。四天前当您收到贝尔特姆勋爵的亲笔信时，难道您就没感到哪怕一点惊讶吗？”
乌兰先生面对这个颇为棘手的问题丝毫没有乱了阵脚。
“我当然听说过贝尔特姆勋爵失踪的事，但我没有义务对此发表任何官方意见。先生，我是个生意人，不是刑警。贝尔特姆勋爵究竟是自愿失踪还是遭逢不测，并没有人请我做出裁断。当我接到他的来信时，我只是一心按照信中的指令办事。换作同等情况，我依然会这么做。”
“您确信那份托运指令当真是贝尔特姆勋爵寄出的吗？”
“先生，我刚才已经向您陈述过，贝尔特姆勋爵是本公司多年的老主顾；我们同他有过许多类似的业务往来。我们从他那里收到的这最后一单指令，表面看来完全无可挑剔：其格式与措辞同我们以往收到的那些亲笔信完全如出一辙。”他说着从皮夹里抽出一封信，递给了朱夫。

===Original===

“Here is the order, sir; if you think proper you can compare it with similar documents filed in our office in the rue d’Hauteville”; and as Juve was silent, Mr. Wooland, with the utmost dignity, enquired: “Is there any further occasion for me to remain here?”
“Thank you, sir, no,” Juve replied. Mr. Wooland made an almost imperceptible bow and was on the point of withdrawing when the detective stayed him once more. “M. Wooland, did you know Lord Beltham?”
“No, sir: Lord Beltham always sent us his orders by letter; once or twice he has spoken to us over the telephone, but he never came to our office, and I have never been to his house.”
“Thank you very much,” said Juve, and with a bow Mr. Wooland withdrew.
---
With meticulous care Juve replaced every article which he had moved during his investigations. He carefully shut the lid of the trunk, thus hiding the unhappy corpse from the curious eyes of the gendarme and the still terrified Mme. Doulenques. Then he leisurely buttoned his overcoat and spoke to the gendarme.
“Stay here until I send a man to relieve you; I am going to your superintendent now.” At the door he called the concierge. “Will you kindly go down before me, madame? Return to your lodge, and please do not say a word about what has happened to anyone whatever.”
“You can trust me, sir,” the worthy creature murmured, and Juve walked slowly away from the house with head bowed in thought.
There could be no doubt about it: the body in the trunk was that of Lord Beltham! Juve knew the Englishman quite well. But who was the murderer?
“Everything points to Gurn,” Juve thought, “and yet would an ordinary murderer have dared to commit such a crime as this? Am I letting my imagination run away with me again? I don’t know: but it seems to me that about this murder, committed in the very middle of Paris, in a crowded house where yet nobody heard or suspected anything, there is an audacity, a certainty of impunity, and above all a multiplicity of precautions, that are typical of the Fantômas manner!” He clenched his fists and an evil smile curled his lips as he repeated, like a threat, the name of that terrible and most mysterious criminal, of whose hellish influence he seemed to be conscious yet once again. “Fantômas! Fantômas! Did Fantômas really commit this murder?

===Chinese===

“这是那份指令，先生；如果您认为合适，大可将其与我们奥特维尔街办公室归档的类似文书进行笔迹核对；”见朱夫默不作声，乌兰先生神态极其庄重矜持地探询道：“我还有继续留在这里的必要吗？”
“谢谢您，先生，不用了，”朱夫回答。乌兰先生微微颔首致意，正准备转身离去，便衣警探再次叫住了他。“乌兰先生，您认识贝尔特姆勋爵本人吗？”
“不认识，先生：贝尔特姆勋爵向来都是通过书信向我们下达托运指令；有一两回他同我们在电话里沟通过，但他从未踏足过我们的办公室，我也从未登门拜访过他的府邸。”
“非常感谢，”朱夫说，乌兰先生欠身一鞠躬，退出了房间。
---
朱夫一丝不苟地将自己在勘查过程中动过的每一件物品恢复原位。他小心翼翼地合上大衣箱的箱盖，将那具可怜的遗体从巡警好奇的目光以及惊魂未定的杜朗克太太眼前遮蔽起来。随后他从容不迫地系好大衣扣子，对巡警吩咐道。
“留在这里守着，直到我派人来接你的班；我现在去见你们的警务总监。”在门口他招呼门房。“太太，劳驾您走在我前面下楼好吗？回您的门房去吧，请千万不要向任何人吐露半句这里发生的事情。”
“放心吧，先生，”这可敬的老妇人喃喃低语道，朱夫低头沉思着，缓缓踱出了大门。
事情已经毫无疑问了：箱子里的尸体正是贝尔特姆勋爵！朱夫对这位英国勋爵太熟悉了。但杀人凶手究竟是谁？
“一切线索全都指向居尔恩，”朱夫心想，“然而一个普通的杀人犯难道敢犯下如此骇人听闻的罪行吗？难道我又一次任由想象力信马由缰了吗？我说不准：但在我看来，在巴黎的心脏地带，在住满了房客的大楼里犯下这桩谋杀，居然没有人听见或怀疑分毫——这种肆无忌惮、对逃脱制裁的绝对笃定，尤其是那层层叠叠的周密防范，全都是芳托马斯作案手法的典型印记！”他攥紧了拳头，嘴角泛起一抹冷酷的苦笑，像在发出威胁一般，在唇齿间反复咀嚼着那个恐怖而神秘莫测的巨枭的名字，他仿佛又一次切实感受到了那来自地狱的阴森魔力。“芳托马斯！芳托马斯！这桩凶杀案真的出自芳托马斯之手吗？

===Original===

And if he did, shall I ever succeed in throwing light upon this new mystery, and learning the secret of that tragic room?”

===Chinese===

如果真系他所为，我到底能否拨开这团崭新的迷雾，彻底揭开那个血腥房间里的惊天秘辛？”

## VIII A Dreadful Confession / 第八章 可怕的自白
===Original===

While Juve was devoting his marvellous skill and incomparable daring to the elucidation of the new case with which the Criminal Investigation Department had entrusted him in Paris, things were marching at Beaulieu, where the whole machinery of the law was being set in motion for the discovery and arrest of Charles Rambert.
---
With a mighty clatter and racket Bouzille came down the slope and stopped before old mother Chiquard’s cottage. He arrived in his own equipage, and an extraordinary one it was!
Bouzille was mounted upon a tricycle of prehistoric design, with two large wheels behind and a small steering wheel in front, and a rusty handlebar from which all the plating was worn off. The solid rubber tyres which once had adorned the machine had worn out long ago, and were now replaced by twine twisted round the felloes of the wheels; this was forever fraying away and the wheels were fringed with a veritable lacework of string. Bouzille must have picked up this impossible machine for an old song at some local market, unless perhaps some charitable person gave it to him simply to get rid of it. He styled this tricycle his “engine,” and it was by no means the whole of his equipage. Attached to the tricycle by a stout rope was a kind of wicker perambulator on four wheels, which he called his “sleeping-car,” because he stored away in it all the bits of rag he picked up on his journeys, and also his very primitive bedding and the little piece of waterproof canvas under which he often slept in the open air. Behind the sleeping-car was a third vehicle, the restaurant-car, consisting of an old soap box mounted on four solid wooden wheels, which were fastened to the axles by huge conical bolts; in this he kept his provisions; lumps of bread and fat, bottles and vegetables, all mixed up in agreeable confusion. Bouzille made quite long journeys in this train of his, and was well known throughout the southwest of France. Often did the astonished population see him bent over his tricycle, with his pack on his back, pedalling with extraordinary rapidity down the hills, while the carriages behind him bumped and jumped over the inequalities in the surface of the road until it seemed impossible that they could retain their equilibrium.
Old mother Chiquard had recognised the cause of the racket.

===Chinese===

正当朱夫在巴黎倾注其惊人的才干与无与伦比的魄力，全力侦破刑事调查处委托给他的新案子时，博利厄城堡这边的调查也在加紧推进，法网正全面铺开，以搜寻并缉捕夏尔·朗贝尔。
---
伴随着一阵巨大的声响与喧闹，布齐耶沿着山坡滑下，在希卡尔老妈的农舍前停了下来。他是乘着自己的坐驾来的，那可真是一辆离奇古怪的坐驾！
布齐耶骑在一辆样式古旧的三轮车上，后边是两个大轮子，前边是一个小转向轮，车把锈迹斑斑，上面的镀层早已磨蚀殆尽。曾经装在车上的实心橡胶轮胎也早就不知所终，如今是用粗麻绳一圈圈缠在轮辋上取而代之；麻绳总是在不断磨损起毛，使得车轮边缘像是缀满了一圈货真价实的绳线蕾丝花边。布齐耶肯定是在某个乡镇集市上花极便宜的价钱淘来了这辆不可思议的破车，要不然就是哪位善心人为了甩掉累赘白送给他的。他把这辆三轮车戏称为自己的“机车头”，但这绝非他坐驾的全部。用一根粗绳系在三轮车后面的，是一种带有四个轮子的藤条婴儿车，他管它叫“卧铺车厢”，因为他在旅途中捡到的所有碎布烂衫，还有他那极其简陋的铺盖卷，以及他露宿荒野时常常遮身的一小块防水油布，统统收纳在里面。在卧铺车厢后面还有第三辆车，也就是“餐车”，那是一个架在四个实心木轮上的旧肥皂箱，木轮用粗大的锥形螺栓固定在车轴上；里面存放着他的口粮：大块的面包和猪油、瓶瓶罐罐和蔬菜，全都在一种令人愉悦的混乱中混杂在一起。布齐耶就是靠着这列自己的“列车”走南闯北，在法国西南部名闻遐迩。当地惊诧的乡民们经常能看到他弓着身子趴在三轮车上，背上驮着行囊，飞快地蹬着脚踏冲下山坡，而身后的几节车厢则在凹凸不平的路面上颠簸跳跃，晃晃悠悠，看起来仿佛随时都可能失去平衡散架。
希卡尔老妈早已听出了这番喧闹的缘由。

===Original===

The healthy life of the country had kept the old woman strong and active in spite of the eighty-three years that had passed over her head, and now she came to her door, armed with a broom, and hailed the tramp in angry, threatening tones.
“So it’s you, is it, you thief, you robber of the poor! It’s shocking, the way you spend your time in evil doing! What do you want now, pray?”
Slowly and sheepishly and with head bowed, Bouzille approached mother Chiquard, nervously looking out for a whack over the head with the broom the old lady held.
“Don’t be cross,” he pleaded when he could get in a word; “I want to come to an arrangement with you, mother Chiquard, if it can be done.”
“That’s all according,” said the old woman, eyeing the tramp with great mistrust; “I haven’t much faith in arrangements with you: rascals like you always manage to do honest folk.”
Mother Chiquard turned back into her cottage; it was no weather for her to stop out of doors, for a strong north wind was blowing, and that was bad for her rheumatism. Bouzille deliberately followed her inside and closed the door carefully behind him. Without ceremony he walked up to the hearth, where a scanty wood fire was burning, and put down his pack so as to be able to rub his hands more freely.
“Miserable weather, mother Chiquard!”
The obstinate old lady stuck to her one idea.
“If it isn’t miserable to steal my rabbit, this is the finest weather that ever I saw!”
“You make a lot of fuss about a trifle,” the tramp protested, “especially since you will be a lot the better by the arrangement I’m going to suggest.”
The notion calmed mother Chiquard a little, and she sat down on a form, while Bouzille took a seat upon the table.
“What do you mean?” the old woman enquired.
“Well,” said Bouzille, “I suppose your rabbit would have fetched a couple of shillings in the market; I’ve brought you two fowls that are worth quite eighteen-pence each, and if you will give me some dinner at twelve o’clock I will put in a good morning’s work for you.”
Mother Chiquard looked at the clock upon the wall; it was eight o’clock. The tramp’s proposal represented four hours’ work, which was not to be despised; but before striking the bargain she insisted on seeing the fowls.

===Chinese===

乡间健康的生活让这位老妇人尽管已八十三岁高龄，依然硬朗利落。此刻她手拿一把扫帚来到门前，用愤怒而威胁的口吻冲着这个流浪汉嚷道：
“原来是你啊，你这个偷穷人东西的贼骨头！你一天到晚净干坏事，真不知害臊！说吧，这会儿又想打什么主意？”
布齐耶慢吞吞、怯生生地低着头走近希卡尔大妈，神情紧张地防备着老太太手里那把随时可能冲脑门抡过来的扫帚。
“消消气嘛，”他趁机插上一句恳求道，“我想跟您商量笔交易，希卡尔大妈，要是能成的话。”
“那得看情况，”老太太用极其怀疑的目光打量着流浪汉，“我可不太相信能跟你做成什么交易，像你这样的无赖总有办法坑害老实人。”
希卡尔大妈转身走回屋里；外面的天气可不适合她在户外逗留，刮着凛冽的北风，对她的风湿病极不利。布齐耶大模大样地跟着她进了屋，随手仔细把门关好。他毫不客气地走到灶火旁——那里正烧着微弱的柴火——放下背上的行囊，以便能更自在地搓手取暖。
“鬼天气啊，希卡尔大妈！”
倔老太太依然咬住先前的事不放。
“要是偷我的兔子不算遭罪，那今天可就是我这辈子见过的最好天气了！”
“为这么点鸡毛蒜皮的小事犯得着大惊小怪嘛，”流浪汉辩解道，“更何况，我提的这笔交易能让您大赚一笔呢。”
这番话让希卡尔大妈稍微平静了些，她在一条长凳上坐下，而布齐耶则大大咧咧地坐到了桌上。
“你到底想说什么？”老妇人问道。
“是这样，”布齐耶说，“我估摸着您那只兔子在集市上能卖两先令；我给您带了两只母鸡，每只少说也能值十八便士。要是您能在十二点管我一顿午饭，我就给您好好干上一早晨的活。”
希卡尔大妈看了看墙上的挂钟，刚好八点整。流浪汉的提议意味着能干四个小时的活，这倒不容小觑；但在成交之前，她坚持要先验一验母鸡。

===Original===

These were extracted from the pack; tied together by the feet, and half suffocated, the unfortunate creatures were not much to look at, but they would be cheap, which was worth considering.
“Where did you get these fowls?” mother Chiquard asked, more as a matter of form than anything else, for she was pretty sure they had not been honestly come by.
Bouzille put his finger to his lip.
“Hush!” he murmured gently; “that’s a secret between me and the poultry. Well, is it a go?” and he held out his hand to the old lady.
She hesitated a moment and then made up her mind.
“It’s a go,” she said, putting her horny fingers into the man’s hard palm. “You shall chop me some wood first, and then go down to the river for the rushes I have put in to soak; they must be well swollen by this time.”
Bouzille was glad to have made it up with mother Chiquard, and pleased at the prospect of a good dinner at midday; he opened the cottage door, and leisurely arranged a few logs within range of the axe with which he was going to split them; mother Chiquard began to throw down some grain to the skinny and famished fowls that fluttered round her.
“I thought you were in prison, Bouzille,” she said, “over stealing my rabbit, and also over that affair at the château of Beaulieu.”
“Oh, those are two quite different stories,” Bouzille replied. “You mustn’t mix them up together on any account. As for the château job, every tramp in the district has been run in: I was copped by M’sieu Morand the morning after the murder; he took me into the kitchen of the château and Mme. Louise gave me something to eat. There was another chap there with me, a man named François Paul who doesn’t belong to these parts; between you and me, I thought he was an evil-looking customer who might easily have been the murderer, but it doesn’t do to say that sort of thing, and I’m glad I held my tongue because they let him go. I heard no more about it, and five days later I went back to Brives to attend the funeral of the Marquise de Langrune. That was a ceremony if you like! The church all lighted up, and all the nobility from the neighbourhood present. I didn’t lose my time, for I knew all the gentlemen and ladies and took the best part of sixteen shillings, and the blind beggar who sits on the steps of the church called me all the names he could put his tongue to!”

===Chinese===

母鸡从包裹里被掏了出来；双脚捆在一起，憋得半死不活，这两只可怜的扁毛畜生看起来卖相不佳，但价钱便宜，这倒很划算。
“你从哪儿弄来的这些母鸡？”希卡尔大妈例行公事般问了一句，因为她心里很清楚这些鸡绝不是靠正经道儿弄来的。
布齐耶把一根手指竖在唇边。
“嘘！”他轻声嘀咕道，“这是我和母鸡之间的秘密。行不行，成交吗？”说着他向老太太伸出了手。
她犹豫了片刻，随即下定了决心。
“成交，”她说，把粗糙布满老茧的手指放进了流浪汉坚硬的掌心里。“你先去给我劈点柴火，然后再去河边把浸泡在水里的芦苇捞上来；这会儿芦苇肯定泡得发涨了。”
能跟希卡尔大妈重归于好，又能指望中午吃上一顿美美的饱饭，布齐耶十分高兴；他拉开屋门，从容不迫地把几根圆木摆在斧头能劈到的范围之内；希卡尔大妈则抓起一把谷粒扔向扑腾在身边的瘦骨嶙峋、饥肠辘辘的母鸡。
“布齐耶，我还以为你蹲大牢去了呢，”她说，“因为偷我兔子的事，还有博利厄城堡出的那桩惨案。”
“哎呀，那完全是风马牛不相及的两码事，”布齐耶回答，“您可千万不能把它们混为一谈。至于城堡的那桩案子，附近的流浪汉全都被抓进去了。命案发生后的第二天早上，莫朗先生就把我逮住了；他把我带进城堡的厨房，露易丝太太给了我一些吃的。当时厨房里还有另一个家伙跟我关在一起，一个叫弗朗索瓦·保罗的人，不是本地人；私下跟您说，我觉得那家伙长得贼眉鼠眼，极有可能就是杀人凶手，但这种话可不能乱讲，幸好我当时管住了自己的舌头，因为后来警察把他给放了。后来的事我就没听说了，五天之后我回布里夫去参加了德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人的葬礼。那场面可真叫气派！教堂里灯火通明，附近所有的贵族全都出席了。我可没白去，那里的老爷太太我都认识，一共讨到了差不多十六先令，坐在教堂台阶上的瞎子乞丐嫉妒得破口大骂，把能想到的脏话全冲我喷了个遍！”

===Original===

The tramp’s story interested mother Chiquard mightily, but her former idea still dominated her mind.
“So they didn’t punish you for stealing my rabbit?”
“Well, they did and they didn’t,” said Bouzille, scratching his head. “M’sieu Morand, who is an old friend of mine, took me to the lockup at Saint-Jaury, and I was to have gone next morning to the court at Brives, where I know the sentence for stealing domestic animals is three weeks. That would have suited me all right just now, for the prison at Brives is quite new and very comfortable, but that same night Sergeant Doucet shoved another man into the clink with, me at Saint-Jaury, a raving lunatic who started smashing everything up, and tried to tear my eyes out. Naturally, I gave him as good as I got, and the infernal row we made brought in the sergeant. I told him the chap wanted to throttle me, and he was nonplussed, for he couldn’t do anything with the man, who was fairly mad, and couldn’t leave me alone there with him. So at last the sergeant took me to one side and told me to hook it and not let him see me again. So there it is.”
While he was chattering like this Bouzille had finished the job set him by mother Chiquard, who meanwhile had peeled some potatoes and poured the soup on the bread. He wiped his brow, and seeing the brimming pot, gave a meaning wink and licked his tongue.
“I’ll make the fire up, mother Chiquard; I’m getting jolly hungry.”
“So you ought to be, at half-past eleven,” the old woman replied. “Yes, we’ll have dinner, and you can get the rushes out afterwards.”
Mother Chiquard was the proud freeholder of a little cottage that was separated from the bank of the Dordogne by the high road between Martel and Montvalent. Round the cottage she had a small orchard, and opposite, through a gap in the trees, was a view of the yellow waters of the Dordogne and the chain of hills that stood up on the far side of the river. Living here summer and winter, with her rabbits and her fowls, mother Chiquard earned a little money by making baskets; but she was crippled with rheumatism, and was miserable every time she had to go down to the river to pull out the bundles of rushes that she put there to soak; the work meant not merely an hour’s paddling in mud up to the knees, but also a fortnight’s acute agony and at least a shilling for medicine.

===Chinese===

流浪汉的这番讲述让希卡尔大妈听得津津有味，不过她脑子里最在意的还是先前那件事。
“这么说，他们偷了我兔子就没罚你？”
“哎，罚了也算没罚，”布齐耶挠了挠头说，“莫朗先生是我的老相识了，他把我送到了圣若里的拘留所，原本打算第二天早晨把我解送到布里夫的法庭，我知道偷窃家畜得判三个星期。这倒挺合我眼下的心意，因为布里夫的监狱刚翻修过，舒服得很。可就在那天晚上，杜塞警长把另一个人也塞进了圣若里的小牢房跟我关在一起，那是个发狂的疯子，一进来就乱砸东西，还企图抠瞎我的眼睛。我自然不甘示弱跟他对打起来，我俩闹出的巨大动静把警长引了过来。我告诉警长那家伙想掐死我，警长也犯了难，因为他拿那个彻底发了疯的人毫无办法，又不能把我和他单独留在里面。所以最后警长把我拉到一边，叫我赶紧滚蛋，别再让他看见。事情就是这么回事。”
布齐耶一边嘴里喋喋不休地讲着，一边干完了希卡尔大妈交代的活计；在此期间，老太太削了些土豆，把热汤浇在面包上。他擦了擦额头的汗水，看到热气腾腾的汤锅，意味深长地眨了眨眼，舔了舔舌头。
“希卡尔大妈，我去添点柴火把火烧旺；我肚子可真饿扁了。”
“都十一点半了，自然该饿了，”老妇人回答说，“行，咱们开饭，吃完饭你再去捞芦苇。”
希卡尔大妈是一栋小农舍的自豪主人，农舍与多尔多涅河之间只隔着一条连接马特尔与蒙瓦朗的公路。农舍四周环绕着她的一片小果园，正对面穿过树林的空隙，可以眺望多尔多涅河浑黄的河水以及耸立在河对岸连绵起伏的山丘。一年四季，无论冬夏，希卡尔大妈都守在这里，伴着她的兔子和母鸡，靠编织草筐赚点微薄的零花钱；然而她饱受风湿病的折磨，每次去河边把浸泡在水里的成捆芦苇拖上岸时，都痛苦不堪；那活儿不仅要在没膝的烂泥里蹚上一个小时，还会带来长达半个月的剧烈阵痛，买药至少还得花上一先令。

===Original===

So whoever wanted to make a friend of the old woman only had to volunteer to get the rushes out for her.
As he ate, Bouzille told mother Chiquard of his plans for the coming spring.
“Yes,” he said, “since I’m not doing any time this winter I’m going to undertake a long journey.” He stopped munching for a second and paused for greater effect. “I am going to Paris, mother Chiquard!” Then, seeing that the old lady was utterly dumbfounded by the announcement, he leant his elbows on the table and looked at her over his empty plate. “I’ve always had one great desire﻿—to see the Eiffel Tower: that idea has been running in my head for the last fifteen years. Well, now I’m going to gratify the wish. I hear you can get a room in Paris for twopence-halfpenny a night, and I can manage that.”
“How long will it take you to get there?” enquired the old woman, immensely impressed by Bouzille’s venturesome plan.
“That depends,” said the tramp. “I must allow quite three months with my train. Of course if I got run in on the way for stealing, or as a rogue and vagabond, I couldn’t say how long it would take.”
The meal was over, and the old woman was quietly washing up her few plates and dishes, when Bouzille, who had gone down to the river to fetch the rushes, suddenly called shrilly to mother Chiquard.
“Mother Chiquard! Mother Chiquard! Come and look! Just fancy, I’ve earned twenty-five francs!”
The summons was so urgent, and the news so amazing, that the old lady left her house and hurried across the road to the river bank. She saw the tramp up to his waist in the water, trying, with a long stick, to drag out of the current a large object which was not identifiable at a first glance. To all her enquiries Bouzille answered with the same delighted cry, “I have earned twenty-five francs,” too intent on bringing his fishing job to a successful issue even to turn round. A few minutes later he emerged dripping from the water, towing a large bundle to the safety of the bank. Mother Chiquard drew nearer, greatly interested, and then recoiled with a shriek of horror.
Bouzille had fished out a corpse!
It was a ghastly sight: the body of a very young man, almost a boy, with long, slender limbs; the face was so horribly swollen and torn as to be shapeless. One leg was almost entirely torn from the trunk.

===Chinese===

因此，谁要是想讨这位老太太的欢心，只要自告奋勇替她把水里的芦苇捞出来就行了。
布齐耶一边吃着饭，一边跟希卡尔大妈聊起自己来年春天的打算。
“对啊，”他说，“既然今年冬天不用坐牢，我就打算出一趟远门。”他停下咀嚼片刻，特意停顿了一下以增强效果。“我要去巴黎，希卡尔大妈！”接着，看到老太太被这番宣告惊得目瞪口呆，他把双肘撑在桌上，越过空盘子凝视着她。“我一直有个大心愿——想亲眼看看埃菲尔铁塔，这个念头在我脑子里盘旋了足足十五年。如今，我终于要去实现这个愿望了。我听说在巴黎住一宿只要两个半便士，这笔钱我还是掏得起的。”
“你走到那儿得花多长时间？”老妇人问道，布齐耶这番大胆的计划让她惊叹不已。
“那得看情况，”流浪汉说，“带着我这列‘车’，少说也得预备足足三个月。当然，要是半道上因为偷东西或者以游荡流浪罪被关进局子，那就说不准得耽搁多久了。”
饭吃完了，老太太正在静静地洗刷着为数不多的几个盘碟，就在这时，跑到河边去取芦苇的布齐耶突然尖声冲着希卡尔大妈大喊大叫起来。
“希卡尔大妈！希卡尔大妈！快来看啊！天哪，我挣了二十五法郎！”
这呼喊声如此急迫，消息又这般惊人，老太太赶忙放下手里的活计跑出屋，穿过公路奔向河岸。她只见这个流浪汉齐腰深地浸在冰凉的河水里，正拿着一根长木棍，拼命试图从湍急的水流中把一个乍看辨不清模样的庞然大物拽出来。面对希卡尔大妈的所有询问，布齐耶只顾兴奋地大喊大叫：“我挣了二十五法郎！”他一心要把这个捕捞大业干到底，甚至连头都顾不上回一下。几分钟后，他浑身湿漉漉地从水里爬了上来，把一大包东西拖到了安全的河岸上。希卡尔大妈好奇地凑近前去，随即发出一声惊恐万状的尖叫，吓得连连后退。
布齐耶居然从水里捞上来了一具尸体！
那是一副极其可怖的景象：死者是个年纪极轻的小伙子，几乎还是个半大少年，四肢细长；整张脸被水泡撕扯得面目全非，变形得不成模样。其中一条腿几乎完全从躯干上撕裂开来。

===Original===

Through rents in the clothing strips of flesh were trailing, blue and discoloured by their long immersion in the water. On the shoulders and back of the neck were bruises and stains of blood. Bouzille, who was quite unaffected by the ghastliness of the object and still kept up his gay chant “I have fished up a body, I’ve earned twenty-five francs,” observed that there were large splinters of wood, rotten from long immersion, sticking in some of the wounds. He stood up and addressed mother Chiquard who, white as a sheet, was watching him in silence.
“I see what it is: he must have got caught in some mill wheel: that’s what has cut him up like that.”
Mother Chiquard shook her head uneasily.
“Suppose it was a murder! That would be an ugly business!”
“It’s no good my looking at him any more,” said Bouzille. “I don’t recognise him; he’s not from the country.”
“That’s sure,” the old woman agreed. “He’s dressed like a gentleman.”
The two looked at each other in silence. Bouzille was not nearly so complacent as he had been a few minutes before. The reward of twenty-five francs prompted him to go at once to inform the police; the idea of a crime, suggested by the worthy woman, disturbed him greatly, and all the more because he thought it was well founded. Another murder in the neighbourhood would certainly vex the authorities, and put the police in a bad temper. Bouzille knew from experience that the first thing people do after a tragedy is to arrest all the tramps, and that if the police are at all crotchety they always contrive to get the tramps sentenced for something else. He had had a momentary inclination to establish his winter quarters in prison, but since then he had formed the plan of going to Paris, and liberty appealed to him more. He reached a sudden decision.
“I’ll punt him back into the water!”
But mother Chiquard stayed him, just as he was putting his idea into execution.
“You mustn’t: suppose somebody has seen us already? It would land us in no end of trouble!”
Half an hour later, convinced that it was his melancholy duty, Bouzille left two-thirds of his train in mother Chiquard’s custody, got astride his prehistoric tricycle and slowly pedalled off towards Saint-Jaury.
---
New Year’s Day is a melancholy and a tedious one for everybody whose public or private relations do not make it an exceptionally interesting one.

===Chinese===

透过残破撕裂的衣衫，一缕缕烂肉耷拉悬挂着，因长时间浸泡在河水中而泛出青乌发白的不祥颜色。肩膀和颈部后侧布满了大片瘀伤和血污。布齐耶对这具骇人的残骸浑不在意，嘴里依旧欢快地哼着小调：“捞到一具尸体，我挣了二十五法郎！”他注意到在几处伤口里还深深扎着大块被河水泡烂的粗糙木片。他直起身子，对着脸色惨白如纸、在一旁默默注视的老太太说：
“我瞧出是怎么回事了：这可怜虫准是被某个水磨坊的轮子给卡住了，才会被绞成这么一副破烂模样。”
希卡尔大妈忧心忡忡地摇了摇头。
“万一是一桩凶杀案呢！那可就麻烦大了！”
“我再怎么看他也认不出来，”布齐耶说，“我根本不认得他；他绝不是这附近乡下的人。”
“那倒是真的，”老太太赞同道，“他的打扮像个有钱人家的少爷。”
两人面面相觑，陷入了沉默。布齐耶此刻的心情远没有几分钟前那么得意自在了。二十五法郎的赏金驱使着他立刻去向警察局报告；但老太太关于凶杀命案的推测又让他心神不宁，尤其是他觉得这推测极有可能八九不离十。本地再添一桩命案肯定会激怒当局，让警察们焦躁狂暴。布齐耶凭经验深知，一旦出了凶杀惨案，人们干的第一件事就是把所有的流浪汉通统抓起来，而警察要是脾气暴躁，总能罗织出其他罪名把流浪汉判刑关押。先前他倒确实有过在监狱里度过寒冬的念头，但打那以后他萌生了前往巴黎的宏愿，自由对他来说变得更加诱人。他突然打定了主意。
“我干脆把他重新推回河里去算了！”
但在他正准备动手执行这一打算时，希卡尔大妈制止了他。
“千万使不得：万一刚才已经有人瞧见咱们了呢？那非把咱们卷进天大的麻烦里不可！”
半个钟头后，深感此举乃是自己无可推卸的凄惨差事，布齐耶将他的“车列”的三分之二留给希卡尔大妈看管，自己跨上那辆样式古旧的三轮车，慢慢蹬着朝圣若里方向骑去。
---
对于那些无论在公共交往还是私人关系中都没有格外引人注目之事的人而言，元旦这一天总是既凄凉又乏味的。

===Original===

There is the alteration in the date, for one thing, which is provocative of thought, and there is the enforced idleness for another, coming upon energetic folk like a temporary paralysis and leaving them nothing but meditation wherewith to employ themselves.
Juve, comfortably installed in his own private study, was realising this just as evening was falling on this first of January. He was a confirmed bachelor, and for several years had lived in a little flat on the fifth floor of an old house in the rue Bonaparte. He had not gone out today, but though he was resting he was not idle. For a whole month past he had been wholly engrossed in his attempt to solve the mystery surrounding the two cases on which he was engaged, the Beltham case, and the Langrune case, and his mind was leisurely revolving round them now as he sat in his warm room before a blazing wood fire, and watched the blue smoke curl up in rings towards the ceiling. The two cases were very dissimilar, and yet his detective instinct persuaded him that although they differed in details their conception and execution emanated not only from one single brain but also from one hand. He was convinced that he was dealing with a mysterious and dangerous individual, and that while he himself was out in the open he was fighting a concealed and invisible adversary; he strove to give form and substance to the adversary, and the name of Fantômas came into his mind. Fantômas! What might Fantômas be doing now, and, if he had a real existence, as the detective most firmly believed, how was he spending New Year’s Day?
A sharp ring at the bell startled him from his chair, and not giving his manservant time to answer it, he went himself to the door and took from a messenger a telegram which he hastily tore open and read:

> Have found in the Dordogne drowned body of young man, face unrecognisable, from description possibly Charles Rambert. Please consider situation and wire course you will take.

===Chinese===

一来，日期的更迭总会引发人们的遐思，二来，被迫的无所事事就像暂时的瘫痪一样降临在精力充沛的人身上，除了陷入冥思苦想之外，别无他事可供消遣。
正当这元旦的一月一日黄昏降临之际，舒适地待在自己私人书房里的朱夫对此深有体会。他是个坚定的单身汉，多年来一直住在波拿巴街一栋老宅五楼的一套小公寓里。今天他并未出门，不过虽说是在休息，脑子却一刻也没有闲着。在过去整整一个月里，他全神贯注地试图解开困扰着他的两起案件的重重迷雾——贝尔特姆案与朗格吕纳案。此刻他坐在温暖的房间里，面对着燃烧得噼啪作响的柴火，看着蓝色的烟圈缓缓升向天花板，思绪在两桩案子间从容盘旋。这两桩案子大相径庭，然而他敏锐的刑侦直觉却让他坚信，尽管细节迥异，但其构思与执行不仅出自同一颗大脑，而且出自同一只黑手。他深信自己是在和一个神秘莫测、凶残危险的人物周旋，自己身处明处，而对手却潜藏在暗处、无影无形；他竭力想让这个隐秘的对手显现出具体的形态与轮廓，芳托马斯这个名字随之跃入了他的脑海。芳托马斯！芳托马斯眼下会在做什么呢？如果他当真确有其人——正如这位警探深信不疑的那样——他又会如何度过这元旦佳节呢？
一阵尖锐的门铃声将他从座椅上惊醒，甚至没等仆人前去开门，他便亲自快步走到门口，从一名送信人手中接过了一封电报，他急忙撕开电报阅览起来：

> 在多尔多涅河中发现一具溺亡年轻男子尸体，面容无法辨认，从外貌特征看可能系夏尔·朗贝尔。请审度情况并电复后续处置方案。

===Original===

The telegram had been handed in at Brives and was signed by M. de Presles.
“Something fresh at last,” the detective muttered. “Drowned in the Dordogne, and face unrecognisable! I wonder if it really is Charles Rambert?”
Since M. Etienne Rambert and his son had disappeared so unaccountably, the detective naturally had formulated mentally several hypotheses, but he had arrived at no conclusion which really satisfied his judgment. But though their flight had not surprised him greatly, he had been rather surprised that the police had not been able to find any trace of them, for rightly or wrongly Juve credited them with a good deal of cleverness and power. So it was by no means unreasonable to accept the death of the fugitives as explanation of the failure of the police to find them. However, this was a fresh development of the case, and he was about to draft a reply to M. de Presles when once more the bell rang sharply.
This time Juve did not move, but listened while his man spoke to the visitor. It was an absolute rule of Juve’s never to receive visitors at his flat. If anyone wanted to see him on business, he was to be found almost every day in his office at headquarters about eleven in the morning; to a few people he was willing to give appointments at a quiet and discreet little café in the boulevard Saint-Michel; but he invited no one to his own rooms except one or two of his own relations from the country, and even they had to be provided with a password before they could obtain admission. So now, to all the entreaties of the caller, Juve’s servant stolidly replied with the assurance that his master would see no one; yet the visitor’s insistence was so great that at last the servant was prevailed upon to bring in his card, albeit with some fear as to the consequences for himself. But to his extreme relief and surprise, Juve, when he had read the name engraved upon the card, said sharply:
“Bring him in here at once!”
And in another couple of seconds M. Etienne Rambert was in the room!
The old gentleman who had fled so mysteriously a few days before, taking with him the son over whom so dread a charge was hanging, bowed deferentially to the detective, with the pitiful mien of one who is crushed beneath the burden of misfortune.

===Chinese===

这封电报是从布里夫发出的，署名为德·普雷勒先生。
“总算有了新动静，”警探喃喃自语道，“淹死在多尔多涅河里，面容无法辨认！当真会是夏尔·朗贝尔吗？”
自从埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生携子神秘失踪以来，警探在脑海中自然构想过数种假说，但没有任何一种能够真正让他心悦诚服。尽管两人的逃亡并未让他感到意外，但警方竟然未能查出两人的丝毫蛛丝马迹，这一点倒颇令他诧异，因为无论对错，朱夫都认为他们具备相当的心计与手段。因此，将逃亡者的死亡视为警方搜捕无果的合理解释，未尝不是一种合乎情理的推论。然而，这无疑是案情的一个全新进展。正当他准备起草给德·普雷勒先生的复电时，门铃再次急促地响了起来。
这一次朱夫没有起身，而是静听仆人与来访者的交谈。朱夫向来有一条铁律，那就是绝不在自己的私人寓所接见来客。若有人因公事找他，通常每天上午十一点左右都能在警察总局的办公室里找到他；对极少数人，他愿意相约在圣米歇尔大道上一家幽静低调的小咖啡馆里碰头；但他从不邀请任何人踏入自己的寓所，除了一两位从乡下来的亲戚，而且即便亲戚也得先对上暗号才能获准进门。因此此刻，面对来客的一再恳求，朱夫的仆人只是冷冰冰地一口回绝，坚称主人概不见客；然而来客态度极其坚决，以至于仆人最终不得不硬着头皮把他的名片送了进来，尽管仆人心里十分担心会招致主人的责罚。然而令仆人极度宽慰又大为惊诧的是，朱夫在看清名片上镌刻的名字后，当即厉声吩咐道：
“立刻请他进来！”
不出两秒钟，埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生就走进了房间！
这位几天前神秘出逃、带走了身负重重可怕嫌疑的儿子的老绅士，毕恭毕敬地向警探躬身致意，浑身上下透出一副被厄运重担彻底压垮的可怜神态。

===Original===

His features were drawn, his face bore the stamp of deepest grief, and in his hand he held an evening paper, which in his agitation he had crumpled almost into a ball.
“Tell me, sir, if it is true,” he said in low trembling tones. “I have just read that.”
Juve pointed to a chair, took the paper mechanically, and smoothing it out, read, below a large headline, “Is this a sequel to the Beaulieu Crime?” a story similar to that he had just gathered from M. de Presles’ telegram.
Juve contemplated M. Etienne Rambert in silence for a few minutes, and then, without replying directly to his visitor’s first question, asked him a question in that quiet voice of his, the wonderful indifferent tonelessness of which concealed the least clue to his inmost thoughts.
“Why do you come to me, sir?”
“To find out, sir,” the old man answered.
“To find out what?”
“If that poor drowned corpse is﻿—my son’s: is my poor Charles!”
“It is rather you who can tell me, sir,” said Juve, impassive as ever.
There was a pause. Despite his emotion, M. Rambert seemed to be thinking deeply. Suddenly he appeared to make an important decision, and raising his eyes to the detective he spoke very slowly:
“Have pity, sir, on a brokenhearted father. Listen to me: I have a dreadful confession to make!”
Juve drew his chair close to M. Etienne Rambert.
“I am listening,” he said gently, and M. Etienne Rambert began his “dreadful confession.”

===Chinese===

他的面容憔悴不堪，脸上深深烙印着极度的悲戚，手中紧攥着一份晚报，由于情绪过于激动，报纸几乎被他揉搓成了一团纸球。
“请您告诉我，先生，这到底是不是真的，”他用颤抖的低沉声音说道，“我刚刚读到了这个。”
朱夫指了指一把椅子，机械地接过报纸抚平。只见在一条赫然醒目的大标题下面写着：“这会是博利厄惨案的下文吗？”报道的内容与他刚刚从德·普雷勒先生电报中获悉的情节如出一辙。
朱夫默不作声地注视了埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生好几分钟，随后并没有直接回答客人的第一个问题，而是用他那贯常平静的语调反问了一句，那副令人惊叹的漫不经心、毫无起伏的淡漠语气，掩藏了他内心最细微的心绪流露。
“先生，您为何来找我？”
“为了查明真相，先生，”老人回答。
“查明什么？”
“那具可怜的溺水尸体到底是不是……我儿子，是不是我那可怜的夏尔！”
“先生，这个问题恐怕该由您来告诉我才是，”朱夫神色如常，依旧波澜不惊。
一阵沉默。尽管情绪激动，朗贝尔先生似乎陷入了沉思。突然间，他仿佛下定了某个重大决心，抬起双眼注视着警探，一字一顿地缓缓说道：
“先生，请怜悯一位伤心欲绝的父亲吧。听我说：我有一桩可怕的隐情必须坦白！”
朱夫把椅子拉得离埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生更近了些。
“我洗耳恭听，”他温和地说道，埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生随即开始了他那番“可怕的自白”。

## IX All for Honour / 第九章 一切为了名誉
===Original===

Society had mustered in force at the Cahors Law Courts, where the Assizes were about to be held. Hooting motorcars and antiquated coaches drawn by pursy horses were arriving every minute, bringing gentry from the great houses in the neighbourhood, squireens and well-to-do country people, prosperous farmers and jolly wine-growers, all of them determined not to miss “the trial” that was causing such immense excitement because the principal figure in it was well known as a friend of one of the oldest families in those parts; and because he was not merely a witness, nor even the victim, but actually the defendant in the case, although he had been admitted to bail in the interval by order of the court.
Compared with those of large towns, this court room at Cahors was small, but it was filled by a considerable and most select crowd. Quiet greetings and low-toned conversation were freely exchanged, but there was an air of melancholy about every person present, and it was obvious that they were drawn there by no mere curiosity or desire for horrid details, but by legitimate interest in the development of great drama.
One of the leading heroines in the case was pointed out with particular sympathy.
“That’s Thérèse Auvernois, over there in the first row! The President of the Court gave her that seat; the officer who took the card of admission over to Querelles told me so.”
“That’s where Mme. de Vibray lives, isn’t it?”
“Yes: she is sitting next to Thérèse now: that pretty woman in grey. Since Mme. de Langrune’s death she has kept the child with her, thinking, very rightly, that it would be too painful for her to be at Beaulieu. The family council have appointed President Bonnet temporary guardian of Thérèse. He is that tall, thin man over there, talking to the steward, Dollon.”
The Baronne de Vibray turned affectionately to Thérèse, who was looking dreadfully pale in her long mourning veil.
“Are you sure this won’t tire you too much, dear? Shall we go outside for a little while?”
“Oh, no, please do not worry about me,” Thérèse replied. “Indeed I shall be all right.”
President Bonnet sat by the two ladies.

===Chinese===

卡奥尔重罪法庭（Cahors Law Courts）即将开庭审判，城中各界人士纷纷倾巢而出。鸣着喇叭的汽车与由气喘吁吁的马匹拉着的古旧马车接踵而至，送来了附近显赫庄园的贵族乡绅、小乡绅、富足的乡民、殷实的农场主以及快活的葡萄园种植主。人人都不愿错过这桩在当地掀起轩然大波的“世纪大审判”，因为本案的核心人物向来被公认为当地最古老望族之一的至交挚友；而且他不仅不是普通的证人，甚至也不是受害人，而是本案的被告，尽管在此期间他曾依法院指令获准保释。
相较于大城市的法庭，卡奥尔的这间审判庭算不上宽敞，但此刻却坐满了数量可观且极有身份地位的旁听人群。人们低声致意，轻言交谈，每个人的脸上都笼罩着一层肃穆而忧郁的神情，显而易见，吸引他们来到这里的绝非低级的好奇心，也不是对血腥细节的猎奇窥探，而是对这一重大悲剧事态发展的合情关切。
案中的一位主要女主角引来了格外同情的瞩目。
“瞧第一排那儿，那位就是泰蕾兹·奥弗努瓦！法庭庭长特意给她留了那个位子；把旁听证送到凯雷勒去的法警亲口告诉我的。”
“凯雷勒是德·维布雷男爵夫人住的地方，对吧？”
“没错，她此刻正挨着泰蕾兹坐着呢，就是那位穿灰衣的漂亮妇人。自从德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人过世后，她便把这孩子接到了身边照料，大家都觉得她这样做十分妥当，毕竟让孩子继续留在博利厄城堡实在太触景生情、太痛苦了。家族会议已经指定邦内庭长担任泰蕾兹的临时监护人。就是那边那个又高又瘦的男人，此刻正跟总管多隆说话的那位。”
德·维布雷男爵夫人满含慈爱地转向身旁的长戴黑色丧纱、面色惨白如纸的泰蕾兹。
“亲爱的，你确定待在这里不会太吃不消吗？要不咱们先去外面待一会儿？”
“噢，不用，请您千万别为我操心，”泰蕾兹轻声回答，“我真的撑得住。”
邦内庭长走过来，坐在了两位女士身旁。

===Original===

He had been engaged solemnly exchanging bows with everyone in the court room whom he considered it flattering to himself to know; now he took part in the conversation, and displayed his special knowledge by explaining the constitution of the court and pointing out where the clerk sat, and where the public prosecutor sat, and where the jury sat, all at great length and much to the interest of the people near him: with, however, one exception; a man dressed entirely in black, with his head half buried in the huge collar of a travelling ulster, and dark glasses over his eyes, appeared to be vastly bored by the old magistrate’s disquisition. Juve﻿—for it was he﻿—knew too much about legal procedure to require explanations from President Bonnet.
Suddenly a thrill ran through the room and conversation stopped abruptly. M. Etienne Rambert had just walked down the gangway in the court to the seat reserved for him, just in front of the witness box and close to a kind of rostrum in which Maître Dareuil, an old member of the Cahors Bar, immediately took his place. M. Etienne Rambert was very pale, but it was obvious that he was by no means overwhelmed by the fatality overhanging him. He was, indeed, a fine figure as he took his seat and mechanically passed his hand through his long white curls, flinging them back and raising his head almost as if in defiance of the inquisitive crowd that was gazing at him.
Almost immediately after he had taken his seat a door was thrown open and the jury filed in, and then a black-gowned usher came forward and shrilly called for silence.
“Stand up, gentlemen! Hats off, please! Gentlemen, the Court!”
With solemn, measured steps, and heads bent as if absorbed in profoundest meditation, the judges slowly proceeded to their seats. The president formally declared the court open, whereupon the clerk rose immediately to read the indictment.
The Clerk of the Court at Cahors was a most excellent man, but modesty was his distinguishing characteristic and his chief desire appeared to be to shun responsibility, figure as little prominently as possible, and even escape observation altogether. Assizes were not often held at Cahors, and he had had few occasions to read an indictment as tragic as this present one, with the result that he lacked confidence now.

===Chinese===

他先前一直在煞有介事地同法庭里每一位他认为结识会让自己脸上有光的体面人物频频点头致意；此刻他加入了女士们的交谈，极力炫耀自己的专业学识，滔滔不绝地解释法庭的构成人员，指点着法官书记官坐在哪儿、公诉人坐在哪儿、陪审团又坐在哪儿。周围的人都听得兴致勃勃，不过倒有一人例外：一个浑身黑衣的男子，大半个脑袋缩在宽大的旅行大衣立领里，脸上架着一副深色墨镜，似乎对这位老法官的夸夸其谈感到极度厌烦。此人正是朱夫——凭他对司法诉讼程序的谙熟，哪里用得着邦内庭长在这里班门弄斧。
突然间，人群中泛起一阵轻微的骚动，交谈声戛然而止。埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生正沿着审判庭中央的通道缓缓走向为他特设的席位，就在证人席正前方，紧挨着卡奥尔律师公会的资深名宿达勒伊律师落座的讲台。埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生面色惨白，但显然丝毫没有被笼罩在头顶的厄运所压垮。当他坐下时，风度依旧令人肃然起敬；他习惯性地用手理了理垂落的长长白鬈发，将发丝向后一甩，昂起头来，神情间几乎带着对周围那些好奇打量他的围观者的凛然蔑视。
就在他刚刚落座后不久，侧门赫然洞开，陪审员们鱼贯而入，接着一位身披黑袍的法庭执事快步上前，尖声高唱肃静。
“全体起立，诸位先生！请脱帽！肃静，法官入席！”
法官们迈着庄严肃穆、沉稳有力的步伐，微微垂首，仿佛正凝神陷入最深沉的思索，缓缓走向审判席。审判长郑重宣布开庭，书记官随即起身宣读起诉书。
卡奥尔法庭的这位书记官为人敦厚纯良，但谦卑怯懦却是他的鲜明本色，他平生最大的心愿似乎就是推卸责任、尽量不引人注目，甚至最好能彻底隐形于众人的视线之外。重罪法庭在卡奥尔并不常设，他极少有机会宣读像眼前这桩如此血腥悲惨的命案起诉书，因此此刻显得底气全无，惶恐不安。

===Original===
