He read in a toneless, monotonous voice, so nervously and softly that nobody in the body of the court could hear a word he said, and even the jury were obliged to lean their elbows on the desk before them and make an ear trumpet of their hands to find out what it was all about.
Etienne Rambert, however, was only a few feet from the clerk; he did not miss a word, and it was evident from his nervous movements every now and then that some passages in the indictment hit him very hard indeed, and even lessened his general confidence.
When the clerk had finished Etienne Rambert sat still, with his forehead resting in his hands, as if crushed by the weight of the memories the indictment had evoked. Then the sharp, thin voice of the President of the Court snapped the chain of his thoughts.
“Stand up, sir!”
And pale as death Etienne Rambert rose and folded his arms across his breast. In firm, yet somehow muffled tones, he answered the preliminary formal questions. His name was Hervé Paul Etienne Rambert; his age, fifty-nine; his occupation, a merchant, owning and working rubber plantations in South America. Then followed the formal enquiry whether he had heard and understood the indictment which had just been read.
“I followed it all, sir,” he replied, with a little gesture expressive of his sense of the gravity of the facts detailed and the weight of the evidence adduced, which won general sympathy for him. “I followed it all, but I protest against some of the allegations, and I protest with my whole energy against the suggestion that I have failed in my duty as a man of honour and as a father!”
The President of the Court checked him irritably.
“Excuse me, I do not intend to permit you to extend the pleadings indefinitely. I shall examine you on the various points of the indictment, and you may protest as much as you please.” The unfeeling rudeness provoked no comment from the defendant, and the President proceeded. “Well, you have heard the indictment. It charges you first with having aided and abetted the escape of your son, whom an enquiry held in another place had implicated in the murder of the Marquise de Langrune; and it charges you secondly with having killed your son, whose body has been recovered from the Dordogne, in order that you might escape the penalty of public obloquy.”

===Chinese===

他用毫无起伏的单调声调念着，嗓音颤抖而微弱，以至于法庭大厅里的旁听者几乎听不清他在嘟囔些什么，就连陪审员们也不得不双肘支在面前的书桌上，把手掌拢在耳后充当传声筒，才勉强能摸清个大概。
然而，埃蒂安·朗贝尔距离书记官仅有几步之遥，他一字不漏地全听进了耳朵里。从他时不时流露出的紧张神态中可以清楚看出，起诉书中的某些段落深深刺痛了他，甚至渐渐瓦解了他先前的镇定与自信。
书记官念完起诉书后，埃蒂安·朗贝尔依然一动不动地坐着，双手托着额头，仿佛被起诉书唤起的沉痛回忆彻底压垮了。这时，审判长尖细尖锐的嗓音斩断了他的思绪。
“被告起立！”
脸色惨白如死灰的埃蒂安·朗贝尔站起身来，双臂交叉抱在胸前。他用坚定却略显低沉压抑的语调，回答了法庭最初的身份核实提问。他的名字是埃尔韦·保罗·埃蒂安·朗贝尔；现年五十九岁；职业是商人，在南美洲拥有并经营着橡胶种植园。随后法庭循例讯问他是否听清并听懂了刚才宣读的起诉书。
“我都听清楚了，审判长先生，”他回答道，同时做了一个手势，既表明他深知指控事实之严重与证词分量之沉重，也博得了全场旁听者的普遍同情。“我都听清楚了，但我对其中的某些指控坚决抗议，尤其是指责我未能恪尽作为一个有荣誉感的人和一位父亲的职责，对此我将拼尽全力予以抗议！”
审判长不耐烦地打断了他。
“对不起，我可不打算由着你无休无止地展开辩解。稍后我会就起诉书中的各个要点逐一讯问你，到那时你爱怎么抗议就怎么抗议。”这种毫无同情心的粗暴态度并未引来被告的反驳，审判长继续说道：“好了，你已经听到了起诉书。检方首先指控你协助并纵容了你儿子的逃跑，而另一处展开的司法侦查已证实你儿子涉嫌杀害德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人；检方其次指控你亲手杀死了自己的儿子——其尸体已从多尔多涅河中打捞出来——目的仅仅是为了让你自己免遭公众的唾骂与耻辱。”

===Original===

At this brutal statement of the case Etienne Rambert made a proud gesture of indignation.
“Sir,” he exclaimed, “there are different ways of putting things. I do not deny the purport of the indictment, but I object to the summary of it that you present. No one has ever dared to contend that I killed my son in order to escape public obloquy, as you have just insinuated. I am entirely indifferent to the worlds opinion. What the indictment is intended to allege, the only thing it can allege, is that I wrought justice upon a criminal who ought to have filled me with horror but whom, nevertheless, I ought not to have handed over to the public executioner.”
This time it was the judge’s turn to be astonished. He was so accustomed to the cheap triumphs that judges look to win in court that he had expected to make mincemeat of this poor, broken old man whom the law had delivered to his tender mercy. But he discovered that the old man had fine courage and replied with spirit to his malevolent remarks.
“We will discuss your right to take the law into your own hands presently,” he said, “but that is not the question now: there are other points which it would be well for you to explain to the jury. Why, in the first place, did you obstinately decline to speak to the examining magistrate?”
“I had no answer to make to the examining magistrate,” Etienne Rambert answered slowly, as if he were weighing his words, “because in my opinion he had no questions to put to me! I do not admit that I am charged with anything contrary to the Code, or that any such charge can be formulated against me. The indictment charges me with having killed my son because I believed him to be guilty of the murder of Mme. de Langrune and would not hand him over to the gallows. I have never confessed to that murder, sir, and nothing will ever make me do so.

===Chinese===

面对法官如此冷酷粗暴的定性，埃蒂安·朗贝尔愤然作出了一个充满傲骨的抗议姿态。
“审判长先生，”他厉声高呼，“说话有不同的表达方式！我并不否认起诉书的法律旨趣，但我坚决抗议您所作的这番概括！从来没有任何人敢断言，我杀死自己的儿子是为了逃避公众的唾骂，正如您刚才含沙射影所说的那样。对于世俗的舆论评价，我向来完全不在乎。起诉书想要指控的，唯一能够指控的，无非是我对一个本该让我感到毛骨悚然的罪犯执行了正义，而这个罪犯无论如何，我都不忍心将他亲手送上刽子手的绞架！”
这一次，轮到法官感到惊愕了。他早已习惯了法官在法庭上轻易碾压被告所获得的廉价虚荣，原以为自己可以随意把玩和揉捏这个被法律交到他手中的穷困潦倒、心力交瘁的老人。然而他发现，这位老人拥有非凡的勇气，并且能够义正词严地反击自己的恶意刁难。
“关于你是否有权擅自以私刑代替法律，我们稍后再讨论，”法官说道，“不过那并非眼下的核心议题：还有其他几个要点，你最好能向陪审团解释清楚。首先，你当初为何顽固地拒绝回答预审法官的任何讯问？”
“我对预审法官无话可说，”埃蒂安·朗贝尔慢条斯理地回答，仿佛每一个字都在字斟句酌，“因为在我看来，他根本没有任何资格向我发问！我绝不承认自己触犯了刑法典中的任何罪名，也绝不承认有人可以对我罗织出任何此类罪名。起诉书指控我杀害了自己的儿子，理由是我认定他杀害了朗格吕纳夫人，而又不愿让他走上断头台。审判长先生，我从来没有承认过那桩谋杀，过去没有，将来也绝不会有任何事情能逼我承认。”

===Original===

And that is why I would not reply to the examining magistrate, because I would not admit that there was anything before the court concerning myself: because, since the dreadful tragedy in my private life was exposed to public opinion, I desired that I should be judged by public opinion, which, sir, is not represented by you who are a professional judge, but by the jury here who will shortly say whether I am really a criminal wretch: by the jury, many of whom are fathers themselves and, when they think of their own sons, will wonder what appalling visions must have passed through my mind when I was forced to believe that my boy, my own son, had committed a cowardly murder! What sort of tragedy will they think that must have been for a man like me, with sixty years of honour and of honourable life behind him?”
The outburst ended on a sob, and the whole court was moved with sympathy, women wiping their eyes, men coughing, and even the jury striving hard to conceal the emotion that stirred them.
The judge glared round the court, and after a pause addressed the defendant again with sarcastic phrases.
“So that is why you stood mute during the enquiry, was it, sir? Odd! very odd! I admire the interpretation you place upon your duty as an honourable man. It is﻿—quaint!”
Etienne Rambert interrupted the sneering speech.
“I am quite sure, sir, that there are plenty of people here who will understand and endorse what I did.”
The declaration was so pointedly personal that the judge took it up.
“And I am quite sure that people of principle will understand me when I have shown them your conduct as it really was. You have a predilection for heroics; it will not be without interest to bring things to the point. Your attitude throughout this affair has been this:﻿—it is not for me to anticipate the issue of the enquiry which will be held some day into the murder of Mme. de Langrune, but I must recall the fact that the moment you believed your son was the murderer, the moment you discovered the bloodstained towel which furnished the circumstantial evidence of his guilt, you﻿—the man of honour, mind you﻿—never thought of handing over the culprit to the police who were actually in the precincts of the château, but only thought of securing his escape, and helping him to get away! You even accompanied him in his flight, and so became in a sense his accomplice.

===Chinese===

“正因如此，我才不屑于回答预审法官的任何讯问，因为我绝不承认法庭审理的是涉及我个人的罪行；因为，既然我个人私生活中的骇人悲剧已经被公之于众，那么我只愿意接受公众舆论的评判！而公众舆论，审判长先生，绝不是由您这样一位职业法官来代表的，而是由坐在这里的陪审团代表的！他们很快就会裁定我究竟是不是一个卑劣的恶棍；这支陪审团里，许多人自己也是身为人父，当他们联想到自己的儿子时，自会明白，当一个人被迫认定自己的亲生骨肉竟然犯下了一桩懦夫般的凶杀时，他的脑海中究竟会闪过何等可怖的梦魇！他们自会掂量得出，对于一个像我这样拥有六十年清白声誉与光明磊落人生的男人而言，这究竟意味着何等撕心裂肺的灭顶之灾！”
这番激昂的倾诉在一声哽咽中戛然而止，整个法庭内被深深的同情所感染，女士们纷纷擦拭泪水，男士们掩嘴咳嗽，就连陪审员们也在极力掩饰内心的波澜起伏。
法官怒目环视全场，沉寂片刻后，再次用尖酸刻薄的语调面对被告。
“这么说，这就是你在司法侦查期间保持沉默的原因了，先生？真稀奇！太稀奇了！我真佩服你对自己所谓正人君子职责的独到理解。这可真是……别出心裁啊！”
埃蒂安·朗贝尔打断了法官的冷嘲热讽。
“审判长先生，我深信在座的各位之中，有大批心智健全的人能够理解并赞同我当年的所作所为。”
这番话针对法官个人的意味如此浓厚，以至于法官立刻予以反唇相讥。
“我也深信，只要我把你的真实行径毫无遮掩地公之于众，所有明事理的人都会站在我这一边。你倒是很擅长玩弄英雄主义的崇高把戏；把事实剥丝抽茧查个水落石出绝不会毫无意义。纵观整桩案件，你的态度始终是这样的：我虽无意在此预先判定有朝一日针对朗格吕纳夫人遇害案的全面审理结果，但我必须指出一个不可辩驳的事实——当你发现那条带血的毛巾、拿到证实他犯罪的确凿间接证据，从而认定你儿子就是杀人凶手的那一刻，你——请注意，你自诩为一个有荣誉感的人——却从没想过要把凶手移交给当时就在城堡周围的警察，你满脑子想的只是如何帮他潜逃，如何协助他远走高飞！你甚至亲自陪同他一同逃亡，从而在实质意义上成为了他的共犯。”

===Original===

I suppose you do not deny that?”
Etienne Rambert shook with emotion and answered in ringing tones.
“If you are of opinion, sir, that that was an act of complicity on my part, I will not only not deny it, I will proclaim it from the housetops! I became the accomplice of a murderer by inducing him to run away, did I? You forget, sir, that at the moment when I first believed my son was the culprit﻿—I was not his accomplice then, I suppose?﻿—there was a bond between him and me already that I could not possibly break: he was my son! Sir, the duty of a father﻿—and I attach the very loftiest meaning to the word ‘duty’﻿—can never entail his giving up his son!”
A fresh murmur of sympathy through the court annoyed the judge, who shrugged his shoulders.
“Let us leave empty rhetoric alone,” he said. “You have plenty of fine phrases with which to defend your action; that, indeed, is your concern, as the jury will doubtless appreciate; but I think it will be more advantageous to clear up the facts a little﻿—not more advantageous to you, perhaps, but that is what I am here to do. So will you please tell me whether your son confessed to having murdered Mme. de Langrune, either during that night when you persuaded him to run away, or afterwards? Yes or no, please.”
“I can’t answer, sir. My son was mad! I will not believe my son was a criminal! There was absolutely no motive to prompt him to the deed, and his mother is in an asylum! That is the whole explanation of the crime! If he committed murder, it was in a fit of temporary insanity! He is dead; I refuse to cover his memory with the stain of infamy!”
“In other words, according to you Charles Rambert did confess, but you don’t want to say so.”
“I do not say he did confess.”
“You leave it to be inferred.”
Etienne Rambert made no reply, and the judge passed on to another point.
“What exactly did you do after you left the château?”
“What anyone does, I suppose, when he runs away. We wandered miserably about, going through fields and woods, I accusing him and he defending himself. We avoided the villages, scarcely venturing even in the early morning to go and buy food, and walked quickly, wishing to get as far away as possible. We spent the most frightful time it is possible to conceive.”
“How long was all this?”
“I was with my son for four days, sir.”
“So it was on the fourth day that you killed him?”
“Have pity, sir!

===Chinese===

“我想这一点你不会否认吧？”
埃蒂安·朗贝尔激动得浑身颤抖，用响亮清晰的声音回答道：
“审判长先生，如果您认为那是我的一种共谋行径，那我不仅绝不否认，还要当着全天下的面大声宣告！我是劝他逃跑、成了杀人犯的共谋，是吗？可是先生，您忘记了，当最初认定我儿子是罪魁祸首的那一瞬间——我想在那个时刻我总还不算他的共谋吧？——我和他之间早已存在着一种我无论如何也斩不断的血肉纽带：他是我的亲生骨肉！先生，一个父亲的天职——我对‘天职’这个词赋予最崇高的敬意——永远不包含把自己的儿子推出去送死！”
法庭内再次涌起一阵同情的低语，法官恼怒地耸了耸肩。
“咱们还是省省这些空洞的修辞吧，”他说，“你多的是巧舌如簧的漂亮话来为自己的行径开脱；那当然是你的权利，陪审团自然也会予以考量；不过我认为，把事实稍微澄清一下会更有益处——或许对你来说未必有益，但这正是我坐在这里的职责所在。那么请你回答我：在你劝他潜逃的那天夜里，或是后来逃亡的途中，你的儿子到底有没有向你坦白承认杀害了朗格吕纳夫人？请明确回答有，还是没有。”
“我无法回答，先生。我儿子当时疯了！我绝不相信我的儿子是一个冷血罪犯！他根本没有任何杀人的动机，而他的亲生母亲至今还关在疯人院里！这才是整桩悲剧的全部根由！如果他真的杀了人，那也是在突发性精神错乱的癫狂状态下所犯下的！他如今已经死了，我拒绝在他的名声上抹上洗不掉的奇耻大辱！”
“换句话说，照你的意思，夏尔·朗贝尔当时确实坦白招认了，只是你不想明说罢了。”
“我可没说他坦白承认了。”
“但你留给人们的正是这种推论。”
埃蒂安·朗贝尔不再作答，法官随即转入下一个问题。
“离开城堡后，你们到底干了些什么？”
“我想任何人在逃亡时会干什么，我们当时就干了什么。我们像丧家之犬一样痛苦地四处流浪，穿行在田野与密林之中，我痛斥他的罪行，他拼命为自己辩解。我们尽量避开村庄，甚至大清早也几乎不敢冒险进村去买食物，一路步履匆匆，一心只想逃得越远越好。我们度过了一段常人所能想象的最可怕的日子。”
“这样的流浪持续了多久？”
“我和儿子在一起待了整整四天，先生。”
“所以，就是在第四天头上，你杀了他？”
“可怜可怜我吧，先生！

===Original===

I did not kill my son. It was a murderer that I had with me, a murderer for whom the police were hunting and for whom the guillotine was waiting!”
“A murderer, if you prefer it so,” said the judge, entirely heedless of the unhappy man’s protests. “But you had no right to assume the functions of executioner. Come, you admit you did kill him?”
“I do not admit it.”
“Do you deny that you killed him?”
“I did what my duty told me to do!”
“Still the same story!” said the judge, angrily drumming his fingers on the desk. “You refuse to answer. But even in your own interests you must have the courage to adopt some definite theory. Well, would you have been glad if your son had taken his own life?”
“May I entreat you to remember that my son is dead!” Etienne Rambert said once more. “I can only remember the one fact that he was my son. I can’t say that I desired his death. I don’t even know now if he was guilty. Whatever horror I may feel for a crime, I can only remember now that Charles was not in his right mind, and that he was the son of my loins!”
Again a tremor of emotion passed through the court, and again the judge made an angry gesture ordering silence.
“So you decline to answer any of the principal points of the indictment? The jury will no doubt appreciate the reason. Well, can you let us know any of the advice you gave your son? If you did not desire him to take his own life, and if you had no intention of killing him, what did you want?”
“Oblivion,” said Etienne Rambert, more calmly this time. “It was not for me to give my son up, and I could only desire for him oblivion, and if that was impossible, then death. I implored him to think of the life that was before him, and the future of shame, and I urged him to disappear forever.”
“Ah, you admit you did recommend him to commit suicide?”
“I mean I wanted him to go abroad.”
The president feigned to be occupied with his notes, purposely giving time for the importance of the last admission he had wrung from Etienne Rambert to sink into the minds of the jury. Then, without raising his head, he asked abruptly:
“You were very surprised to hear of his death?”
“No,” said Rambert dully.
“How did you part from each other?”
“The last night we slept out of doors, under a stack; we were both worn out and heartsick; I prayed God of His mercy to have pity on us. It was by the bank of the Dordogne.

===Chinese===

我没有杀我的儿子！当时跟我待在一起的是一个杀人犯，一个全警局都在大肆搜捕、断头台早已在等着他的杀人犯！”
“如果你更乐意这么叫，就算他是个杀人犯吧，”法官对这个不幸之人的泣血抗议充耳不闻，冷冷说道。“但你根本无权擅自行使刽子手的裁决权。说吧，你到底承不承认是你杀了他？”
“我绝不承认。”
“那你否认是你杀了他吗？”
“我只是恪尽了一个人良心与责任所驱使的本分！”
“又是这套陈词滥调！”法官恼怒地用手指敲打着审判桌，“你总是拒绝正面回答。不过哪怕单从你自身的利益出发，你也必须有勇气给出一个明确的说法。那么，要是你儿子自己了结了生命，你会感到庆幸吗？”
“我恳求您记住，我的儿子已经不在人世了！”埃蒂安·朗贝尔再次哀痛地喊道。“我脑子里只能记住一个事实：他是我的亲骨肉。我说不上自己是否渴望他的死。我现在甚至根本不知道他究竟有没有罪。无论我对罪恶怀有多么深恶痛绝的恐惧，我现在只记得夏尔当时神志不清，他是从我身上掉下来的骨肉啊！”
法庭上再次泛起一阵动容的悲戚颤动，法官又一次愤怒地挥手喝令肃静。
“这么说，你拒绝就起诉书中的任何核心指控作出实质性回答？陪审团自然会掂量出其中的深意。那么，你能否告诉我们，你当时究竟给了你儿子什么样的劝告？如果你既不希望他自杀，又无意亲手置他于死地，那你究竟想要怎样？”
“彻底的湮灭遗忘，”埃蒂安·朗贝尔这一次平静了许多，缓缓说道。“要我亲手把儿子交出去，我做不到；我所能期盼的唯有让他彻底被世人遗忘，如果连这都做不到，那便只有死亡。我恳求他多想想横亘在他面前的人生，以及注定饱受耻辱的未来，我劝他永远从这个世界上消失。”
“哈，你承认你确实劝导过他自杀了吧？”
“我的意思是，我希望他隐姓埋名流亡海外。”
审判长假装低头翻阅案卷卷宗，故意留出充裕的沉默时间，好让埃蒂安·朗贝尔吐露出的这句至关重要的招认在陪审团的心中充分发酵。随后，他头也不抬，突兀地厉声发问：
“当你得知他的死讯时，感到十分意外吗？”
“不意外，”朗贝尔神情呆滞地回答。
“你们当时是怎么分手的？”
“最后一晚我们露宿在荒野中，躲在一个草垛底下；我们两个人都精疲力竭、心如刀绞；我祈求仁慈的上帝能够怜悯我们。那地方就在多尔多涅河畔。

===Original===

Next morning when I woke up I was alone. He﻿—my son﻿—had disappeared. I know no more.”
The judge quelled the emotion in the court by a threatening glance, and sprang a question on the defendant which was like a trap to catch him lying.
“If at that time you knew no more, how was it that a few days later you called on Inspector Juve and asked him at once what was known about the dead body of your son? The body had only been recovered within the previous hour or two, and had not been absolutely identified; the newspapers, at any rate, only suggested the identity, with the utmost reserve. But you, sir, had no doubt on the subject! You knew that the corpse was that of your son! Why? How?”
It was one of the strongest points that could be made in support of the theory that Etienne Rambert had murdered his son, and the defendant immediately saw the difficulty he would have in giving an adequate answer without compromising himself. He turned to the jury, as though he had more hope in them than in the court.
“Gentlemen,” he cried, “this is torture! I can bear no more! I cannot answer any more. You know quite enough to form your judgment of me! Form it now! Say if I failed in my duty as a man of honour and a father! I, at least, can answer no more questions!” and he sank back in his place like a beaten man, crushed by the distress evoked by all these painful memories.
The judge nodded to the jury with the grim complacency of a man who has run down his game.
“This refusal to answer my questions is in itself tantamount to a confession,” he said acidly. “Well, we will proceed to call the witnesses. I should like to say that the most interesting witness would undoubtedly be Bouzille, the tramp who recovered the body of Charles Rambert; but unfortunately that individual has no fixed abode and it has not been possible to serve him with a subpoena.”
A number of witnesses succeeded one another in the box, without, however, throwing any fresh light upon the matter; they were peasants who had met the two Ramberts when they were flying from the château, village bakers who had sold them bread, and lockkeepers who had seen, but been unable to recover, the floating corpse.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨我醒来时，身边只有我孤身一人。他——我的儿子——已经无影无踪了。其他的事情我一概不知。”
法官用凌厉威吓的眼神压制住法庭内涌动的骚动，随即抛出了一个精心设下、意在诱使被告露出破绽的刁钻问题。
“如果你当时确实一无所知，那为何几天之后你却主动找上朱夫警探，一见面就直接打听关于你儿子尸体的调查进展？当时尸体从河里捞上来不过短短一两个钟头，死者身份根本未曾确凿查明；各大报纸在报道时也仅仅极为谨慎地推测可能是他。然而先生，你却对死者身份深信不疑！你一口咬定死者就是你的儿子！为什么？凭什么？”
这是支持埃蒂安·朗贝尔杀害亲子假说的最有力证据之一，被告瞬间意识到，自己要想给出合理解释而又不彻底将自己拖入深渊，将是何等艰难。他转向陪审席，仿佛比起审判台上的法官，他更寄希望于这些同为凡人的陪审员。
“诸位先生，”他绝望地喊道，“这是无休止的折磨！我实在承受不住了！我再也回答不了任何问题了！你们所掌握的事实已经足够对我的为人作出裁决了！那就请你们现在就作出裁决吧！判定我究竟有没有辜负一个正直之人和一位父亲的职责！至于我，我再也不想回答任何问题了！”说完，他像一个彻底被击溃的败者一样瘫倒在被告席的椅子上，被这一连串痛苦回忆所唤起的灭顶哀伤彻底压垮了。
法官以捕获猎物般的冷酷得意向陪审团微微点头示意。
“拒绝回答我的质询，本身就等同于畏罪招供，”他尖酸地宣布，“好，我们接下来传唤证人。我本想说明的是，本案中最有价值的证人无疑当属捞起夏尔·朗贝尔尸体的流浪汉布齐耶；遗憾的是此人居无定所、四处游荡，法庭未能成功向他送达出庭传票。”
多名证人随后依次登上证人席，但并未给案情带来任何突破性的新进展；他们大多是在两名朗贝尔出逃途中碰见过他们的乡下农夫、卖给过他们面包的乡村面包师，以及曾看到过浮尸却未能将其打捞上岸的水闸看守工。

===Original===

The people in the court began to weary of the proceedings, the more so as it was confidently rumoured that Etienne Rambert had proudly declined to call any witnesses on his behalf, and even to allow his counsel to make any rhetorical appeal to the jury. It might be imprudent, but there was something fine in his defiance.
There was, however, one more thrill of interest for the public. The judge had explained that he deemed it unnecessary to call the detective Juve, inasmuch as all the information he had to give was already detailed in the long indictment, but as Mme. de Langrune’s granddaughter was present in court, he would exercise his discretion and request her to answer one or two questions. And, much taken aback by this unexpected publicity, Thérèse Auvernois followed the usher to the witness-box.
“Mademoiselle Thérèse Auvernois, I need hardly ask if you recognise M. Rambert: but do you identify him as the person whose conversation with young Charles Rambert you overheard on that fatal night at the château of Beaulieu?”
“Yes, sir, that is M. Etienne Rambert,” she replied in low tones, and with a long and tender look of pity at the defendant.
“Will you please tell us anything you know that has any bearing upon the charge brought against the defendant, the charge of having killed his son?”
Thérèse made a visible effort to restrain her distress.
“I can only say one thing, sir: that M. Rambert was talking to his son in tones of such terrible distress that I knew his heart was broken by the tragedy. I have heard so much from my dear grandmother about M. Etienne Rambert that I can only remember that she always declared him to be a man of the very highest principle, and I can only tell him here how dreadfully sorry I am for him, and that everybody pities him as much as I do.”
The judge had expected that Thérèse would be a witness hostile to the defendant, whereas anything she was going to say would obviously be much to his advantage. He cut her short.
“That is enough, mademoiselle. Thank you,” and while Thérèse was going back to her seat, wiping away the tears that would come to her eyes despite her bravest efforts to keep her self-control in the presence of so many strangers, the judge announced that there were no other witnesses to be heard, and called upon the Public Prosecutor to address the court.

===Chinese===

旁听席上的众人开始对审理程序感到厌倦，加之坊间言之凿凿地盛传，埃蒂安·朗贝尔傲骨嶙峋地拒绝传唤任何有利于自己的辩护证人，甚至不准辩护律师向陪审团发表任何煽情的悲情陈词。这做法固然有失谨慎，但这份傲然不屈的姿态中却自有一种令人钦佩的凛然风骨。
不过，公众最终还是迎来了又一次扣人心弦的波澜。法官当庭解释称，他认为无需再传唤警探朱夫出庭作证，因为朱夫所掌握的全部侦查线索均已在冗长详尽的起诉书中悉数载明；但鉴于朗格吕纳夫人的外孙女此刻正端坐在法庭旁听席上，他决定行使自由裁量权，请她上前回答一两个问题。泰蕾兹·奥弗努瓦对这一突如其来的当庭传唤颇感猝不及防，在法警的引路下缓缓走向证人席。
“泰蕾兹·奥弗努瓦小姐，我无需问你是否认得朗贝尔先生；但你能否指认，他就是你在博利厄城堡那个致命凶案之夜，无意中偷听到的同年轻的夏尔·朗贝尔密谈的那个人？”
“是的，审判长先生，那正是埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生，”她低声回答，目光深情而怜惜地长久注视着被告。
“那么，关于检方指控被告杀害其亲生儿子的罪名，你能否提供任何与之相关的知情线索？”
泰蕾兹极力克制着内心的巨大悲恸。
“审判长先生，我只能说一件事：当时朗贝尔先生同他儿子说话的声音充满了无尽的凄苦与绝望，我知道他的心已经被那场悲剧彻底撕碎了。我从亲爱的外祖母那里听过无数关于埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生的为人，我只记得外祖母生前总是赞誉他是一位人格极其高尚、无可挑剔的正人君子；我今天只能在这里对他说，我为他所遭遇的一切感到痛彻心扉，在场的所有人都像我一样深深同情他的不幸。”
法官原本预料泰蕾兹会是一名指证被告的敌意证人，没料到她的每一句话全都在为被告说话，反而对被告大为有利。法官当即冷冷打断了她。
“足够了，小姐。谢谢你，”趁着泰蕾兹在法庭众多陌生人的注视下极力维持自尊、一边擦拭着夺眶而出的热泪一边退回座位的当口，法官宣布证人传讯完毕，传唤公诉人向法庭发表总结陈词。

===Original===

That personage rose at once and made a harangue that was eloquent enough, no doubt, but introduced no new features into the case. He relied upon his law rather than his facts: rapidly recapitulated the defendant’s contradictions and pitifully weak arguments, if arguments they could be called: claimed that the facts had been proved despite the defendant’s steady refusal to answer questions: and insisted on the point that the defendant had no right whatever to take the law into his own hands, and either kill his son or aid and abet in his flight. He concluded by asking for a verdict of guilty, and a sentence of penal servitude for life.
To him succeeded counsel for the defendant, whose speech was brevity itself. He declined to make any appeal *ad misericordiam*, but simply asked the jury to decide whether the defendant had not acted as any high-principled father would act when he discovered that his son had committed a crime during a fit of insanity. He asked only for an impartial decision on the facts, from men of high principle, and he sat down conscious of having focused the issue on the proper point and secured the sympathy of the public.
The judges withdrew to their room, the jury retired to consider their verdict, and Etienne Rambert was removed between two warders. Juve had not stirred during the whole trial, or displayed the least sign of approval or disapproval at any of the questions and answers exchanged. He sat now unobtrusively listening to the conversation that passed near him, relative to the issue of the case.
President Bonnet opined that Etienne Rambert had blundered in refusing to put up any defence: he had shown contempt of court, which was always unwise, and the court would show him no mercy. Dollon was of another opinion: according to him Etienne Rambert was a sport of fate, deserving pity rather than severity, and the court would be very lenient. Another man declared that Etienne Rambert had been in an impasse: however fondly he loved his son he could not but hope that he might commit suicide: if a friend committed an offence against the laws of honour, the only thing to do was to put a pistol into his hand. And so on: the only point on which all were unanimous was their sympathy with the defendant.

===Chinese===

公诉人随即起立发表了一通演说，言辞固然不失雄辩滔滔，但并未能给案情注入任何新论据。他全篇侧重于法理推演而非实据剖析：飞快地重申了被告口供中的自相矛盾之处，以及那些堪称苍白可怜的辩解——如果那些能勉强称之为辩解的话；宣称尽管被告顽固拒绝回答关键质询，但犯罪事实已然昭然若揭；并极力强调被告绝无任何权力凌驾于法律之上擅自执行私刑，无论是亲手杀死儿子，抑或是协助纵容其出逃，皆属国法难容。他最终请求法庭认定被告罪名成立，判处终身苦役。
紧接着是被告辩护律师起立发言，其辩护词堪称字字千钧、简短至极。他明确拒绝发表任何乞求怜悯（ad misericordiam）的煽情说辞，只是坦诚地请求陪审团作出公断：当一位恪守崇高原则的父亲骤然发现自己的骨肉在突发狂乱的精神错乱下犯下滔天大罪时，他的所作所为难道不正是任何一位有良知、重荣誉的父亲都会做出的本能抉择吗？他只恳求这些同样秉持崇高操守的陪审员们基于事实本身作出公正裁决；随后他从容落座，深知自己已将争议焦点精准引向了正轨，并彻底赢得了全场公众的同情与共鸣。
法官们随即退入合议室，陪审团亦退席评议裁决结果，埃蒂安·朗贝尔在两名法警押解下被暂时带离法庭。在整场庭审过程中，朱夫自始至终未曾挪动分毫，对于法庭上的唇枪舌剑也未曾流露出丝毫赞许或不悦之色。此刻，他依然不动声色地静坐原位，暗自倾听着周围旁听者就本案结局展开的窃窃私语。
邦内庭长断言埃蒂安·朗贝尔拒绝进行任何抗辩实属大错特错：这无异于藐视法庭，在任何时候都是极不明智的蠢举，法庭绝不会对他网开一面。多隆则持相反看法：在他看来，埃蒂安·朗贝尔不过是惨遭命运戏弄的受害者，理应获得同情而非严惩，法庭必定会从轻发落。另一位人士则宣称，埃蒂安·朗贝尔当年陷入了走投无路的绝境：无论他多么溺爱儿子，他当时唯一的期盼也只能是儿子自行了断；当一个朋友玷污了荣誉法则时，唯一体面的办法就是亲手递给他一把手枪。诸如此类议论纷纷不一而足，但唯有一点所有人达成了惊人的一致，那就是对这位悲剧被告由衷的深切同情。

===Original===

But a bell rang sharply; grave and impassive, the jury returned, the judges filed once more into their seats, Etienne Rambert was led back into court by the warders. In tense silence the foreman of the jury spoke:
“In the presence of God and of man, and upon my honour and my conscience I declare that the answer of the jury is ‘no’ to all the questions put, and that is the answer of them all.”
It was acquittal!
There was no applause, but yet it seemed as if the words that set the defendant free had relieved every bosom of an overwhelming dread; the air seemed easier to breathe; and there was no one there but seemed physically better and also happier, for hearing a verdict which gave sanction for the general pity they had felt for the unhappy defendant, a man of honour and a most unhappy father!
By their verdict the jury had implicitly applauded and commiserated Etienne Rambert; but he still sat in the dock, broken and prostrated by terrible distress, sobbing unreservedly and making no effort to restrain his immeasurable grief.

===Chinese===

突然，一阵尖锐的法铃骤响；神情肃穆、波澜不惊的陪审团重返法庭，法官们亦依次重回审判席，埃蒂安·朗贝尔在法警的押送下被重新带上法庭。在令人窒息的死寂中，陪审团首席陪审员庄严宣读裁决：
“在上帝与世人面前，凭我的荣誉与良知，我郑重宣告：针对法庭提出的所有公诉问题，陪审团一致裁定——不成立，这是全体陪审员共同的结论！”
宣判无罪！
法庭内没有爆发掌声，但那句宣告被告重获自由的判词，却仿佛顷刻间卸下了在场所有人胸口沉压已久的千钧巨石；连空气都似乎变得畅快轻盈起来；在场的每一个人都感到由衷的释怀与欣慰，因为这份判决为他们内心深处对这位不幸被告的无限同情赋予了神圣的合法认可——他是一个重荣誉的正人君子，更是一位世间最不幸的父亲！
陪审团用他们的判决，在无声中向埃蒂安·朗贝尔致以最高的敬意与同情；然而他依然呆坐在被告席上，被灭顶的剧痛彻底击垮瘫倒，放声痛哭，再也无力遏制那无边无际的巨大哀伤。

## X Princess Sonia’s Bath / 第十章 索尼娅公主的沐浴
===Original===

Four months had passed since Etienne Rambert had been acquitted at the Cahors Assizes, and the world was beginning to forget the Beaulieu tragedy as it had already almost forgotten the mysterious murder of Lord Beltham. Juve alone did not allow his daily occupation to put the two cases out of his mind. True, he had ceased to make any direct enquiries, and gave no sign that he still had any interest in those crimes; but the detective knew very well that in both of them he had to contend with no ordinary murderer and he was content to remain in the shadow, waiting and watching, in seeming inactivity, for some slip which should betray the person or persons who had perpetrated two of the most puzzling murders that he had ever had to deal with.
It was the end of June, and Paris was beginning to empty. But the spring had been late and cold that year, and although it was within a couple of days of July society had lingered on in the capital; luxuriously appointed carriages still swept along the Champs Élysées when the audiences poured out of theatres and concert rooms, and fashionably attired people still thronged the broad pavements and gathered before the brilliantly lighted cafés on the Rond-Point; even at that late hour the Champs Élysées were as animated as in the busiest hours of the day.
At the Royal Palace Hotel the greatest animation prevailed. The entire staff was hurrying about the vast entrance halls and the palatial rooms on the ground floor; for it was the hour when the guests of the Royal Palace Hotel were returning from their evening’s amusements, and the spacious vestibules of the immense hotel were crowded with men in evening dress, young fellows in dinner jackets, and women in low-cut gowns.
A young and fashionable woman got out of a perfectly appointed victoria, and M. Louis, the manager of the staff, came forward and bowed low, as he only did to clients of the very highest distinction. The lady responded with a gracious smile, and the manager called a servant.
“The lift for Mme. la Princesse Sonia Danidoff,” and the next moment the beautiful vision, who had created quite a sensation merely in passing through the hall, had disappeared within the lift and was borne up to her apartments.
Princess Sonia was one of the most important clients that the Royal Palace Hotel possessed.

===Chinese===

自埃蒂安·朗贝尔在卡奥尔重罪法庭（Cahors Assizes）获判无罪以来，已经过去了四个月，世人渐渐开始淡忘博利厄惨案，正如他们几乎已经彻底忘却了贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）的神秘遇害案一样。唯独朱夫没有让日常的琐碎公务冲淡脑海中对这两桩大案的执念。诚然，他表面上不再进行任何直接的调查走访，也丝毫没有流露出对这两桩命案仍抱有兴趣的迹象；然而这位警探心中深知，在这两起案件中自己面对的绝非寻常的凶手，他甘愿蛰伏在暗处，在看似无所作为的表象下暗中观察、耐心守候，等待着那个或那伙制造了他职业生涯中最棘手命案的凶徒露出破绽。
时值六月下旬，巴黎城中开始逐渐冷清下来。然而那一年的春季来得迟缓而寒冷，尽管距离七月仅剩两天光景，社交界的达官显贵们依然留恋在这座都城中；当散场的人群从剧院和音乐厅涌出时，陈设奢华的马车依然在香榭丽舍大街（Champs Élysées）上飞驰，衣着光鲜的红男绿女依然挤满了宽阔的人行道，聚集在圆点广场（Rond-Point）灯火通明的咖啡馆门前；即便到了这深更半夜，香榭丽舍大街依然如同白昼最繁华的时刻一般人声鼎沸、生机勃勃。
皇家宫殿大饭店（Royal Palace Hotel）内更是一片鼎沸的喧嚣。宽敞的门厅与底楼金碧辉煌的各个大厅里，全体侍者正忙得不可开交；此时正是皇家宫殿大饭店的贵宾们消遣了一夜归来的时刻，巨大饭店宽绰的前厅里，挤满了身着燕尾服的绅士、穿便宴礼服的年轻公子以及身着低胸晚礼服的贵妇名媛。
一位年轻时尚的贵妇从一辆陈设极其考究的维多利亚式轻便敞篷马车上款款走下，饭店总领班路易先生快步上前深深鞠躬致意——唯有面对身份最尊贵的显赫贵宾，他才会施以如此大礼。贵妇报以一抹优雅迷人的微笑，总领班随即高声招呼侍者。
“为索尼娅·达尼多夫公主（Princess Sonia Danidoff）殿下备电梯！”转瞬间，这位光彩夺目、仅仅穿越大堂便引来全场瞩目惊艳的绝代佳人已步入电梯，升向了她下榻的豪华套房。
索尼娅公主是皇家宫殿大饭店最为尊贵的常客之一。

===Original===

She belonged to one of the greatest families in the world, being, by her marriage with Prince Danidoff, cousin to the Emperor of Russia and, so, connected with many royal personages. Still barely thirty years of age, she was not pretty but remarkably lovely, with wonderful blue eyes which formed a strange and most bewitching contrast to the heavy masses of black hair that framed her face. A woman of immense wealth, and typically a woman of the world, the Princess spent six months of the year in Paris, where she was a well-known and much-liked figure in the most exclusive circles; she was clever and cultivated, a first-rate musician, and her reputation was spotless, although it was very seldom that she was accompanied by her husband, whose duties as Grand Chamberlain to the Tsar kept him almost continuously in Russia. When in Paris she occupied a suite of four rooms on the third floor of the Royal Palace Hotel, a suite identical in plan and in luxury with that reserved for sovereigns who came there incognito.
The Princess passed through her drawing-room, a vast, round room, with a superb view over the Arc de Triomphe, and went into her bedroom where she switched on the electric light.
“Nadine,” she called, in her grave, melodious voice, and a young girl, almost a child, sprang from a low divan hidden in a corner. “Nadine, take off my cloak and unfasten my hair. Then you can leave me: it is late, and I am tired.”
The little maid obeyed, helped her mistress to put on a silken dressing gown, and loosened the masses of her hair. The Princess passed a hand across her brow, as if to brush away a headache.
“Before you go, get a bath ready for me; I think that would rest me.”
Ten minutes later Nadine crept back like a shadow, and found the Princess standing dreamily on the balcony, inhaling deep breaths of the pure night air. The child kissed the tips of her mistress’s fingers. “Your bath is quite ready,” she said, and then withdrew.
A few more minutes passed, and Princess Sonia, half undressed, was just going into her dressing-room when suddenly she turned and went back to the middle of the bedroom which she had been on the point of leaving.
“Nadine,” she called, “are you still there?” No answer came. “I must have been dreaming,” the Princess murmured, “but I thought I heard someone moving about.”

===Chinese===

她出身于世上最显赫的豪门望族之一，因下嫁达尼多夫亲王（Prince Danidoff），成了俄国沙皇（Tsar）的堂妹，与众多欧洲王室皆有姻亲关系。她年方三十岁上下，虽算不上寻常意义上的标致，却美得动人心魄；一双蔚蓝如海的明眸，与勾勒着她面容轮廓的一头乌黑浓密的秀发形成了奇异而极其勾魂摄魄的反差。作为一位坐拥巨万财富的典型上流社会名媛，公主每年有六个月的时间客居巴黎，在最顶层的名流圈子里是位尽人皆知且备受青睐的焦点人物；她聪慧过人、教养极高，是一位第一流的音乐家，且名声冰清玉洁、无可挑剔，尽管她的丈夫鲜少陪同在侧——亲王身为沙皇的大内侍从长，职责在身，几乎常年驻留俄国。客居巴黎期间，她在皇家宫殿大饭店三楼长包了一套由四个开间组成的奢华套房，其格局与豪华程度与专门接待微服出访的各国君主的御用套房完全相同。
公主穿过自己的客厅——那是一间宽敞的圆形大厅，拥有俯瞰凯旋门的壮丽绝伦视野——径直走进卧室，按亮了电灯。
“娜丁（Nadine），”她用低沉婉转的声音轻唤了一声，只见一个尚显稚气、几乎还是个半大孩子的少女从角落里的一张矮沙发上轻盈跃起。“娜丁，帮我解开斗篷，散开头发。然后你就可以去睡了，夜深了，我有些累。”
小女仆顺从地侍奉着，帮女主人换上一件轻柔的丝绸睡袍，散开了那一头浓密的秀发。公主抬手揉了揉额角，仿佛想要拂去头痛的倦意。
“去睡之前，给我备好洗澡水；泡个热水澡或许能让我松泛些。”
十分钟后，娜丁像影子般轻手轻脚地走了回来，只见公主正若有所思地倚在阳台上，贪婪地深吸着清冽的夜风。小女仆轻轻亲吻了女主人的指尖。“您的沐浴水备好了，”她说罢，便悄步退了出去。
又过了几分钟，宽衣褪去大半的索尼娅公主正准备步入盥洗室，突然间她停下脚步，折返回到了刚刚正要离开的卧室中央。
“娜丁，”她扬声叫道，“你还在那儿吗？”四周一片寂静，毫无回音。“大概是我看花眼或听错了吧，”公主喃喃自语，“可我刚才分明听见有人走动的声音。”

===Original===

Sonia Danidoff was not unduly nervous, but like most people who live much alone and in large hotels, she was wont to be careful, and wished to make sure that no suspicious person had made his way into her rooms. She made a rapid survey of her bedroom, glanced into the brilliantly lighted drawing-room, and then moved to her bed and saw that the electric bell board, which enabled her to summon any of her own or of the hotel’s servants, was in perfect order. Then, satisfied, she went into her dressing-room, quickly slipped off the rest of her clothes, and plunged into the perfumed water in her bath.
She thrilled with pleasure as her limbs, so tired after a long evening, relaxed in the warm water. On a table close to the bath she had placed a volume of old Muscovite folk tales, and she was glancing through these by the shaded light from a lamp above her, when a fresh sound made her start. She sat up quickly in the water and looked around her. There was nothing there. Then a little shiver shook her and she sank down again in the warm bath with a laugh at her own nervousness. And she was just beginning to read once more, when suddenly a strange voice, with a ring of malice in it, sounded in her ear. Someone was looking over her shoulder, and reading aloud the words she had just begun!
Before Sonia Danidoff had time to utter a cry or make a movement, a strong hand was over her lips, and another gripped her wrist, preventing her from reaching the button of the electric bell that was fixed among the taps. The Princess was almost fainting. She was expecting some horrible shock, expecting to feel some horrible weapon that would take her life, when the pressure on her lips and the grip upon her wrist gradually relaxed; and at the same moment, the mysterious individual who had thus taken her by surprise, moved round the bath and stood in front of her.
He was a man of about forty years of age, and extremely well dressed. A perfectly cut dinner jacket proved that the strange visitor was no unclean dweller in the Paris slums: no apache such as the Princess had read terrifying descriptions of in luridly illustrated newspapers. The hands which had held her motionless, and which now restored her liberty of movement to her, were white and well manicured and adorned with a few plain rings.

===Chinese===

索尼娅·达尼多夫向来不是那种神经过敏的人，但正如大多数常年独居在大型高档饭店的人一样，她行事向来谨慎周密，务求确认绝无任何形迹可疑之徒潜入自己的套房。她飞快地巡视了一眼卧室，又探头看了一眼灯火通明的客厅，随后走到床边，仔细检查了通往私人仆役与饭店服务台的电铃呼叫电板，确认一切运转正常。随后，她这才放下心来，迈入盥洗室，迅速褪去剩余的衣衫，浸入了飘散着沁人香氛的浴缸热水中。
经过了一整晚漫长的社交应酬，疲惫不堪的肢体在温热的水中彻底舒展放松，令她感到一阵愉悦的战栗。在浴缸旁的小几上，她摆了一本古老的莫斯科民间故事集；在上方灯罩投下的柔和光线下，她正随手翻阅着书页，突然间，又一声异响令她悚然一惊。她迅速从水里坐直身子，警惕地环顾四周。然而周围空无一物。一阵轻微的寒战掠过周身，她不禁为自己的疑神疑鬼哑然失笑，重新沉入温热的浴缸之中。正当她刚刚重新拾起书本准备继续阅读时，突兀地，一声充满戏谑与恶意的陌生嗓音在她耳畔骤然响起。居然有人正伏在她的肩头，大声朗读着她刚刚落目的那行字句！
没等索尼娅·达尼多夫来得及发出一声尖叫或做出任何挣扎，一只强有力的手掌已经死死捂住了她的双唇，另一只手则如铁钳般扣住了她的手腕，硬生生阻断了她伸手去按嵌在水龙头中间的电铃求救按钮的企图。公主吓得几乎当场昏厥。她预感到某种可怕的暴力即将降临，预料会有冰冷的凶器刺穿自己的身躯夺走性命；然而就在这时，捂在她唇上的重压和扣在手腕上的钳制却渐渐松开了；几乎就在同时，那个以如此鬼魅手段将她制伏的神秘男子绕过了浴缸，端端正正地站在了她的正前方。
那是一名年约四十岁上下的男子，衣冠楚楚，极为考究。一身剪裁无可挑剔的晚宴礼服彰显出这位不速之客绝非来自巴黎阴暗贫民窟的肮脏下九流，也绝非公主在耸人听闻的画报上所读到的那些凶狠残暴的阿帕奇流氓歹徒。刚才那双将她制得动弹不得、此刻又将行动自由还给她的双手，白皙修长，修剪养护得极为整洁，指间仅佩戴着几枚式样低调素雅的素圈戒指。

===Original===

The man’s face was a distinguished one, and he wore a very fine black beard; slight baldness added to the height of a forehead naturally large. But what struck the Princess most, although she had little heart to observe the man very closely, was the abnormal size of his head and the number of wrinkles that ran right across his temples, following the line of the eyebrows.
In silence and with trembling lips Sonia Danidoff made an instinctive effort again to reach the electric bell, but with a quick movement the man caught her shoulder and prevented her from doing so. There was a cryptic smile upon the stranger’s lips, and with a furious blush Sonia Danidoff dived back again into the milky water in the bath.
The man still stood in perfect silence, and at length the Princess mastered her emotion and spoke to him.
“Who are you? What do you want? Go at once or I will call for help.”
“Above all things, do not call out, or you are a dead woman!” said the stranger harshly. Then he gave a little ironical shrug of his shoulders. “As for ringing﻿—that would not be easy: you would have to leave the water to do so! And, besides, I object.”
“If it is money, or rings you want,” said the Princess between clenched teeth, “take them! But go!”
The Princess had laid several rings and bracelets on the table by her side, and the man glanced at them now, but without paying much attention to what the Princess said.
“Those trinkets are not bad,” he said, “but your signet ring is much finer,” and he calmly took the Princess’s hand in his and examined the ring that she had kept on her third finger. “Don’t be frightened,” he added as he felt her hand trembling. “Let us have a chat, if you don’t mind! There is nothing especially tempting about jewels apart from their personality,” he said after a little pause, “apart, I mean, from the person who habitually wears them. But the bracelet on a wrist, or the necklace round a neck, or the ring upon a finger is another matter!”
Princess Sonia was as pale as death and utterly at a loss to understand what this extraordinary visitor was driving at. She held up her ring finger, and made a frightened little apology.
“I cannot take this ring off: it fits too tight.”
The man laughed grimly.
“That does not matter in the least, Princess. Anyone who wanted to get a ring like that could do it quite simply.”

===Chinese===

男子的面容显得很有教养，蓄着修剪极其精致的黑色胡须；前额微秃，更显得原本就开阔的天庭格外高阔。然而最让公主心惊肉跳的——尽管她此刻毫无心思仔细端详眼前的男人——是他那异乎寻常的巨大头颅，以及横贯整个太阳穴、紧贴眉毛线条而生的数道深刻横纹。
在令人窒息的死寂中，索尼娅·达尼多夫颤抖着嘴唇，本能地又试图伸手去够电铃按钮，但男子迅捷地一探手抓住了她的香肩，再次阻止了她的动作。不速之客的唇角泛着一抹高深莫测的微笑，索尼娅·达尼多夫羞愤得满面绯红，猛地重新缩回乳白色的浴水之中。
男子依然一言不发地静静伫立，良久，公主终于勉强克制住狂乱的心绪，开口斥问：
“你到底是谁？你想干什么？立刻给我滚出去，否则我就喊救命了！”
“千千万万别出声呼救，否则你立刻就是个死人！”陌生男子厉声喝道。随即，他带着嘲弄的意味微微耸了耸肩。“至于按铃嘛——恐怕也不太方便：你总得走出水面才够得着吧！更何况，我可不答应。”
“要是你图的是钱，或者是珠宝首饰，”公主咬牙切齿地说道，“全都拿走！但立刻滚出去！”
公主刚才在浴缸旁的几案上摘放了好几枚戒指和手镯，男子瞥了它们一眼，却并未对公主的话显出多大兴趣。
“这些小玩意儿倒还算凑合，”他说，“不过您手上戴着的那枚印章戒指可要名贵得多。”说着，他从容不迫地执起公主的玉手，端详起她无名指上尚未褪下的戒指。“别害怕，”察觉到她的玉手在剧烈颤抖，他补充道，“如果不介意的话，咱们先聊聊天吧！珠宝首饰脱离了主人的气质，本身其实并没有什么特别勾人之处，”他稍稍停顿了一下继续说道，“我的意思是，脱离了惯常佩戴它的人，它就失了神采。但戴在手腕上的手镯、挂在脖颈上的项链，或是套在玉指上的指环，那可就截然不同了！”
索尼娅公主面色惨白如纸，完全摸不透这个行径诡异的不速之客究竟意欲何为。她怯生生地抬起那根戴着戒指的手指，战战兢兢地辩解道：
“这枚戒指我摘不下来，套得太紧了。”
男子发出了一阵阴冷的低笑。
“那一点关系都没有，公主殿下。任何人要是真想要弄到这样一枚戒指，办法可简单得很。”

===Original===

He felt negligently in his waistcoat pocket and produced a miniature razor, which he opened. He flashed the blade before the terrified eyes of the Princess. “With a sharp blade like this a skilful man could cut off the finger that had such a splendid jewel on it, in a couple of seconds,” and then, seeing that the Princess, in fresh panic, was on the very point of screaming, quick as a flash he laid the palm of his hand over her lips, while still speaking in gentle tones to her. “Please do not be so terrified; I suppose you take me for some common hotel thief, or highway robber, but, Princess, can you really believe that I am anything of the kind?”
The man’s tone was so earnest, and there was so deferent a look in his eyes, that the Princess recovered some of her courage.
“But I do not know who you are,” she said half questioningly.
“So much the better,” the man replied; “there is still time to make one another’s acquaintance. I know who you are, and that is the main thing. You do not know me, Princess? Well, I assure you that on very many an occasion I have mingled with the blessed company of your adorers!”
The Princess’s anger rose steadily with her courage.
“Sir,” she said, “I do not know if you are joking, or if you are talking seriously, but your behaviour is extraordinary, hateful, abominable﻿—”
“It is merely original, Princess, and it pleases me to reflect that if I had been content to be presented to you in the ordinary way, in one or other of the many drawing-rooms we both frequent, you would certainly have taken much less notice of me than you have taken tonight; from the persistence of your gaze I can see that from this day onwards, not a single feature of my face will be unfamiliar to you, and I am convinced that, whatever happens, you will remember it for a very long time.”
Princess Sonia tried to force a smile. She had recovered her self-possession, and was wondering what kind of man she had to deal with. If she was still not quite persuaded that this was not a vulgar thief, and if she had but little faith in his professions of admiration of herself, she was considerably exercised by the idea that she was alone with a lunatic. The man seemed to read her thoughts for he, too, smiled a little.
“I am glad to see, Princess, that you have a little more confidence now: we shall be able to arrange things ever so much better.

===Chinese===

他不慌不忙地伸手在西装背心口袋里摸索了一阵，掏出一把小巧玲珑的袖珍剃刀，随手将刀片弹开。他在吓得魂飞魄散的公主眼前晃了晃寒光闪闪的刀刃。“用这么一把锋利的刀片，一个身手敏捷的人只要两秒钟，就能把戴着如此绝美宝石的整根手指干净利落地切下来。”紧接着，看到公主在极度的恐慌中几乎就要失声尖叫，他如闪电般迅速将手掌覆在了她的双唇之上，嘴里却依然用温柔得令人发指的语调宽慰着她。“求求您千万别这么害怕；我猜您准是把我当成了某个寻常的饭店芼贼，或者是拦路抢劫的强盗了吧，可是，公主殿下，您真的相信我会是那种下三滥的货色吗？”
男子的语气如此诚恳真挚，眼神中更流露出一种毕恭毕敬的谦卑，这反倒让公主找回了一丝勇气。
“可我根本不认得你，”她带着半信半疑的质问口吻说道。
“那再好不过了，”男子回答，“咱们现在结识也为时不晚。我知道您是谁，这才是顶要紧的。您不认得我，公主殿下？噢，我向您保证，在无数个场合里，我都曾混迹在仰慕您的那一群荣幸之至的拜倒者之中！”
随着勇气的恢复，公主心中的怒火也开始节节攀升。
“先生，”她说，“我不知道你是在开玩笑，还是在说正经的，但你的行径简直荒唐透顶、令人憎恶、卑鄙至极——”
“这不过是别出心裁罢了，公主殿下。只要一想到，如果我仅仅满足于在咱们频繁出入的那些社交沙龙里、循规蹈矩地由人引荐给您，您今晚对我的关注必定会比此刻少得多，我便感到由衷的欣慰。从您那双目不转睛凝视我的眼神里，我可以断定，从今往后，我脸上的每一个轮廓特征都将深深烙印在您的脑海中；我深信，无论发生什么，您都会在往后极其漫长的岁月里将它铭记于心。”
索尼娅公主勉强挤出一丝苦笑。她已经彻底恢复了镇定，心中暗暗揣摩着自己面对的究竟是个什么样的人物。即便她仍未完全相信此人不是个普通的窃贼，即便她对他嘴里那些倾慕之词嗤之以鼻，她心中却越来越被另一个可怕的念头所占据——自己很可能是和一个疯子独处一室。男子仿佛看穿了她的心事，唇角也浮起一抹淡淡的微笑。
“看到您终于对我多了几分信任，我由衷高兴，公主殿下，这样一来，咱们就能把事情商量得更加圆满了。

===Original===

You are certainly much more calm, much less uneasy now. Oh, yes, you are!” he added, checking her protest. “Why, it is quite five minutes since you last tried to ring for help. We are getting on. Besides, I somehow can’t picture the Princess Sonia Danidoff, wife of the Grand Chamberlain and cousin of His Majesty the Emperor of All the Russias, allowing herself to be surprised alone with a man whom she did not know. If she were to ring, and someone came, how would the Princess account for the gentleman to whom she had accorded an audience in the most delightful, but certainly the most private of all her apartments?”
“But tell me,” pleaded the unhappy woman, “how did you get in here?”
“That is not the question,” the stranger replied. “The problem actually before us is, how am I to get out? For, of course, Princess, I shall not be so indelicate as to prolong my visit unduly, too happy only if you will permit me to repeat it on some other evening soon.” He turned his head, and plunging his hand into the bath in the most natural manner possible, took out the thermometer which was floating on the perfumed water. “Thirty degrees, centigrade, Princess! Your bath is getting cold: you must get out!”
In her blank astonishment Princess Sonia did not know whether to laugh or cry. Was she alone with a monster who, after having played with her as a cat plays with a mouse, would suddenly turn and kill her? Or was this merely some irresponsible lunatic, whom chance alone had enabled to get into her rooms? Whatever the fact might be, the man’s last words had made her aware that her bath really was getting cold. A shiver shook her whole frame, and yet﻿—
“Oh, go, please go!” she implored him.
He shook his head, an ironical smile in his eyes.
“For pity’s sake,” she entreated him again, “have mercy on a woman﻿—a good woman!”
The man appeared to be considering.
“It is very embarrassing,” he murmured, “and yet we must decide upon something soon, for I am most anxious you should not take a chill. Oh, it is very simple, Princess: of course you know the arrangement of everything here so well that you could find your dressing-gown at once, by merely feeling your way? We will put out the light, and then you will be able to get out of the bath in the dark without the least fear.”

===Chinese===

您现在显然平静得多了，也没刚才那么心慌了。噢，千真万确！”看到她试图辩解，他补充道。“您瞧，距离您上一次试图按铃求救，已经足足过去了五分钟。咱们的进展相当不错。况且，我实在很难想象，索尼娅·达尼多夫公主殿下——沙皇陛下大内侍从长的夫人兼全俄国皇帝的堂妹——竟然会允许自己被撞见与一个素昧平生的陌生男子幽会独处。要是您当真按响了电铃，有人应声而来，堂堂公主殿下又该如何向旁人解释，这位获准在您最迷人、却也无疑是私密至极的闺房重地接受单独觐见的绅士，究竟是何方神圣呢？”
“可求你告诉我，”这个不幸的女人哀求道，“你到底是怎么进来的？”
“那并非眼下的关键，”陌生男子回答，“摆在咱们面前的现实难题是，我究竟该怎么脱身出去？因为，公主殿下，我自然不至于不知趣到无休止地耽搁下去，只要您宽宏大量准许我在不久后的某个夜晚再度登门造访，我便死而无憾了。”他侧过头，极其自然地将手探入浴缸，捞出了漂浮在芬芳浴水上的水温计。“摄氏三十度，公主殿下！您的洗澡水正在变凉，您该出浴了！”
在极度的错愕茫然之中，索尼娅公主不知该哭还是该笑。自己究竟是和一个先像猫捉老鼠一样把玩受害者、随后便会猝然暴起行凶的恶魔关在一起，还是仅仅碰上了一个凭侥幸潜入闺房的疯癫狂人？无论真相如何，男子最后这番话确实让她真切地意识到洗澡水正在变凉。一阵寒颤袭遍全身，然而——
“求你，求你快走吧！”她哀告道。
他摇了摇头，眼眸中闪烁着嘲弄的笑意。
“看在上帝的分上，”她再次恳求道，“发发慈悲，可怜可怜一个女人吧——一个清清白白的体面女人！”
男子似乎陷入了思索。
“这确实有些棘手，”他喃喃低语道，“不过咱们必须尽快拿出个章程来，因为我实在不忍心看到您着凉受冻。噢，办法其实很简单，公主殿下：您对这间屋子的陈设布置自然了如指掌，哪怕仅凭摸索也能立刻找到睡袍吧？咱们把灯熄了，这样您就能在黑暗中从浴缸里走出来，丝毫不必担心有任何失仪。”

===Original===

He was on the very point of turning off the switch of the lamp, when he stopped abruptly and came back to the bath. “I was forgetting that exasperating bell,” he said. “A movement is so very easily made: suppose you were to ring, by mere inadvertence, and regret it afterwards?” Putting his idea into action, the man made a quick cut with his razor and severed the two electric wires several feet above the ground. “That is excellent,” he said. “By the way, I don’t know where these other two wires go that run along the wall, but it is best to be on the safe side. Suppose there were another bell?” He lifted his razor once more and was trying to sever the electric wires when the steel blade cut the insulator and an alarming flash of light resulted. The man leaped into the air, and dropped his razor. “Good Lord!” he growled, “I suppose that will make you happy, madame: I have burnt my hand most horribly! These must be wires for the light! But no matter: I have still got one sound hand, and that will be enough for me to secure the darkness that you want. And anyhow, you can press the button of your bell as much as you like: it won’t ring. So I am sure of a few more minutes in your company.”
Sudden darkness fell upon the room. Sonia Danidoff hesitated for a moment and then half rose in the bath. All her pride as a great lady was in revolt. If she must defend her honour and her life, she was ready to do so, and despair would give her strength; but in any event she would be better out of the water, and on her feet, prepared. The darkness was complete, both in the bathroom and in the adjacent bedroom, and the silence was absolute. Standing up in the bath, Sonia Danidoff swept her arms round in a circle to feel for any obstacle. Her touch met nothing. She drew out one foot, and then the other, sprang towards the chair on which she had left her dressing-gown, slipped into it with feverish haste, slid her feet into her slippers, stood motionless for just a second and then, with sudden decision, moved to the switch by the door and turned on the light.
The man had gone from the bathroom, but taking two steps towards her bedroom Sonia Danidoff saw him smiling at her from the far end of that room.
“Sir,” she said, “this﻿—pleasantry﻿—has lasted long enough. You must go. You shall, you shall!”
“Shall?” the stranger echoed. “That is a word that is not often used to me.

===Chinese===

他正伸手准备拧灭电灯开关，突然动作一顿，又折返回到了浴缸旁。“我差点把那个令人讨厌的电铃给忘了，”他说，“人在慌乱中很容易手足失措：万一您纯粹出于无心误按了电铃，事后可就追悔莫及了。”说到做到，男子举起手中的剃刀凌空一划，干净利落地在距离地面几英尺高处割断了两根电线。“这就妥当了，”他说，“顺便提一句，沿着墙壁延伸的另外两根电线我还摸不清是通向哪儿的，但以防万一总是好的。万一那是另一个电铃呢？”他再次扬起剃刀，试图切断那两根电线，不料锋利的钢制刀刃割破了绝缘层，刹那间爆出一团骇人的刺目电火花。男子被电得凌空跳起，剃刀脱手跌落。“见鬼！”他恨恨地咒骂了一声，“我想这下您该心满意足了吧，夫人：我的手被严重烧伤了！这必定是照明用的电线！不过没关系，我还有一只完好的手，足够帮您营造出您所期盼的黑暗了。反正现在任凭您怎么按电铃按钮也无济于事，它再也不会响了。这样一来，我就能多陪您待上几分钟了。”
霎时间，整间浴室陷入了一片漆黑。索尼娅·达尼多夫稍稍迟疑了片刻，随即在浴缸中半撑起身子。身为贵妇的一切尊严与傲骨在这一刻彻底爆发。若是必须誓死捍卫自己的荣誉与生命，她已做好了殊死一搏的准备，绝望将赋予她无尽的力量；但无论如何，走出冰冷的水面、双脚踩在坚实的地面上严阵以待，总要好得多。浴室与相邻的卧室里全是一片伸手不见五指的死寂与黑暗。索尼娅·达尼多夫在浴缸中直立起身，双臂在身前画了个半圆摸索着障碍物。四周空无一物。她先迈出一只脚，接着又迈出另一只脚，飞身扑向搁置着睡袍的椅子，以狂乱的速度披上睡袍，将双脚滑入拖鞋，屏息凝神伫立了一秒钟，随后毅然决然地挪向门旁的开关，啪地按亮了卧室的电灯。
男子已经从浴室里消失了，但当索尼娅·达尼多夫跨入卧室两步后，赫然看到那个男人正站在房间那一头，笑吟吟地望着自己。
“先生，”她说，“这场……恶作剧……已经闹得够久了。你必须走。你必须立刻离开，必须离开！”
“必须？”陌生人玩味地重复着这个词，“还从来没有人敢对我用这种命令的字眼呢。

===Original===

But you are forgiven for not knowing that, Princess. I forgot for the moment that I have not been presented to you. But what is in your mind now?”
Between them was a little escritoire, on the top of which was lying the tiny inlaid revolver that Sonia Danidoff always carried when she went out at night. Could she but get that into her hands it would be a potent argument to induce this stranger to obey her. The Princess also knew that in the drawer of that escritoire which she could actually see half open, she had placed, only a few minutes before going in to her bath, a pocketbook filled with banknotes for a hundred and twenty thousand francs, money she had withdrawn from the strongroom of the hotel that very morning in order to meet some bills next day. She looked at the drawer and wondered if the pocketbook was still there, or if this mysterious admirer of hers was only a vulgar hotel thief after all. The man had followed her eyes to the revolver.
“That is an unusual knickknack to find in a lady’s room, Princess,” and he sprang in front of her as she was taking a step towards the escritoire, and took possession of the revolver. “Do not be alarmed,” he added, noticing her little gesture of terror. “I would not do you an injury for anything in the world. I shall be delighted to give this back to you in a minute, but first let me render it harmless.” He deftly slipped the six cartridges out of the barrel and then handed the now useless weapon to the Princess with a gallant little bow. “Do not laugh at my excess of caution: but accidents happen so easily!”
It was in vain that the Princess tried to get near her escritoire to ascertain if the drawer had been tampered with: the man kept between her and it all the time, still smiling, still polite, but watching every movement that she made. Suddenly he took his watch from his pocket.
“Two o’clock! Already! Princess, you will be vexed with me for having abused your hospitality to such an extent. I must go!” He appeared not to notice the sigh of relief that broke from her, but went on in a melodramatic tone. “I shall take my departure, not through the window like a lover, nor up the chimney like a thief, nor yet through a secret door behind the arras like a brigand of romance, but like a gentleman who has come to pay his tribute of homage and respect to the most enchanting woman in the world﻿—through the door!”

===Chinese===

不过念在您尚不知情的份上，我就原谅您了，公主殿下。我一时倒忘了自己还没正式由人引荐给您呢。不过，您此刻脑子里在打什么主意呢？”
横亘在两人之间的，是一张小巧的写字台，台面上赫然摆放着一把精巧的镶嵌工艺左轮小手枪——那是索尼娅·达尼多夫每逢夜间外出防身必备之物。只要能将那把手枪抢夺在手，必定会成为迫使这个不速之客乖乖就范的有力依仗。公主心里同样一清二楚，在写字台此刻正半敞着的抽屉里，就在她步入浴室前短短几分钟，她亲手放入了一只装有十二万法郎钞票的皮夹，那是她为了应付次日开销在当天上午刚刚从饭店保险库里提取出来的巨款。她的目光死死盯着那个抽屉，暗自揣测皮夹是否安在，亦或是眼前这位神秘的仰慕者归根结底不过是个下三滥的客房飞贼。男子的目光顺着她的视线落在了手枪上。
“在一位淑女的闺房里见到这等精致的玩意儿，可真叫人稀奇啊，公主殿下，”就在她朝着写字台迈出一步的刹那，男子一个箭步抢上前去，将手枪一把抄在手中。“别害怕，”注意到她惊骇退缩的神态，他补充道，“在这个世界上我伤害谁也绝不会伤害您分毫。我很乐意马上将它奉还给您，不过得容我先让它失去杀伤力。”他动作敏捷利落地退出了弹仓里的全部六发子弹，随后风度翩翩地向公主微微躬身，将如今已沦为废铁的枪身递还到她手中。“请别嘲笑我过于谨小慎微，意外事故这种事向来太容易发生了！”
公主几番试图靠近写字台查验抽屉是否被翻动过，但一切努力皆归于徒劳：男子始终不偏不倚地横亘在她与写字台之间，面带微笑，温文尔雅，却如鹰隼般警惕地捕捉着她的一举一动。突然间，他从怀中掏出怀表看了一眼。
“两点整！居然这么晚了！公主殿下，如此过分叨扰您的盛情款待，您必定要生我的气了。我必须告辞了！”他仿佛对她如释重负的长叹充耳不闻，转而用戏剧舞台般的夸张腔调高谈阔论道：“我的离去，既不学情夫那样翻窗遁走，也不似梁上君子那般钻烟囱溜走，更不像传奇小说里的绿林强盗那般闪入织锦壁毯后的密道潜逃，而要像一位前来向世间最迷人的绝代佳人奉上崇高敬意与爱慕的绅士一样——堂堂正正走大门！”

===Original===

He made a movement as if to go, and came back. “And what do you think of doing now, Princess? Perhaps you will be angry with me? Possibly some unpleasant discovery, made after my departure, will raise some animosity in your breast against me? You might even ring, directly my back is turned, and alarm the staff, merely to embarrass me in my exit, and without paying any attention to the subsequent possible scandal. That is a complicated arrangement of bells and telephones beside your bed! It would be a pity to spoil such a pretty thing, and besides, I hate doing unnecessary damage!” The Princess’s eyes turned once more to the drawer: it was practically certain that her money was not there now! But the man broke in again upon her thoughts. “What can I be thinking of? Just fancy my not having presented myself to you even yet! But as a matter of fact I do not want to tell you my name out loud: it is a romantic one, utterly out of keeping with the typically modern environment in which we are now. Ah, if we were only on the steep side of some mountain with the moon like a great lamp above us, or by the shore of some wild ocean, there would be some fascination in the proclamation of my identity in the silence of the night, or in the midst of lightning and thunder as the hurricane swept the seas! But here﻿—in a third-floor suite of the Royal Palace Hotel, surrounded by telephones and electric light, and standing by a window overlooking the Champs Élysées﻿—it would be a positive anachronism!” He took a card out of his pocket and drew near the little escritoire. “Allow me, Princess, to slip my card into this drawer, left open on purpose, it would seem,” and while the Princess uttered an exclamation she could not repress, he suited the action to the word. “And now, Princess,” he went on, compelling her to retreat before him right to the door of the anteroom opening on to the corridor, “you are too well bred, I am sure, not to wish to conduct your visitor to the door of your suite.” His tone altered abruptly, and in a deep imperious voice that made the Princess quake he ordered her: “And now, not a word, not a cry, not a movement until I am outside, or I will kill you!”
Clenching her fists, and summoning all her strength to prevent herself from swooning, Sonia Danidoff led the man to the anteroom door.

===Chinese===

他做出了拔步欲走的姿态，却又折返了回来。“那么，公主殿下，您眼下打算怎么办呢？或许您会对我大发雷霆？说不定在我离开后，某些不愉快的发现会激起您胸中对我的无尽敌意？您甚至可能在我的前脚刚刚迈出门槛的那一刻，便迫不及待地按响电铃惊动全楼的仆役，纯粹为了在我脱身之际制造麻烦，而全然不顾事后可能引发的风流丑闻。您床头那套集成了电铃与电话的呼叫设备可真够复杂的！毁掉如此精巧的玩意儿未免太可惜了，况且，我向来讨厌造成不必要的破坏！”公主的目光再次瞥向那个抽屉：几乎可以断定，里面的巨款此刻早已不翼而飞了！然而男子的言语再次打断了她的思绪。“我这脑子都在想些什么呢？您瞧瞧，直到现在我都还没来得及向您正式自报家门！不过说句实在话，我可不想大声说出自己的名字：那是一个极具浪漫传奇色彩的名号，同咱们眼前这个充满现代气息的环境完全格格不入。啊，倘若咱们此刻正置身于巍峨险峻的崇山之巅，头顶悬挂着如巨灯般的明月，亦或是漫步在波涛汹涌的狂暴荒海之滨，在万籁俱寂的子夜时分，抑或在飓风席卷怒海、电闪雷鸣的狂澜之中宣示我的真名，那该是何等扣人心弦的壮美！可是在这里——在皇家宫殿大饭店的三楼豪华套房里，四周环绕着电话与电灯，窗外俯瞰着香榭丽舍大街——那可就纯粹成了滑稽的时代错位了！”他从怀中摸出一张卡片，缓步走到小写字台旁。“请容许我，公主殿下，将我的名片塞进这个似乎是特意敞开着的抽屉里，”在公主忍不住发出一声惊呼的同时，他已经说到做到地将卡片塞了进去。“那么现在，公主殿下，”他一边说着，一边步步紧逼，迫使她一步步退向通往走廊的前厅大门，“凭您的教养与风度，我深信您绝不会吝啬亲自将贵客送到套房门外吧。”他的语气骤然剧变，用一种威严冰冷、令公主不寒而栗的深沉低音命令道：“现在，听好了：在我迈出大门之前，不许吐露半个字，不许发出一声尖叫，不许挪动分毫，否则我立刻取你性命！”
索尼娅·达尼多夫紧攥双拳，竭尽全身最后一丝力气克制住几乎令她昏厥的眩晕，引着男子走到了前厅门口。

===Original===

Slowly she unlocked the door and held it open, and the man stepped quietly through. The next second he was gone!
Leaping back into her bedroom Sonia Danidoff set every bell a-ringing; with great presence of mind she telephoned down to the hall porter: “Don’t let anybody go out! I have been robbed!” and she pressed hard upon the special button that set the great alarm bell clanging. Footsteps and voices resounded in the corridor: the Princess knew that help was coming and ran to open her door. The night watchman, and the manager of the third floor came running up and waiters appeared in numbers at the end of the corridor.
“Stop him! Stop him!” the Princess shouted. “He has only just gone out: a man in a dinner jacket, with a great black beard!”
---
A lad came hurrying out of the lift.
“Where are you going? What is the matter?” enquired the hall porter, whose lodge was at the far end of the hall, next to the courtyard of the hotel, the door into which he had just closed.
“I don’t know,” he answered. “There is a thief in the hotel! They are calling from the other side.”
“It’s not in your set, then? By the way, what floor are you on?”
“The second.”
“All right,” said the hall porter, “it’s the third floor that they are calling from. Go up and see what is wrong.”
The lad turned on his heel, and disregarding the notice forbidding servants to use the passenger lift, hurried back into it and upstairs again. He was a stoutly built fellow, with a smooth face and red hair. On the third floor he stopped, immediately opposite Sonia Danidoff’s suite. The Princess was standing at her door, taking no notice of the watchman Muller’s efforts to soothe her excitement, and mechanically twisting between her fingers the blank visiting card which her strange visitor had left in place of her pocketbook and the hundred and twenty thousand francs. There was no name whatever on the card.
“Well,” said Muller, to the redheaded lad, “where do you come from?”
“I’m the new man on the second floor,” the fellow answered. “The hall porter sent me up to find out what was the matter.”
“Matter!” said Muller. “Somebody has robbed the Princess. Here, send someone for the police at once.”
“I’ll run, sir,” and as the lift, instead of being sent down, had carelessly been sent up to the top floor, the young fellow ran down the staircase at full speed.

===Chinese===

她颤抖着缓缓拧开门锁，推开房门，男子神色自若地跨步而出。下一秒，他便消失得无影无踪！
索尼娅·达尼多夫发疯般扑回卧室，把所有的电铃全都按得震天响；凭着极大的沉着与机智，她抓起电话向底楼门厅的值班领班喊道：“别放任何人出去！我被抢劫了！”同时她狠狠按下了触发全楼火警警报的紧急按钮。走廊里顿时回荡起密集的脚步声与喧嚷的呼喊：公主知道援兵赶到了，急忙冲出去拉开大门。夜间巡逻守卫米勒与三楼楼层经理飞奔而至，数名侍者也纷纷涌现在走廊那一头。
“拦住他！快拦住他！”公主嘶声高喊道。“他刚刚才跑出去：一个穿晚礼服、留着大把黑胡子的男人！”
---
一个年轻杂役急匆匆从电梯里冲了出来。
“你往哪儿跑？出什么事了？”守在底楼大厅尽头、紧挨着饭店庭院门房的大堂侍役喝问道，他刚刚把通往庭院的大门死死闩上。
“我不知道，”小伙子回答，“饭店里进贼了！那头正在大呼小叫呢。”
“那也不是你们那层楼的事啊？顺便问一句，你是哪层楼的？”
“二楼的。”
“这就对了，”大堂侍役说，“喊叫声是从三楼传来的。快上去看看出了什么乱子。”
小伙子当即转身，全然不顾禁止仆役乘坐客用电梯的告示，急匆匆重新钻进电梯直奔楼上。那是个身材魁梧健硕的家伙，白净脸庞，留着一头姜黄色的红发。电梯在三楼停下，正对着索尼娅·达尼多夫的套房。公主正伫立在门前，全然不理会守卫米勒竭力安抚她情绪的言语，手指机械地反复翻弄着那位诡异的不速之客留下的一张空白名片——用来替换她那只装有十二万法郎的皮夹。名片上光溜溜的，连半个字迹都没有。
“喂，”米勒冲着那个红发小伙子喝道，“你从哪儿冒出来的？”
“我是二楼新来的杂役，”这家伙回答，“大堂侍役打发我上来查看到底出了什么事。”
“出事！”米勒叫道，“有人抢劫了公主殿下！快，立刻派人去叫警察！”
“我这就跑着去，长官！”眼见电梯非但没有降下去，反倒被粗心大意地升到了顶楼，小伙子拔腿便顺着楼梯飞奔而下。

===Original===

Through the telephone, Muller was just ordering the hall porter to send for the police, when the second-floor servant rushed up and caught him by the arm, dragging him away from the instrument.
“Open the door for the Lord’s sake! I’m off to the police station,” and the hall porter made haste to facilitate his departure.
---
On the top floor cries of astonishment reechoed. The servants had been alarmed by the uproar and, surprised to see the lift stop and nobody get out of it, they opened the door and found a heap of clothing, a false beard, and a wig. Two housemaids and a valet gazed in amazement at these extraordinary properties, and never thought of informing the manager, M. Louis. Meantime, however, that gentleman had hurried through the mazes of the hotel, and had just reached the third floor when he was stopped by the Baronne Van den Rosen, one of the hotel’s oldest patronesses.
“M. Louis!” she exclaimed, bursting into sobs. “I have just been robbed of my diamond necklace. I left it in a jewel-case on my table before going down to dinner. When I heard the noise just now, I got up and looked through my jewel-case, and the necklace is not there.”
M. Louis was too dazed to reply. Muller ran up to him.
“Princess Sonia Danidoff’s pocketbook has been stolen,” he announced; “but I have had the hotel doors shut and we shall be sure to catch the thief.”
The Princess came near to explain matters, but at that moment the servants came down from upstairs, bringing with them the makeup articles which they had found in the lift. They laid these on the ground without a word and M. Louis was staring at them when Muller had a sudden inspiration.
“M. Louis, what is the new man on the second floor like?”
Just at that instant a servant appeared at the end of the corridor, a middle-aged man with white whiskers and a bald head.
“There he is, coming towards us,” M. Louis replied. “His name is Arnold.”
“Good God!” cried Muller; “and the redheaded fellow: the carroty chap?” M. Louis shook his head, not understanding, and Muller tore himself away and rushed down to the hall porter. “Has he gone out? Has anyone gone out?”
“No one,” said the porter, “except, of course, the servant from the second floor, whom you sent for the police.”
“The carroty chap?” Muller enquired.
“Yes, the carroty chap.”

===Chinese===

米勒正抓起电话命令大堂侍役立刻报警，二楼的那个仆役突然狂奔而至，一把抓住了他的胳膊，硬生生将他从电话机旁拽开。
“看在上帝的分上快开门！我正要赶去警察局报警呢！”大堂侍役见状，急忙麻利地打开大门放他飞奔了出去。
---
顶楼上，一阵阵惊呼声此起彼伏。仆役们被楼下的骚乱彻底惊醒，惊异地发现电梯升到顶层停稳后却不见有人走出来，他们拉开电梯门，赫然发现轿厢地板上散落着一堆衣物、一把假胡须以及一顶假发。两名女仆和一名贴身男仆目瞪口呆地端详着这些离奇的乔装道具，一时竟没想起来去向总领班路易先生报告。然而在此期间，路易先生已经在错综复杂的饭店楼道间穿行寻查，刚迈上三楼，就被饭店最资深的常客之一范登罗森男爵夫人（Baronne Van den Rosen）一把拦住。
“路易先生！”男爵夫人失声痛哭道，“我的钻石项链刚刚被人偷了！下楼吃晚饭前我明明把它放在桌上的首饰盒里。刚才听见外面的吵闹声我爬起来查看首饰盒，项链已经不翼而飞了！”
路易先生惊愕得不知所措。米勒急匆匆跑上前来。
“索尼娅·达尼多夫公主的钱夹被盗了，”他报告道，“但我已经下令封锁了饭店的所有大门，咱们一定能把贼人瓮中捉鳖！”
公主走上前准备陈述案情，恰在此刻，顶楼的仆役们端着在电梯里缴获的乔装变装用具快步走下楼来。他们一言不发地将这些东西摆在地上，路易先生正盯着道具发愣，米勒脑海中突然灵光一闪。
“路易先生，二楼新来的那个杂役长什么模样？”
就在这个当口，走廊尽头晃晃悠悠走来一名侍者，那是个年过半百的中年男人，蓄着花白的络腮胡，头顶微秃。
“那不正是他嘛，正朝咱们走过来呢，”路易先生回答道，“他叫阿诺德。”
“我的天哪！”米勒惊呼失色，“那刚才那个红头发的家伙呢？那个满头姜黄色头发的混账是谁？”路易先生茫然地摇了摇头，不知所以；米勒一把挣脱开来，狂奔到底楼大堂找到侍役。“他跑出去了吗？刚才有人溜出去了吗？”
“谁也没放出去，”门房侍役回答道，“当然了，除了二楼那个被你打发去警察局报案的仆役以外。”
“那个姜黄色头发的家伙？”米勒急问。
“对啊，就是那个姜黄色头发的家伙。”

===Original===

Princess Sonia Danidoff lay back in an easy chair, receiving the anxious attentions of Nadine, her Circassian maid. M. Louis was holding salts to her nostrils. The Princess still held in her hands the card left by the mysterious stranger who had just robbed her so cleverly of a hundred and twenty thousand francs. As she slowly came to herself the Princess gazed at the card as if fascinated, and this time her haggard eyes grew wide with astonishment. For upon the card, which hitherto had appeared immaculately white, marks and letters were gradually becoming visible, and the Princess read:
“Fan﻿—tô﻿—mas!”

===Chinese===

索尼娅·达尼多夫公主虚弱地斜倚在安乐椅中，接受着切尔克斯女仆娜丁焦急万分的侍奉照料。路易先生正将嗅盐凑到她的鼻翼前。公主的手中依然紧攥着那位神秘陌生人留下的那张名片——此人以如此神鬼莫测的手段轻而易举地卷走了她十二万法郎的巨款。当她渐渐苏醒回过神来，公主神情恍惚、仿佛着了魔一般死死盯视着手中的名片，刹那间，她那双原本憔悴空洞的眼眸因极度的惊骇而猛然圆睁。只见在那张方才还洁白无瑕的名片纸面上，一抹抹墨痕与字母正缓缓浮现成型，公主颤声读出了那个名字：
“芳——托——马斯！”

## XI Magistrate and Detective / 第十一章 预审法官与警探
===Original===

M. Fuselier was standing in his office in the law courts at Paris, meditatively smoothing the nap of his silk hat. His mind was busy with the enquiries he had been prosecuting during the day, and although he had no reason to be dissatisfied with his day’s work he had no clear idea as to what his next steps ought to be.
Three discreet taps on the door broke in upon his thoughts.
“Come in,” he said, and then stepped forward with a hearty welcome as he recognised his visitor. “Juve, by all that is wonderful! What good wind has blown you here? I haven’t seen you for ages. Busy?”
“Frightfully.”
“Well, it’s a fact that there’s no dearth of sensational crime just now. The calendar is terribly heavy.”
Juve had ensconced himself in a huge easy chair in a corner of the room.
“Yes,” he said, “you are quite right. But unfortunately the calendar won’t be a brilliant one for the police. There may be lots of cases, but there are not lots that they have worked out to a finish.”
“You’ve got nothing to grumble at,” M. Fuselier smiled. “You have been in enough cases lately that were worked out to a finish. Your reputation isn’t in any danger of diminishing.”
“I don’t know what you mean,” Juve said deprecatingly. “If you refer to the Beltham and Langrune cases, you must admit that your congratulations are not deserved. I have achieved no definite result in either of those affairs.”
M. Fuselier also dropped into a comfortable chair. He lighted a cigarette.
“You have found out nothing fresh about that mysterious murder of Lord Beltham?”
“Nothing. I’m done. It is an insoluble mystery to me.”
“You seem to be very sorry for yourself, but really you needn’t be, Juve. You cleared up the Beltham case, and you solved the Langrune case, although you try to make out you didn’t. And allow me to inform you, those two successes count, my friend.”
“You are very kind, but you are rather misinformed. Unfortunately I have not cleared up the Beltham case at all.”
“You found the missing peer.”
“Well, yes, but﻿—”
“That was an amazing achievement. By the way, Juve, what led you to go to the rue Lévert to search Gurn’s trunks?”
“That was very simple. You remember what an excitement there was when Lord Beltham disappeared? Well, when I was called in I saw at once that all ideas of accident or suicide might be dismissed, and that consequently the disappearance was due to crime.

===Chinese===

菲兹利埃先生（M. Fuselier）正伫立在巴黎法院大楼的办公室里，若有所思地摩挲着大礼帽上的丝绒。他满脑子都在琢磨着这一整天展开的司法调查讯问，尽管他没有理由对今天的工作进展感到不满，但对于下一步该采取何种行动，他依然没有清晰的头绪。
门上三记谨慎的轻叩打断了他的沉思。
“请进，”他应声道，随即认出来客，热情洋溢地迎上前去。“朱夫，天哪，真是稀客！什么好风把你给吹来了？咱们有些日子没见了。最近很忙吗？”
“忙得不可开交。”
“也是，眼下轰动一时的重案要案确实层出不穷。待办案件名册上沉重得很呢。”
朱夫已经在房间角落里的一张宽大安乐椅中坐了下来。
“是啊，”他说，“你说得一点没错。不过可惜的是，这份名册对警方而言可算不上什么光彩的战绩。案子固然多如牛毛，但真正水落石出、彻底侦破结案的却寥寥无几。”
“你可没什么好抱怨的，”菲兹利埃先生微笑着说，“最近你参与侦破的案子中，彻查到底的还少吗？你的威名可丝毫没有减损的危险。”
“我不知道你指的是什么，”朱夫谦逊地摇了摇头，“如果你是指贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）案和德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）案，你必须承认你的祝贺实在有些名不副实。在这两桩案子里，我都未曾取得任何确凿无疑的定论。”
菲兹利埃先生也在一张舒适的椅子上坐了下来，点燃了一支香烟。
“关于贝尔特姆勋爵神秘遇害的那桩案子，你难道就没查出任何新线索？”
“毫无头绪。我彻底没辙了。对我来说，那依然是一个解不开的谜团。”
“你似乎对自己太苛刻了，朱夫，但真的大可不必。你查清了贝尔特姆案，而且不管你怎么辩解，你也侦破了朗格吕纳案。请容我提醒你一句，老兄，这两次大获全胜可是货真价实的殊勋啊。”
“承蒙夸奖，不过你恐怕有些误解。不幸的是，贝尔特姆案我根本算不上破案。”
“可你找到了那位失踪的勋爵啊。”
“嗯，话虽如此，但是……”
“那本身就是一项了不起的惊人壮举。顺便问一句，朱夫，当初究竟是什么启发你跑到莱韦尔街（rue Lévert）去搜查居尔恩（Gurn）的大皮箱的？”
“那其实再简单不过了。你还记得贝尔特姆勋爵失踪时轰动全国的景象吧？当我受命接手此案时，我立刻意识到无论是意外事故还是自杀的假设全都可以排除，因此勋爵的失踪必定源自犯罪。

===Original===

Once convinced of that, I very naturally suspected every single person who had ever had relations with Lord Beltham, for there was no single individual for me to suspect. Then I found out that the ex-Ambassador had been in continuous association with an Englishman named Gurn whom he had known in the South African war, and who led a very queer sort of life. That of course took me to Gurn’s place, if for nothing else than to pick up information. And﻿—well, that’s all about it. It was just by going to Gurn’s place to pump him, rather than anything else, that I found the noble lord’s remains locked away in the trunk.”
“Your modesty is delightful, Juve,” said M. Fuselier with an approving nod. “You present things as if they were all matters of course, whereas really you are proving your extraordinary instinct. If you had arrived only twenty-four hours later the corpse would have been packed off to the Transvaal, and only the Lord knows if after that the extraordinary mystery ever would have been cleared up.”
“Luck,” Juve protested: “pure luck!”
“And were your other remarkable discoveries luck too?” enquired M. Fuselier with a smile. “There was your discovery that sulphate of zinc had been injected into the body to prevent it from smelling offensively.”
“That was only a matter of using my eyes,” Juve protested.
“All right,” said the magistrate, “we will admit that you did not display any remarkable acumen in the Beltham case, if you would rather have it so. That does not alter the fact that you have solved the Langrune case.”
“Solved it!”
M. Fuselier flicked the ash off his cigarette, and leant forward towards the detective.
“Of course you know that I know you were at the Cahors Assizes, Juve? What was your impression of the whole affair﻿—of the verdict, and of Etienne Rambert’s guilt or innocence?”
Juve got up and began to walk up and down the room, followed by the magistrate’s eyes. He seemed to be hesitating as to whether he would answer at all, but finally he stopped abruptly and faced his friend.
“If I were talking to anybody but you, M. Fuselier, I would either not answer at all, or I would give an answer that was no answer! But as it is﻿—, well, in my opinion, the Langrune case is only just beginning, and nothing certain is known at all.”
“According to that, Charles Rambert is innocent?”
“I don’t say that.”
“What then?

===Chinese===

一旦确信了这一点，我自然而然地开始怀疑每一个曾与贝尔特姆勋爵打过交道的人，因为当时根本没有任何特定目标可供怀疑。随后我查明，这位前任大使一直与一个名叫居尔恩的英国人往来密切，两人在南非战争中相识，而这个居尔恩的生活行径极其诡谲古怪。这就顺理成章地引着我去了居尔恩的住处，哪怕仅仅是为了打探些消息也好。事情大致就是这么回事。我跑到居尔恩的住处原本只是想套套他的口风，结果却在那口锁紧的大皮箱里赫然发现了那位贵族勋爵的遗体。”
“你的谦逊真令人赞赏，朱夫，”菲兹利埃先生赞许地点了点头，“你总把一切说得仿佛顺理成章，然而这恰恰证明了你异乎寻常的敏锐直觉。要是你晚去哪怕二十四个小时，那具尸体早就被打包发往德兰士瓦（Transvaal）了，到那时天知道这桩离奇至极的悬案还能不能水落石出。”
“纯属运气，”朱夫辩解道，“纯粹是瞎猫碰上死耗子！”
“那你其他的那些惊人发现难道也全靠运气吗？”菲兹利埃先生微笑着发问，“比如你发现尸体内被注射了硫酸锌以防止散发恶臭腐味？”
“那不过是用眼睛仔细看罢了，”朱夫抗议道。
“好吧，”预审法官说，“如果你非要这么坚持，咱们姑且算你在贝尔特姆案中没有展现出多么惊人的洞察力吧。但这依然改变不了你侦破了朗格吕纳案这一铁打的事实。”
“侦破了那桩案子？”
菲兹利埃先生弹了弹烟灰，身子向前微倾，凑近警探。
“你当然明白，我知道你当时就在卡奥尔重罪法庭（Cahors Assizes）的旁听席上，朱夫。你对整桩审判有什么看法——对判决结果，以及埃蒂安·朗贝尔到底有罪还是清白？”
朱夫站起身来，在办公室里踱来踱去，预审法官的目光紧紧追随着他的步伐。警探似乎在犹豫究竟要不要回答，但最终他骤然停下脚步，面对着自己的老朋友。
“如果是换作其他任何人，菲兹利埃先生，我要么一言不发，要么就给出一个等于没说的含糊回答！但既然是对你……好吧，依我看，朗格吕纳案才刚刚揭开序幕，所有事情都还根本没有盖棺定论。”
“照你这么说，夏尔·朗贝尔（Charles Rambert）是清白的？”
“我可没这么说。”
“那究竟是怎么回事？

===Original===

I suppose you don’t think the father was the murderer?”
“The hypothesis is not absurd! But there! What is the real truth of the whole affair? That is what I am wondering all the time. That murder is never out of my head; it interests me more and more every day. Oh, yes, I’ve got lots of ideas, but they are all utterly vague and improbable: sometimes my imagination seems to be running away with me.”
He stopped, and M. Fuselier wagged a mocking finger at him.
“Juve,” he said, “I charge you formally with attempting to implicate Fantômas in the murder of the Marquise de Langrune!”
The detective replied in the same tone of raillery.
“Guilty, my lord!”
“Good lord, man!” the magistrate exclaimed, “Fantômas is a perfect obsession with you,” and as Juve acquiesced with a laugh the magistrate dropped his bantering tone. “Shall I tell you something, Juve? I too am beginning to have an obsession for that fantastic miscreant! And what I want to know is why you have not come to me before to ask me about that sensational robbery at the Royal Palace Hotel?”
“The robbery from Princess Sonia Danidoff?”
“Yes: the Fantômas robbery!”
“Fantômas, eh?” Juve protested. “That remains to be seen.”
“Why, man,” M. Fuselier retorted, “you have heard that detail about the card the man left, haven’t you?﻿—the visiting card that was blank when the Princess found it, and on which the name of Fantômas afterwards became visible?”
“There’s no Fantômas about that, in my opinion.”
“Why not?”
“Well, it isn’t one of Fantômas’ little ways to leave clear traces behind him. One might as well picture him committing robbery or murder in a cap with a neat little band round it: ‘Fantômas and Co.’ He might even add ‘Discretion and Dispatch!’ No, it’s most unlikely.”
“You don’t think Fantômas capable of throwing down his glove to the police in the shape of some such material proof of his identity?”
“I always base my arguments on the balance of probabilities,” Juve replied. “What emerges from this Royal Palace story is that some common hotel thief conceived the ingenious idea of casting suspicion on Fantômas: it was just a trick to mislead the police: at least, that is my opinion.”
But M. Fuselier declined to be convinced.
“No, you are wrong, Juve: it was no common hotel thief who stole Mme.

===Chinese===

总不至于连你都怀疑杀人凶手其实是当父亲的吧？”
“这种假说并非毫无道理！不过话又说回来，整桩案子的真正真相究竟是什么？这正是我日夜苦思冥想的问题。那桩谋杀案从来没有从我的脑海中离开过分秒；随着日子一天天过去，它反倒令我越来越着迷。噢，没错，我脑子里有无数个念头，但全都是些极其模糊且看似荒诞不经的构想：有时候我都觉得自己是不是被想象力冲昏了头脑。”
他停了下来，菲兹利埃先生冲他戏谑地晃了晃手指。
“朱夫，”他说，“我正式指控你：企图将芳托马斯（Fantômas）同德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人的谋杀案扯上干系！”
警探用同样的戏谑口吻回答道：
“法官大人，我认罪！”
“我的老天爷啊！”法官惊叹道，“芳托马斯简直成了你的心魔了！”看到朱夫哈哈大笑表示默认，法官收敛了调侃的口吻。“朱夫，想听我说句实话吗？就连我也开始对这个神出鬼没的巨枭魔头着魔了！我现在最想知道的是，皇家宫殿大饭店（Royal Palace Hotel）那桩轰动全城的劫案发生后，你为什么竟然没主动来找我打听案情？”
“你是说索尼娅·达尼多夫公主（Princess Sonia Danidoff）失窃的那桩案子？”
“没错，正是芳托马斯干的那桩大劫案！”
“芳托马斯？哈，”朱夫反驳道，“那还有待查证呢。”
“天哪，老兄，”菲兹利埃先生反唇相讥，“那贼人留下的名片细节你总该听说了吧？——就是公主最初发现时洁白无瑕、随后字迹渐渐浮现、赫然显出‘芳托马斯’大名的那张名片！”
“依我看，那绝不可能是芳托马斯所为。”
“为什么不可能？”
“因为在作案现场留下如此扎眼的确凿痕迹，向来不是芳托马斯的行事风格。照这么看，人们干脆可以想象他每次行凶抢劫时脑袋上都戴着一顶绣着花体的制服帽，上面端端正正印着：‘芳托马斯商号’，甚至还可以加上一句座右铭：‘守口如瓶，利落利索！’不，这绝无可能。”
“你难道认为芳托马斯不屑于以这种留下真凭实据的方式向警方公然下战书？”
“我向来只根据概率大小来进行逻辑推演，”朱夫回答，“从这桩皇家宫殿大饭店的劫案中，真正能看出的不过是某个寻常的饭店客房大盗灵机一动，想出了嫁祸给芳托马斯以混淆警方视线的狡黠花招——纯属误导警方的诡计，至少我是这么认为的。”
但菲兹利埃先生坚决不肯苟同。
“不，你错了，朱夫：能偷走范登罗森夫人（Mme. Van den Rosen）的项链并洗劫索尼娅公主十二万法郎巨款的，绝不是普通的客房毛贼。

===Original===

Van den Rosen’s necklace and Princess Sonia’s hundred and twenty thousand francs; the prize was big enough to appeal to Fantômas: and the amazing audacity of the crime is suggestive too. Just think what coolness the man must have had to be able to paralyse the Princess’s power of resistance when she tried to call for help: and also to get clear away in spite of the hosts of servants in the hotel and all the precautions taken!”
“Tell me all about the robbery, M. Fuselier,” said Juve.
The magistrate sat down at his desk and took up the notes he had made in the course of his official enquiry that day. He told Juve everything he had been able to elicit.
“The most amazing thing to me,” he said in conclusion, “is the way the fellow, when he had once got out of Princess Sonia’s room, contrived to get into the lift, shed his evening dress, get into livery, and make his first attempt to escape. When the hall porter stopped him he did not lose his head, but got into the lift again, sent that flying up to the top of the hotel with the clothes that would have betrayed him, calmly presented himself before Muller, the night watchman, and contrived to be told to go for the police, ran down the stairs again, and took advantage of the night watchman’s telephoning to the hall porter to get the latter to open the door for him, and so marched off as easily as you please. A man who kept his nerve like that and could make such amazing use of every circumstance, who was so quick and daring, and who was capable of carrying through such a difficult comedy in the middle of the general uproar, richly deserves to be taken for Fantômas!”
Juve sat in deep consideration of the whole story.
“That isn’t what interests me most,” he said at last. “His escape from the hotel might have been effected by any clever thief. What I think more remarkable is the means he took to prevent the Princess from screaming when he was just leaving her rooms: that really was masterly.

===Chinese===

这么一大笔惊人财富足以勾起芳托马斯的胃口；而整桩罪行那令人咋舌的胆大妄为，更充满了典型的魔头印记。想想看，这贼人得拥有何等泰山崩于前而不动色的心理素质，才能在公主试图呼救时彻底瘫痪她的抵抗意识！更不用说在整栋饭店仆役如云、层层严加防范的重围之下，竟能从容不迫地全身而退！”
“菲兹利埃先生，把这桩劫案的来龙去脉原原本本跟我讲讲吧，”朱夫说道。
预审法官坐回书桌前，拿起了当天正式司法侦查讯问的案卷笔记。他将自己查证到的所有细节巨细靡遗地讲给朱夫听。
“依我看最令人叹为观止的一幕，”法官在结案陈词般说道，“当属这家伙一旦迈出索尼娅公主的房门，竟能在电梯里飞快褪去晚礼服、换上侍者制服，并展开他的第一次突围尝试。在大堂侍役拦住去路时，他不仅毫无慌乱，反倒敏捷地重新钻入电梯，把足以暴露身份的衣物打发到顶楼，随后从容现身在夜班巡逻守卫米勒面前，巧妙地诱使对方派自己去警察局报案；紧接着飞奔下楼，趁着守卫给大堂打内线电话的空当哄骗门房大开正门，就这么大摇大摆、易如反掌地扬长而去！一个能在全楼大乱的人声鼎沸中保持如此冷酷的神经、如此精妙地化一切意外为己所用、身手如此敏捷悍勇，且能在众目睽睽下将一出大戏演得天衣无缝的家伙，完全配得上被当成芳托马斯！”
朱夫陷入了沉思，仔细咀嚼着故事中的每一个细节。
“那些倒不是最吸引我的地方，”他良久才开口道，“能从饭店全身而退，任何一个身手敏捷的高明窃贼或许都能办到。我认为更加非同凡响的，是他临出门前用来封住公主嘴巴、让她不敢尖叫求救的心理操控手段——那手段才真正称得上是登峰造极的大师手笔。

===Original===

Instead of trying to get her as far away as possible and shut her up in her bedroom, to take her with him to the very door opening on to the corridor, where the faintest cry might have involved the worst possible consequences, and to be sure that the terror he had inspired would prevent her from uttering that cry, to be able to assume that the victim was so overwrought that she would make no effort at all and could do nothing﻿—that is really very good indeed: quite admirable psychology! Fine work!”
“So you see there are some unusual features in the case,” said M. Fuselier complacently: “this, for instance: why do you suppose the fellow stayed such a long time with the Princess and went through all that comedy business in the bathroom? Don’t forget that she came in late, and it is extremely probable that he might have finished his job before she returned.”
Juve passed his hand through his hair, a characteristic trick when his mind was working.
“I can imagine only one answer to that question, M. Fuselier. But you have inspected the scene of the crime: tell me first, where do you think the rascal was hidden?”
“Oh, I can answer that definitely. The Princess’s suite of rooms ends in the bathroom, you know, and the chief things there are the famous bath, some cupboards, and a shower bath: the shower bath is one of those large model Norchers with lateral as well as vertical sprays, and a waterproof curtain hanging from rings at the top right down to the tub at the bottom. There were footmarks on the enamel of the tub, so it is clear that the thief hid there, behind the curtain, until the Princess got into her bath.”
“And I suppose the shower bath is in the corner of the room near the window?” Juve went on. “And the window was partly open, or had been until the maid went in to prepare her mistress’s bath? It’s quite interesting! The man had just succeeded in stealing the necklace from Mme. Van den Rosen, whose rooms are next to Princess Sonia’s: for some reason or other he had not been able to escape through the corridor, and so he naturally made up his mind to get into the Princess’s suite, which he did by the simple process of stepping over the railing on the balcony and walking in through the open window of the dressing-room.”
“And then Nadine came in, and he had to hide?”
“No, no!” said Juve, “you are getting on too fast.

===Chinese===

他没有试图把她推得远远的锁在卧房深处，反而带着她一路走到正对着走廊的前厅大门旁——在那个位置，只要受害者喉咙里发出哪怕最微弱的一声尖叫，都会引来万劫不复的严重后果；而他却能百分之百确信自己先前施加的恐怖威慑足以死死封住她的喉咙，能够精准断定受害者此时的精神防线已经彻底瓦解崩溃、连动一动手指的力气都没有——这确实精妙绝伦，对心理学的拿捏简直令人拍案叫绝！干得太漂亮了！”
“所以你看，这案子里确实有许多匪夷所思的疑点吧，”菲兹利埃先生得意地说道，“比方说这一点：你认为那家伙究竟为什么要在公主房里逗留那么长时间，甚至在浴室里大费周章地演上那么一出荒诞大戏？别忘了公主回房很晚，他原本极有可能在她回来之前就利索地完成盗窃脱身。”
朱夫习惯性地用手理了理头发——这是他大脑高速运转时标志性的小动作。
“对这个问题，菲兹利埃先生，我想象中只有一种合理解释。不过你亲自去现场勘验过，先告诉我，你认为那家伙最初藏在什么地方？”
“噢，这我可以明确回答。你知道，公主套房的最深处就是浴室，里面的大件家当无非是那只著名的浴缸、几面储物壁橱，以及一套淋浴花洒设备；淋浴花洒是那种诺歇尔公司的大型豪华款式，带有上下喷淋与侧面环形喷嘴，防水帘从顶部的滑环一路严严实实垂挂到底部的冲淋盆。搪瓷冲淋盆的地板上留有脚印，所以很明显，在公主步入浴缸之前，窃贼一直藏身在防水帘后面的冲淋盆里。”
“我猜那套淋浴花洒正好装在浴室靠近窗户的墙角吧？”朱夫紧接着问道，“而窗户当时是半开着的，或者在女仆进去给女主人放洗澡水之前一直开着？这可太有意思了！这家伙刚刚从隔壁范登罗森夫人的房间里得手了钻石项链，由于某种突发原因，他无法穿过走廊脱身，于是顺理成章地下定决心潜入公主的套房；他所要做的不过是跨过阳台上的铁栏杆，直接从盥洗室敞开的窗户翻身跳进去。”
“然后娜丁（Nadine）恰好走了进来，逼得他不得不就地隐藏？”
“不，不！”朱夫说，“你想得太快了。

===Original===

If that had been so, there would have been no need for all the bath business; besides, the Princess was robbed, too, you know. That was not just chance, it was planned; and so if the thief hid in the shower bath he did so on purpose to wait for the Princess.”
“But he did not want her!” Fuselier retorted: “very much the reverse. If he was in the room before anybody else, all he had to do was, take the pocketbook and go!”
“Not a bit of it!” said Juve. “This robbery took place at the end of the month, when the Princess would have big monthly bills to meet, as the thief must have known. He must have found out that she had withdrawn her portfolio and money from the custody of the hotel. But he must have been ignorant of where she had placed the portfolio; and he waited for her to ask her﻿—and she told him!”
“That’s a pretty tall yarn!” M. Fuselier protested. “What on earth do you base it all upon? The Princess would never have shown the man the drawer where the money was taken from!”
“Yes, she did!” said Juve. “Look here: this is what happened: the fellow wanted to steal this pocketbook, and did not know where it was. He hid in the shower bath and waited, either for the Princess to go to bed or take a bath, either of which would place her at his mercy.
