“Snug” and “comfortable” were the right words with which to describe Juve’s private abode. The detective had attained an honourable and lucrative position in his profession, and, exposed as he was in the course of his work to all manner of dangers and privations, had compensated himself by making an entirely satisfactory, if not luxurious, nest where he could rest after his labours.
When he had finished his breakfast he lighted a big cigar and sank into an easy chair, crossing his hands behind his head. He turned a steady gaze upon Charles Rambert, who was still completely puzzled, and half frightened by this sudden amiability, and did not know whether he was a prisoner or not.
“I will give you a bit of good news; that is, that you are innocent of the Langrune affair when you were Charles Rambert, and innocent also of the Danidoff affair, when you were Mademoiselle Jeanne. I need not say anything about the scrap last night, in which you played a still more distinguished part.”
“Why tell me that?” asked Charles Rambert nervously.

===Chinese===

朱夫口中吹着欢快轻巧的快步舞曲——这在他身上向来是心情畅快的确凿表征。他推开那间关押着夏尔·朗贝尔（Charles Rambert）的小屋房门，打量着熟睡中的少年。
“年轻可真好啊，”他对留守看管的看守随口赞叹道，“这小子一头扎进最凶险的亡命风波，脖子离断头台就差一寸，可熬了一个大夜之后，睡得居然比法国荣誉军团（Legion of Honour）的勋章院长还要安稳香甜！”他伸出一只手，亲切地推了推少年。“起来吧，小懒虫！已经十点钟了，我该把你带走了。”
“去哪儿？”这个可怜的孩子揉着惺忪的睡眼问道。
“好奇心重果然是你改不掉的毛病，”朱夫话带玄机地回答，“行了，前头有十五分钟的车程在等着咱们。不过你可不是去坐牢；我要带你回我自己家！”
---
朱夫解下了硬领和领带，换上一件旧夹克，把一大碗牛奶泡面包摆到夏尔·朗贝尔面前，自己则悠闲自得地享用起早餐来。
“刚才在车上我懒得回答你的问题，”他说，“因为我顶讨厌在马车里跟人谈正事，两个人并排坐着，既瞧不见对方的脸，又有一大半话听不真切。不过眼下咱们舒舒服服地待在这儿，我可就没理由再吊着你的胃口了，我要告诉你一桩好消息。”
用“温馨”和“惬意”来形容朱夫的这处私人寓所再恰当不过了。这位警探在行当里赢得了受人尊敬且收入颇丰的地位，加之他在办案过程中时刻面临各种危险与清苦，便在私下里给自己营造了一处虽算不上奢华、却绝对惬意妥帖的安乐窝，以便在繁重劳碌之余能好好休整一番。
吃完早餐，他点燃了一支大雪茄，整个人陷进一张安乐椅里，双手交叠枕在脑后。他目光沉稳地凝视着夏尔·朗贝尔；少年此时依然满腹狐疑，面对这突如其来的和颜悦色更平添了几分惶恐，全然吃不准自己眼下究竟算不算一名囚犯。
“我要告诉你的一桩好消息就是：你在作为夏尔·朗贝尔时，与朗格吕纳案毫无干系；而在化身为让娜小姐（Mademoiselle Jeanne）时，也绝非达尼多夫案的元凶。至于昨晚那场混战，你在其中扮演的角色更是功不可没，我就无须赘言了。”
“为什么单单跟我说这些？”夏尔·朗贝尔紧张地问道。

===Original===

“Of course I know I did not rob Princess Sonia Danidoff; but how did you recognise me last night, and how did you find out that I was Mademoiselle Jeanne?”
Juve smiled, and shook back a lock of hair that was falling over his eyes.
“Listen, my boy: do you suppose that thundering blow you dealt the excellent Henri Verbier when he was making love to Mademoiselle Jeanne, could fail to make me determined to find out who that young lady was who had the strength of a man?”
The allusion made Charles Rambert most uneasy.
“But that does not explain how you recognised me in Paul tonight. I recognised you in Henri Verbier at the hotel, but I had no idea that it was you last night.”
“That’s nothing,” said Juve with a shake of the head. “And you may understand once for all that when I have once looked anybody square in the face, he needs to be an uncommonly clever fellow to escape me afterwards by means of any disguise. You don’t know how to make up, but I do; and that’s why I took you in and you did not take me in.”
“What makes you believe I did not rob Princess Sonia Danidoff?” Charles Rambert asked after a pause. “I am quite aware that everything points to my having been the thief.”
“Not quite everything,” Juve answered gently. “There are one or two things you don’t know, and I’ll tell you one of them. The Princess was robbed by the same man who robbed Mme. Van den Rosen, wasn’t she? Well, Mme. Van den Rosen was the victim of a burglary: some of the furniture in her room was broken into, and the tests I made this morning with the dynamometer proved to me that you are not strong enough to have caused those fractures.”
“Not strong enough?” Charles Rambert ejaculated.
“No. I told you at the time that your innocence would be proved if you were strong enough, but I said that to prevent you from playing tricks and not putting out all your strength. As a matter of fact it was your comparative weakness that saved you. The dynamometer tests and the figures I obtained just now prove absolutely that you are innocent of the Van den Rosen robbery and, consequently, of the robbery from Sonia Danidoff.”
Again the lad reflected for a minute or two.
“But you didn’t know who I was when you came to the hotel, did you? And therefore had no suspicion that I was Charles Rambert? That’s true, isn’t it? How did you find out? I was supposed to be dead.”
“That was a child’s job,” Juve replied.

===Chinese===

“我自然清楚自己没有抢劫索尼娅·达尼多夫公主（Princess Sonia Danidoff）；但你昨晚是怎么认出我来的，又是怎么查出我就是让娜小姐的？”
朱夫微微一笑，甩了甩遮在眼前的碎发。
“听着，孩子：那位可敬的亨利·韦尔比耶（Henri Verbier）在向让娜小姐示爱调情时，挨了你势大力沉的一记闷棍，你觉得这能不让我下定决心、非要把这位力气大得赛过男人的年轻小姐查个水落石出吗？”
这番话让夏尔·朗贝尔感到极不自在。
“但这依然解释不通你今晚是怎么在保罗身上认出我的。在饭店时我认出了你假扮的亨利·韦尔比耶，可昨晚我根本不知道那个人就是你。”
“那根本算不了什么，”朱夫摇了摇头说，“你趁早给我记牢了：只要我堂堂正正端详过一个人的脸，那家伙之后任凭怎么乔装改扮，要是没通天的本事，休想逃过我的法眼。你根本不懂易容术，而我是此中行家；这就是为什么我能瞒过你，而你却瞒不过我。”
“那究竟是什么让你相信我没有抢劫索尼娅·达尼多夫公主呢？”夏尔·朗贝尔沉默了片刻后问道，“我很清楚，所有证据都死死指明我就是窃贼。”
“倒也并非一切，”朱夫温和地回答，“有些内情你并不知晓，不妨跟你挑明其中一件。洗劫公主的凶手和抢劫范·登·罗森夫人（Mme. Van den Rosen）的是同一人，对吧？要知道，范·登·罗森夫人是一桩入室破门盗窃案的受害者：她房间里的好几件家具都遭到了暴力撬砸。而我今天清晨用测力计对你进行的测试向我证明，以你的力气根本不足以砸裂撬破那些家具。”
“力气不够？”夏尔·朗贝尔失声惊呼。
“没错。当时我跟说如果你力气够大就能自证清白，那不过是防着你耍滑头故意不使出全力罢了。实际上，恰恰是你相对羸弱的臂力救了你的命。测力计的测试结果以及我刚才获取的精确数据，确凿无疑地证明你与范·登·罗森案毫无瓜葛，因而索尼娅·达尼多夫的失窃案也绝非你所为。”
少年再次低头沉思了一两分钟。
“可你当初来到饭店时，并不知道我的真实身份，不是吗？当时你根本没怀疑过我就是夏尔·朗贝尔，对不对？那你究竟是怎么查出来的？外界所有人都以为我早就死了。”
“那不过是小菜一碟，”朱夫回答道。

===Original===

“I got the anthropometric records of the body that had been buried as yours, and I planned to get symmetrical photographs of you in your character of Mademoiselle Jeanne, as I did of you today at headquarters. My first job was to lay hands upon Mademoiselle Jeanne, and I very soon found her, as I expected, turned into a man again, and living in the most disreputable company. I made any number of enquiries, and when I went to the Saint-Anthony’s Pig last evening I knew that it was some unknown person who had been buried in your stead; that Paul was Mademoiselle Jeanne; and that Mademoiselle Jeanne was Charles Rambert. It was my intention to arrest you, and to ascertain definitely by means of the dynamometer that you were innocent of the Langrune and the Danidoff crimes.”
“What you tell me about the dynamometer explains how you know I am not the man who committed the robbery at the hotel, but what clears me in your eyes of the Langrune murder?”
“Bless my soul!” Juve retorted, “you are arguing as if you wanted to prove you were guilty. Well, my boy, it’s the same story as the other. The man who murdered the Marquise de Langrune smashed things, and the dynamometer has proved that you are not strong enough to have been the man.”
“And suppose I had been mad at the time,” Charles Rambert said, his hesitation and his tone betraying his anxiety about the answer, “could I have been strong enough then? Might I have committed these crimes without knowing anything about it?”
But Juve shook his head.
“I know: you are referring to your mother, and are haunted by an idea that through some hereditary taint you might be a somnambulist and have done these things in your sleep. Come, Charles Rambert, finish your breakfast and put all that out of your head. To begin with, you would not have been strong enough, even then; and in the next place there is nothing at present to show that you are mad, nor even that your poor mother﻿—But I need not go on: I’ve got some rather odd notions on that subject.”
“Then, M. Juve﻿—”
“Drop the ‘monsieur’; call me ‘Juve.’ ”
“Then, if you know that I am innocent, you can go and tell my father? I have nothing to fear? I can reappear in my own name?”
Juve looked at the lad with an ironical smile.
“How you go ahead!” he exclaimed. “Please understand that although I do believe you are innocent, I am almost certainly the only person who does.

===Chinese===

“我调阅了顶替你下葬的那具尸体的人体测量记录，并打算在你乔装成让娜小姐时拍下对称的相貌测量照片，就像我今天在警局对你做的那样。我的当务之急是把让娜小姐弄到手，而正如我所料，我很快就发现她又变回了男儿身，并且成天同最声名狼藉的下流之徒鬼混在一起。我展开了大量明察暗访，昨晚去‘圣安东尼猪’（Saint-Anthony’s Pig）酒馆时，我早已查明顶替你入土的是个无名之辈；保罗就是让娜小姐；而让娜小姐正是夏尔·朗贝尔。我当时的意图就是要将你逮捕归案，并借助测力计确凿查实你在朗格吕纳案与达尼多夫案中是无辜清白的。”
“你关于测力计的这套说辞解释了你为何断定我不是饭店劫案的窃贼，可究竟凭什么能在你眼里洗清我谋害朗格吕纳的嫌疑？”
“我的老天爷！”朱夫反唇相讥道，“听你这口气，倒像是非要证明自己有罪不可似的！哎，我的孩子，这跟上一桩案子完全是一个道理。杀害德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）的凶手砸毁打碎过不少物件，而测力计已经证明，你的力气根本不足以充当那个凶手。”
“那假若我当时犯了歇斯底里的疯病呢，”夏尔·朗贝尔说话间吞吞吐吐，语调暴露出他对答复的极度惶恐，“那时的我力气难道不能大得惊人吗？我难道不会在完全不自知的情形下犯下这些滔天大罪吗？”
然而朱夫却摇了摇头。
“我心里有数：你是指你的母亲，并且一直被某种念头苦苦纠缠，怀疑自己受遗传病根的拖累患有梦游症，是在睡梦之中做下了这些勾当。得了吧，夏尔·朗贝尔，快把你的早饭吃完，把那些胡思乱想统统抛到九霄云外去。首先，即便真是在那种情形下，你的力气也依然远远不够；其次，眼下没有任何证据表明你是个疯子，甚至也没有任何迹象表明你可怜的母亲——不过我用不着继续往下说了，在这个问题上我自有某些非同寻常的独到见解。”
“这么说来，朱夫先生——”
“把‘先生’去掉，直呼我‘朱夫’就行。”
“这么说来，既然你明知我是无辜的，你能不能去告诉我父亲？我是不是就什么都不用怕了？我能不能用回自己的真实姓名抛头露面了？”
朱夫带着一抹讽刺意味的苦笑端详着少年。
“你这性子未免太急躁冒进了！”他嚷道，“你得给我弄清楚：尽管我坚信你是无辜的，但全天下眼下几乎唯独我一个人这么认为。”

===Original===

And unfortunately I have not yet got any evidence that would be sufficiently convincing and certain to put the persuasion of your guilt out of your father’s head, or anybody else’s. This is not the time for you to reappear: it would simply mean that you would be arrested by some detective who knows less than I do, and thrown into prison as you confidently expected to be this morning.”
“Then what is to become of me?”
“What do you think of doing yourself?”
“Going to see my father.”
“No, no,” Juve protested once more. “I tell you not to go. It would be stupid and utterly useless. Wait a few days, a few weeks if need be. When I have put my hand on Fantômas’ shoulder, I will be the very first to take you to your father, and proclaim your innocence.”
“Why wait until Fantômas is arrested?” Charles Rambert asked, the mere sound of the name seeming to wake all his former enthusiasm on the subject of that famous criminal.
“Because if you are innocent of the charge brought against you, it is extremely likely that Fantômas is the guilty party. When he is laid by the heels you will be able to protest your innocence without any fear.”
Charles Rambert sat silent for some minutes, musing on the odd chance of destiny which required him to make his own return to normal life contingent on the arrest of a mysterious criminal, who was merely suspected, and had never been seen nor discovered.
“What do you advise me to do?” he asked presently.
The detective got up and began to pace the room.
“Well,” he began, “the first fact is that I am interested in you, and the next is, that while I was having that rough-and-tumble last night with that scoundrel in the supper-room, I thought for a minute or two that it was all up with me: your chipping in saved my life. On the other hand I may be said to have saved your life now by ascertaining your innocence and preventing your arrest. So we are quits in a way. But you began the delicate attentions, and I have only paid you back, so it’s up to me to start a new series and not turn you out into the street where you would inevitably get into fresh trouble. So this is what I propose: change your name and go and take a room somewhere; get into proper clothes and then come back to me, and I’ll give you a letter to a friend of mine who is on one of the big evening papers. You are well educated, and I know you are energetic.

===Chinese===

“遗憾的是，我手头目前掌握的证据，还远远不足以让你的父亲或者其他任何人彻底打消对你有罪的认定。眼下绝不是你抛头露面的良机：那只会意味着你会被某个不如我知情通透的刑警当街逮捕，然后像你今天清晨深信不疑的那样，被径直关进大牢。”
“那我究竟该怎么办？”
“你自己心里是怎么打算的？”
“去见我父亲。”
“不行，绝不行，”朱夫再次出言反对道，“我说了你不能去。那纯粹是犯蠢，根本无济于事。再耐着性子等上几天，哪怕等上几周也无妨。等我的手按在芳托马斯（Fantômas）的肩膀上时，我必定头一个亲自领你去见你父亲，向天下昭告你的清白。”
“为什么非要等到芳托马斯被捕呢？”夏尔·朗贝尔问道；光是听到这个名字，似乎就重新唤醒了他以往对这位臭名昭著的大盗所怀揣的全部痴迷狂热。
“因为如果你在针对你的控罪上确实是无辜的，那么罪魁祸首极有可能正是芳托马斯。只要将他绳之以法，你就可以毫无惧色地为自己申辩清白了。”
夏尔·朗贝尔默然枯坐了数分钟，暗自揣摩着命运那离奇莫测的安排——自己重返正常体面生活的前提，竟然取决于缉捕一名至今仅存疑云之中、从无人目击更无处寻觅的神秘恶魔。
“那你建议我眼下该做些什么？”片刻后他探问道。
警探站起身，开始在房间里来回踱步。
“嗯，”他开口道，“头一个由头是我对你另眼相看，格外关照；第二个由头是，昨晚我在小餐厅里跟那个恶棍扭打撕扯时，有一两分钟我真以为自己大限已到、性命休矣——是你及时插手替我解围，救了我一命。反过来，我现在查实了你的清白、免去了你的牢狱之灾，也可以说是救了你一命。因此从某种意义上说，咱们俩算是两清了。但既然是你先施以关照援手，而我不过是投桃报李，那理应由我再主动开启新的人情往来，绝不能将你直接撵到大街上，任由你不可避免地惹上新的大麻烦。所以我的提议是这样的：隐姓埋名换个化名，去外面随便租一间屋子；换上一身体面整洁的行头，然后再回到我这里来，我会写一封引荐信把你介绍给我在一家大型晚报社供职的朋友。你受过良好教育，而且我深知你浑身充满干劲。

===Original===

You are keen on everything connected with the police, and you’ll get on splendidly as a reporter. You will be able to earn an honest and respectable name that way. Would you like to try that idea?”
“It’s awfully good of you,” Charles Rambert said gratefully. “I should love to be able to earn my living by work so much to my taste.”
Juve cut his thanks short, and held out some banknotes.
“There’s some money; now clear out; it’s high time we both got a little sleep. Get busy settling into rooms, and in a fortnight I shall expect you to be editor of *La Capitale*.”
“Under what name shall you introduce me to your friend?” Charles Rambert asked, after a little nervous pause.
“H’m!” said Juve with a smile: “it will have to be an alias of course.”
“Yes; and as it will be the name I shall write under it ought to be an easy one to remember.”
“Something arresting, like Fantômas!” said Juve chaffingly, amused by the curious childishness of this lad, who could take keen interest in such a trifle when he was in so critical a situation. “Choose something not too common for the first name; and something short for the other. Why not keep the first syllable of Fantômas? Oh, I’ve got it﻿—Fandor; what about Jérôme Fandor?”
Charles Rambert murmured it over.
“Jérôme Fandor! Yes, you are right, it sounds well.”
Juve pushed him out of the door.
“Well, Jérôme Fandor, leave me to my slumbers, and go and rig yourself out, and get ready for the new life that I’m going to open up for you!”
Bewildered by the amazing adventures of which he had just been the central figure, Charles Rambert, or Jérôme Fandor, walked down Juve’s staircase wondering. “Why should he take so much trouble about me? What interest or what motive can he have? And how on earth does he find out such a wonderful lot of things?”

===Chinese===

你对跟警察破案沾边的一切都兴致盎然，当个跑外勤的新闻记者包管能大有作为。靠着这门营生，你就能给自己挣得一个清白体面的好名声。你愿意试试这个主意吗？”
“您对我实在太好了，”夏尔·朗贝尔满怀感激地说道，“要是能凭着一份如此合我心意的工作自食其力，我求之不得。”
朱夫打断了他的千恩万谢，伸手递过去几张纸币。
“这儿有些现钱；现在赶紧走人吧，咱俩也老大不小该抓紧补个觉了。赶紧去把租房安顿的事办妥，我指望两周之内你就能当上《首都报》（La Capitale）的主编！”
“您打算用什么名字把我介绍给您的朋友？”略带一丝紧张地停顿了片刻后，夏尔·朗贝尔开口问道。
“嗯！”朱夫微笑着说，“那自然得用个化名。”
“是的；而且既然这个名字将用作我的见报署名，那最好是个朗朗上口、容易让人记住的名字。”
“得是个引人注目的名头，比方说像芳托马斯那样！”朱夫打趣取笑道；眼见这个少年身处这般凶险要命的紧要关头，竟然还会对这种鸡毛琐事兴致勃勃，不禁觉得他孩子气得滑稽可笑。“名起得不要落于俗套，姓取得短小精炼些。干脆保留芳托马斯的头一个音节如何？噢，我有主意了——凡多尔；热罗姆·凡多尔（Jérôme Fandor）怎么样？”
夏尔·朗贝尔口中反复念叨着。
“热罗姆·凡多尔！对，您说得极是，听起来确实顺耳响亮。”
朱夫推着他走出了门外。
“行了，热罗姆·凡多尔，行行好让我安心睡一觉吧，赶紧去给自己张罗一套像样行头，准备迎接我为你铺就的崭新人生吧！”
夏尔·朗贝尔——抑或热罗姆·凡多尔——被自己刚才亲历的一连串惊天奇遇搞得晕头转向，他一边沿着朱夫寓所的楼梯步下楼去，一边在心底暗自犯着嘀咕：“他平白无故为何要在我身上费这么大周折？他究竟图什么、怀着什么心思动机？还有，这世上那么多神乎其神的秘闻内情，他究竟是怎么打探得这般底掉的呢？”

## XX A Cup of Tea / 第二十章 一杯茶
===Original===

After the tragic death of her husband, Lady Beltham﻿—whose previous life had inclined to the austere﻿—withdrew into almost complete retirement. The world of gaiety and fashion knew her no more. But in the world where poverty and suffering reign, in hospital wards and squalid streets, a tall and beautiful woman might often be seen, robed all in black, with distinguished bearing and eyes serene and grave, distributing alms and consolation as she moved. It was Lady Beltham, kind, good and very pitiful, bent on the work of charity to which she had vowed her days.
Yet she had not allowed herself to be crushed by sorrow; after the tragedy which left her a widow, she had assumed the effective control of her husband’s property, and, helped by faithful friends, had carried on his interests and administered his estates, spreading a halo of kindness all around her.
To help her in the heavy correspondence entailed by all these affairs, she found three secretaries none too many. On M. Etienne Rambert’s recommendation, Thérèse Auvernois was now one of these, and the young girl was perfectly happy in her new surroundings; time was helping her to forget the tragedy which had taken her grandmother from her at Beaulieu, and she enjoyed the company of the wellborn, well-bred English gentlewomen.
Lady Beltham was reclining on a sofa in the great hall of her house at Neuilly. It was a spacious room, furnished half as a lounge and half as an office, and Lady Beltham liked to receive people there. A large glass-enclosed balcony commanded a view over the garden and the boulevard Richard Wallace beyond, with the Bois de Boulogne beyond that again. A few minutes before, a footman had brought in a table and set out tea-things, and Lady Beltham was reading while Thérèse and the two young English girls were chattering among themselves.
The telephone bell rang and Thérèse answered it.
“Hullo? Yes﻿ ﻿… yes: you want to know if you may call this evening? The Reverend﻿—oh, yes: you have just come from Scotland? Hold on a minute.” She turned to Lady Beltham. “It is Mr. William Hope, and he wants to know if you will see him tonight. He has just come from your place in Scotland.”
“The dear man!”

===Chinese===

丈夫悲惨罹难之后，平日生活便倾向素朴严谨的贝尔特姆夫人（Lady Beltham），几乎彻底过上了深居简出的隐居生活。欢歌笑语的社交浮华界再也见不到她的身影。然而，在贫困与苦难盘踞的角落，在医院病房和污秽肮脏的陋巷之中，人们却时常能见到一位身材高挑、容貌端庄的美丽妇人：她周身素黑，举止高雅，双眸沉静庄重，一路走过，广施布施与抚慰。那正是贝尔特姆夫人——她仁慈善良，满怀悲悯，一心扑在自己立誓终身奉献的慈善事业上。
然而，她并未让自己被巨大的悲痛击垮；在那场令她沦为寡妇的惨剧过后，她实际接掌了丈夫的遗产，在忠实友人的协助下，经营着他的产业，打理着他的庄园，将仁爱的光辉播撒向周遭的每一个人。
面对这纷繁事务所牵涉的浩繁书信往来，她雇佣了三名秘书，却依然觉得人手紧凑。经由埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生（M. Etienne Rambert）的引荐，泰蕾兹·奥弗努瓦（Thérèse Auvernois）如今成了其中一员；在这处崭新的天地里，年轻姑娘过得极为惬意顺心。时光正慢慢抚平痛失博利厄祖母的创伤，她十分喜欢同这几位出身名门、教养良好的英国年轻女士朝夕相处。
贝尔特姆夫人正斜倚在讷伊寓所大厅的沙发上。这是一间宽敞的大厅，陈设一半像起居客厅，一半像办公书房，贝尔特姆夫人向来喜欢在此迎客。一处宽敞的玻璃封闭阳台，可以俯瞰下方的花园，远眺外面的理查·华莱士林荫道，而在林荫道更远处，则是郁郁葱葱的布洛涅林苑（the Bois de Boulogne）。几分钟前，一名男仆搬来一张茶几摆上了茶具；贝尔特姆夫人手捧书卷静静翻阅，泰蕾兹和另外两位年轻的英国姑娘则在一旁轻声闲聊打趣。
电话铃声骤然响起，泰蕾兹走上前接通了电话。
“喂？是的……请讲。您想问今晚能否过来拜访？尊贵的牧师——噢，是的，您刚从苏格兰赶回来？请稍等片刻。”她转过身望向贝尔特姆夫人，“是威廉·霍普先生（Mr. William Hope），他想知道您今晚能否接见他。他刚从您在苏格兰的庄园赶回来。”
“这位可敬的大好人！”

===Original===

exclaimed Lady Beltham; “of course he may come,” and as Thérèse turned lightly to convey her permission to the clergyman waiting at the other end of the line, she caught a smile on the face of one of the other girls. “What is the joke, Lisbeth?” she enquired.
The girl laughed brightly.
“I think the worthy parson must have smelt the tea and toast, and wants to make up for the wretched dinner he got in the train.”
“You are incorrigible,” Lady Beltham replied. “Mr. Hope is above such material matters.”
“Indeed he isn’t, Lady Beltham,” the girl persisted. “Why, only the other day he told Thérèse that all food deserved respect and esteem directly a blessing had been asked upon it, and that a badly cooked steak was a kind of sacrilege.”
“A badly cooked pheasant,” Thérèse corrected her.
“You are both wicked little slanderers,” Lady Beltham protested gently, “and don’t know the blessing a good appetite is. You do, Susannah, don’t you?”
Susannah, a pretty Irish girl, looked up from a letter she was reading, and blushed.
“Oh, Lady Beltham, I’ve been ever so much less hungry since Harry’s ship sailed.”
“I don’t quite see the connection,” Lady Beltham answered. “Love is good nourishment for the soul, but not for the body. However, a good appetite is nothing to be ashamed of, and you ought to keep your roses for your future husband, and qualify in every way to be an excellent mother of a family.”
“With lots and lots of children,” Lisbeth went on wickedly: “seven or eight daughters at the very least, all of whom will marry nice young clergymen when their time comes and﻿—”
She stopped speaking and the light chatter died away as a footman entered and announced the Reverend William Hope, who followed him immediately into the room, an elderly man with a full, clean-shaven face and a comfortable portliness of figure.
Lady Beltham offered him a cordial hand.
“I am delighted you are back,” she said. “Will you have a cup of tea with us?”
The parson made a general bow to the girls gathered about the table.
“I got a wretched dinner in the train,” he began, but Lisbeth interrupted him.
“Don’t you think this tea smells delicious?” she asked.
The parson put out his hand to take the cup she offered to him, and bowed and smiled.
“Precisely what I was going to observe, Miss Lisbeth.”
Thérèse and Susannah turned away to hide their amusement, and Lady Beltham adroitly changed the subject.

===Chinese===

贝尔特姆夫人脱口欢呼道：“他当然可以过来。”泰蕾兹轻巧地转过身，将女主人的首肯转告给电话另一头等候的牧师，此时她捕捉到了另一位姑娘脸上的一抹促狭笑意。“有什么好笑的事吗，利斯贝思？”她探问道。
那个姑娘开怀大笑起来。
“我想这位尊贵的牧师准是闻到了红茶和烤面包的香味，急着想要弥补他在火车上咽下的那顿糟糕透顶的晚饭呢。”
“你呀，真是拿你没办法，”贝尔特姆夫人回答，“霍普先生可绝不是汲汲于这等口腹之欲的人。”
“真的不是吗，贝尔特姆夫人，”姑娘不依不饶地坚持道，“就在前些天，他还一本正经跟泰蕾兹说，任何食物只要经过祈祷祝谢，就理应受到万分尊重与敬重，而一块煎糊了的牛排简直就是对神明的亵渎！”
“是没做好的野鸡肉，”泰蕾兹笑着纠正她。
“你们俩啊，全是爱搬弄是非的小坏蛋，”贝尔特姆夫人温和地责备道，“根本不懂得好胃口是何等难得的天赐之福。苏珊娜，你总该深有体会吧，不是吗？”
苏珊娜是个漂亮的爱尔兰姑娘，她从正在展读的信笺上抬起头来，羞红了脸蛋。
“噢，贝尔特姆夫人，自从哈里的船出海远航之后，我的胃口可差远了。”
“我倒看不出这二者之间有什么干系，”贝尔特姆夫人回答，“爱情固然是对心灵的无上滋养，却养活不了皮囊肉身。不过，胃口好可没什么好害臊的，你应该为未来的夫君保持红润的好气色，各方面都做好准备，将来当一个出色的大家庭母亲。”
“生上一大群、一大群的孩子，”利斯贝思又坏笑着接茬道，“至少生上七八个千金，等她们长大成人，全都嫁给体面年轻的牧师，然后……”
她猛地收住了话头，欢快的闲聊声戛然而止——一名男仆走了进来，通报威廉·霍普牧师（Reverend William Hope）驾到。牧师紧随其后迈入大厅，这是一位上了年纪的长者，脸庞圆润饱满、刮得干干净净，体态丰腴安详。
贝尔特姆夫人热情地向他伸出手。
“得知您平安归来，我真是太高兴了，”她说，“要同我们一道喝杯茶吗？”
牧师朝围在茶几旁的姑娘们泛泛欠身行礼。
“我在火车上吃了一顿糟糕透顶的晚饭，”他刚一开口，利斯贝思就出言打断了他。
“您不觉得这壶茶香气扑鼻吗？”她问道。
牧师伸手接过姑娘递来的茶杯，微笑着躬身致意。
“这也正是我刚想赞叹的，利斯贝思小姐。”
泰蕾兹和苏珊娜连忙转过身去掩饰笑意，贝尔特姆夫人则机敏地岔开了话题。

===Original===

She moved towards her writing-table.
“Mr. Hope must have much to tell me, girls, and it is getting late. I must get to business. Did you have a good journey?”
“Quite as good as usual, Lady Beltham. The people at Scotwell Hill are very plucky and good, but it will be a hard winter; there is snow on the hills already.”
“Have the women and children had all their woollen things?”
“Oh, yes: twelve hundred garments have been distributed according to a list drawn up by the under-steward; here it is,” and he handed a paper to Lady Beltham, who passed it on to Susannah.
“I will ask you to check the list,” she said to the girl, and turned again to the clergyman. “The under-steward is a good fellow, but he is a rabid politician; he may have omitted some families that are openly radical; but I think charity should be given equally to all, for poverty makes no political distinctions.”
“That is the right Christian view,” the clergyman said approvingly.
“And what about the sanatorium at Glasgow?” Lady Beltham went on.
“It is very nearly finished,” the good man answered. “I have got your lawyers to cut down the contractor’s accounts by something like fifteen percent, which means a saving of nearly three hundred pounds.”
“Excellent,” said Lady Beltham, and she turned to Thérèse. “You must add that three hundred pounds to the funds of the Scotwell Hill coal charity,” she said. “They will want all of it if the winter is going to be a hard one,” and Thérèse made a note of the instruction, full of admiration for Lady Beltham’s simple generosity.
But Mr. Hope was fidgeting on his chair. He seized an opportunity when Lady Beltham, busy making notes, had turned her deep and steady eyes away from him, to say in a low tone:
“Have I your permission just to mention﻿—poor Lord Beltham?”
Lady Beltham started, and her face betrayed an emotion which she bravely controlled. Hearing the name pronounced, the three girls withdrew to the far end of the room, where they began to talk among themselves. Lady Beltham signified her assent, and Mr. Hope began.
“You know, dear friend, this has been my first visit to Scotland since Lord Beltham’s death. I found your tenants still grievously upset by the tragedy that occurred nearly a year ago.

===Chinese===

她款步走向自己的书桌。
“姑娘们，霍普先生一定有许多要紧事要向我禀报，时辰也不早了，我得谈正事了。您这趟旅途顺利吗？”
“跟往常一样顺遂，贝尔特姆夫人。斯科特韦尔山（Scotwell Hill）的佃户们既坚韧又善良，不过今年冬天恐怕会格外难熬；山头如今已经白雪皑皑了。”
“妇女和孩子们的毛料御寒衣物都发放了吗？”
“噢，是的：副总管遵照造好的花名册，总共分发了一千二百件衣物；名册就在这儿。”说着他将一份文件呈递给贝尔特姆夫人，夫人顺手转交给了苏珊娜。
“劳驾你核对一下这份清单，”她对姑娘吩咐道，接着又转向牧师，“副总管是个厚道人，可政治立场未免太过狂热激进；他很可能把那些公开放话支持激进党的穷苦人家给漏掉了；但我认为行善济困应当一视同仁，贫穷可不分什么政治党派。”
“这正是最纯正的基督徒见地，”牧师赞许地点头附和。
“格拉斯哥（Glasgow）的那座疗养院进展如何了？”贝尔特姆夫人接着发问。
“差不多快要竣工了，”善良的牧师回答道，“我已经让您的律师迫使承包商削减了大约百分之十五的工程款账目，这意味着节省了将近三百英镑。”
“太好了，”贝尔特姆夫人说，随后转头看向泰蕾兹，“你把这三百英镑如数拨给斯科特韦尔山的冬季用煤慈善专款吧。要是今冬格外严酷，他们一分钱都省不下来。”泰蕾兹满怀敬佩地将这道吩咐记录下来，对贝尔特姆夫人那份朴实无华的慷慨倾囊肃然起敬。
然而霍普先生却在椅子上有些坐立不安。趁着贝尔特姆夫人正低头记笔记、那双深邃沉稳的双眸移开视线的空当，他压低嗓门轻声说道：
“请问我是否可以冒昧提一提——可怜的贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）？”
贝尔特姆夫人浑身一震，脸上流露出难以掩饰的悲怆，但她极力克制住了情绪。听到这个名字被提起，三位年轻姑娘识趣地退到了大厅另一头，自顾自低声交谈起来。贝尔特姆夫人点头示意许可，霍普先生便开口说了下去。
“您是知道的，亲爱的朋友，这是勋爵罹难后我头一遭前往苏格兰。我发现您的佃户们至今依然对将近一年前发生的那场惨剧深感悲恸难抑。

===Original===

They have got by heart all the newspaper accounts of the mysterious circumstances attending Lord Beltham’s death, but those are not enough to satisfy the sympathetic curiosity of these excellent people, and I was obliged to tell them over and over again in full detail﻿—all we knew.”
“I hope no scandal has gathered round his name,” said Lady Beltham quickly.
“You need have no fear of that,” the clergyman replied in the same low tone. “The rumour that got about when the crime was first discovered, that Lord Beltham had been surprised in an intrigue and killed in revenge, has not won acceptance. Local opinion agrees that he was decoyed into a trap and killed by the man Gurn, who meant to rob him, but who was either surprised or thought he was going to be, and fled before he had time to take the money or the jewels from the body of his victim. They know that the murderer has never been caught, but they also know that there is a price on his head, and they all hope the police﻿—Oh, forgive me for recalling all these painful memories!”
While he had been speaking, Lady Beltham’s face had expressed almost every shade of emotion and distress; it seemed to be drawn with pain at his concluding words. But she made an effort to control herself, and spoke resignedly.
“It cannot be helped, dear Mr. Hope. Go on.”
But the clergyman changed the topic.
“Oh, I was quite forgetting,” he said more brightly. “The under-steward has turned out the two Tillys, quite on his own authority: you must remember them, two brothers, blacksmiths, who drank a great deal and paid very little, and created so much scandal in the place.”
“I object to the under-steward doing any such thing without referring to me first,” Lady Beltham exclaimed warmly. “Man’s duty is to persuade and forgive, not to judge and punish. Kindness breeds kindness, and it is pity that wins amendment. Why should a subordinate, my under-steward, presume to do what I would not permit myself to do?”
She had sprung to her feet and was pacing excitedly about the room; she had wholly dropped the impassive mask she habitually wore, concealing her real personality.
The three girls watched her in silence.
The door opened anew, and Silbertown came in, the majordomo of Lady Beltham’s establishment at Neuilly.

===Chinese===

报纸上刊登的有关贝尔特姆勋爵死因种种离奇疑云的报道，他们早已倒背如流；但那些语焉不详的字句根本填不满这些善良庄稼人满怀同情的关切，我只得一遍又一遍、事无巨细地向他们转述——咱们所掌握的全部内情。”
“但愿没有辱没他清誉的流言蜚语传开吧，”贝尔特姆夫人急促地插话道。
“这一点您大可宽心，”牧师同样压低嗓门宽慰道，“凶案初露端倪时曾有传言，妄称贝尔特姆勋爵是因私通幽会被当场撞破、招致仇杀，这类谣言在当地根本站不住脚。乡亲们一致确信，他是中了圈套被诱入陷阱，遭到了那个叫居尔恩（Gurn）的恶徒杀害；此人本意旨在劫财，但中途不是受惊败露就是疑心被人发觉，仓皇逃窜之际甚至来不及从受害者尸身上搜走钱财首饰。大伙都知道凶手至今仍在潜逃，但也清楚警方已悬赏通缉捉拿他，众人都热切期盼着警察早日——噢，求您宽恕我，我又勾起了您这些痛彻心扉的伤心往事！”
在牧师倾诉之际，贝尔特姆夫人的面容变换着各种复杂痛苦的凄楚神色；及至他最后那几句话，她的脸庞因极度的痛苦而剧烈抽搐扭曲。但她强打精神克制住了自己，语气带着认命的哀婉：
“这也是无可奈何的事，亲爱的霍普先生。您继续往下说吧。”
然而牧师却识趣地换了话题。
“噢，我差点忘了件要紧事，”他的语气轻快了些，“副总管擅自做主，把蒂利兄弟俩给扫地出门了。您想必还记得他们，那对打铁的亲兄弟，嗜酒如命，欠租不还，在当地闹出了数不清的丑事。”
“我坚决反对副总管在未曾向我请示前就擅自行使这种大权！”贝尔特姆夫人语气激动地疾呼道，“人之为人，本分在于劝勉与宽恕，而非审判与惩戒。仁慈滋生善意，唯有怜悯方能令人幡然悔悟。凭什么我手下的一个区区仆从、我的副总管，竟敢擅专连我本人都不忍做出的惩戒之举？！”
她霍然起身，情绪激动地在大厅里来回踱步；她平日里习惯戴上的那副掩藏真实性情的冷漠面具，此刻荡然无存。
三位姑娘屏息凝神，默默注视着她。
大门再次被推开，贝尔特姆夫人讷伊府邸的大管家（majordomo）西尔伯敦（Silbertown）迈步走了进来。

===Original===

He brought the evening letters, and the girls speedily took all the envelopes and newspapers from the tray and began to sort and open them, while the majordomo entered into conversation with his mistress, and the Rev. William Hope seized the opportunity to say good night, and take his leave.
Many of the letters were merely appeals to help in money or in kind, but one long letter Lisbeth handed to Lady Beltham. She glanced at the signature.
“Ah, here is news of M. Etienne Rambert,” she exclaimed, and as Thérèse instinctively drew near, knowing that she, too, might hear something of what her old friend had written, Lady Beltham put the letter into her hand. “You read it, my dear, and then you can tell me presently what he has to say.”
Thérèse read the letter eagerly. M. Etienne Rambert had left Paris a week before, upon a long and important journey. The energetic old fellow was to make a trip in Germany first, and then go from Hamburg to England, where he had some business to attend to on behalf of Lady Beltham, with whom he was on more confidential terms than ever. Then he meant to sail from Southampton and spend the winter in Colombia, where he had important interests of his own to look after.
While Thérèse was reading, Lady Beltham continued her conversation with her majordomo.
“I am glad you had the park gate seen to this afternoon,” she said. “You know how nervous I am. My childhood in Scotland was very lonely, and ever since then I have had a vague terror of solitude and darkness.”
The majordomo reassured her: he had no lack of self-confidence.
“There is nothing for your ladyship to be afraid of; the house is perfectly safe, and carefully guarded. Walter, the porter, is a first-rate watchdog and always sleeps with one eye open. And I, too﻿—”
“Yes, I know, Silbertown,” the young widow replied; “and when I give myself time to think I am not nervous. Thank you; you can leave me now.”
She turned to the three girls.
“I am tired, dears; we won’t stay up any later.”
Lisbeth and Susannah kissed her affectionately and went away. Thérèse lingered a moment, to bring a book, a Bible, and place it on a table close to Lady Beltham’s chair. Lady Beltham laid a hand upon her head as if in blessing, and said gently:
“Good night; God bless you, dear child!”

===Chinese===

他呈递上傍晚送来的信件，姑娘们手脚麻利地从托盘里收拢起所有信封和报纸，着手分拣拆阅；大管家则同女主人低声回话，威廉·霍普牧师趁此契机道了晚安，起身告退。
来信大多不过是恳求金钱救济或物资援助的求助信，但利斯贝思将一封长信递到了贝尔特姆夫人手中。夫人瞥了一眼信末署名。
“啊，是埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生来信了，”她轻呼道。泰蕾兹本能地凑上前去，心知自己也能探听到这位老相识写了些什么；贝尔特姆夫人顺手将信笺塞到她手里。“亲爱的，你来读吧，读完回头再告诉我他说了些什么。”
泰蕾兹急不可耐地展信细读。埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生一周前动身离开了巴黎，踏上了一趟漫长而重要的远行。这位精神矍铄的老先生先是前去德国考察，随后打算从汉堡赶赴英格兰，在那儿代为处理贝尔特姆夫人的若干业务——他如今同夫人的信赖私交比以往任何时候都要密切。随后他打算从南安普敦扬帆启航，前往哥伦比亚度过寒冬，他在那儿有一大笔属于自己的重大利益需要亲自打理。
在泰蕾兹阅信之际，贝尔特姆夫人继续同她的大管家交谈。
“我很欣慰你今天下午去检查修缮了庄园大门，”她说，“你知道我向来胆子小、容易神经紧绷。我童年在苏格兰度过，孤苦伶仃无依无靠，从那时起我就对形单影只和无边黑夜怀有一种莫名的恐惧。”
大管家出言宽慰她，他对自己看家护院的本事向来胸有成竹。
“夫人完全无须担惊受怕；宅邸万无一失，防卫森严。门房沃尔特（Walter）是条一等一的看家好狗，夜里睡觉都睁着一只眼。还有我呢，也绝不会——”
“是的，我心里有数，西尔伯敦，”年轻的孀妇打断了他，“只要我容自己定下心来理智想想，我其实并不慌乱。多谢你；你眼下可以退下了。”
她转向三位年轻姑娘。
“我有些乏了，孩子们；咱们今晚就不熬夜了。”
利斯贝思和苏珊娜亲昵地吻了吻她，相继退去。泰蕾兹多耽搁了片刻，取来一本《圣经》，轻轻摆在贝尔特姆夫人座椅旁的几案上。贝尔特姆夫人把一只手轻抚在姑娘头顶，宛如给予赐福一般，柔声说道：
“晚安；愿上帝保佑你，亲爱的孩子！”

## XXI Lord Beltham’s Murderer / 第二十一章 杀害贝尔特姆勋爵的凶手
===Original===

It was on the point of midnight, and absolute stillness reigned throughout the house.
But Lady Beltham had not gone to bed. Although she had remained in the great hall where she did her work, she had been unable to settle down to any occupation. She had read a little, and begun a letter, got up and sat down; and finally, beginning to feel chilly, she had drawn an easy chair up to the hearth, where a log was just burning out, and stretching out her slippers to the warmth had fallen into a waking dream.
A sound caught her ear and she sat upright. At first she thought it was some trick of the imagination, but in another minute the noise grew louder; there was the hurrying of feet and voices, muffled at first but rapidly becoming louder, and at last a regular uproar, doors banging, glass breaking, and shouts from all parts of the house. Lady Beltham jumped up, nervous and trembling; she was just going to the window when she heard a shot and stopped dead where she stood. Then she rushed out into the vestibule.
“Help!” she screamed. “What on earth is the matter?” and remembering the girls for whom she had assumed responsibility, she called out anxiously for them. “Lisbeth! Thérèse! Susannah! Come to me!”
Doors upstairs were flung open, and with their hair streaming over their nightdresses Thérèse and Susannah rushed downstairs and crouched down by her side, stifling moans of terror.
“Lisbeth? Where is Lisbeth?” Lady Beltham asked sharply.
At the same moment she appeared, her face distorted with fright.
“Oh, Lady Beltham, it’s dreadful! There’s a man, a burglar in the garden! And Walter is throttling him! They are fighting dreadfully! They’ll kill one another!”
Silbertown, the majordomo, came rushing in just then. Seeing the three girls in their nightdresses he made as if to draw back, but Lady Beltham called him in and demanded explanations.
“We had just finished our rounds,” he answered breathlessly, “when we caught sight of a man hiding in the shadows, a thief probably. When we shouted to him he ran away, but we ran after him and seized him; he resisted and there was a fight. But we have got him and the police will take him away in a few minutes.”
Lady Beltham listened, with jaw set and hands clenched.
“A thief?” she said, controlling her emotion. “How do you know he is a thief?”

===Chinese===

时值子夜时分，整座宅邸万籁俱寂。
然而贝尔特姆夫人（Lady Beltham）却并未就寝。尽管她一直留在那间平日料理事务的宽敞大厅里，却始终无法静下心来做任何事。她翻了几页书，提笔开了个信头，又站起身来，坐下又起；最后，周身渐生寒意的她把一张安乐椅拉到了壁炉旁——炉膛里一截木柴正徐徐燃尽——她把蹬着拖鞋的脚伸向炉火取暖，陷入了半梦半醒的出神沉思中。
一阵响动钻入耳中，她猛地坐直了身子。起初她还以为是自己疑神疑鬼的幻觉，可不过一分钟光景，响动便愈演愈烈；先是急促杂乱的脚步声和压抑的呼喊声，旋即嗓门迅速拔高，最后演变成一场彻头彻尾的轩然大波：摔门声、玻璃碎裂声，以及从宅邸四下传来的惊呼怒号。贝尔特姆夫人浑身颤抖、惊慌失措地跳了起来；她正打算奔向窗边，突然听到了一声枪响，吓得她当场僵立在原地。紧接着，她发疯似地冲向门厅。
“救命啊！”她尖叫道，“究竟出什么事了？！”念及自己担负看顾之责的那几位年轻姑娘，她焦急万分地高声呼唤她们：“利斯贝思！泰蕾兹！苏珊娜！快到我这儿来！”
楼上的房门猛地被撞开，泰蕾兹和苏珊娜披头散发、身着睡袍飞奔下楼，紧紧蜷缩在夫人身侧，极力压抑着惊恐的呜咽抽泣。
“利斯贝思呢？利斯贝思在哪儿？”贝尔特姆夫人急促地厉声发问。
就在此时，利斯贝思现身了，面孔因极度恐惧而扭曲走样。
“噢，贝尔特姆夫人，太可怕了！花园里有个男人，是个破门强盗！沃尔特正死死掐着他的脖子呢！他们扭打得凶残极了！他们会闹出人命、把彼此活活打死的！”
恰在此时，大管家（majordomo）西尔伯敦（Silbertown）气喘吁吁地冲了进来。一瞥见三位身穿睡衣的姑娘，他下意识想要退回避嫌，但贝尔特姆夫人厉声将他叫住，勒令他把事情原委交代清楚。
“我们刚巡视完一圈，”他上气不接下气地回答道，“就突然瞥见有个男人鬼鬼祟祟躲在阴影里，多半是个贼。我们一冲他喝问，他就拔腿就跑，但我们紧追不舍将他按住；他拼死负隅顽抗，双方就扭打在了一起。不过我们已经把他拿下了，要不了几分钟警察就会赶来把他押走。”
贝尔特姆夫人咬紧牙关，双拳紧攥，凝神倾听着。
“贼？”她极力克制着激荡的情绪问道，“你们凭什么断定他就是个贼？”

===Original===

“Well,” stammered the majordomo, “he is very poorly dressed, and besides, what was he doing in the garden?”
Lady Beltham was recovering her calm.
“What excuse did he give for being there?” she asked coldly.
“We didn’t give him time to invent one,” said the majordomo. “We collared him almost as soon as we saw him. And you know, madame, how tremendously powerful Walter is: Walter gave him all he deserved!” and the majordomo clenched his fists and made an expressive exhibition of the porter’s reception of the stranger.
Lisbeth was still overcome by what she had seen.
“Oh, the blood!” she muttered hysterically; “it was streaming!”
Lady Beltham spoke angrily to the majordomo.
“I hate brutality: is the man seriously hurt? I hope not. You ought to have questioned him before assaulting him. No one in my house has a right to use violence. ‘Whoso smites with the sword shall perish by the sword’!”
The majordomo heard her in silent astonishment: it was not at all what he expected to be told, in view of all the circumstances.
Lady Beltham went on more gently:
“I suppose I shall have to apologise to this man for your wrong and thoughtless behaviour.”
“Apologise?” exclaimed Silbertown in amazement. “Surely your ladyship will not do that?”
“One must not shrink from humiliation when one has been in the wrong,” said Lady Beltham, in the pulpit manner she affected. “Tell Walter to come to me.”
A few minutes later the porter, a muscular giant of a man, came into the room and made a clumsy bow.
“How was it possible for anyone to get into the house at this time of night?” his mistress enquired coldly.
Walter dropped his eyes and twisted his cap nervously.
“I hope your ladyship will forgive me. I caught the fellow, and as he was struggling I hit him. Then two of the footmen came, and they are looking after him in the kitchen.”
“Has he given any explanation of his presence here since you assaulted him﻿—at which I am very angry?” said Lady Beltham.
“He hasn’t said anything; at least﻿—”
“Well?”
“I don’t like to tell you.”
“Please do like!” said Lady Beltham irritably.
“Well,” Walter replied, overcoming his nervousness with an effort, “he says your ladyship is well known for your charity to everybody, and﻿—he wants to see you.”
There was a moment’s pause.
“I will see him,” said Lady Beltham at last, in a half-stifled voice.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，”大管家结结巴巴地答道，“他衣衫褴褛、穷酸寒碜，再说，深更半夜他猫在花园里能干什么好事呢？”
贝尔特姆夫人渐渐恢复了镇静。
“他对自己为何出现在那里给出了什么由头？”她冷冷地质问道。
“我们哪容得他编造借口啊，”大管家说，“几乎一眼瞧见他，我们就一把扭住了他的衣领。而且您是知道的，夫人，沃尔特力大如牛：沃尔特狠狠赏了他一顿好果子吃！”大管家攥紧拳头，生动逼真地比划着门房是如何痛揍那个陌生人的。
利斯贝思仍沉浸在方才目睹的骇人惨状中无法自拔。
“噢，全是血！”她歇斯底里地喃喃自语道，“血流如注啊！”
贝尔特姆夫人怒气冲冲地斥责大管家。
“我最深恶痛绝的就是粗暴蛮横！那个人伤得严重吗？但愿没有大碍！你们动手打人之前，本该先向他盘问清楚！在我府上，任何人都没有权利诉诸暴力！‘凡动刀的，必死在刀下’！”
大管家瞠目结舌地听着夫人的训斥；鉴于当时的凶险情势，这番责备完全出乎他的意料。
贝尔特姆夫人的语气缓和了些许，接着说道：
“因你们这般卤莽不当的举动，我想我不得不亲自向那个人赔礼道歉。”
“赔礼道歉？！”西尔伯敦惊愕万分地失声叫道，“夫人，您千金之躯断不可做出这种事来啊！”
“自知有错，就绝不该在屈辱面前退缩，”贝尔特姆夫人用她惯常习惯的布道口吻说道，“去叫沃尔特到我这儿来。”
几分钟后，生得五大三粗、宛如铁塔巨汉的门房迈步走进大厅，笨拙地躬身行礼。
“深更半夜这个节骨眼上，怎么可能有人潜入宅邸？”女主人冷冰冰地质问道。
沃尔特垂下双眼，局促不安地揉搓着手中的制帽。
“求夫人恕罪。我抓住了那家伙，见他拼死挣扎，我就狠狠揍了他一顿。后来两名男仆赶了过来，他们眼下正在厨房里看着他。”
“在遭到你们这般殴打之后——我对此深感震怒——他有没有交代自己为何擅闯此地？”贝尔特姆夫人问道。
“他什么都没招；至少……”
“至少什么？”
“我……我不好意思跟您开口。”
“少废话，给我说！”贝尔特姆夫人烦躁地命令道。
“好吧，”沃尔特勉力克服着紧张答道，“他说夫人的乐善好施天下皆知，而且……他想要亲自面见您。”
大厅里陷入了短暂的死寂。
“我要见他，”贝尔特姆夫人终于开口道，嗓音低沉发颤。

===Original===

“Will your ladyship allow me to point out the danger of doing any such thing?” Silbertown exclaimed. “Very likely the man is a lunatic! Or it may be a trick: Lord Beltham was murdered, and perhaps﻿—”
Lady Beltham looked intently at the majordomo, seemingly trying to read his thoughts. Then she answered slowly:
“I will see him. I will be more pitiful than you,” and as the majordomo and the porter made a gesture of futile protest, she added peremptorily: “I have given my orders: kindly obey.”
When the two men had reluctantly left the room, Lady Beltham turned to the three girls.
“You had better leave me, darlings,” she said, kindly but firmly. “Run away: excitement is bad for you. Go back to bed. No, I assure you I shall be in no danger whatever,” and for a few minutes she was left alone.
---
“Speak,” said Lady Beltham in a toneless voice.
The majordomo and the porter had led in, and placed before her, a man with unkempt hair and ragged beard; he was dressed entirely in black, and his face was tired and haggard. Lady Beltham, ghastly pale, was leaning for support against the back of an armchair. The man did not raise his eyes to her.
“I will not speak unless we are alone,” he answered dully.
“Alone?” said Lady Beltham, fighting down her emotion. “Then it is something serious you have to tell me?”
“If you know anything of people in misfortune, Madame,” the man answered gently, “you know that they do not like to humiliate themselves before﻿—before those who cannot understand,” and he nodded towards the majordomo and the porter.
“I do know something of misfortune,” Lady Beltham replied, in firmer tones; “and I will hear you alone.” She looked at her two servants. “Leave us, please.”
The majordomo started.
“Leave you alone with him? It’s madness!” and as Lady Beltham merely looked at him in haughty surprise, he began to withdraw in confusion, but still protesting. “It’s﻿—it’s﻿—Your ladyship has no idea what this fellow wants: do please﻿—”
But Lady Beltham curtly cut him short.
“That is enough!”
A heavy velvet curtain fell over the closing door, and in the room, that was dimly lighted by a small electric lamp, Lady Beltham was alone with the strange individual to whom she had so readily, so oddly, consented to accord a private interview. She followed her servants to the door and locked it after them.

===Chinese===

“夫人能否容我提醒您此举是何等危险？！”西尔伯敦疾呼道，“那家伙极有可能就是个疯子！要么就是个圈套：贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）死于非命，没准……”
贝尔特姆夫人目光如炬地凝视着大管家，似乎在极力揣摩他的心思。随后她慢条斯理地回答道：
“我要见他。我会比你们更怀怜悯之心。”眼见大管家和门房想要徒劳地出言劝阻，她不容置疑地厉声补充道：“我已经下达了指令：请遵命照办。”
待两名仆从心不甘情不愿地退出大厅后，贝尔特姆夫人转向三位姑娘。
“孩子们，你们最好退下吧，”她的语气温柔却十分坚定，“快去吧：受惊过度对你们身子不好。回房睡觉去吧。别担心，我向你们保证，我绝不会有半点危险。”姑娘们依言退下，一时间大厅里只剩下她孤身一人。
---
“说吧，”贝尔特姆夫人用毫无起伏的平板声调开口道。
大管家和门房押解着一个男人走到她面前：此人蓬头垢面，胡须凌乱粗糙；他周身着一袭黑衣，面容憔悴倦怠。脸色惨白如纸的贝尔特姆夫人不得不死死扶着一张扶手椅的椅背来支撑身躯。那个男人始终没有抬眼看她。
“除非只剩咱们单独相处，否则我绝不开口，”他语气沉闷地回答。
“单独相处？”贝尔特姆夫人极力压抑着翻江倒海的情绪，“这么说，你有极重要的大事要对我说？”
“夫人，倘若您对身陷绝境的苦难之人稍有体恤，”男人轻柔地答道，“您就会明白，他们绝不愿在……在那些无法理解苦难的人面前自取其辱。”说着他朝大管家和门房所在的方向微微点了点头。
“我对苦难并非一无所知，”贝尔特姆夫人的语气变得愈发笃定，“我会单独听你陈述。”她望向两名仆从，“请你们退下。”
大管家浑身一震。
“让您跟他单独待在一块儿？！这简直是发疯！”眼见贝尔特姆夫人只是带着傲慢而诧异的神色瞪着他，他只得慌乱地往后退缩，嘴里却仍旧抗议不休：“这……这简直是……夫人，您根本不清楚这家伙葫芦里卖的什么药，求您务必……”
然而贝尔特姆夫人毫不客气地冷冷打断了他。
“够了！”
厚重的丝绒门帘随着房门的闭合垂落下来。在小电灯昏暗微弱的光线掩映下，大厅里只剩下了贝尔特姆夫人和这个怪人——而她竟然如此爽快、如此蹊跷地应允与他单独密会。她紧随两名仆从走到门边，在他们身后将门反锁。

===Original===

Then with a sudden movement she sprang towards the man, who was standing motionless in the middle of the room following her with his eyes, and flung herself into his arms.
“Oh, Gurn, my darling, my darling!” she cried. “I love you! I love you, darling!” She looked up at him and saw blood upon his forehead. “Good God! The brutes have hurt you! What pain you must be in! Give me your eyes, your lips!” With kisses from her own lips she stanched the blood that was trickling down his cheeks, and with her fingers she smoothed his hair. “I am so happy!” she murmured, and broke off again. “But you are mad! Why, why come here like this, and let yourself be caught and tortured so?”
Moodily Gurn answered, returning kiss for kiss.
“Time has been so long without you! And this evening I was prowling round and saw a light. I thought that everyone would be asleep﻿—except you, of course. And so I came straight to you, over walls, and gates﻿—drawn to you like a moth to a candle: and that is all!”
With shining eyes and heaving breast Lady Beltham clung to her lover.
“I love you so! How brave you are! Yes, I am wholly, only yours. But this is madness! You might be arrested and given up to no one knows what horror, without my knowing!”
Gurn seemed to be hypnotised by the fierce and passionate love of this great lady.
“I never gave that a thought,” he murmured. “I only thought of you!”
Silence fell upon these tragic lovers as they stood reading love in one another’s eyes, and recalling memories common to both, utterly unlike as they were to outward seeming, yet linked by the strongest bond of all, the bond of love.
“What happy hours we lived together out there!” Lady Beltham whispered. Her thoughts had wandered to the far Transvaal and the battlefield where first she had set eyes on Gurn, the sergeant of artillery with powder-blackened face; and then to the homeward voyage on the mighty steamer that bore them across the blue sea, towards the dull white cliffs of England.
Gurn’s thoughts followed hers.
“Out there! Yes; and then on the vast ocean, on the ship homeward bound! The quiet and peace of it all! And our meetings every day: our long, long talks, and longer silences﻿—in the clear starlight of those tropical skies! We were learning to know each other﻿—”
“We were learning to love each other,” she said.

===Chinese===

随后，她猛地一个箭步扑向那个男人——他原本静立在大厅正中央、目光紧紧跟随着她——一下子扑进了他的怀抱。
“噢，居尔恩（Gurn），我的心肝，我的挚爱！”她哭喊道，“我爱你！我爱你啊，亲爱的！”她抬头凝视着他，瞥见了他额头上的斑斑血迹。“天哪！这帮畜生把你打伤了！你该有多疼啊！让我亲亲你的双眼，你的双唇！”她用自己的唇吻替他止住顺着面颊流淌的鲜血，用纤细的手指轻抚梳理着他的乱发。“我真是太幸福了！”她喃喃低语，旋即又哽咽顿住，“可你真是疯了！为什么，究竟为什么要这般跑来冒失涉险，任由自己被他们抓住、受这等严刑拷打？！”
居尔恩郁郁地回应着，以吻还吻。
“没有你的日子是那么漫长难熬！今天傍晚我在附近徘徊游荡，看到了这里的灯光。我以为所有人都睡下了——除了你，当然除了你。所以我径直奔你而来，翻过了围墙，越过了铁门——就像飞蛾扑向烛火一样被你深深吸引：事情就是这样！”
贝尔特姆夫人双眸闪烁着炽热的光芒，胸脯剧烈起伏，死死依偎在情人的怀抱中。
“我太爱你了！你是多么勇敢啊！是的，我整个人完完全全、只属于你一人！可这简直是自寻死路！你随时都可能被捕，甚至可能在我毫不知情的情形下，被推向万劫不复的无间地狱！”
居尔恩仿佛被这位尊贵贵妇那排山倒海般激烈狂热的爱意催眠迷醉了。
“我根本没顾得上想那些，”他喃喃低语道，“我满心所想的唯独只有你！”
这对悲剧的情侣陷入了静默；他们相拥而立，从彼此的双眸中辨读着炽烈的爱恋，共同追忆着属于他们两人的往事。外表看似天差地别的两个人，此刻却被世间最坚不可摧的纽带——爱情的纽带——死死紧扣在一起。
“当年在那个遥远的地方，咱们共同度过的时光是何等欢愉啊！”贝尔特姆夫人低声耳语道。她的思绪早已飘向了遥远的德兰士瓦（Transvaal），飘向了她初次得见居尔恩的那片硝烟弥漫的战场：当时他是炮兵中士，满脸被火药熏得焦黑；思绪继而又飘向了回程航线上那艘庞大的远洋轮船，它载着他们劈开湛蓝的大海，驶向英格兰那灰白苍凉的白垩断崖。
居尔恩的思绪也紧随着她。
“在那个遥远的地方！是的；还有茫茫浩瀚的大洋上，在返乡的轮船甲板上！那是何等安详宁谧的岁月啊！咱们每天的幽会，长久的促膝长谈，以及在热带夜空澄澈星光下那更为悠长的相顾无言！当时咱们正渐渐走入彼此的心底……”
“当时咱们正渐渐坠入爱河，”她说。

===Original===

“And then﻿—London, and Paris, and all the fever of life threatening our love. But that is the strongest thing in the world: and﻿—do you remember? Oh, the ecstasy of it all! But, do you remember too what you did for me﻿—through me﻿—thirteen months ago?”
She had risen, and with white lips and haggard eyes held Gurn’s hands within her own in an even tighter grip. Emotion choked her further utterance.
“Yes, I remember,” Gurn went on slowly: “it was in our little room in the rue Lévert, and I was on my knees beside you when the door opened quietly, and there stood Lord Beltham, mad with rage and jealousy!”
“I don’t know what happened then,” Lady Beltham whispered in a hopeless undertone, drooping her head again.
“I do,” muttered Gurn. “His eyes sought you, and a pistol was pointed at your heart! He would have fired, but I sprang and struck him down! And then I strangled him!”
Lady Beltham’s eyes were fixed on the man’s hands, that she still held between her own.
“And I saw the muscles in these hands swell up beneath the skin as they tightened on his throat!”
“I killed him!” groaned the man.
But Lady Beltham, swept by a surge of passion, sprang up and sought his lips.
“Oh, Gurn!” she sobbed﻿—“my darling!”
---
“Listen,” said Gurn harshly, after a pause of anxious silence. “I had to see you tonight, for who knows if tomorrow﻿—” Lady Beltham shrank at the words, but Gurn went on unheeding. “The police are after me. Of course I have made myself almost unrecognisable, but twice just lately I have been very nearly caught.”
“Do you think the police have any accurate idea of what happened?” Lady Beltham asked abruptly.
“No,” said Gurn after a moment’s hesitation. “They think I killed him with the mallet. They have not found out that I had to strangle him. As far as I know, they found no marks of my hands on his throat. At all events, they could not have been clear, for his collar﻿—you understand.” The man spoke of his crime without the least sign of remorse or repugnance now; his only dread was lest he should be caught. “But, none the less, they have identified me. That detective Juve is very clever.”
“We did not have enough presence of mind,” Lady Beltham said despairingly. “We ought to have led them to suspect someone else: have made them think that it was, say, Fantômas.”
“Not that!” said Gurn nervously; “don’t talk about Fantômas! We did all we could.

===Chinese===

“随后便是……伦敦，巴黎，以及周遭所有躁动疯狂的尘世喧嚣，都在时刻威胁着咱们的爱情。可爱情却是这世上最坚不可摧的力量；而且……你还记得吗？噢，那是何等销魂的狂喜！可是，你是否也还记得，在十三个月前，你为了我——因我之故——做下了何等惊天动地的大事？”
她站起身来，嘴唇煞白，眼神憔悴空洞，将居尔恩的双手死死攥在自己掌心，攥得愈发紧窒。激荡的情绪堵住了她的喉咙，令她一时语塞。
“是的，我都记得，”居尔恩缓缓开口道，“那是在咱们莱韦尔街（rue Lévert）的那间小套房里；我当时正跪伏在你的身侧，房门突然无声无息地被推开了，贝尔特姆勋爵赫然伫立在门前，因狂怒与嫉恨而面目扭曲、状如疯魔！”
“我完全不清楚后来究竟发生了什么，”贝尔特姆夫人绝望地低声耳语，再次颓然垂下了头。
“我清楚，”居尔恩低声咕哝道，“他的眼睛死死搜寻着你，手中的手枪直直瞄准了你的心脏！他当时就要扣动扳机开火了，但我猛扑上去将他打倒在地！紧接着我死死掐住了他的脖子，把他活活掐死了！”
贝尔特姆夫人的目光死死盯在男人的双手上，那双手至今仍被她紧紧握在掌心。
“我亲眼看着这双手上的青筋肌肉在皮肤下寸寸暴起，死死收紧在他的咽喉上！”
“是我杀了他！”男人痛苦地呻吟道。
然而贝尔特姆夫人被一股突如其来的炽烈激情所吞噬，她猛地扑上前去，疯狂寻觅着他的双唇。
“噢，居尔恩！”她抽泣着，“我的挚爱！”
---
“听着，”在一阵令人窒息的焦虑沉寂过后，居尔恩嗓音沙哑粗粝地开口道，“我今晚无论如何也必须见你一面，因为谁知道明天……”贝尔特姆夫人听到这话不禁畏缩了一下，但居尔恩毫不理会，自顾自说了下去。“警察正在满世界搜捕我。当然我已经把自己乔装改扮得几乎面目全非，可就在最近，我有两次险些落入法网。”
“你觉得警方对案发经过到底有没有确凿清晰的掌握？”贝尔特姆夫人冷不丁打断问道。
“没有，”居尔恩迟疑了片刻后回答，“他们认定我是用木槌砸死他的。他们至今没发觉我是被迫将他活活掐死的。据我所知，他们没有在他脖颈上找到我双手的指痕掐印。不管怎么说，那些痕迹即便有也不可能清晰，因为他的硬领——你懂的。”这个男人此刻谈及自己的滔天罪行，脸上竟没有流露出哪怕半点悔恨或嫌恶的神色；他心头唯一的恐惧，不过是唯恐自己被捕归案。“但即便如此，他们还是彻底查清了我的身份。那个叫朱夫的警探狡猾精明得很。”
“当时咱们太缺乏临机应变的镇定了，”贝尔特姆夫人绝望地叹道，“咱们本该把警方的疑窦引向旁人的：比方说，让他们认定凶手是芳托马斯（Fantômas）。”
“别提那个！”居尔恩神经质地打断道，“千万别提芳托马斯！当时咱们已经竭尽所能了。

===Original===

But the main thing now is that I should escape them. I had better get away﻿—across the Channel﻿—across the Atlantic﻿—anywhere. But﻿—would you come too?”
Lady Beltham did not hesitate. She flung her arms around the neck of the man who had murdered her own husband, and yielded to a paroxysm of wild passion.
“You know that I am yours, wherever you may go. Shall it be tomorrow? We can meet﻿—you know where﻿—and arrange everything for your flight.”
“My flight?” said Gurn, with reproachful emphasis on the pronoun.
“For our flight,” she replied, and Gurn smiled again.
“Then that is settled,” he said. “I have seen you, and I am happy! Goodbye.”
He made a step towards the door, but Lady Beltham stayed him gently.
“Wait,” she said. “Walter shall let you out of the house. Do not say anything: I will explain; I will invent some story to satisfy the servants as to your coming here, and also to justify your being allowed to go.”
They clung to one another in a parting caress. Lady Beltham tore herself away.
“Till tomorrow!” she whispered.
She stole to the door and unlocked it noiselessly, then crossed the room and rang the bell placed near the fireplace. Resuming her impassive mask, and the haughty air and attitude of cold indifference that were in such utter contrast to her real character, she waited, while Gurn stood upright and still in the middle of the room.
Walter, the porter, came in.
“Take that man to the door, and let no harm be done to him,” said Lady Beltham proudly and authoritatively. “He is free.”
Without a word, or sign, or glance, Gurn went out of the room, and Walter followed behind him to obey his mistress’s command.
---
Once more alone in the great hall, Lady Beltham waited nervously to hear the sound of the park gate closing behind Gurn. She did not dare go on to the balcony to follow her departing lover with her eyes. So, shaken by her recent emotions, she stood waiting and listening, in an agony to know that he was safe. Then, of a sudden, the noise that she had heard an hour before broke on her ears again: the noise of hurrying feet and broken shouts, and words, vague at first but rapidly growing clearer. She crouched forward listening, filled with a horrible fear, her hand laid upon her scarcely beating heart.
“There he is: hold him!” someone shouted. “That’s him all right! Look out, constable!”
“This way, Inspector! Yes, it’s him, it’s Gurn!

===Chinese===

眼下最要紧的大事是我必须逃脱他们的追捕。我最好立刻远走高飞——横渡英吉利海峡，横渡大西洋，逃到天涯海角去。可是……你愿意同我一道走吗？”
贝尔特姆夫人没有哪怕一秒钟的迟疑。她一把搂住了眼前这个亲手残杀自己丈夫的男人的脖颈，彻底沉溺在狂乱炽烈的激情爆发之中。
“你知道我是完完全全属于你的，无论你走到天涯海角我都生死相随。就定在明天好吗？咱们可以碰头——就在那个老地方——把你出逃的所有事宜安排妥当。”
“我出逃？”居尔恩带着责备的口吻特意加重了这个代词的读音。
“是咱们出逃，”她改口回答道，居尔恩脸上这才重新浮现出一抹笑意。
“那就这么说定了，”他说，“能见上你一面，我死而无憾了！永别了。”
他朝着房门迈出了一步，但贝尔特姆夫人轻轻拉住了他。
“等等，”她说，“让沃尔特领你走出宅邸。你什么话都别说；由我来出面解释；我会编出一套由头，既能打消下人们对你深夜潜入的疑心，又能合理交代我放你安然离去的理由。”
两人紧紧依偎纠缠在一起，做着依依不舍的吻别温存。贝尔特姆夫人硬生生将自己从情人的怀抱中抽离出来。
“明天见！”她轻声耳语道。
她悄无声息地溜到门边，轻轻拔下了门闩，接着穿过大厅，拉响了壁炉旁设有的传唤铃。重新戴上那副冷漠的面具，摆出那副与她真实性情截然相反的傲慢神态与冰冷姿态，她静静等候着；居尔恩则笔直挺立在大厅正中央，纹丝不动。
门房沃尔特应声走了进来。
“送这个人出大门，任何人不得为难伤害他，”贝尔特姆夫人神态倨傲、语气不容置喙地吩咐道，“他是自由之身了。”
居尔恩一言不发，既没有任何手势，也未曾投去哪怕一眼注视，径直走出了大厅；沃尔特紧随其后，遵奉女主人的命令送客出门。
---
大厅里再次只剩下了贝尔特姆夫人一人，她神经紧绷地倾听着，急切盼着听到居尔恩身后庄园大门闭合的声响。她甚至不敢迈上阳台，用目光去追随远去的情人。经历了一番翻江倒海的剧烈情绪激荡，她浑身颤抖地伫立在原地侧耳倾听，在无尽的焦灼痛苦中期盼着他能平安脱险。然而冷不防，大约一个小时前听到过的喧嚣嘈杂声再次炸响在她的耳畔：急促杂乱的奔跑脚步声、断断续续的狂呼怒号，以及起初模糊不清、旋即迅速清晰可辨的厉声呼喝。她惊恐万状地弓着身子侧耳倾听，内心被无边的恐怖所吞噬，一只手死死按压在几乎停止跳动的心口上。
“他在这儿！按住他！”有人放声大喊，“就是这家伙没错！当心，巡警！”
“这边走，长官！没错，就是他，就是居尔恩！

===Original===

Ah, would you!”
Paler than death, Lady Beltham cowered down upon a sofa.
“Good God! Good God!” she moaned. “What are they doing to him!”
The uproar in the garden decreased, then voices sounded in the corridor, Silbertown’s exclamations rising above the frightened cries of the three young girls.
“Gurn! Arrested! The man who murdered Lord Beltham!” Lisbeth called out in anxious terror.
“But Lady Beltham? Dear God, perhaps he has murdered her too!”
The door was flung open and the girls rushed in. Lady Beltham by a tremendous effort of will had risen to her feet, and was standing by the end of the sofa.
“Lady Beltham! Alive! Yes, yes!” and Thérèse and Lisbeth and Susannah rushed sobbing to her, and smothered her with caresses.
But the agonised woman motioned them away. With hard eyes and set mouth she moved towards the window, straining her ears to listen. From the park outside Gurn’s voice rang distinctly; the lover wished to let his mistress know what had happened, and to take a last farewell.
“I am caught, I am caught! Yes, I am Gurn, and I am caught!”
The fatal words were still ringing in Lady Beltham’s ears when the majordomo, Silbertown, came bursting into the room, with radiant face and shining eyes and smiling lips, and hurried to his mistress.
“I thought as much!” he exclaimed excitedly. “It was the villain all right. I recognised him from the description, in spite of his beard. I informed the police! As a matter of fact they have been watching for the last two days. Just fancy, your ladyship, a detective was shadowing Gurn﻿—and when he was going out of the house I gave him the signal!”
Lady Beltham stared at the majordomo in mute horror.
“Yes?” she muttered, on the point of swooning.
“I pointed him out to the police, and it’s thanks to me, your ladyship, that Gurn, the murderer, has been arrested at last!”
For just another moment Lady Beltham stared at the man who gave her these appalling tidings, seemed to strive to utter something, then fell prone to the floor, unconscious.
The majordomo and the girls sprang to her side to lavish attentions upon her.
At that moment the door was pushed a little way open, and the figure of Juve appeared.
“May I come in?” said he.

===Chinese===

想跑？没那么容易！”
脸色比死人还要惨白的贝尔特姆夫人瑟瑟发抖地蜷缩在沙发上。
“老天爷啊！老天爷啊！”她痛苦地呻吟着，“他们究竟在对他干什么啊！”
花园里的喧嚣渐渐平息，紧接着走廊里传来了嘈杂的人声，西尔伯敦兴奋激动的呼喊压过了三位年轻姑娘惊恐万状的尖叫啼哭。
“居尔恩！落网了！那个杀害贝尔特姆勋爵的凶手！”利斯贝思惊惶失措地尖叫道。
“可贝尔特姆夫人呢？！天哪，保不准他把夫人也给杀害了！”
房门被一把猛推开，姑娘们发疯似地涌了进来。贝尔特姆夫人凭着极其惊人的顽强意志强撑着站起身来，倚立在沙发一端。
“贝尔特姆夫人！还活着！谢天谢地，真的还活着！”泰蕾兹、利斯贝思和苏珊娜抽泣着扑向她，将她紧紧围拢搂抱。
然而这位痛苦欲绝的女人却挥了挥手示意她们走开。她目光呆滞冰冷，嘴角紧抿，一步步挪向窗边，竖起耳朵拼命捕捉外面的动静。庄园外的树林里清晰地回荡着居尔恩的嘶吼声；这位情人一心想让自己的女主人知晓眼下的大祸，向她道出最后的永诀：
“我被抓住了！我被抓住了！没错，我就是居尔恩，我落网了！”
这绝望致命的呼号声仍在贝尔特姆夫人耳畔回荡震颤，大管家西尔伯敦已然满面红光、两眼放光、满嘴挂着笑意破门而入，兴冲冲直奔女主人而来。
“我就知道是这么回事！”他兴奋得语无伦次地大喊道，“果然就是那个恶贯满盈的恶棍！尽管他留了胡子，但我还是根据通缉相貌特征一眼认出了他！我暗中报了警！实际上便衣警察过去这两天一直在外围布控蹲守。您能想象吗，夫人，一名刑警早就在尾随跟踪居尔恩了——就在他刚一迈出宅邸大门时，我立刻向他们发出了暗号！”
贝尔特姆夫人陷入了无声的极度惊骇之中，死死瞪着大管家。
“是吗？”她喃喃咕哝道，几乎快要当场昏厥过去。
“是我向警方指明了他的行踪！全仗着我的功劳，夫人，杀人凶手居尔恩终于被捉拿归案了！”
贝尔特姆夫人死死盯视着眼前这个向她邀功通报这记晴天霹雳的仆人，嘴唇颤抖着似乎拼死想要吐出几个字来，紧接着身子一软，重重扑倒在冰冷的地板上，不省人事。
大管家和姑娘们惊叫着扑上前去，围在她身旁手忙脚乱地照料施救。
就在这千钧一发之际，房门被轻轻推开了一道缝隙，朱夫的身影赫然出现在门前。
“我可以进来吗？”他开口问道。

## XXII The Scrap of Paper / 第二十二章 纸片
===Original===

It was three o’clock when Juve arrived at the rue Lévert, and he found the concierge of number 147 just finishing her coffee.
Amazed at the results achieved by the detective, the details of which she had learned from the sensational articles in the daily paper she most affected, Mme. Doulenques had conceived a most respectful admiration for the Inspector of the Criminal Investigation Department.
“That man,” she constantly declared to Madame Aurore, “it isn’t eyes he has in his head, it’s telescopes, magnifying glasses! He sees everything in a minute﻿—even when it isn’t there!”
She gave him an admiring “good afternoon, Inspector,” as he came into her lodge, and going to a board on which numbers of keys were hanging, took one down and handed it to him.
“So there’s something fresh today?” she said. “I’ve just seen in the paper that M. Gurn has been arrested. So it was my lodger who did it? What a dreadful man! Whoever would have thought it? It turns my blood cold to think of him!”
Juve was never a man for general conversation, and he was still less interested in the garrulity of this loquacious creature. He took the key and cut short her remarks by walking to the door.
“Yes, Gurn has been arrested,” he said shortly; “but he has made no confession, so nothing is known for certain yet. Please go on with your work exactly as though I were not in the house, Mme. Doulenques.”
It was his usual phrase, and a constant disappointment to the concierge, who would have asked nothing better than to go upstairs with the detective and watch him at his wonderful work.
Juve went up the five floors to the flat formerly occupied by Gurn, reflecting somewhat moodily. Of course Gurn’s arrest was a success, and it was satisfactory to have the scoundrel under lock and key, but in point of fact Juve had learned nothing new in consequence of the arrest, and he was obsessed with the idea that this murder of Lord Beltham was an altogether exceptional crime. He did not yet know why Gurn had killed Lord Beltham, and he did not even know exactly who Gurn himself was; all he could declare was that the murder had been planned and carried out with marvellous audacity and skill, and that was not enough.
Juve let himself into the flat and closed the door carefully behind him. The rooms were in disorder, the result of the searches effected by the police.

===Chinese===

朱夫赶到莱韦尔街（rue Lévert）时已是下午三点钟，他发现147号的门房（concierge）杜朗克太太（Mme. Doulenques）刚喝完咖啡。
从平日最爱读的日报上一篇篇耸人听闻的报道中，杜朗克太太获悉了这位警探屡破奇案的详尽细节，对这位刑事调查处（Criminal Investigation Department）的警官油然而生一股无比崇敬的钦佩之情。
“那个人啊，”她屡屡对奥罗尔太太念叨，“他脑袋上长的哪里是肉眼，分明是望远镜、放大镜！他眨巴眼的工夫就能把一切看得一清二楚——哪怕压根没影儿的事他都能给瞧出来！”
朱夫刚迈进门房值班室，她便满眼仰慕地道了声“警官下午好”，接着快步走向一面挂满各式钥匙的挂板，摘下一把钥匙递到他手中。
“这么说，今儿个又有新线索啦？”她说，“我刚才在报上看到，居尔恩先生（M. Gurn）落网了。这么说，当真是我那个房客干的？多吓人的恶棍啊！谁能料得到呢？一想到他，我浑身的血都要吓凉了！”
朱夫向来不是个喜欢闲扯拉家常的人，对这个多嘴多舌婆娘的絮叨更是不胜其烦。他接过钥匙，拔腿就朝门口走去，硬生生掐断了她的话头。
“是的，居尔恩已经被捕了，”他冷冷地应道，“但他尚未招供，所以眼下还没有任何确凿定论。请您继续忙自己的活计吧，权当我不在宅子里就行，杜朗克太太。”
这是他一贯的口头禅，而这总让门房太太大失所望，她巴不得能跟在这位警探身后登上楼去，亲眼瞻仰他是如何出神入化破案的。
朱夫若有所思、神色阴郁地爬上五楼，来到居尔恩昔日承租的公寓套房门前。逮捕居尔恩固然是一记胜仗，把这个恶棍收押锁进铁窗也确实大快人心，但实际上朱夫并未因这次抓捕获得任何新的线索。他脑海中始终被一个念头所纠缠挥之不去：谋杀贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）的这桩惨案，绝非寻常凶案可比。他至今摸不清居尔恩究竟出于何种动机杀害贝尔特姆勋爵，甚至根本吃不准居尔恩本人到底何许人也；他唯一能够断言的，不过是这起凶案预谋周详、作案手法骇人胆大且老练诡谲——但这远远不够。
朱夫用钥匙开门走进套房，并在身后小心翼翼地随手带上了门。屋里一片凌乱狼藉，全是警方此前地毯式搜查留下的翻腾痕迹。

===Original===

The rent had not been paid for some time, and as no friend or relation had come forward to assume control of Gurn’s interests, the furniture and ornaments of the little flat were to be sold by auction.
The detective walked through the rooms, then flung himself into an armchair. He did not know precisely why he had come. He had searched the place a dozen times already since his discovery of the corpse within the trunk, and had found nothing more, no telltale marks or fresh detail, to assist in the elucidation of the mystery. He would have given very much to be able to identify Gurn with some other of the many criminals who had passed through his hands, and still more to be able to identify him with that one most mysterious criminal whose fearful deeds had shocked the world so greatly. Somehow the particular way in which this murder was committed, the very audacity of it, led him to think, to “sense,” almost to swear that﻿—
Juve got up. It was little in accord with his active temperament to sit still. Once more he went all round the flat.
“The kitchen? Let me see: I have been through everything? The stove? The cupboards? The saucepans? Why, I went so far as to make sure that there was no poison in them, though it seemed a wild idea. The anteroom? Nothing there: the umbrella stand was empty, and the one interesting thing I did see, the torn curtain, has been described and photographed officially.” He went back into the dining-room. “I’ve searched all the furniture: and I went through all the parcels Gurn had done up before he left, and would, no doubt, have come back for at his leisure, had it not been for my discovery of the body, and the unfortunate publicity the newspapers gave to that fact.” In one corner of the room was a heap of old newspapers, crumpled and torn, and thrown down in disorder. Juve kicked them aside. “I’ve looked through all that, even read the agony columns, but there was nothing there.” He went into the bedroom and contemplated the bed, that the concierge had stripped, the chairs set one on top of another in a corner, and the wardrobe that stood empty, its former contents scattered on the floor by the police during their search. There, too, nothing was to be found.
Against the wall, near the fireplace, was a little escritoire with a cupboard above it, containing a few battered books.

===Chinese===

房租已经拖欠了有些时日，鉴于居尔恩没有任何亲友出面料理接管他的财物，这间小公寓里的家具摆设即将被送去公开拍卖。
警探在各间屋子里踱来踱去，随后一头将自己摔进了一张扶手椅里。他甚至连自己究竟为何要来也说不大清楚。自从他在大皮箱里起获那具尸体以来，他早已将这处住所里里外外搜查了不下十几次，却再也没有搜出任何蛛丝马迹，既没有可以定案的痕迹，也没有能够厘清谜团的任何新细节。他真愿意付出极大的代价，只要能将居尔恩与过往经他之手的诸多重犯中的某一人对上号，而更渴望的，则是能将此人与那个滔天恶行震惊举世的最神秘罪魁画上等号。不知为何，这桩凶案独具一格的作案手法、那份肆无忌惮的胆大妄为，使他不自觉地去揣测、去“直觉”、乃至近乎敢赌咒发誓……
朱夫霍然起身。枯坐呆立向来不符合他那风风火火的行动派性子。他又把整间套房从头到尾转悠了一遍。
“厨房？让我好好想想：我当真全都搜遍了吗？炉灶？橱柜？炖锅？哎，我甚至还费尽心思查验过锅里有没有残毒，虽说那念头看似异想天开。门厅？空空如也：伞架是空的，而我唯一瞧出蹊跷的那副扯破的门帘，早就由公家作了笔录并拍下了现场照片。”他折返回餐厅。“所有的家具我都搜过了；居尔恩临行前打包收拾的包裹我也挨个翻过——若不是我提前查获了箱中藏尸、加之报纸横插一杠将此事闹得满城风雨，他必定会悠哉游哉折返回来取走这些包裹的。”在房间的一个角落里堆着一堆废旧报纸，皱皱巴巴破烂不堪，杂乱无章地扔在地上。朱夫用脚将它们踢到一边。“这些报纸我也统统翻阅过，连寻人启事栏都没放过，可也是一无所获。”他步入卧室，凝视着那张已被门房扒掉了被褥床单的床铺，角落里高高叠放在一起的椅子，以及那座敞开着的大衣柜——里面的衣物早在此前警方的地毯式翻找中被乱糟糟扔了一地。在卧室里，同样毫无斩获。
在紧挨壁炉的墙边，立着一座带顶柜的小写字台，上面的柜格里杂乱摆放着几本破旧不堪的旧书。

===Original===

“My men have been all through that,” Juve muttered; “it’s most unlikely that they missed anything, but perhaps I had better see.”
He sat down before it and began methodically to sort the scattered papers; with quick, trained glance he scanned each document, putting one after another aside with a grimace expressive of disappointment. Almost the last document he picked up was a long sheet of parchment, and as he unfolded it an exclamation escaped his lips. It was an official notice of Gurn’s promotion to the rank of sergeant when fighting under Lord Beltham in the South African War. Juve read it through﻿—he knew English well﻿—and laid it down with a gesture of discouragement.
“It is extraordinary,” he muttered. “That seems to be perfectly authentic; it is authentic, and it proves that this fellow was a decent fellow and a brave soldier once; that is a fine record of service.” He drummed his fingers on the desk and spoke aloud. “Is Gurn really Gurn, then, and have I been mistaken from start to finish in the little romance I have been weaving round him? How am I to find the key to the mystery? How am I to prove the truth of what I feel to be so very close to me, but which eludes me every time, just as I seem to be about to grasp it?”
He went on with his search, and then, looking at the bookcase, took the volumes out and, holding each by its two covers, shook it to make sure that no papers were hidden among the leaves. But all in vain. He did the same with a large railway timetable and several shipping calendars.
“The odd thing is,” he thought, “that all these timetables go to prove that Gurn really was the commercial traveller he professed to be. It’s exactly things such as these one would expect to find in the possession of a man who travelled much, and always had to be referring to the dates of sailing to distant parts of the world.”
In the bookcase was a box, made to represent a bound book, and containing a collection of ordnance maps. Juve took them out to make sure that no loose papers were included among them, and one by one unfolded every map.
Then a sharp exclamation burst from his lips.
“Good Lord! Now there﻿—”
In his surprise he sprang up so abruptly that he pushed back his chair, and overturned it. His excitement was so great that his hands were shaking as he carefully spread out upon the desk one of the ordnance maps he had taken from the case.

===Chinese===

“手下弟兄已经把那里翻了个底朝天，”朱夫喃喃自语道，“他们不大可能会遗漏什么，不过我或许最好还是亲自过目一眼。”
他在写字台前坐下，有条不紊地理顺散落的纸页；凭借训练有素的敏锐目光，他飞快地扫过每一份文件，每把一份文件搁到一旁，脸上便露出失望的苦笑。就在他几乎拿到最后一份文件时，那是一张长条羊皮纸，甫一展开，一声惊呼便从他唇间脱口而出。那是南非战争期间，居尔恩在贝尔特姆勋爵麾下作战时晋升中士军衔的官方正式通令。朱夫将其从头到尾细细读了一遍——他精通英语——随后带着沮丧的手势将它搁了下来。
“不可思议，”他低声咕哝道，“这通令看似毫无作伪之嫌；它千真万确是真的，而且证明这家伙昔日曾是个本分正派的汉子、一名英勇无畏的士兵，立下过赫赫战功。”他用手指轻轻叩击着桌面，自言自语道：“难道居尔恩当真就是居尔恩本人？难道我自始至终围绕他苦心构建的那些推想，全都南辕北辙错得离谱？我究竟该怎么找到解开谜团的钥匙？我分明觉得真相近在咫尺，可每当我自以为就要将其一把攥在掌心时，它却次次从指缝溜走，我到底该如何证实自己的预感？”
他继续翻找搜寻，随后盯上了那几排旧书；他把书本逐一抽出，双手捏着书脊封面死死抖搂，以防书页间夹带私藏纸片。然而徒劳无功。他又对一本厚重的大号列车时刻表和几本轮船航次表如法炮制。
“蹊跷古怪的是，”他心下暗忖，“所有这些时刻表全都在印证居尔恩的确就是他自称的跑单推销员。一个整年四处奔波、时刻需要核对开往天涯海角班轮航期的人，手头备着的恰恰就是这些家当。”
书架上摆着一个做成精装书模样的匣子，里面收纳着一套参谋部军用测绘地图。朱夫将它们取了出来，以防其中夹带单页纸张，接着把每一张地图一张接一张逐一展开。
突然，一声尖锐的惊呼从他嘴里冲破而出：
“老天爷啊！瞧这……”
因极度惊愕，他猛地弹起身来，顺势撞翻了身后的椅子。他内心激动万分，双手止不住地剧烈颤抖，小心翼翼地将从匣子里抽出的其中一张军用地图平铺在写字台上。

===Original===

“It’s the map of the centre district all right: the map which shows Cahors, and Brives, and Saint-Jaury and﻿—Beaulieu! And the missing piece﻿—it is the missing piece that would give that precise district!”
Juve stared at the map with hypnotised gaze; for a piece had been cut out of it, cut out with a penknife neatly and carefully, and that piece must have shown the exact district where the château stood which had been occupied by the Marquise de Langrune.
“Oh, if I could only prove it: prove that the piece missing from this map, this map belonging to Gurn, is really and truly the piece I found near Verrières Station just after the murder of the Marquise de Langrune﻿—what a triumph that would be! What a damning proof! What astounding consequences this discovery of mine might have!”
Juve made a careful note of the number of the map, quickly and nervously, folded it up again, and prepared to leave the flat.
He had made but a step or two towards the door when a sharp ring at the bell made him jump.
“The deuce!” he exclaimed softly; “who can be coming to ring Gurn up when everybody in Paris knows he has been arrested?” and he felt mechanically in his pocket to make sure that his revolver was there. Then he smiled. “What a fool I am! Of course it is only Mme. Doulenques, wondering why I am staying here so long.”
He strode to the door, flung it wide open, and then recoiled in astonishment.
“You?” he exclaimed, surveying the caller from top to toe. “You? Charles Rambert! Or, I should say, Jérôme Fandor! Now what the deuce does this mean?”

===Chinese===

“这分明就是中部地区的地图：上面清晰标有卡奥尔、布里夫（Brives）、圣若里（Saint-Jaury），还有……博利厄！而那块缺失的碎片——正巧就是原本标明这片特定区域的那一块！”
朱夫如痴如醉地死死盯着那张地图；地图上赫然有一块被人裁掉了，是用折叠小刀仔细周正裁切下来的，而被裁下的那部分，恰恰必定完整显示着德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）那座城堡所坐落的精确区域！
“噢，只要我能证明这一点：证明从居尔恩这幅地图上缺失的这块残片，确确实实就是德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人遇害后我在韦里埃尔车站（Verrières Station）附近拾获的那块碎片——那将是何等辉煌的胜利！何等铁证如山的铁证！我这项惊天发现又将掀起何等翻天覆地的波澜！”
朱夫神经紧绷而麻利地仔细记下了这幅地图的编录编号，将它重新折叠整齐，准备离开套房。
他刚朝门口迈出一两步，一阵尖锐刺耳的门铃声吓得他浑身猛地一颤。
“见鬼！”他压低嗓门轻呼道，“全巴黎的人都知道居尔恩被捕了，这当口还有谁会跑来按他的门铃呢？”他下意识地把手伸进口袋，摸索确认自己的左轮手枪是否带在身上。随后他哑然失笑：“我真是犯傻！这准是杜朗克太太在犯嘀咕，好奇我为何在上面耽搁了这么久。”
他大步走到门前，一把将门大敞开来，旋即因极度惊愕而连退两步。
“怎么是你？！”他失声惊呼，从头到脚细细打量着眼前的来客。“是你？！夏尔·朗贝尔（Charles Rambert）！或者我该叫你，热罗姆·凡多尔（Jérôme Fandor）！这他娘的究竟是怎么一回事？！”

## XXIII The Wreck of the *Lancaster* / 第二十三章 “兰开斯特号”遇难
===Original===

Jérôme Fandor entered the room without a word. Juve closed the door behind him. The boy was very pale and manifestly much upset.
“What is the matter?” said Juve.
“Something terrible has happened,” the boy answered. “I have just heard awful news: my poor father is dead!”
“What?” Juve exclaimed sharply. “M. Etienne Rambert dead?”
Jérôme Fandor put a newspaper into the detective’s hand. “Read that,” he said, and pointed to an article on the front page with a huge headline: “Wreck of the *Lancaster*: 150 Lives Lost.” There were tears in his eyes, and he had such obvious difficulty in restraining his grief, that Juve saw that to read the article would be the speediest way to find out what had occurred.
The Red Star liner *Lancaster*, plying between Caracas and Southampton, had gone down with all hands the night before, just off the Isle of Wight, and at the moment of going to press only one person was known to have been saved. There was a good sea running, but it was by no means rough, and the vessel was still within sight of the lighthouse and making for the open sea at full speed, when the lighthousemen suddenly saw her literally blown into the air and then disappear beneath the waves. The alarm was given immediately and boats of all kinds put off to the scene of the disaster, but though a great deal of wreckage was still floating about, only one man of the crew was seen, clinging to a spar; he was picked up by the *Campbell* and taken to hospital, where he was interviewed by *The Times*, without, however, being able to throw any light upon what was an almost unprecedented catastrophe in the history of the sea. All he could say was that the liner had just got up full speed and was making a perfectly normal beginning of her trip, when suddenly a tremendous explosion occurred. He himself was engaged at the moment fastening the tarpaulins over the baggage hold, and he was confident that the explosion occurred among the cargo. But he could give absolutely no more information: the entire ship seemed to be riven asunder, and he was thrown into the sea, stunned, and knew no more until he recovered consciousness and found himself aboard the *Campbell*.
“It’s quite incomprehensible,” Juve muttered; “surely there can’t have been any powder aboard? No explosives are carried on these great liners; they only take passengers and the mails.” He scanned the list of passengers.

===Chinese===

热罗姆·凡多尔（Jérôme Fandor）一言不发地迈入房间。朱夫随手关上了身后的房门。少年脸色惨白如纸，显然受了极大的打击。
“出什么事了？”朱夫关切地问道。
“发生了极其可怕的事，”少年回答道，“我刚听到一个晴天霹雳：我可怜的父亲去世了！”
“什么？！”朱夫失声疾呼，“埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生（M. Etienne Rambert）死了？！”
热罗姆·凡多尔将一份报纸塞进警探手中。“您瞧瞧这个，”他说着，伸手指向头版上一篇顶着醒目特大号黑体标题的报道：“‘兰开斯特号’遇难：150人丧生”。他眼眶泛红噙满泪水，极力克制着内心的巨大悲恸，朱夫看出尽快把报道读完才是弄清原委的最快途径。
往返于加拉加斯（Caracas）与南安普敦之间的红星轮船公司客轮“兰开斯特号”（Lancaster），前一天深夜在怀特岛（Isle of Wight）近海附近全船沉没；截至发稿时，已知仅有一人生还。当时海面风浪平缓，绝谈不上汹涌恶劣，轮船尚在灯塔视线范围内，正开足马力驶向公海。突然间，灯塔守望员惊骇地目睹整艘巨轮竟被生生炸飞上半空，旋即沉入滔滔波涛之下消失得无影无踪。警报随即拉响，各色救援船只火速赶赴海难现场；然而尽管海面上漂浮着大量残骸碎片，人们却只发现了一名紧紧抱住一根桅杆圆木的水手。他被“坎贝尔号”（Campbell）救起并送往医院，并在那里接受了《泰晤士报》（The Times）的独家采访，然而对于这场在世界海难史上近乎空前绝后的惨剧，他也无法提供更多明晰线索。他唯一能说的，不过是客轮当时刚刚提升至全速，航程的开端一切正常平稳，紧接着猛然发生了一场惊天动地的大爆炸。爆炸发生时他本人正在货舱上方捆绑固定遮盖行李的防水油布，他笃定爆炸必定发生在底舱货物之中。但他无法提供更多底细了：整艘巨轮仿佛瞬间被撕裂成两半，他被巨大的冲击波直接掀飞落海、震晕过去，直至重新苏醒恢复意识，才发觉自己已经置身于“坎贝尔号”上了。
“这简直匪夷所思，”朱夫喃喃自语道，“船上绝不可能装运火药啊！这类大型豪华远洋客轮上严禁携带任何爆炸易燃物，它们向来只搭载旅客和邮政邮件。”他目光迅速扫过乘客名单。

===Original===

“Etienne Rambert’s name is given among the first-class passengers, right enough,” he said. “Well, it’s odd!”
Jérôme Fandor heaved a profound sigh.
“It is a fatality which I shall never get over,” he said. “When you told me the other day that you knew I was innocent, I ought to have gone to see my father, in spite of what you said. I am sure he would have believed me and come to see you; then you could have convinced him, and I should not have this horrible grief of remembering that he had died without learning that his son was not a bad man, but was quite deserving of his affection.”
Jérôme Fandor was making a brave struggle to maintain his self-control, and Juve looked at him without concealing the real sympathy he felt for him in his grief. He put his hand kindly on his shoulder.
“Listen, my dear boy; odd as you may think it, you can take my word for it that there is no need for you to despair; there is nothing to prove that your father is dead; he may not have been on board.”
The boy looked up in surprise.
“What do you mean, Juve?”
“I don’t want to say anything, my boy, except that you would be very wrong to give way to distress at present. If you have any confidence in me, you may believe me when I say that. There is nothing yet to prove that you have had this loss: and, besides, you still have your mother, who is perfectly sure to get quite well: do you understand?﻿—*perfectly sure!*” He changed the subject abruptly. “There is one thing I should like to know: what the dickens brought you here?”
“You were the first person I thought of in my trouble,” Fandor replied. “Directly I read about the disaster in that paper I came to tell you at once.”
“Yes, I quite understand that,” Juve answered. “What I do not understand is how you guessed that you would find me here, in Gurn’s flat.”
The question seemed to perturb the boy.
“It﻿—it was quite by chance,” he stammered.
“That is the kind of explanation one offers to fools,” Juve retorted. “By what chance did you see me come into this house? What the deuce were you doing in the rue Lévert?” The lad showed some inclination to make for the door, but Juve stayed him peremptorily. “Answer my question, please: how did you know I was here?”
Driven into a corner, the boy blurted out the truth:
“I had followed you.”
“Followed me?” Juve exclaimed. “Where from?”
“From your rooms.”

===Chinese===

“头等舱旅客名单里果然赫然列着埃蒂安·朗贝尔的名字，”他说，“这可真是古怪离奇！”
热罗姆·凡多尔发出了一声沉重的叹息。
“这是一场我终生都无法释怀的宿命之痛，”他说，“前些天你告诉我你明知我是清白无辜的时候，我当初无论如何都该不顾你的劝阻去见我父亲一面的。我坚信他一定会相信我的话，并亲自来找你核实；到时候你就能彻底说服他，我也就不至于背负着这般痛彻心扉的悔恨——眼睁睁看着他含恨离世，临终前甚至都未能获悉自己的儿子并非十恶不赦的坏胚，而是完完全全配得上他的慈父之爱。”
热罗姆·凡多尔在极力维持着克制，朱夫看着他，毫不掩饰自己对少年痛苦遭遇的由衷怜惜与同情。他亲切地把手搭在少年的肩膀上。
“听着，我的好孩子；尽管你可能会觉得匪夷所思，但你可以把我的话放在心上：你完全没有必要绝望心死；眼下没有任何确凿证据表明你父亲已经罹难；他当时极有可能根本就没登船。”
少年惊愕地抬起头来。
“朱夫，你这话是什么意思？”
“好孩子，眼下我还不便多说什么，只是你眼下万万不该让自己被悲痛击垮。倘若你对我还有哪怕半分信任，你就该听信我的断言。目前绝没有任何确凿事实证明你当真失去了至亲；况且，你还有你的母亲，她必定会彻底康复痊愈的——你明白我的意思吗？是必定痊愈！”他冷不丁话锋一转。“另外有一件事我倒想好好弄弄清楚：你究竟怎么会撞到这儿来的？”
“大祸临头时我头一个想到的就是你，”凡多尔回答道，“我在报上读到海难消息的第一眼，就火急火燎赶来要头一个告诉你。”
“是的，这一点我完全想得通，”朱夫回答说，“可我想不通的是，你怎么猜得到会在这儿——在这间属于居尔恩（Gurn）的公寓套房里——堵到我呢？”
这个问题似乎戳中了少年的心事，让他局促不安起来。
“这……纯粹是个巧合，”他结结巴巴地辩解道。
“这种借口只能拿去糊弄三岁小孩，”朱夫反唇相讥，“凭什么巧合能让你撞见我走进这栋楼？你平白无故跑到莱韦尔街（rue Lévert）来干什么？！”少年下意识地向门口挪步想溜，但朱夫严厉地拦住了他。“给我老老实实回答：你怎么知道我在这儿？”
被逼到死角的少年只得脱口吐露了实情：
“我是一路跟踪你过来的。”
“跟踪我？！”朱夫失声叫道，“从哪儿跟过来的？”
“从你的寓所一路跟过来的。”

===Original===

“You mean, and you may as well own up to it at once, that you were shadowing me.”
“Well, yes, M. Juve, it is true,” Fandor confessed, all in one breath. “I was shadowing you: I do every day!”
Juve was dumbfounded.
“Every day? And I never saw you! Gad, you are jolly clever! And may I enquire why you have been exercising this supervision over me?”
Jérôme Fandor hung his head.
“Forgive me,” he faltered; “I have been very stupid. I thought you﻿—I thought you were﻿—Fantômas!”
The idea tickled the detective so much that he dropped back into a chair to laugh at his ease.
“ ’Pon my word,” he said, “you have an imagination! And what made you suppose that I was Fantômas?”
“M. Juve,” Fandor said earnestly, “I made a vow that I would find out the truth, and discover the scoundrel who has made such awful havoc of my life. But I did not know where to begin. From all you have said I realised that Fantômas was a most extraordinarily clever man; I did not know anyone who could be cleverer than you; and so I watched you! It was merely logical!”
Far from being angry, Juve was rather flattered.
“I am amazed by what you have just told me, my boy,” he said with a smile. “In the first place your reasoning is not at all bad. Of course it is obvious that I cannot suspect myself of being Fantômas, but I quite admit that if I were in your place I might make the supposition, wild as it may seem. And, in the next place, you have shadowed me without my becoming aware of the fact, and that is very good indeed: a proof that you are uncommonly smart.” He looked at the lad attentively for a few moments, and then went on more gravely: “Are you satisfied now that your hypothesis was wrong? Or do you still suspect me?”
“No, I don’t suspect you now,” Fandor declared; “not since I saw you come into this house; Fantômas certainly would not have come to search Gurn’s rooms because﻿—”
He stopped, and Juve, who was looking at him keenly, did not make him finish what he was saying.
“Shall I tell you something?” he said at last. “If you continue to display as much thought and initiative in the career you have chosen as you have just displayed, you will very soon be the first newspaper detective of the day!” He jumped up and led the boy off. “Come along: I’ve got to go to the Law Courts at once.”
“You’ve found out something fresh?”

===Chinese===

“你的意思是——你干脆痛痛快快直说吧——你一直在暗中盯我的梢。”
“这个嘛，是的，朱夫先生，确实是这么回事，”凡多尔一口气全招了，“我一直在尾随盯你的梢：天天都在盯！”
朱夫听得瞠目结舌。
“天天盯？可我居然从没察觉到你！见鬼，你小子可真够神通广大的！那我能否请教一下，你干嘛非要这般监视我呢？”
热罗姆·凡多尔愧疚地低下了头。
“求您宽恕我吧，”他支支吾吾地说道，“我真是蠢到家了。我原以为你……我原以为你就是……芳托马斯（Fantômas）！”
这个离谱绝伦的念头把警探逗乐了，他一屁股跌坐回椅子里，开怀畅快地大笑起来。
“我凭良心讲，”他说，“你这小子的想象力可真够天马行空的！你到底凭什么认定我就是芳托马斯呢？”
“朱夫先生，”凡多尔诚恳切切地说道，“我曾暗自发誓，一定要查明水落石出的真相，把那个将我整个人生毁得支离破碎的恶魔给揪出来。可我当时全然不知该从何下手。从您口中透露的种种言谈里，我意识到芳托马斯是个狡黠精明到极致的绝顶人物；而我所认识的人里，再没有谁能比您更聪明能干了；于是我就盯上了您！这完全符合常理逻辑嘛！”
朱夫不仅毫无愠色，反倒颇受奉承讨好。
“听了你这番话我真给惊着了，好孩子，”他微笑着说，“首先，你的推论逻辑其实相当合情合理。当然，我很显然不可能怀疑我自己就是芳托马斯，但我完全承认，换作我是你，即便这设想看似荒唐透顶，我也很可能会产生同样的怀疑。其次，你暗中跟踪了我这么久，我居然自始至终蒙在鼓里毫无察觉，这身本事确实极其了得，足以证明你是个罕见机敏的好苗子。”他目不转睛地审视了少年片刻，语气变得庄重了些许：“那你现在确信你的假设大错特错了吗？还是说你心底依然对我存有疑心？”
“不，我现在一点也不怀疑你了，”凡多尔斩钉截铁地声明，“自从我亲眼瞧见你走进这栋宅子起就彻底打消了怀疑；芳托马斯断然不会跑来搜查居尔恩的套房，因为……”
他猛地刹住了话头，而朱夫犀利地注视着他，并未强迫他把后半句咽回去的话说完。
“要我挑明告诉你一桩预言吗？”他终于开口说道，“倘若你在自己选定的这门营生里，能继续展现出方才这般深刻缜密的思考与果决的主动干劲，用不了多久，你就会成为当世独占鳌头的首席侦探记者！”他霍然起身，拉着少年朝外走去。“走吧，我得立刻赶往司法宫（Law Courts）一趟。”
“你发现什么新线索了？”

===Original===

“I’m going to ask them to call an interesting witness in the Gurn affair.”
---
Rain had been falling heavily all the morning and afternoon, but within the last few minutes it had almost stopped. Dollon, the steward, put his hand out of the window and found that only a few drops were falling now from the heavy grey sky.
He was an invaluable servant, and a few months after the death of the Marquise de Langrune, the Baronne de Vibray had gladly offered him a situation, and a cottage on her estate at Querelles.
He walked across the room, and called his son.
“Jacques, would you like to come with me? I am going down to the river to see that the sluices have been opened properly. The banks are anything but sound, and these rains will flood us out one of these days.”
The steward and his son went down the garden towards the stream which formed one boundary of Mme. de Vibray’s park.
“Look, father,” Jacques exclaimed, “the postman is calling us.”
The postman, a crusty but good-hearted fellow, came hurrying up to the steward.
“You do make me run, M. Dollon,” he complained. “I went to your house this morning to take you a letter, but you weren’t there.”
“You might have left it with anybody.”
“Excuse me!” the man retorted; “it’s against the regulations: I’ve got an official letter for you, and I can only give it to you yourself,” and he held out an envelope which Dollon tore open.
“Magistrates’ office?” he said enquiringly, as he glanced at the heading of the notepaper. “Who can be writing to me from the Law Courts?” He read the letter aloud:
“Sir: As time does not permit of a regular summons being sent to you by an usher of the court, I beg you to be so good as to come to Paris immediately, the day after tomorrow if possible, and attend at my office, where your depositions are absolutely required to conclude a case in which you are interested. Please bring, without exception, all the papers and documents entrusted to you by the Clerk of Assizes at Cahors, at the conclusion of the Langrune enquiry.”
“It is signed Germain Fuselier,” Dollon remarked. “I’ve often seen his name in the papers. He is a very well-known magistrate, and is employed in many criminal cases.” He read the letter through once more, and turned to the postman. “Will you take a glass of wine, Muller?”
“That’s a thing I never say ‘no’ to.”

===Chinese===

“我要去请法庭传唤居尔恩案的一位极有分量的关键证人。”
---
大雨淅淅沥沥下了一整天，直到刚刚几分钟前才终于渐次收歇。总管（steward）多隆（Dollon）把手探出窗外，发觉沉沉的灰色阴云下如今只零星飘落着几星雨点。
他是一位极其得力忠厚的老仆，在德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）遇害身亡几个月后，德·维布雷男爵夫人（Baronne de Vibray）便十分欣喜地雇用了他，并在自己在凯雷勒（Querelles）的庄园里拨了一栋乡间小屋供他居住。
他穿过房间，招呼起自己的儿子。
“雅克（Jacques），想跟我一道出去走走吗？我打算去河边看看水闸有没有妥当拉开。河堤本来就不大结实，照这般下雨法，早晚有一天会发大水把咱们给淹了。”
总管带着儿子穿过花园，走向界定德·维布雷男爵夫人庄园边界的那条溪流。
“瞧啊，爸爸，”雅克忽然喊道，“邮递员在冲咱们招手叫喊呢。”
邮递员是个脾气古怪但心肠极好的汉子，他急匆匆朝总管迎面小跑过来。
“多隆先生，您可真够折腾人的，”他抱怨道，“我今天早上去您府上送信，可您家里连个人影都没有。”
“你随便搁在谁手里不就结了嘛。”
“那可万万不成！”邮差反唇相驳道，“那可是违反邮政章程的：我手里有您的一封公函，按规矩我必须亲手交到您本人手上。”说着他递过一个大信封，多隆一把将信封撕开。
“法官预审法庭？”他扫了一眼信笺上方的抬头，满腹狐疑地嘀咕道，“司法宫里有谁会给我写信呢？”他高声诵读起信中的文字：
“先生：鉴于情势急迫，无暇由法庭执行吏按部就班向您送达正式传票，特恳请您尽快启程赶赴巴黎，如蒙方便务请于后天准时莅临敝人公署；在您切身相关的一桩重大要案定案之际，急需调取您的正式证词。请务必将朗格吕纳案初审终结时、卡奥尔重罪法庭书记官正式托付给您保管的全部卷宗与公文原件，悉数无一遗漏随身带至巴黎。”
“落款签名是热尔曼·菲泽利耶（Germain Fuselier），”多隆赞叹道，“我经常在报纸上见到这位大人的名头。他是一位名声显赫的大预审法官，经手办理过无数要案大案。”他又把信笺翻来覆去研读了一遍，转头望向邮差：“要不要来上一杯葡萄酒解解乏，米勒（Muller）？”
“敬酒这种美事，我打一辈子就从来没说过‘不’字。”

===Original===

“Well, go into the house with Jacques, and while he is attending to you I will write a reply telegram which you can take to the office for me.”
While the man was quenching his thirst Dollon wrote his reply:

> Will leave Verrières tomorrow evening by 7:20 train, arriving Paris 5 a.m. Wire appointment at your office to me at Hôtel Francs-Bourgeois, 152 rue du Bac.

He read the message over, signed it “Dollon” and considered.
“I wonder what they can want me for? Oh, if only they have found out something about the Langrune affair, how glad I shall be!”

===Chinese===

“那就同雅克一道进屋歇脚吧；趁着他招呼你的工夫，我正好写一封回电，劳驾你替我捎到电报局去发了。”
趁着邮差在屋里大口饮酒解渴的当口，多隆提笔写下了复电：

> 明晚乘7点20分客车由韦里埃尔（Verrières）动身，清晨5点抵巴黎。面见公署时辰敬请拍发至巴克街152号弗朗-布尔乔亚饭店。

他把电文从头到尾默读了一遍，签上“多隆”的大名，随后陷入了深思。
“真纳闷他们召我进京究竟所为何事？噢，只要他们当真查出了有关朗格吕纳案的实质线索，那我该有多快慰高兴啊！”

## XXIV Under Lock and Key / 第二十四章 铁窗之下
===Original===

After the preliminary examination as to his identity and so on, Gurn had been transferred to the Santé prison.
