At first the prisoner seemed to have terrible difficulty in accustoming himself to the rigours of confinement; he suffered from alternate paroxysms of rage and despair, but by sheer strength of character he fought these down. As a prisoner on remand he was entitled to the privilege of a separate cell, also during the first forty-eight hours he had been able to have his meals sent in from outside. Since then, however, his money had given out, and he was obliged to content himself with the ordinary prison dietary. But Gurn was not fastidious; this man whom Lady Beltham had singled out, or accepted, as her lover had often given proofs of an education and an intelligence above the average, yet now he appeared quite at ease in the atmosphere of a prison.
---
Gurn was walking quickly and alone round the exercise yard, when a breathless voice sounded in his ear.
“ ’Gad, Gurn, you know how to march! I was going to join you for a bit, but I could not keep up with you.”
Gurn turned and saw old Siegenthal, the warder in charge of his division, in whose custody he was particularly placed.
“My word!” the old fellow panted, “anybody could tell you had been in the infantry. Well, so have I; though that wasn’t yesterday, nor yet the day before; but we never marched as fast as you do. We made a fine march once though﻿—at Saint-Privat.”
Out of pity for the decent old fellow Gurn slackened his pace. He had heard the story of the battle of Saint-Privat a dozen times already, but he was quite willing to let Siegenthal tell it again. The warder, however, wandered to another point.
“By the way, I heard you were promoted sergeant out in the Transvaal: is that so?” and as Gurn nodded assent, he went on: “I never rose above the rank of corporal, but at any rate I have always led an honest life.” A sudden compassion for his prisoner seized the old man, and he laid a kindly hand on Gurn’s shoulder. “Is it really possible that an old soldier like you, who seem to be such a steady, serious, kind of man, can have committed such a crime?”
Gurn dropped his eyes and did not reply.
“I suppose there was a woman at the bottom of it?” Siegenthal said tentatively. “You acted on impulse, in a fit of jealousy, eh?”

===Chinese===

在对身份等事项进行初步预审验明之后，居尔恩（Gurn）被押送转送至桑泰监狱（Santé prison）。起初，囚徒似乎极难适应这冰冷严酷的囹圄幽闭；他时而暴跳如雷，时而陷入绝望深渊，交替发作痛苦不堪，但他全凭着极其坚毅顽强的意志硬生生压制住了心魔。作为一名未决在押犯，他享有独居单间牢房的特权；在入狱最初的四十八小时内，他甚至还能从狱外叫餐送入。然而自那以后，他囊中羞涩余钱耗尽，便不得不接受普通的牢饭配给。不过居尔恩并不挑剔苛求；这个被贝尔特姆夫人（Lady Beltham）相中青睐、接纳为秘密情人的男人，过往屡屡展现出超越常人的良好教养与过人智识，眼下却仿佛对监牢里的污浊氛围甘之如饴、安之若素。
---
居尔恩正孤身一人在放风天井里大步疾走，耳畔突然传来了一道气喘吁吁的嗓音。
“好家伙，居尔恩，你可真够能健步如飞的！我原本打算跟你结伴溜达一会儿，可连你的脚后跟都撵不上。”
居尔恩转过身，瞧见了老齐根塔尔（Siegenthal）——负责他所在监区的看守，居尔恩正是由他专门看管押解的。
“我凭良心说！”老头子喘着粗气说道，“明眼人一看就知道你当年是在步兵旅里混出来的。咳，我也当过步兵；虽说那既不是昨天也不是前天的事了；可我们当年行军打仗也绝赶不上你这么迅猛。不过有一回咱们倒是打出过一场漂亮的急行军——就在圣普里瓦（Saint-Privat）。”
出于对这个厚道老头的怜悯，居尔恩放缓了脚步。圣普里瓦战役的故事他早已听过不下十几遍，但他倒十分乐意由着齐根塔尔再讲上一遍。然而看守的话头却冷不丁拐到了别处。
“对了，我听说你当年在德兰士瓦被提拔成了中士，确有其事吗？”见居尔恩点头默认，他又接着说道：“我当年打仗最高也不过混了个下士，但好歹我这辈子向来堂堂正正做人。”一股突如其来的同情之意涌上了老看守的心头，他亲切地把手搭在居尔恩的肩头。“像你这样一个受过戎马历练的老兵，平日瞧着又是这么个沉稳、本分踏实的人，怎么可能当真犯下这等惊天重罪呢？”
居尔恩垂下眼帘，默不作声。
“我猜归根结底多半脱不开女人的纠葛吧？”齐根塔尔试探性地探询道，“你是一时冲动行事，在嫉火中烧的当口下了毒手，对不对？”

===Original===

“No,” Gurn answered with sudden bluntness, “I may as well own up that I did it in anger, because I wanted money﻿—for the sake of robbery.”
“I’m sorry,” said the old warder simply. “You must have been desperately hard up.”
“No I wasn’t.”
Siegenthal stared at his prisoner. The man must be utterly callous to talk like that, he thought. Then a clock struck and the warder gave a curt order.
“Time, Gurn! We must go back,” and he conducted the unresisting prisoner up the three flights of stairs that led to the division in which his cell was. “By the way,” he remarked as they went, “I forgot to tell you that you and I have got to part.”
“Oh?” said Gurn. “Am I to be transferred to another prison?”
“No, it’s I who am going. Just fancy, I have been appointed head warder at Poissy; I go on leave tonight, and take up my new post in a week.” Both halted before the door of cell number 127. “In with you,” said Siegenthal, and when Gurn had obeyed he turned to go. Then he wheeled round again quickly, and put out his hand hurriedly, as if half afraid of being seen. “Put it there, Gurn,” he said; “no doubt you are a murderer and, as you have confessed yourself, a thief; but I can’t forget that if you had kept straight, you were the sergeant and I should have had to obey you. I’m sorry for you!” Gurn was touched and murmured a word of thanks. “That’s all right, that’s all right,” Siegenthal muttered, not attempting to hide his emotion; “let us hope that everything will turn out well,” and he left Gurn alone in the cell to his meditations.
Twice, Gurn reflected, relying on the sympathy which he knew he had evoked in the old warder’s heart despite the number of criminals who had passed through his hands, he had been on the point of broaching a serious and delicate matter to him; but he had not actually spoken, being deterred by some undefinable scruple, as well as half suspecting that his application would be made in vain. And now he was glad he had been so cautious, for even if the warder had been amenable, his approaching removal to another prison would have prevented the idea from coming to fruition.
---
A singsong voice echoed in the corridor.
“Number 127, you are wanted in the barristers’ room. Get ready,” and the next minute the door of the cell was thrown open, and a cheery-looking warder, with a strong Gascon accent, appeared.

===Chinese===

“不，”居尔恩冷不丁直截了当地打断道，“我倒不如索性痛痛快快招供：我是在狂怒之下下了死手，图的就是钱财——为了谋财劫掠。”
“真令人惋惜，”老看守朴素地叹息道，“你当时必定是穷困潦倒、走投无路了吧。”
“压根不是。”
齐根塔尔愕然盯着眼前的囚犯。他心下暗忖，能用这般轻描淡写的口吻谈及此事的男人，心肠必定早已冷硬麻木到了极点。此时钟声敲响，看守发出一道短促的指令。
“放风时辰到了，居尔恩！咱们该回去了。”他押解着毫不反抗的囚犯走上通往牢房监区的三层楼梯。“顺便提一句，”他在路上说道，“我忘了告诉你，你和我恐怕马上就得分道扬镳了。”
“噢？”居尔恩问，“我是要被押解转送其他监狱了吗？”
“不，是我要调走了。你保准想不到，我被升拔为普瓦西（Poissy）监狱的看守长了；我今晚就休假，一周后去新岗位履职。”两人在127号牢房门前停下了脚步。“进去吧，”齐根塔尔说，等居尔恩顺从迈入牢房后，他转身欲走。接着他又猛地扭过身来，急匆匆地伸出一只手，神态仿佛唯恐被旁人撞见一般。“握个手吧，居尔恩，”他说，“毫无疑问你是个杀人凶手，而且正如你亲口招认的那样，还是个窃贼；可我忘不了，如果你当年没走歪门邪道，你可是中士，我原本是得乖乖听从你调遣的。我真心为你感到惋惜！”居尔恩心中微动，低声念叨了一句感谢。“好啦，好啦，”齐根塔尔嘟囔着，毫不掩饰自己心头的动容，“但愿凡事最后都能有个好结果吧。”说罢他把居尔恩独自留在牢房中冥思沉想。
居尔恩暗自忖度，仗着自己在老看守心底激起的同情好感——尽管经这老汉之手的罪犯多如牛毛——他此前曾有两次险些忍不住要向对方试探托付一桩非同小可的隐秘要事；但他最终都没吐露半个字，一方面是被某种难以言喻的顾虑所阻拦，另一方面也吃不准对方会不会一口回绝。如今他暗自庆幸自己当初这般谨慎机警：因为即便老看守当真愿意通融配合，他眼看即将调离转任别处监狱的事实，也注定会使得整个密谋彻底化为泡影。
---
走廊里回荡起一阵抑扬顿挫的吆喝声：
“127号，律师会见室有人找你！赶紧准备！”紧接着，牢门被一把拉开，一名面容和善、操着浓重加斯科涅口音的看守赫然现身。

===Original===

Gurn had noticed him before: he was the second warder in this division, a man named Nibet, and no doubt he would be promoted to Siegenthal’s place when the chief warder left. Nibet looked curiously at Gurn, a certain sympathy in his quick brown eyes. “Ready, Gurn?”
Gurn growled an answer and pulled on his coat again. His counsel was Maître Barberoux, one of the foremost criminal barristers of the day; Gurn had thought it prudent to retain him for his defence, more especially as it would cost him nothing personally. But he had no particular desire to talk to him now; he had already told him everything he intended to tell him, and he had no intention of allowing the case to be boomed as a sensation; quite the reverse indeed: in his opinion, the flatter the case fell, the better it would be for his interests, though no doubt Maître Barberoux would not be of the same way of thinking.
But he said nothing, and merely walked in front of Nibet along the corridor towards the barristers’ room, the way to which he was already familiar with. On the way they passed some masons who were at work in the prison, and these men stopped to watch him pass, but contrary to Gurn’s apprehensions they did not seem to recognise him. He hoped it meant that the murder was already ceasing to be a nine days’ wonder for the public at large.
Nibet pushed Gurn into the barristers’ room, saying respectfully to the person in it already, “You only have to ring, sir, when you have finished,” and then withdrew, leaving Gurn in presence, not of his counsel as he had expected, but of that personage’s assistant, a young licentiate in law named Roger de Seras, who was also a most incredible dandy.
Roger de Seras greeted Gurn with an engaging smile and advanced as if to shake hands with him, but suddenly wondering whether that action might not suggest undue familiarity, he raised his hand to his own head instead and scratched it; the young fellow was still younger in his business, and did not rightly know whether it was etiquette for a barrister, or even a barrister’s junior, to shake hands with a prisoner who was implicated in a notorious murder.
Gurn felt inclined to laugh, and on the whole was glad that it was the junior whom he had to see; the futile verbosity of this very young licentiate might possibly be amusing.
Maître Roger de Seras began with civil apologies.

===Chinese===

居尔恩之前便留意过此人：他是该监区的二把手看守，名叫尼贝（Nibet）；看守长调离之后，无疑将由他接替齐根塔尔的肥缺。尼贝好奇地打量着居尔恩，敏锐的棕色眼眸中闪烁着几分同情。“准备好了吗，居尔恩？”
居尔恩低声哼唧着应了一声，重新套上了大衣。他的辩护律师是巴伯鲁律师（Maître Barberoux），当世最负盛名的刑事大律师之一；居尔恩先前觉得聘请此人为自己辩护乃明智之举，更何况这笔律师费根本用不着他自掏腰包。然而他眼下并无多少兴致与律师交谈；该交代的话他早已原原本本全交代过了，而且他压根不打算由着旁人把这桩案子炒作得满城风雨；恰恰相反，在他看来，这桩案子掀起的风浪越平淡无奇，对他切身的利益就越发有利，尽管巴伯鲁律师的想法无疑与他截然相悖。
但他什么也没多说，只是走在尼贝前头，顺着熟悉的走廊朝着律师会见室走去。半路上他们与几个正在监狱里干活的泥瓦匠擦肩而过，工人们停下手里的活计驻足目送他走过，但出乎居尔恩的忧虑，他们似乎并未将他认出来。他暗自期盼，这是否意味着这桩谋杀惨案对于市井普罗大众而言，热度已经开始降温冷却。
尼贝把居尔恩领进律师会见室，毕恭毕敬地对已经在里面候着的人说道：“先生，您谈完了只管拉铃唤我。”随后便退了出去。然而留下面对居尔恩的，却并非他料想中的大律师本人，而是那位大律师的助手——一个名叫罗歇·德·塞拉（Roger de Seras）的年轻法学士，同时也是个打扮得花里胡哨到荒唐地步的花花公子。
罗歇·德·塞拉满面堆笑地迎向居尔恩，摆出一副要同他握手的架势，但旋即又疑心此举是否显得过分亲狎失仪，手伸到半截硬生生抬起来挠了挠自己的脑门；这个小青年入行资历尚浅，全然摸不准按照律师行当的礼数规矩，一名律师——乃至一名见习初级律师——究竟该不该同一名卷入惊天凶案的命案嫌犯握手。
居尔恩心下直觉好笑，总的来说他倒暗自庆幸来见自己的是这个毛头小伙；这个初出茅庐小法学士那毫无营养的废话连篇，保不准还能替自己解解闷。
罗歇·德·塞拉律师客套寒暄着赔礼道歉起来。

===Original===

“You will excuse me if I only stay for a few minutes, but I am most frightfully busy; besides, two ladies are waiting for me outside in my carriage: I may say confidentially that they are actresses, old friends of mine, and, just fancy, they are most frightfully anxious to see you! That’s what it means to be famous, M. Gurn; eh, what?” Gurn nodded, not feeling unduly flattered. Roger de Seras continued. “Just to please them I have made any number of applications to the governor of the prison, but there was nothing doing, my dear chap; that beast of a magistrate, Fuselier, insists on your being kept in absolute seclusion. But none the less, I’ve got some news for you. I know heaps: why, my friends at the Law Courts call me ‘the peripatetic paragraph!’ Not bad, eh, what?” Gurn smiled and Roger de Seras was encouraged. “It’s given me no end of a boom, my leader acting for you, and my being able to come and see you whenever I like! Everybody asks me how you are, and what you are like, and what you say, and what you think. You can congratulate yourself on having caused a sensation in Paris.”
Gurn began to be irritated by all this chatter.
“I must confess I’m not the least interested in what people are saying about me. Is there anything new in my case?”
“Absolutely nothing that I am aware of,” Roger de Seras replied serenely, without stopping to think whether there was or not. “I say﻿—Lady Beltham﻿—”
“Yes?” said Gurn.
“Well, I know her very well, you know: I go out a frightful lot and I have often met her: a charming woman, Lady Beltham!”
Gurn really did not know how to treat the idiot. Never one to suffer fools gladly, he grew irritable and would almost certainly have said something that would have put the garrulous young bungler in his place, had not the latter suddenly remembered something, just as he was on the point of getting up to go.
“Oh, by the way,” he said with a laugh, “I was nearly forgetting the most important thing of all. Just fancy, that beast Juve, the marvellous detective whom the newspapers rave about, went to your place yesterday afternoon to make another official search!”
“Alone?” enquired Gurn, much interested.
“Quite alone. Now what do you suppose he found; the place has been ransacked dozens of times, you know; of course I mean something sensational in the way of a find. I bet you a thousand﻿—”
“I never bet,” Gurn snapped.

===Chinese===

“您多包涵，我只能在这儿耽搁上几分钟，手头的要紧差事实在忙得不可开交；再说外头的马车里还有两位女士在候着我呢。私下跟您透露一句，她们可全都是当红女戏子，我的昔日红颜老相好；您绝对想不到，她们俩做梦都急着想要亲眼瞻仰瞻仰您呢！这可就是名声大噪的滋味啊，居尔恩先生，可不是嘛？”居尔恩点了点头，心头未曾感到半分荣耀。罗歇·德·塞拉自顾自说了下去：“为了讨好她们，我向典狱长递交了不知多少回申请，可根本行不通啊，我亲爱的老兄；菲泽利耶（Fuselier）那个顽固不化的该死法官，死咬着非要把您绝对单独监禁、隔绝外界不可。不过话虽如此，我这儿可有大把内幕消息要透露给您。我手头的包打听灵通得很，要知道，司法宫（Law Courts）里的哥们儿都管我叫‘移动的花边新闻专栏’！这外号不赖吧，哈？”居尔恩挤出一丝微笑，罗歇·德·塞拉愈发得意洋洋。“我们大律师接下您的辩护大任，我又能随时随地名正言顺来探视您，这可给我脸上贴了天大的金！所有人都追着打听您的近况，问您长什么模样、说了什么话、脑子里想些什么。您尽可以为自己在全巴黎掀起的滔天轰动举杯庆贺了！”
居尔恩被这通连篇累牍的喋喋不休惹得烦躁起来。
“恕我直言，外界对我如何议论，我半点兴趣也没有。我的案子眼下有没有任何新实质进展？”
“据我所知压根没有，”罗歇·德·塞拉轻描淡写地回答，脑子里甚至压根没去细想究竟有没有，“对了——贝尔特姆夫人……”
“她怎么了？”居尔恩追问道。
“这个嘛，您要知道，我同她熟络得很：我平日社交应酬频繁，跟她碰过不少次面；迷人极了的一位贵妇，贝尔特姆夫人！”
居尔恩真不知道该拿眼前这个蠢货怎么办。他向来对愚蠢之徒缺乏耐心，心头怒火渐起，若不是那个年轻的冒失鬼在正要起身告辞时冷不丁想起了什么，居尔恩必定会甩出几句狠话让这个饶舌小辈下不来台。
“噢，对了，”他嘻嘻哈哈地笑道，“我差点把最要紧的天大正事给忘了。您保准猜不到，报纸上捧上天的那个叫朱夫的神探，昨天下午居然又跑到您的寓所进行了一次搜查！”
“独自一人去的？”居尔恩大感兴趣地探询道。
“完完全全单枪匹马。您猜他在那儿起获了什么？要知道那地方早被翻箱倒柜搜过了几十遍；我指的自然是一桩耸人听闻的重大发现。我敢跟您赌上一千法郎……”
“我从不赌博，”居尔恩没好气地厉声打断道。

===Original===

“Tell me at once what it was.”
The young fellow was proud of having caught the attention of his leader’s notorious client, if only for a moment; he paused and wagged his head, weighing each word to give them greater emphasis.
“He found an ordnance map in your bookcase, my dear chap﻿—an ordnance map with a bit torn out of it.”
“Oh! And what then?” said Gurn, a frown upon his face.
The young barrister did not notice the expression on the murderer’s countenance.
“Well, then it appears that Juve thought it was very important. Between you and me, my opinion is that Juve tries to be frightfully clever and succeeds in looking a fool. How, I ask you, can the discovery of that map affect your case or influence the decision of the jury? By the way, there is no need for you to worry about the result; I have had a frightful lot of experience in criminal cases, and so be assured you are all right: extenuating circumstances, you know. But﻿—oh, yes, there is one thing more I wanted to tell you. A fresh witness is going to be called at the examination; let me see, what’s his name? Dollon: that’s it: the steward, Dollon.”
“I don’t understand,” said Gurn; his head was bent and his eyes cast down.
A glimmer of light dawned in the young licentiate’s brain.
“Wait, there is some connection,” he said. “The steward, Dollon, is in the employment of a lady who calls herself the Baronne de Vibray. And the Baronne de Vibray is guardian to the young lady who was staying with Lady Beltham the day, or rather the night, when you﻿—you﻿—well, you know. And that young lady, Mlle. Thérèse Auvernois, was placed with Lady Beltham by M. Etienne Rambert. And M. Etienne Rambert is the father of the young man who murdered the Marquise de Langrune last year. I tell you all these things without attempting to draw any deductions from them, for, for my own part, I haven’t the least idea why the steward, Dollon, has been summoned in our case at all.”
“Nor have I,” said Gurn, and the frown on his brow was deeper.
Roger de Seras hunted all round the little room for his gloves and found them in his pocket.
“Well, my dear chap, I must leave you. We have been chatting for a whole half-hour, and those ladies are still waiting for me. What on earth will they say to me?”
He was about to ring for the warder when Gurn abruptly stayed him.

===Chinese===

“赶紧直说究竟是什么东西！”
年轻小伙为自己哪怕仅有一刻抓住了这位名震巴黎的杀人重犯的全部注意力而暗自得意；他故意顿了顿，摇头晃脑地一字一顿，以便让话语显得更有分量。
“他在您的书架上找到了一副军用测绘地图，我亲爱的老兄——一幅被人裁掉了一块的军用地图。”
“噢！那又怎么样？”居尔恩眉头紧锁。
年轻律师压根没察觉到凶手脸上的异样神情。
“得，看来朱夫觉得那玩意儿非同小可。私下里跟您说句掏心窝子的话，我倒觉得朱夫这纯粹是聪明反被聪明误，活脱脱像个跳梁小丑。我倒想问问，搜出这么张破地图到底怎么可能影响您的案情判决，又怎么可能左右陪审团的心意呢？顺带说一句，您对判决结果大可不必杞人忧天；我经手过的刑事大案海了去了，您放宽心保管没事：酌情从轻情节嘛，您懂的。不过……噢，对了，我还有一桩要紧事得向您通报。预审法官即将传唤一位新的关键证人出庭作证；让我想想，他叫什么名头来着？多隆：对，就是这名字，总管（steward）多隆（Dollon）。”
“我听不明白，”居尔恩低垂着头，目光死死盯着地面。
年轻法学士的脑海中冷不丁闪过一丝灵光。
“等等，这里头还真有些千丝万缕的干系，”他说，“这位多隆总管眼下正受雇于一位自封德·维布雷男爵夫人（Baronne de Vibray）的贵妇。而这位德·维布雷男爵夫人恰恰就是那位年轻姑娘的监护人——就是您……您……咳，您懂的，就是案发当晚寄宿在贝尔特姆夫人府上的那位年轻姑娘。而那位泰蕾兹·奥弗努瓦小姐（Mlle. Thérèse Auvernois），偏偏又是经埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生介绍安置给贝尔特姆夫人的。而埃蒂安·朗贝尔先生呢，正是去年杀害德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）的那个年轻凶犯的生父！我原原本本把这些线索摆在您眼前，倒不是想从中推导出什么高头讲章，因为坦白讲，对于多隆总管平白无故为何会被传唤到咱们这桩案子里来，我本人也是丈二和尚摸不着头脑。”
“我也同样摸不着头脑，”居尔恩说道，眉宇间的阴云愈发凝重。
罗歇·德·塞拉在逼仄的会见室里四下搜寻自己的手套，最后在口袋里摸了出来。
“好啦，我亲爱的老兄，我得告辞了。咱们不知不觉畅谈了整整半个钟头，那两位女士还在外头眼巴巴候着我呢。她们待会儿指不定怎么埋怨我呢！”
他正要伸手去拉召唤看守的门铃，居尔恩冷不丁出言拦住了他。

===Original===

“Tell me,” he said with a sudden air of interest, “when is that man coming﻿—what’s his name? Dollon?”
The young barrister was on the point of saying he did not know, when a brilliant recollection came into his mind.
“ ’Gad, how frightfully stupid I am! Why, I have a copy of the telegram he sent the magistrate in my portfolio here now.” He opened the portfolio and picked out a sheet of blue paper. “Here it is.”
Gurn took it from him and read:

> “Will leave Verrières tomorrow evening by 7:20 train, arriving Paris 5 a.m.﻿ ﻿…”

Gurn appeared to be sufficiently edified: at all events he paid no attention to the rest of the message. Lord Beltham’s murderer handed the document back to the barrister without a word.
A few minutes later Maître Roger de Seras had rejoined his lady friends, and the prisoner was once more in his cell.

===Chinese===

“告诉我，”他冷不丁流露出浓厚的兴致发问道，“那个人什么时候到——他叫什么来着？多隆？”
年轻律师刚要脱口说自己不知情，脑海中猛然划过一道闪光的记忆。
“见鬼，瞧我这记性有多该死！哎呀，他拍发给预审法官的电报抄件，眼下就正端端正正躺在我的公文包里呢！”他打开公文皮包，抽出一张蓝色的公文纸。“就在这儿。”
居尔恩从他手中接了过去，定睛细读：

> “明晚乘7点20分客车由韦里埃尔（Verrières）动身，清晨5点抵巴黎……”

居尔恩似乎已经彻底摸透了所需的信息；无论如何，他对电文余下的字句未曾多看一眼。谋害贝尔特姆勋爵的凶手一言不发，将公文纸递还给了律师。
几分钟后，罗歇·德·塞拉律师重返马车去会合他的女伴，而囚徒则重新被关回了冰冷的牢房。

## XXV An Unexpected Accomplice / 第二十五章 意想不到的同谋
===Original===

Gurn was walking nervously up and down in his cell after this interview, when the door was pushed open and the cheery face of the warder Nibet looked in.
“Evening, Gurn,” he said; “it’s six o’clock, and the restaurant-keeper opposite wants to know if he is to send your dinner in to you.”
“No,” Gurn growled. “I’ll have the prison ordinary.”
“Oh﻿—ho!” said the warder; “funds low, eh? Of course, it’s not for you to despise our dietary, but still, Government beans﻿—” He came further into the cell, ignoring Gurn’s impatient preference for his room to his company, and said in a low tone: “There, take that,” and thrust a banknote into the hand of the dumbfounded prisoner. “And if you want any more, they will be forthcoming,” he added. He made a sign to Gurn to say nothing, and went to the door. “I’ll be back in a few minutes: I’ll just go and order a decent dinner for you.”
Gurn felt as if a tremendous weight had been lifted from him; the cell seemed larger, the prison walls less high; he had an intuition that Lady Beltham was not deserting him. He had never doubted the sincerity of her feelings for him, but he quite realised how a woman in her delicate position might feel embarrassed in trying to intervene in favour of any prisoner, and much more so in the case of the one whom the entire world believed to be the single-handed murderer of her husband. But now Lady Beltham had intervened. She had succeeded in communicating with him through the medium of this warder. And almost certainly she would do much more yet.
---
The door opened again, and the warder entered, carrying a long rush basket containing several dishes and a bottle of wine.
“Well, Gurn, that’s a more agreeable sort of dinner, eh?”
“Gad, I wanted it after all,” said the murderer with a smile. “It was a good idea of yours, M. Nibet, to insist on my getting my dinner sent in from outside.”
Nibet winked; he appreciated his prisoner’s tact; obviously he was not one to make untimely allusions to the warder’s breach of discipline in conveying money to him so simply, but so very irregularly.
As he ate Gurn chatted with Nibet.
“I suppose it is you who will get Siegenthal’s place?”
“Yes,” said Nibet, sipping the wine Gurn had offered him.

===Chinese===

这次会见结束之后，居尔恩（Gurn）神经紧绷地在牢房里来回踱步，牢门忽然被推开，看守尼贝探出了那张乐呵呵的面孔。
“晚上好，居尔恩，”他说，“已经六点钟了，对过餐馆的老板差人来问，今晚要不要把晚饭给您送进来。”
“不要，”居尔恩没好气地闷声回道，“我就吃监狱的公家牢饭。”
“哟嗬！”看守说，“手头紧了，是吧？当然啦，您不嫌弃咱们这儿的伙食固然好，可话又说回来，公家配给的豆子……”他无视居尔恩那一脸巴不得他赶紧走人的烦躁神色，径直朝牢房深处又迈进两步，压低嗓门轻声说道：“拿着，收下这个。”说着便把一张大面额钞票塞进了目瞪口呆的囚犯手心里。“要是还需要，后头管够，”他补充道。他朝居尔恩比划了个噤声的手势，便转身朝门口走去。“我几分钟后就回来，这就去替您张罗一顿像样的大餐。”
居尔恩顿觉心头一块巨石轰然落地；牢房仿佛瞬间宽敞了许多，高耸的监狱围墙也不再那般令人窒息；他直觉笃定贝尔特姆夫人（Lady Beltham）绝没有弃他不顾。他从未怀疑过夫人对他情意的真挚赤诚，但他深知以夫人这般微妙难堪的显赫处境，想要出面为一个囚犯斡旋周旋是何等尴尬凶险，更遑论此人还是全世界公认亲手残杀她丈夫的独门凶手。然而眼下，贝尔特姆夫人终究出手干预了。她设法买通了这名看守，成功与他接上了暗号。而且几乎可以断言，她后头要做的大手笔必定远不止于此。
---
牢门再次被推开，看守拎着一只装有几样热菜和一瓶葡萄酒的长条草编篮子走了进来。
“怎么样，居尔恩，这顿晚饭可惬意舒坦多了吧，哈？”
“天哪，我还真饿得前胸贴后背了，”凶手微笑着说，“非要坚持替我从外头餐馆叫餐送进来，尼贝先生，您这主意可真够高明的。”
尼贝眨了眨眼；他十分欣赏这位囚犯的识趣与老练：对方显然是个聪明人，绝不会不合时宜地拿看守严重违纪私下偷递钱财这种把柄来乱嚼舌根。
居尔恩一边狼吞虎咽，一边同尼贝闲聊起来。
“我想接替齐根塔尔缺位的人准是你了吧？”
“没错，”尼贝抿了一口居尔恩递过来的葡萄酒，喜滋滋地应道。

===Original===

“I have asked for the berth no end of times, but it never came; I was always told to wait because the place was not free, and another berth must be found first for Siegenthal, who was my senior. But the old beast would never make any application. However, three days ago, I was sent for to the Ministry, and one of the staff told me that someone in the Embassy, or the Government, or somewhere, was taking an interest in me, and they asked me a lot of questions and I told them all about it. And then, all of a sudden, Siegenthal was promoted to Poissy and I was given his billet here.”
Gurn nodded: he saw light.
“And what about the money?”
“That’s stranger still, but I understood all the same. A lady met me in the street the other night and spoke to me by name. We had a chat there on the pavement, for the street was empty, and she shoved some banknotes in my hand﻿—not just one or two, but a great bunch﻿—, and she told me that she was interested in me﻿—in you﻿—, and that if things turned out as she wished there were plenty more banknotes where those came from.”
While the warder was talking Gurn watched him carefully. The murderer was an experienced reader of character in faces, and he speedily realised that his lady’s choice had fallen on an excellent object. Thick lips, a narrow forehead, and prominent cheekbones suggested a material nature that would hesitate at nothing which would satisfy his carnal appetites, so Gurn decided that further circumlocution was so much waste of time, and that he might safely come to the point. He laid his hand familiarly on the warder’s shoulder.
“I’m getting sick of being here,” he remarked.
“I dare say,” the warder answered uneasily; “but you must be guided by reason; time is going on, and things arrange themselves.”
“They do when you help them,” Gurn said peremptorily; “and you and I are going to help them.”
“That remains to be seen,” said the warder.
“Of course, everything has got to be paid for,” Gurn went on. “One can’t expect a warder to risk his situation merely to help a prisoner to escape.” He smiled as the warder made an exclamation of nervous warning. “Don’t be frightened, Nibet. We’re not going to play any fool games, but let us talk seriously. Of course you have another appointment with the worthy lady who gave you that money?”

===Chinese===

“这个肥缺我向上头递过无数回申请，可次次都石沉大海；他们总让我耐心等着，说什么坑位没腾出来，得先替资格比我老的齐根塔尔物色好下家去处才行。可那个老东西死活就是不肯主动打报告请调。谁知三天前，司法部突然差人召我去问话，一位长官跟我说，使馆还是政府里头有某位通天的大人物格外赏识看重我；他们盘问了我一大堆底细，我也全都如实交代了。结果紧接着，齐根塔尔就冷不丁被擢升调去了普瓦西，而我则顺理成章接下了他在这个监区的肥差。”
居尔恩连连点头：他心如明镜。
“那这些现钱又是怎么回事呢？”
“那就更离奇邪乎了，不过我心里也明白了个七七八八。前天夜里有个贵妇在街上迎面叫出了我的名字。当时街上空无一人，我们就站在人行道边上聊了一阵，接着她把一大把厚厚的钞票硬塞进我手里——可不是一两张，而是结结实实的一大沓！她跟我说她很关照我……也很关照你；还说只要事情能遵照她的心意办成，后头想要多少这种大钞票就有多少！”
在看守眉飞色舞倾诉之际，居尔恩目不转睛地审视着他。这位杀人凶手向来是个阅人无数的面相行家，他一眼就看出自己的情人这回挑中的是个绝佳的突破口：厚嘴唇、窄额头、高颧骨，活脱脱是一副贪婪短视、只要能满足口腹物欲便不惜铤而走险的市侩嘴脸。于是居尔恩断定再兜圈子纯属浪费工夫，完全可以直接开门见山切入正题。他亲热随和地把手搭在看守的肩膀上。
“老待在这鬼地方，我可真有些腻味了，”他轻描淡写地说道。
“我想也是，”看守局促不安地应道，“可您凡事总得讲究理智沉着啊；日子一天天过，事情早晚会水到渠成的。”
“只有主动推一把，事情才能水到渠成，”居尔恩以不容置疑的口吻打断道，“而你和我，正好来推它一把。”
“那可得另当别论了，”看守心虚地嘀咕道。
“当然啦，凡事都是有代价的，”居尔恩接着说道，“谁也不能指望一个狱警平白无故押上自己的公职饭碗，单凭义气去帮一个死囚越狱。”见看守惊骇失色地发出倒吸凉气的噤声警告，居尔恩微微一笑：“别怕，尼贝。咱们绝不玩那些蠢把戏，咱们敞开天窗说亮话。那位赏给你大把钞票的尊贵妇人，毫无疑问跟你另有下一次密会之约吧？”

===Original===

“I am to meet her tonight at eleven, in the boulevard Arago,” Nibet said, after a moment’s hesitation.
“Good,” said Gurn. “Well, you are to tell her that I must have ten thousand francs.”
“What?” exclaimed the man, in utter astonishment, but his eyes shone with greed.
“Ten thousand francs,” Gurn repeated calmly, “and by tomorrow morning. Fifteen hundred of those are for you; I will go away tomorrow evening.”
There was a tense silence; the warder seemed doubtful, and Gurn turned the whole of his will power upon him to persuade him.
“Suppose they suspect me?” said Nibet.
“Idiot!” Gurn retorted; “all you will do will be to make a slip in your duty: I don’t want you to be an accomplice. Listen: there will be another five thousand francs for you, and if things turn out awkwardly for you, all you will have to do will be to go across to England, and live there comfortably for the rest of your days.”
The warder was obviously almost ready to comply.
“Who will guarantee me?” he asked.
“The lady, I tell you﻿—the lady of the boulevard Arago. Here, give her this,” and he tore a leaf out of his pocketbook and, scribbling a few words on it, handed it to Nibet.
“Well,” said the warder hesitatingly: “I don’t say ‘no.’ ”
“You’ve got to say ‘yes,’ ” Gurn retorted.
The two looked steadily in each other’s eyes; then the warder blenched.
“Yes,” he said.
Nibet was going away, and was already almost in the corridor when Gurn calmly called him back.
“You will evolve a plan, and I will start tomorrow. Don’t forget to bring me a timetable; the Orléans Company timetable will do.”
---
The murderer was not disappointed in his expectations. The next morning Nibet appeared with a mysterious face and eager eyes. He took a small bundle from underneath his jersey and gave it to Gurn.
“Hide that in your bed,” he said, and Gurn obeyed.
The morning passed without further developments; numerous warders came and went in the corridor, attending to the prisoners, and Gurn could get no private talk with Nibet, who contrived, however, to come into his cell several times on various pretexts and assure him with a nod or a word that all was going well. But presently, when walking in the exercise yard, the two men were able to have a conversation.

===Chinese===

“今晚十一点我得去阿拉戈大道（boulevard Arago）同她碰头，”尼贝迟疑了片刻后老实招认道。
“很好，”居尔恩说，“听着，你转告她，我需要一万法郎现钱。”
“多少？！”看守惊愕得目瞪口呆，双眼却贪婪地放着精光。
“一万法郎，”居尔恩平静地重复道，“而且必须在明天清晨前备妥。其中一千五百法郎归你；我明晚就动身离开。”
空气陷入了一阵令人窒息的死寂；看守显得犹疑不决，居尔恩倾注起全身的威势与意志死死压制游说他。
“万一他们怀疑到我头上怎么办？”尼贝问。
“蠢货！”居尔恩叱责道，“你要做的不过是值勤时打个盹犯个小疏忽罢了；我压根不需要你抛头露面当同谋。听好了：事后还会额外赏你五千法郎；要是势头不妙对你极为不利，你大可脚底抹油渡海逃往英格兰，下半辈子在异国他乡舒舒服服安享富贵！”
看守显然已经被彻底击中心防，几乎就要点头依允了。
“谁来替我担保？”他追问道。
“那位贵妇，我不是挑明告诉你了嘛——就是阿拉戈大道的那位夫人！拿着，把这个带给她。”说着，他从怀中的记事本上撕下一页纸，在上面飞快地涂抹了几个字，递到了尼贝手中。
“好吧，”看守吞吞吐吐地表态道，“我也没说‘不行’……”
“你必须说‘行’！”居尔恩厉声截断。
两人目光死死对视着；最终看守败下阵来，目光瑟缩退却。
“行，”他说。
尼贝转身欲走，大半个身子已经迈入走廊，居尔恩冷不丁不动声色地将他唤了回来。
“拿出一个周密的逃跑方案来，我明晚就动身。别忘了顺便替我捎一本火车时刻表来；奥尔良铁路公司的时刻表就行。”
---
凶手的如意算盘没有落空。次日清晨，尼贝神色神秘、两眼放光地现身了。他从贴身毛衣底下抽出一小包包裹，悄悄递到了居尔恩手中。
“把它藏在你的床铺里，”他说，居尔恩依言照办。
整个上午风平浪静，走廊里众多看守来来往往巡视照看犯人，居尔恩根本找不到机会同尼贝私下交谈；不过尼贝倒是借着各种名目几度踏进他的牢房，用一个眼神或一两句耳语向他担保一切进展顺遂。直到随后在放风天井散步的空当，两人终于得以再次促膝密谈。

===Original===

Nibet manifested an intelligence of which his outer appearance gave no indication; but it seems to be an established fact that the inventive faculties, even of men of inferior mental quality, are sharpened when they are engaged in mischief.
“For the last three weeks,” he said, “about a score of masons have been working in the prison, repairing the roof and doing up some of the cells. Cell number 129, the one next yours, is empty, and there are no bars on the window; the masons go through that cell and that window to get on to the roof. They knock off work soon after six o’clock. The gatekeeper knows them all, but he does not always look closely at their faces when they go by, and you might perhaps be able to go out with them.
“In the bundle that I gave you there is a pair of workman’s trousers, and a waistcoat and a felt hat; put those on. At about a quarter to six, the men who went up on to the roof through the cell, come down by way of the skylights to the staircase that leads to the clerk’s office, pass the office, where they are asked no questions, cross the two yards and go out by the main gate. I will open the door of your cell a few minutes before six, and you must go into the empty cell next yours, slip up on to the roof and take care to hide behind the chimney stacks until the men have done work. Let them go down in front of you, and follow behind with a pick or a shovel on your shoulder, and when you are passing the clerk, or anywhere where you might be observed, mind you let the men go a yard or two in front of you. When the gate is just being shut after the last workman, call out quietly, but as naturally as you can, ‘Hold on, M. Morin; mind you don’t lock me in; I’m not one of your lodgers; let me out after my mates.’ Make some joke of that sort, and when you are once outside the gate, by George, my boy, you’ll have to vamoose!”
Gurn listened attentively to the warder’s instructions. Lady Beltham must, indeed, have been generous and have made the man perfectly easy on the score of his own future.
“In one of the pockets of the clothes,” Nibet went on, “I have put ten hundred-franc notes; you asked for more, but I could not raise it: we can settle that some other time.”
Gurn made no comment.
“When will my escape be discovered?” he asked.
“I am on night duty,” the warder answered.

===Chinese===

尼贝展现出了一种与其粗鄙外貌毫不相称的狡黠智谋；然而事实似乎一再证明，即便智识平庸之辈，一旦投身作奸犯科的勾当，其钻营算计的创造天赋也会陡然变得极其敏锐。
“过去这三个星期，”他说，“大约有二十来个泥瓦匠一直在监狱里干活，修缮屋顶并粉刷翻新部分牢房。你隔壁的129号牢房眼下空着，而且窗户上没有装铁栅栏；工人们正是穿过那间牢房顺着窗户爬上屋顶去干活的。他们每天六点刚过就收工下班。大门守卫对他们面孔虽然熟络，但他们列队经过时守卫并不总会长着眼睛细瞧，你或许完全可以混在他们当中大摇大摆走出去。
“我刚才递给你的包裹里有一条工装裤、一件背心和一顶呢帽；你把它们换上。到了大约差一刻六点的时候，那帮顺着牢房爬上屋顶的工人就会顺着天窗下到通往书记官办公室的楼梯，穿过办公室——在那儿谁也不会盘问他们——然后穿过两个天井院子，由正大门鱼贯而出。我会在六点差几分钟的时候打开你的牢门，你立刻溜进隔壁的空牢房，顺着窗户翻上屋顶，千万注意躲在烟囱后面藏好，直到工人们彻底收工。由着他们走在前头下楼，你扛着一把十字镐或铁锹跟在队尾；当路过书记官办公室或任何容易引人注目的关卡时，务必注意跟前头的工人拉开一两码的距离。当正大门在最后一名工人迈出去后正要关拢合上的当口，你压低嗓门、用尽量自然的语气放声吆喝：‘等等，莫兰先生（M. Morin），千万别把我反锁在里头啊；我可不是你们这儿包食宿的常客；快放我出去赶上我工友！’就开上这么句玩笑，等一旦跨出大门，好家伙，我的老兄，你就得拼出吃奶的力气脚底抹油彻底远走高飞了！”
居尔恩凝神细听着看守的这番布置。贝尔特姆夫人必定出了极其惊人的重赏，彻底打消了这个狱警对自身后半生前途的后顾之忧。
“在那些衣服的其中一个口袋里，”尼贝接着耳语道，“我塞了十张百元大钞；你原本狮子大开口要得更多，但我手头实在倒腾不出那么多现钱了，咱们往后再慢慢结清。”
居尔恩对此未作置评。
“我的越狱什么时候会被发觉？”他探问道。
“今晚轮到我值夜班，”看守回答道。

===Original===

“Arrange your clothes on your bed to make it look as if you were in bed, and then they will think I might have been deceived. I go off duty at five; the next round is at eight. My mate will open the door of the cage, and by that time you will be miles away.”
Gurn nodded comprehension. Time did not permit of longer conversation. The bell had rung some minutes ago, proclaiming that the exercise time was over. The two men hurried upstairs to cell number 127 on the third floor, and the prisoner was locked in alone, while Nibet went about his duty as usual.

===Chinese===

“你把换下来的衣物在床铺上摆弄好，伪装成有人蜷缩在被窝里睡觉的模样，这样一来别人就会以为连我也被你的障眼法给蒙蔽了过去。我清晨五点交接下班；下一次查房巡视要等到八点钟。到时候我的同僚会打开铁笼门，而到那个节骨眼上，你早就逃到几英里开外了。”
居尔恩点头表示心领神会。当时的局势已不容许他们继续多言。宣告放风结束的钟声早在几分钟前就敲响了。两人匆匆步上楼梯，来到三楼的127号牢房门前，囚徒被独自反锁在铁门之后，尼贝则神色自若地继续履行着他的日常巡警之责。

## XXVI A Mysterious Crime / 第二十六章 离奇命案
===Original===

Arriving in good time at the little station at Verrières, where he was about to take a train to Paris to keep his appointment at the Law Courts, the old steward Dollon gave his parting instructions to his two children, who had come to see him off.
“I must, of course, call upon Mme. de Vibray,” he said, “and I don’t yet know what time M. Fuselier wants to see me at his office. Anyhow, if I don’t come back tomorrow, I will the next day, without fail. Well, little ones, I’m just off now, so say goodbye and get home as fast as you can. It looks to me as if there was going to be a storm, and I should like to know that you were safe at home.”
With heavy creaking of iron wheels, and hoarse blowing off of steam from the engine, the Paris train drew into the station. The steward gave a final kiss to his little son and daughter and got into a second-class carriage.
---
In a neighbouring village a clock had just struck three.
The storm had been raging since early in the evening, but now it seemed informed with a fresh fury: the rain was lashing down more fiercely, and the wind was blowing harder still, making the slender poplars along the railway line bow and bend before the squalls and assume the most fantastic shapes, but vaguely shown against the night. The night was inky black. The keenest eye could make out nothing at all distinctly, even at the distance of a few yards: the darkness was so dense as to seem absolutely solid.
Nevertheless, along the railway embankment, a man was making his way with steady step, seeming not a whit disturbed by the tragic horror of the storm.
He was a man of about thirty, rather well dressed in a large waterproof coat, the collar of which, turned up to his ears, hid the lower part of his face, and a big felt hat with brim turned down protecting him fairly well from the worst of the weather. The man fought his way against the wind, which drove into his overcoat with such force that sometimes it almost stopped his progress, and he trod the stony track without paying heed to the sorry plight into which it would most surely put the thin boots he was wearing.
“Awful weather!” he growled: “I don’t remember such a shocking night for years: wind, rain, every conceivable thing! But I mustn’t grumble, for the total absence of moon will be uncommonly useful for my purpose.”

===Chinese===

老总管（steward）多隆（Dollon）早早赶到了韦里埃尔（Verrières）的小火车站；他准备在此搭乘列车前往巴黎，前往司法宫（Law Courts）赴约。临行前，他向赶来为他送行的两个孩子交代着最后的嘱托。
“到了巴黎，我自然得先去拜谒德·维布雷夫人（Mme. de Vibray），”他说，“眼下我还吃不准菲泽利耶先生（M. Fuselier）究竟定在何时在公署见我。无论如何，如果我明天赶不回来，后天也必定准时返家。好啦，小宝贝们，火车马上就要开了，快同爸爸道别，早点赶回家去吧。我看这天色八成是要起大风暴了，我得确信你们平平安安待在家里才能踏实。”
伴随着铁轮沉重的尖锐摩擦声和火车头喷涌而出的嘶哑蒸汽轰鸣，开往巴黎的列车缓缓滑入站台。老总管俯身给了小儿子和小女儿最后的亲吻，随即跨上了一节二等车厢。
---
在邻近的一座村落里，教堂的座钟刚刚敲响了三点。
狂风暴雨从傍晚起便肆虐咆哮，而此刻仿佛被注入了一股崭新的凶性：暴雨愈发狂暴地倾盆抽打，狂风愈发凶猛地呼啸撕扯，吹得铁路沿线那一排排纤细的白杨树在狂飙中剧烈摇曳弯折，在茫茫夜色中扭曲成一具具光怪陆离的诡异剪影。夜色黑得如浓墨重彩。哪怕是最锐利的眼眸，在短短几码开外也根本辨认不出任何轮廓：无边无际的黑暗浓稠得宛如一块坚实凝固的铁板。
然而，沿着铁路的路基路堤，一个男人正迈着沉稳从容的步伐逆风而行，丝毫不为这场凶险可怖的暴风雨所动摇。
这是个三十岁上下的男子，穿着颇为讲究，套着一件宽大的防水胶布雨衣；翻立起来的领口一直遮到了耳朵，将他的下半截面孔严严实实遮挡在内；头上扣着一顶压低帽檐的宽边呢帽，将恶劣的风雨相当周全地挡在外面。男人顶风前行，呼啸的狂风死死灌入他的大衣，有好几次险些逼得他寸步难行；他踏在碎石嶙峋的道碴轨道上，全然不顾这粗砺的碎石将把自己脚上那双单薄考究的皮靴磨损得何等狼狈不堪。
“该死的天气！”他低声咕哝着咒骂道，“我都记不清多少年没碰上过这般骇人的鬼天气了：暴风、骤雨，老天爷算是把所有恶劣的玩意儿全倾倒下来了！不过我可没资格抱怨，没有一丝月光的黑夜，倒恰恰成了能助我一臂之力的天赐良机。”

===Original===

A flash of lightning streaked the horizon, and the man stopped and looked quickly about him. “I can’t be far from the place,” he thought, and again went on his way. Presently he heaved a sigh of relief. “Here I am at last.”
At this spot the line was completely enclosed between two high slopes, or ran at the bottom of a deep cutting.
“It’s better here,” the man said to himself; “the wind passes well above my head, and the cutting gives good shelter.” He stopped and carefully deposited on the ground a rather bulky bundle he had been carrying under his arm; then he began to pace up and down, stamping his feet in an effort to keep warm. “It has just struck three,” he muttered. “From the timetable I can’t expect anything for another ten minutes. Well, better too soon than too late!” He contemplated the bundle which he had laid down a few minutes before. “It’s heavier than I thought, and deucedly in the way. But it was absolutely necessary to bring it. And down here in this cutting, there is nothing for me to be anxious about: the grass is thick, so I can run, and the line is so straight that I shall see the lights of the train a long way off.” A thin smile curled his lips. “Who would have thought, when I was in America, that I should ever find it so useful to have learnt how to jump a train?”
A dull sound in the distance caught his ear. In a second he had sprung to his bundle, picked it up, and, choosing a spot on the ballast, crouched down listening. At the place where he stood the line ran up a steep acclivity. It was from the lower end of this that the noise he had heard proceeded, and now was growing louder, almost deafening. It was the heavy, regular puffing of a powerful engine coming up a steep gradient, under full steam.
“No mistake: my star is with me!” the man muttered, and as the train approached he stretched his muscles and, taking a firmer grip of his bundle, he bent forward in the stooping attitude that runners take when about to start off in a race.
With a heavy roar, and enveloped in clouds of steam, the train came up to where he was, travelling slowly because of the steep gradient, certainly less than twenty miles an hour. The moment the engine had passed him, the man started off, lithe as a cat, and ran at the top of his speed.

===Chinese===

一道耀眼的闪电划破天际，男人猛地驻足，警惕地四下张望。“离那个地方应该不远了，”他心下暗忖，随即继续拔腿前行。不多时，他如释重负地长吁了一口气。“总算是赶到了。”
在这个地段，铁路线完全被两侧高耸的山坡所夹峙，沿着一条深凿的路堑底部蜿蜒穿行。
“这儿舒坦多了，”男人自言自语道，“狂风全从我头顶呼啸掠过，这道堑壕真是个天然的避风港。”他停下脚步，小心翼翼地把夹在腋下的一只相当沉甸甸的包裹轻放在地上；随后他在堑壕里来回踱步，使劲跺着脚取暖。“刚才刚敲过三点，”他喃喃自语，“按照时刻表，再过十分钟列车才开得过来。咳，宁早勿晚，赶早总比误点强！”他凝视着几分钟前搁在脚边的那个包裹。“这玩意儿比我料想的还要沉，碍手碍脚麻烦透顶。可不带上它又万万不行。而在底下这道堑壕里，我完全没啥好担心的：两旁荒草丛生踩上去悄无声息便于奔跑，而且铁路线笔直笔挺，我在老远就能瞧见火车的车灯。”一抹冷酷的微笑在他嘴角微微泛起。“谁能料得到呢，当年我在美洲闯荡时学会的扒火车的下三滥勾当，如今居然能派上这等天大的用场！”
远处一阵沉闷单调的轰响隐隐钻入他的耳廓。眨眼之间，他一把抄起包裹紧抱在怀，在道碴上择了一处隐蔽角落，弓下身子屏息凝神倾听。在他立足之处，铁路线正攀爬上一道极其陡峭的长坡道。他听到的声响正是从陡坡下端滚滚传来，而且此刻正以震耳欲聋的势头节节逼近：那是一台大马力蒸汽机车在全速开足马力、奋力冲爬陡峭坡道时喷涌而出的沉重而富有节奏的喘息咆哮。
“毫无疑问：幸运之星果然在庇佑着我！”男人喃喃自语。随着列车呼啸逼近，他浑身肌肉紧绷，将怀中的包裹抱得更紧，身体前倾，摆出了赛跑运动员起跑冲刺时的预备姿态。
伴随着低沉沉的轰隆巨响，列车裹挟在一团团白色蒸汽烟雾之中咆哮而至；因坡道陡峭吃力，车速显得颇为迟缓，时速绝计不超过二十英里。机车头刚一从他眼前呼啸掠过，男人便如同一只矫健机敏的狸猫，猛地蹬地蹿出，开足马力拼死狂奔起来。

===Original===

The train, of course, gained upon him; the tender, luggage vans, and third-class carriages passed him, and a second-class carriage was just coming up with him. The pace alone would have deprived almost anyone else of power of thought, but this man was evidently a first-rate athlete, for the moment he caught sight of the second-class carriage he took his decision. With a tremendous effort he caught hold of the handrail and sprang upon the footboard, where, with extraordinary skill, he contrived to remain.
Reaching the summit of the slope, the train gathered speed, and with an even louder roar began its headlong journey through the darkness and the storm, which seemed to increase in intensity with every passing minute.
For a few seconds the man hung on where he was. Then, when he had regained his breath, he got on to the upper step and listened at the door of the corridor at which he found himself. “No one there,” he muttered. “Besides, everyone will be asleep,” and, chancing everything, he rose up, opened the door, and stepped into the second-class carriage with a grunt of relief.
Making no attempt to conceal himself, he walked boldly into the lavatory and washed his face that was blackened with the smoke from outside, and then, in the most leisurely, natural way possible, he came out of the lavatory and walked along the corridor, soliloquising aloud, manifestly not minding whether he were overheard.
“It’s positively maddening! No one can sleep, with travelling companions like that!”
As he spoke he went along the corridor, rapidly glancing into every compartment. In one, three men were asleep, obviously unaware that anyone was surveying them from outside. The door of the compartment was ajar, and the stranger noiselessly stepped within. The fourth corner was unoccupied, and here the man took his seat, laying his bundle down beside him, and feigning sleep. He waited, motionless, for a good quarter of an hour, until he was quite satisfied that his companions were really sleeping soundly, then he slid his hand into the bundle by his side, seemed to be doing something inside it, then withdrew his hand noiselessly, stepped out of the compartment, and carefully closed the door.
In the corridor he drew a sigh of relieved satisfaction, and took a cigar from his pocket.
“Everything is going splendidly,” he said to himself.

===Chinese===

列车的速度自然逐渐超过了他；煤水车、行李车厢以及一节节三等车厢相继从他身侧滑过，紧接着一节二等车厢正好迎头追平了他。单是这风驰电掣的速度就足以令寻常人吓得魂飞魄散失去理智，但这名男子显然是个身手矫健的一等一好手：一眼瞥见那节二等车厢的瞬间，他便当机立断。伴随着孤注一掷的猛力一跃，他的双手死死抠住了车门扶手，整个人凌空跃上了车厢外侧的踏脚板，并凭着惊人的平衡绝技，稳稳贴伏固定在上面。
攀爬至坡道顶端之后，列车陡然开始加速，伴随着愈发震耳的轰鸣巨响，在无边的黑夜与暴风雨中风驰电掣般疾驰向前——而窗外的暴风雨似乎分分秒秒都在变得愈发狂暴。
男子紧贴在车外踏板上咬牙坚持了几秒钟。随后，待喘匀了气息，他挪步登上最高一级阶梯，把耳朵贴在跟前的车厢过道门上凝神细听。“里头没人，”他低声咕哝道，“再说，这会儿所有人都该睡得像死猪一样了。”他把心一横孤注一掷，霍然直起身，拧开车门，轻手轻脚跨进了这节二等车厢，如释重负地舒了口粗气。
他丝毫没有鬼祟躲藏的意思，而是大大方方径直步入盥洗室，将脸上被外头浓烟熏黑的污迹清洗得干干净净；接着，他用一副悠闲自得、再自然不过的做派迈出盥洗室，踱步走在走廊里，嘴里故意大声自言自语，显然丝毫不介意被旁人听见：
“简直要把人活活逼疯！摊上这么一帮同车旅伴，谁他妈睡得着觉啊！”
他一边骂骂咧咧，一边顺着走廊缓步前行，目光迅疾地扫过每一间包厢。在一间包厢里，三名男子正酣然大睡，显然压根没有察觉到外头有人在窥探他们。这间包厢的门正好虚掩着一道缝隙，陌生人悄无声息地溜了进去。第四个角落里的座位空着，男人便顺理成章在那儿坐了下来，把包裹搁在身侧，阖上双眼假装熟睡。他一动不动地干等了足足一刻钟，直到百分之百确信身旁的同伴确实个个沉睡如泥，这才把一只手悄悄探进身旁的包裹里，在里头暗中鼓捣摆弄了一阵，接着无声无息地抽出手，轻手轻脚迈出包厢，小心翼翼地将包厢门严丝合缝带上。
来到走廊里，他心满意足地舒了口长气，从口袋里摸出一支雪茄。
“一切进展得再顺利不过了，”他暗自窃喜道。

===Original===

“I was cursing this awful storm just now, but it is wonderfully useful to me. On such a night as this no one would dream of opening the windows.” He strolled up and down, holding on to the handrail with one hand to maintain himself against the rocking of the train, and every now and then taking out his watch with the other to see the time. “I haven’t any too much time,” he muttered. “I shall have to be quick, or my friend will miss his train!” He smiled, as if amused at the idea, and then, holding his cigar away from him so as not to inhale the smoke, he drew several deep breaths. “There is a faint smell,” he said, “but you would have to be told of it to detect it. The devil of it is that it so often causes nightmare; that would be awful!” He suspended his patrol and listened again. There was no sound to be heard from within the compartments except the snoring of a few travellers and the monotonous, rhythmical noise of the wheels passing over the joints of the rails. “Come: I’ve waited twenty minutes; it would be risky to wait longer; let’s get to work!”
He stepped briskly back into the compartment, and furtively glancing into the corridor to make sure that no one was there, he went across to the opposite window and opened it wide. He put his head out into the air for a minute or two, and then turned to examine his travelling companions. All three were still sound asleep.
The man gave vent to a dry chuckle. He drew his bundle towards him, felt until he found something within it, and flung it back on to the seat. Then he walked up to the man opposite him, slipped his hand inside his coat and abstracted a pocketbook and began to examine the papers it contained. “Ah!” he exclaimed suddenly; “that was what I was afraid of!” and taking one of the papers he put it inside his own pocketbook, chose one from his own and put it into the other man’s pocketbook, and then, having effected this exchange, replaced the man’s property and chuckled again. “You do sleep!”
And indeed, although the pickpocket took no particular precaution, the man continued to sleep soundly, as did the other two men in the compartment.
The thief looked once more at his watch.
“Time!”
He leaned out of the open window and slipped back the safety catch.

===Chinese===

“刚才我还一个劲咒骂这场鬼风暴，可眼下它倒帮了我天大的忙。在这样的鬼天气里，谁也绝想不到去打开车窗通气。”他一手抓着过道扶手稳住随着列车颠簸晃动的身形，在走廊里来回踱步，另一只手时不时掏出怀表核对时辰。“留给我的充裕时间可不多了，”他低声咕哝道，“我得抓紧麻利点，否则我那位老朋友可就要误了他的下趟车喽！”他冷笑着，仿佛被这个恶毒的念头逗乐了；接着他把雪茄拿得远远的以防吸入烟气，深吸了几口气。“空气里有一股淡淡的气味，”他说，“但除非有人事先提醒，否则寻常人根本察觉不出来。最要命的麻烦在于这玩意儿极容易诱发恶梦；万一闹腾醒了可就糟透了！”他停下踱步，再次贴门侧耳细听。各个包厢里除了几声沉闷的鼾声以及车轮碾过钢轨接头处那单调富有韵律的哐当声外，再无半点动静。“行了：我已经足足等了二十分钟；再等下去风险未免太大，该干正事了！”
他健步重新迈入包厢，警惕地朝走廊两侧飞快瞥了一眼，确信空无一人之后，径直走到对侧窗前，将车窗猛地一把推开到底。他把头探出窗外吹了一两分钟冷风，随后转过身仔细审视着包厢里的三名同伴。三人依然沉沉酣睡毫无知觉。
男人喉咙里发出了一声干瘪刺耳的冷笑。他把包裹拉到跟前，伸手探入摸索着什么，随后又将它扔回座位上。接着他踱步走到正对面的旅客跟前，把手悄无声息地伸进对方的大衣怀中，抽出一本公文皮夹，翻检起里面的文件底细。“啊！”他冷不丁轻呼了一声，“这正是我最忌惮心虚的祸根！”他抽出了其中一份文件塞进自己的皮夹里，又从自己的皮夹里抽出一张文件塞入对方的皮夹；做完这记偷天换日之后，他把皮夹原封不动塞回对方怀中，再次嗤笑了一声：“你睡得可真够死的！”
果不其然，尽管这个窃贼并未刻意轻手轻脚，那个男人却依旧呼呼大睡，包厢里的另外两人也同样死睡如泥。
窃贼再次看了一眼怀表。
“时辰到了！”
他探身靠近敞开的车窗，将车门上的安全门闩轻轻拨开解脱。

===Original===

Then he opened the door quite wide, took the sleeping traveller by the shoulders and picked him up from the seat, and with all his strength sent him rolling out on to the line!
The next moment he seized from the rack the light articles that evidently belonged to his victim, and threw them out after him.
When he had finished his ghastly work he rubbed his hands in satisfaction. “Good!” he said, and closing the door again, but leaving the window down, he left the compartment, not troubling to pick up his belongings, and walked along the corridors to another second-class compartment, towards the front of the train, in which he calmly installed himself.
“Luck has been with me,” he muttered as he stretched himself out on the seat. “Everything has gone off well; no one has seen me, and those two fools who might have upset my plans will wake up quite naturally when they begin to feel the cold; and they will attribute the headache they will probably feel to their tiring journey.”
A train, travelling in the opposite direction, suddenly roared past the window and made him jump. He started up, and smiled.
“ ’Gad! I said my friend would miss his train, but he’ll catch it in another five minutes! In another five minutes, luggage and body and the entire caboodle will be mincemeat!” and as if completely reassured by the idea he chuckled again. “Nothing could have gone better: I can have a rest, and in an hour’s time I shall be at Juvisy, where, thanks to my forethought, I shall be able to whitewash myself﻿—literally.” One thing, however, still seemed to worry him: he did not know exactly where on the line he had thrown his unhappy victim, but he had an idea that the train had run through a small station shortly afterwards; if that was so, the body might be found sooner than he would have liked. He tried to dismiss the notion from his mind, but he caught sight of the telegraph posts speeding past the windows, and he shook his fist at them malignantly. “That is the only thing that can harm me now,” he muttered.
---
“Juvisy! Juvisy! Wait here two minutes!”
It was barely half-past six, and the porters hurried along the train, calling out the name of the station, and rousing sleepy travellers from their dreams. A man jumped nimbly out of a second-class carriage and walked towards the exit from the station, holding out his ticket. “Season,” he said, and passed out rapidly.

===Chinese===

紧接着他将车门彻底推开，一把抓住熟睡中旅客的双肩，猛地将他从座位上提溜起来，使出浑身吃奶的力气将他一把狠狠推下飞驰的列车，摔向轨道！
紧接着下一秒，他一把抓下行李架上明显属于受害者的几件随身轻便行李，一股脑全扔出了窗外。
干完这桩令人毛骨悚然的惨毒勾当，他心满意足地搓了搓双手。“成了！”他说。他将车门重新带上，却由着车窗大开灌风，头也不回地跨出包厢——甚至懒得去收拾自己遗留在座位上的家当——穿过走廊走向列车前端的另一间二等包厢，神定气闲地在里头坐了下来。
“好运果然眷顾着我，”他在座位上舒展着四肢，得意洋洋地喃喃自语，“一切都顺遂极了；谁也没撞见我，而那两个原本可能坏我大事的蠢材，等被冻醒时只会以为是自然醒转；至于他们多半会感受到的头痛欲裂，也只会归咎于长途颠簸的疲惫劳碌。”
一列朝相反方向疾驰而过的列车突然呼啸着从车窗外掠过，惊得他浑身猛地一颤。他惊跳而起，随即狞笑起来。
“我的老天爷！我刚才还说我那位朋友会错过列车，这不，再过五分钟他就赶上下一趟车了！再过五分钟，行囊、尸首以及那堆零碎破烂就会被对向列车碾压得粉身碎骨成一滩肉泥！”仿佛被这残忍的景象彻底安抚了心神，他又阴恻恻地笑出声来。“再也没有比这更天衣无缝的了：我可以安安心心歇上一歇，一个小时后我就能抵达瑞维西（Juvisy）；多亏了我未雨绸缪的先见之明，在那儿我将能名正言顺洗白自己——字面意义上的洗白。”然而有一件事似乎仍在困扰着他：他摸不准自己究竟把那个可怜虫推下了铁路线的哪一个具体地段，但他隐约觉得列车在事发后不久曾风驰电掣穿过了一座小车站；倘若真是如此，尸体保不准会比他料想的更早被人发觉。他极力试图打消心头的这丝疑虑，但瞥见车窗外飞速倒退的电报线杆，他忍不住恶狠狠地挥了挥拳头。“如今唯一能坏我大局的，也就只有这玩意儿了，”他低声咒骂道。
---
“瑞维西！瑞维西到了！停车两分钟！”
天色才刚六点半，站务员便沿着列车快步小跑，高声呼喊着站名，将昏昏欲睡的旅客从美梦中唤醒。一个男人敏捷地跳下一节二等车厢，大步流星走向出站口，掏出车票一晃。“月票，”他轻声应了一句，随即飞快穿过了检票闸口。

===Original===

“Good idea, that season ticket,” he said to himself; “much less dangerous than an ordinary ticket which the police could have traced.”
He walked briskly towards the subway, crossed the main road, and took a side turning that led down towards the Seine. Taking no notice of the mud, the man went into a field and hid himself in a little thicket on the river bank. He looked carefully all around him to make sure that he was unobserved, then took off his overcoat, jacket and trousers, and drawing a bundle from one of the pockets of his large waterproof, proceeded to dress himself anew. As soon as he was dressed, he spread the waterproof out on the ground, folded up in it the clothes and hat he had previously been wearing, added a number of heavy stones, and tied the whole bundle up with a piece of string. He swung it once or twice at the full length of his arm, and sent it hurtling right into the middle of the river, where it sank at once.
A few minutes later a bricklayer in his working clothes presented himself at the Juvisy booking office.
“A workman’s ticket to Paris, please, missus,” he said, and having got it, the man went on to the departure platform. “It would have been risky to use my own ticket,” he muttered. “This return ticket will put them off the scent,” and with a smile he waited for the train that would take him to Paris.
---
The slow train from Luchon was drawing near its Paris terminus and the travellers were all making hasty toilettes and tidying themselves up after their long night journey. Just, however, as it was approaching the goods station it slowed down and stopped. The passengers, surprised, put their heads out of the windows, to ascertain the reason for the unexpected delay, hazarding various conjectures but unanimous in their vituperation of the company.
Three men were walking slowly along the line, looking carefully at every door. Two were porters, and they were manifesting the most respectful attention to everything the third man said: he was a grave individual, very correctly attired.
“Look there, sir,” one of the porters exclaimed; “there is a door where the safety catch has either been undone or not fastened; that is the only one on the train.”
“That is so,” said the gentleman, and grasping the handle he opened the door of the compartment and got in.

===Chinese===

“办这张通勤月票可真是一步妙棋，”他暗自寻思道，“比起寻常能被警察顺藤摸瓜追查到底的单程票，这招可要安全隐蔽得多。”
他快步穿过地下通道，跨过大马路，拐进了一条通往塞纳河畔的僻静小径。男人全然不顾脚下的泥泞烂污，径直步入一片荒地，将身形隐匿在河岸旁的一处小树丛中。他机警地环顾四周，确信没有任何旁人窥探，随即脱下了大衣、西装上衣和长裤；接着从那件宽大防水胶布雨衣的内袋里掏出一个包裹，手脚麻利地重新换上了一身新行头。换好衣裳后，他把防水胶布雨衣平摊在泥地上，把刚才换下来的旧衣服和旧帽子仔细裹在里头，又在里面塞进了好几块沉甸甸的大石头，用一截麻绳将整个包裹死死捆扎牢靠。他抡起胳膊狠狠空挥了两圈借力，将包裹猛力掷向塞纳河中央，包裹咕咚一声当即沉入水底不见了踪影。
几分钟后，一名身着泥瓦匠工装的汉子大摇大摆出现在瑞维西车站的售票窗口前。
“大姐，买一张去巴黎的工人优待票，”他说。拿到车票后，男人迈步走向发车月台。“继续用我原先那张车票风险太大，”他喃喃自语，“这张往返工人票足以把追查的狗腿子彻底引入歧途。”嘴角挂着一抹得意的冷笑，他静候着将他载往巴黎的早班列车。
---
从吕雄（Luchon）开来的那趟慢车正渐渐逼近巴黎终点站，旅客们纷纷在漫长颠簸的长夜旅途后匆忙梳洗打扮整理行装。然而，就在列车行将抵达货运编组站的当口，车速却陡然慢了下来，随即彻底停滞不前。大感诧异的旅客们纷纷把脑袋探出车窗，想要打探这无端延误的究竟，众人七嘴八舌做出各种揣测，嘴里则不约而同对铁路公司破口大骂。
三名男子正顺着铁道缓缓巡视，聚精会神审视着每一扇车门。其中两人是火车站搬运工，对第三个人的每句吩咐显得毕恭毕敬；那是一位神情肃穆、衣着极其得体周正的体面绅士。
“瞧那儿，长官，”其中一名搬运工失声叫道，“那儿有一扇车门的安全栓要么被人拔开了，要么就是根本没扣上；整列火车就单单只有这么一处！”
“确实如此，”绅士赞同道，他一把抓住门把手，拉开包厢门跨了进去。

===Original===

Two travellers were busy strapping up their bags, and they turned round in simultaneous surprise.
“You will pardon me, gentlemen, when you know who I am,” said the intruder, and throwing open his coat he showed his tricolour scarf. “I have to make enquiry relative to a dead body that has been found on the line near Brétigny; it probably fell from this train, and perhaps from this compartment, for I have just observed that the safety catch is not fastened. Where did you get into the train?”
The two passengers looked at one another in astonishment.
“What a dreadful thing!” one of them exclaimed. “Why, sir, tonight, while my friend here and I were asleep, one of our fellow-travellers did disappear. I made a remark about it, but this gentleman very reasonably pointed out that he must have got out at some station while we were asleep.”
The official was keenly interested.
“What was this passenger like?”
“Quite easily recognised, sir; a man of about sixty, rather stout, and wearing whiskers.”
“That tallies with the description. Might he have been a butler or a steward?”
“That is exactly what he looked like.”
“Then that must be the man whose body has been found upon the line. But I do not know whether it is to be regarded as a case of suicide or of murder, for some hand baggage has been picked up as well: a suicide would not have thrown his luggage out, and a thief would not have wanted to get rid of it.”
The passenger who had not yet spoken, broke in.
“You are wrong, sir; at any rate all his luggage was not thrown on to the line,” and he pointed to the bundle left upon the seat. “I thought that belonged to the gentleman here, but he has just told me it isn’t his.”
The official rapidly unfastened the straps and started back.
“Hullo! A bottle of liquid carbonic acid! Now what does that mean?” He looked at it. “Did this bundle belong to the man who disappeared?”
The two passengers shook their heads.
“I don’t think so,” one of them said; “I should certainly have noticed that Scotch rug; but I did not see it.”
“Then there was a fourth passenger in this compartment?” the official enquired.
“No, we travelled alone,” said one of the men, but the other dissented.
“It is very odd, and I am not sure about it, but I really am wondering whether someone did not get into our compartment last night while we were asleep.

===Chinese===

两名旅客正忙着给旅行袋系上皮带，冷不丁齐刷刷回过头来，满脸惊愕。
“等二位知晓我的身份，想必就能包涵我这般唐突了，”闯入者说着，一把掀开大衣，露出了象征官方执法身份的三色绶带，“在布雷蒂尼（Brétigny）附近的铁道线上起获了一具男尸，我奉命前来调查；死者极有可能是从这趟车上坠落的，甚至很可能正是从这间包厢里坠下去的，因为我刚才发现车门的安全门闩并未插上。请问二位先生是在哪一站上的车？”
两名旅客面面相觑，惊骇不已。
“天哪，太可怕了！”其中一人惊呼道，“警官先生，今天后半夜我和这位朋友睡着的时候，同车的一名旅伴当真莫名其妙失踪了！我当时还随口嘀咕了一句，可这位先生十分合情合理地推测说，那个人准是在咱们熟睡的时候在哪座中途车站下车了。”
警官兴致大发，目光灼灼。
“那个乘客生得什么模样？”
“相貌特征很好认，长官；一个六十岁上下的老汉，体态颇为发福，留着两腮络腮胡。”
“这同通报的体貌特征完全吻合。他看起来像不像个管家或是老总管？”
“没错，活脱脱就是那副管家模样！”
“那在铁道上起获的尸体必定就是此人无疑了。但眼下我还吃不准这桩惨案究竟该定性为自寻短见还是蓄意谋杀，因为现场还搜集到了几件被扔在路边的随身轻便行李：一个想要自尽的人断不会先把自己的行李丢出窗外，而谋财害命的强盗也绝没理由随手把战利品扔掉。”
先前一直未曾开腔的那名旅客冷不丁插话道：
“长官，您这话可说差了；不管怎么说，他的行李并没有全部被扔到铁道线上。”说着他伸手指向被遗留在座位上的那个包裹。“我原本还以为那是属于这位同伴的，可他刚才明确告诉我那东西不是他的。”
警官手脚利索地解开皮带扣，骤然倒吸了一口凉气连退两步。
“喂！一整瓶液态碳酸气（二氧化碳）！这究竟算怎么一回事？！”他死死盯着那瓶化学品，“这个包裹难道属于那个失踪的男人吗？”
两名旅客齐刷刷摇了摇头。
“我想绝不是，”其中一人说，“要是他的，我必定会注意到裹在外头的苏格兰毛毯；可我之前压根没见过这玩意儿。”
“这么说，昨夜这间包厢里曾有过第四名乘客？！”警官厉声追问道。
“没有，自始至终就咱们三个人，”其中一人答道，但另一人却提出了异议。
“说来真够邪门的，我也吃不准，可我心里实在直犯嘀咕，昨晚趁咱们熟睡的时候，到底有没有外人偷偷溜进过咱们的包厢。

===Original===

I have a vague impression that someone did, but I can’t be sure.”
“Do try to remember, sir,” the official urged him; “it is of the very highest importance.”
But the passenger shook his shoulders doubtfully.
“No, I really can’t say anything definite; and, besides, I have a shocking headache.”
The official was silent for a minute or two.
“In my opinion, gentlemen, you have been uncommonly lucky to escape murder yourselves. I do not quite understand yet how the murder was done, but I incline to think it proves almost incredible daring. However﻿—” He stopped and put his head out of the window. “You can send the train on now,” he called to a porter, and resumed: “However, I must ask you to accompany me to the stationmaster’s office and give me your names and addresses, and to help me afterwards in the conduct of the legal investigation.”
The two travellers looked at one another in distressed surprise.
“It is really appalling,” said one of them; “you’re not safe anywhere nowadays.”
“You really aren’t,” the other agreed. “Such a number of awful murders and crimes are being perpetrated every day that you would think not one, but a dozen Fantômas were at work!”

===Chinese===

我脑海里隐隐约约有这么个模糊印象，仿佛真有这么一个人来过，但我实在不敢打包票。”
“请务必仔细回想回想，先生，”警官急切地敦促道，“这可是至关重要的大节！”
然而那名旅客却犹疑不决地耸了耸肩。
“不成，我实在提供不出任何确切的底细；再说，我现在头痛得快要裂开了。”
警官沉吟了片刻。
“依我看，二位先生，你们俩能从杀身之祸中死里逃生捡回一条命，简直算得上天大的造化。我眼下虽尚未彻底参透凶手究竟是如何得手的，但我倾向于认定，作案凶徒胆大妄为到了近乎匪夷所思的境地。不过……”他打住话头，把头探出窗外对一名搬运工高声吩咐道：“眼下可以准许列车进站了。”接着他回过头来继续说道：“不过，我必须请二位随我一道前往站长室一趟，登记二位的姓名与寓所住址，并在随后协助司法部门进行正式的立案调查。”
两名旅客面面相觑，脸上满是晦气苦恼的惊愕。
“这世道真是骇人听闻，”其中一人叹道，“如今出门在外哪儿都谈不上太平安全了。”
“可不是嘛，”另一人附和道，“天底下每天都在发生这么多令人发指的凶杀大案，简直让人觉得犯案作恶的不是一个、而是足足有一打芳托马斯（Fantômas）在同时为非作歹！”

## XXVII Three Surprising Incidents / 第二十七章 三桩惊人变故
===Original===

Nibet went off duty at five in the morning, and returned to his own home to go to bed. As a general rule he slept like a top, after a night on duty, but on this occasion he could not close an eye, being far too uneasy about the consequences of his cooperation in Gurn’s escape.
A few minutes before six in the evening he had taken advantage of no warders being about to slip Gurn from cell number 127 into number 129, whence he could make his way to the roof. At six, when he actually came on duty, Nibet opened the peephole in the door of number 127, as he did in all the others, and saw that Gurn had made an admirable dummy figure in the bed: it was so good that it even deceived a head warder who made a single rapid inspection of all the cells when Nibet was on one of his several rounds during the night. Obviously Gurn must have got clear away from the prison, for if he had been caught it would certainly have become generally known.
These reflections somewhat comforted the restless man, but he knew that the most difficult part of his task was still before him: the difficulty of simulating astonishment and distress when he should get back to the prison presently and be told by his fellow-warders of the prisoner’s escape, and the difficulty of answering in a natural manner to the close interrogation to which he would be subjected by the governor and the police, and possibly even M. Fuselier, who would be in a fine rage when he learned that his captive had escaped him. Nibet meant to pretend ignorance and even stupidity. He would far rather be called a fool, than found out to be a knave and an accomplice.
About half-past eleven Nibet got up; Gurn’s escape must certainly be known at the prison by this time. The warder on duty would have gone to the cell about seven to wake the prisoner, and though nothing might have been detected then, the cell would infallibly have been found to be empty at eight o’clock, when the morning broth was taken round.

===Chinese===

尼贝在清晨五点交班下岗，返回自己的住所上床就寝。通常而言，值完夜班后他向来睡得死沉如猪，但这回他却辗转反侧、彻夜难以合眼，心头极度担忧自己暗助居尔恩（Gurn）越狱所招致的滔天后患。
昨天傍晚六点差几分的时候，他趁着四下无看守走动，悄悄把居尔恩从127号牢房转移进了129号牢房，居尔恩便可以从那里顺着窗户爬上屋顶。六点整当他正式接班值勤时，尼贝跟巡查其他监室一样打开了127号牢房的窥视孔，瞧见居尔恩在床铺上用衣物伪装出了一个极其逼真的假人替身：这障眼法巧妙绝伦，甚至连夜间尼贝几度查房时、前来进行走马观花突击巡视的一位看守长也被彻底蒙骗了过去。显而易见，居尔恩必定早已安然逃出了监狱，因为倘若他当场落网，这桩惊天大乱子早就在狱中人尽皆知了。
这些盘算多少抚平了这个坐立不安男人的心神，但他清楚，自己大任中最凶险棘手的难关还在后头：等他待会儿返回监狱、同僚向他通报犯人逃脱时，他必须费尽心机装出一副惊愕与懊丧的模样；而在典狱长、警方甚至菲泽利耶先生（M. Fuselier）——得知嘴边的肥肉逃之夭夭后必定会暴跳如雷的法官大人——对他展开严密盘问时，他更得以自然妥帖的言辞蒙混过关。尼贝打定主意要装聋作哑、甚至装出一副愚不可及的蠢相。他宁可被上峰当成尸位素餐的蠢货痛斥，也绝不能被查出是个吃里扒外的恶棍同谋。
上午大约十一点半光景，尼贝起了床；这个时候监狱里想必早已查明居尔恩越狱脱逃的真相了。当班的看守在清晨七点左右必定会前往牢房叫醒囚犯，即便那时假人尚未败露，到了八点钟分发早间肉汤的时候，牢房里空空如也的真相也必定会原形毕露。

===Original===

And then﻿—
As he walked from his home round to the prison, Nibet met the gang of masons coming out for dinner; he crossed the street towards them, hoping to hear some news, but they passed by him in silence, one or two of them giving a careless nod or word of greeting; at first Nibet took their silence for a bad sign, thinking they might have been warned to give him no alarm, but he reflected that if Gurn’s escape were discovered, as it surely must be, the authorities would probably prefer not to let the matter become widely known.
As he reached the porter’s lodge his heart beat violently. What would old Morin have to tell him? But old Morin was very busy trying to make his kitchen fire burn properly instead of sending all the smoke pouring out into the room; the old man’s slovenly figure was just visible in a clearing in the smoke, and he returned Nibet’s salutation with nothing more than a silent salute.
“That’s funny!” thought Nibet, and he passed through the main courtyard towards the clerks’ offices at the end. Through the windows he could see the staff, a few bending over their work, most of them reading newspapers, none of them obviously interested in anything special. Next he presented himself before the warders’ turnkey, and again he was allowed to pass on without a word.
By this time Gurn’s accomplice was in a state of such nervous tension that he could hardly restrain himself from catching hold of one or other of the warders whom he saw at their work, and asking them questions. How could the escape of so important a prisoner as the man who had murdered Lord Beltham create so little excitement as this? Nibet longed to rush up the flights of stairs to number 127 and interrogate the warder who had gone on duty after himself, and whom he was now about to relieve in turn. He must surely know all about it. But it would not do to create suspicion, and Nibet had sufficient self-control left to go upstairs at his usual leisurely pace. Outwardly calm and steady, he reached his post just as the clock was striking twelve; he was ever punctuality itself, and he was due on duty at noon.
“Well, Colas,” he said to his colleague, “here I am; you can go now.”
“Good!” said the warder. “I’ll be off at once. I’m on again at six tonight,” and he moved away.
“Everything all right?”

===Chinese===

再接着……
从自家寓所赶往监狱的路上，尼贝迎面撞见了列队出来吃午饭的那帮泥瓦匠；他穿过马路迎上前去，巴望着能打探出些许风声，但工人们默默从他身旁走过，只有一两个人漫不经心地朝他点头寒暄；起初尼贝把他们的沉默当成了不祥的凶兆，疑心工人们是受到了警方的暗中敲打、不得向自己打草惊蛇，但转念一想，居尔恩越狱如果败露——那几乎是板上钉钉的事——狱方高层多半宁可家丑不外扬，断不会由着消息在市井走漏。
踏入门房值班室的当口，他的心怦怦狂跳。老莫兰（Morin）会向他透露些什么呢？然而老莫兰正忙得不可开交，使劲捅着厨房的炉火，想让炉膛旺起来，省得呛人的黑烟老往屋里倒灌；在弥漫浓烟的空当里，勉强能瞧见老头邋里邋遢的身影，他对于尼贝的问候仅仅以一个默不作声的抱拳致意作为回应。
“真邪门！”尼贝暗自嘀咕。他穿过主天井庭院，走向尽头的书记官办公室。隔着车窗玻璃，他看到里面的职员有的正埋头伏案公干，大部分人在悠闲翻看着报纸，显然谁也没把心思搁在什么天塌下来的变故上。接着他来到看守值班铁门前，狱警同样一言不发便放他通行。
到了这个节骨眼上，居尔恩的同谋已经紧张到了快要崩溃的边缘，他恨不得一把抓住眼前任何一个正在当值的看守同事，厉声向他们打破砂锅问到底。像杀害贝尔特姆勋爵（Lord Beltham）这般名震巴黎的重犯越狱脱逃，怎么可能在狱中掀不起哪怕半点惊涛骇浪呢？！尼贝恨不得一口气顺着楼梯冲上三楼的127号牢房，当面质问接替自己白班、而自己眼下正要与之交接的看守同僚。对方必定知道事情的底细！但引起旁人疑窦可是万万使不得的，尼贝尚存有一丝克制力，仍旧按照平日里慢条斯理的步态缓步上楼。表面上端庄沉稳的他，在钟声敲响十二点的当口准时抵达了岗位；他在守时上向来分秒不差，而他正该在正午接班。
“喂，科拉（Colas），”他对同僚打招呼道，“我到了；你可以下岗了。”
“太好了！”那个看守应道，“我这就走人。今晚六点我还得来值班呢。”说着他拔腿便走。
“上头一切都还安生吧？”

===Original===

Nibet enquired, in a tone he tried to make as casual as possible, but that trembled a little nevertheless.
“Quite,” said Colas, perfectly naturally, and he went away.
---
Nibet could contain himself no longer, and the next second he threw caution to the winds: rushing to Gurn’s cell he flung the door open.
Gurn was there, sitting on the foot of his bed with his legs crossed and a notebook on his knees, making notes with the quietest attention: he scarcely appeared to notice Nibet’s violent invasion.
“Oh! So you are there?” stammered the astonished warder.
Gurn raised his head and looked at the warder with a cryptic gaze.
“Yes, I’m here.”
All manner of notions crowded through Nibet’s brain, but he could find words for none of them. Had the plot been discovered before Gurn had had time to get away, or had a trap been laid for himself through the medium of one of the prisoners to test his own incorruptibility? Nibet went white, and leaned against the wall for support. At last Gurn spoke again, reassuring him with a smile.
“Don’t look so miserable,” he said. “I am here. That is a matter of absolutely no importance. We will suppose that nothing passed between us yesterday, and﻿—that’s an end to it.”
“So you haven’t gone, you didn’t go?” said Nibet again.
“No,” Gurn replied; “since you are so interested, all I need say is that I was afraid to risk it at the last minute.”
Nibet had cast a keen and experienced eye all over the cell; under the washstand he saw the little bundle of clothes which he had brought the prisoner the previous day. He rightly opined that the first thing to do was to remove these dangerous articles, whose presence in Gurn’s cell would appear very suspicious if they happened to be discovered. He took the bundle and was hurriedly stowing it away under his own clothes, when he uttered an exclamation of surprise; the things were wet, and he knew from his own experience that the rain had never ceased throughout the whole of the night.
“Gurn,” he said reproachfully, “you are up to some trick! These things are soaked. You must have gone out last night, or these things would not be like this.”
Gurn smiled sympathetically at the warder.
“Not so bad!” he remarked; “that’s pretty good reasoning for a mere gaoler.” And as Nibet was about to press the matter, Gurn anticipated his questions, and made frank confession.

===Chinese===

尼贝探询道，尽量让自己的语调显得若无其事，但嗓音依旧不可遏制地微微发颤。
“一切好得很，”科拉极其自然地回了一句，随即便走远了。
---
尼贝再也按捺不住内心的狂潮，下一秒他把一切谨小慎微统统抛到了九霄云外：他发疯似地冲到居尔恩的牢房门前，一把将铁门推开到底。
居尔恩赫然正坐在那里！他盘腿坐在床尾，膝头上搁着一本笔记本，正全神贯注、极其安详地在上面做着笔记；他甚至几乎未曾抬眼留意尼贝这般破门而入的粗暴动静。
“噢！你……你居然还在这儿？！”惊骇万分的看守结结巴巴失声叫道。
居尔恩抬起头，用意味深长的神秘目光注视着看守。
“是的，我就在这儿。”
无数匪夷所思的念头在尼贝脑海中疯狂翻滚，他却惊骇得一个字也吐不出来。难道阴谋在居尔恩来得及远走高飞之前就败露了？还是说有人故意借着这个囚犯设下圈套，来考验他本人的廉洁忠诚？！尼贝面如死灰，不得不死死靠着冰冷的墙壁支撑摇摇欲坠的身子。最终居尔恩再次开口，微笑着宽慰他。
“别摆出一副大难临头的凄惨嘴脸，”他说，“我就在这儿。这压根是无关紧要的鸡毛蒜皮。咱们就当昨天你我之间什么勾当都没发生过——事情到此为止。”
“这么说你没逃走？你当真没走？！”尼贝再次追问道。
“没有，”居尔恩回答，“既然你这么刨根问底，我只能告诉你，到了最后一刻我打起了退堂鼓，不敢冒这个险。”
尼贝凭借敏锐老练的眼力将整间牢房扫视了一圈；在洗脸架底下，他瞧见了他前一天替囚犯捎进来的那一小包衣服。他心里有数，当务之急是必须把这些危险的罪证转移销毁，一旦在居尔恩的牢房里起获这些衣物，后果将不堪设想。他抄起包裹，急急忙忙就要往自己宽大的制服底下塞，冷不丁失声发出一声惊呼：那些衣物全是湿漉漉的！而他凭借自身的执勤经验再清楚不过，昨夜整整后半夜暴风雨根本自始至终未曾停歇！
“居尔恩，”他带着责备的口吻低吼道，“你在跟我玩花招！这些衣服全湿透了！你昨夜必定偷偷溜出去了，否则这些衣服怎么可能会变成这副模样？！”
居尔恩冲着看守投去一抹赞赏体谅的笑意。
“真不赖嘛！”他赞叹道，“区区一个牢头狱警能有这般缜密的推理，可真称得上罕见了。”正当尼贝准备打破砂锅问到底时，居尔恩抢先一步打断了他的质问，坦率招供起来。

===Original===

“Well, yes, I did try to get out﻿—got as far as the clerk’s office last evening, but at the last minute I funked it, and went back on to the roof. But when I got into number 129 again I found I could not get back into my own cell, for, as you know, 129 was locked outside; so to avoid detection I returned to the roof and spent the night there; at daybreak I took advantage of the little disturbance caused by the workmen coming in, and slipped down from the roof just as they were going up. As soon as I found myself on this floor I ran along this corridor and slipped into my cell. When your friend Colas brought me my broth he did not notice that my cell was unlocked﻿—and there you are!”
The explanation was not altogether convincing, but Nibet listened to it and pondered the situation. On the whole, it was much better that things should be as they were, but the warder was wondering how the great lady, who paid so mighty well, might take the matter. She most certainly had not promised so large a sum of money, nor paid the good round sum of ten thousand francs down in advance, merely in order that Gurn might have a little walk upon the tiles. What was to be done with regard to that personage? With much ingenuousness Nibet confided his anxiety to the prisoner, who laughed.
“It’s not all over yet,” he declared. “Indeed, it is only just beginning. What if we only wanted to test you, and prove your quality? Make your mind easy, Nibet. If Gurn is in prison at the present moment it is because he has his own reasons for being there. But who is able to predict the future?”
It was time for Gurn to go to the exercise yard, and Nibet, reassuming the uncompromising attitude that all warders ought to maintain when in custody of prisoners, led the murderer down to the courtyard.

In his office at the Law Courts, M. Fuselier was having a private interview with Juve, and listening with much interest to what the clever detective inspector was saying to him.
“I tell you again, sir, I attach great importance to the finding of this ordnance map in Gurn’s rooms.”
“Yes?” said M. Fuselier, with a touch of scepticism.
“And I will tell you why,” Juve went on. “About a year ago, when I was engaged on the case of the murder of the Marquise de Langrune at her château of Beaulieu, down in Lot, I found a small piece of a map showing the district in which I was at the time. I took it to M.

===Chinese===

“得，没错，我昨晚确实试图越狱——傍晚一路摸到了书记官办公室跟前，可到了临门一脚的关头我吓破了胆，又重新退回了屋顶。可当我重新钻回129号牢房时，我发觉自己根本回不去自己的牢房了，因为正如你所知，129号牢门从外头反锁着；为了防止行踪败露，我只得重返屋顶，在冷雨里熬过了一整夜；破晓天明时分，我趁着工人们进监区干活引发的一阵小小骚乱，趁着他们顺窗往上爬的当口顺势溜了下来。一踩上这层楼面的走廊，我撒腿便跑回了自己的牢房。你的哥们儿科拉来给我送肉汤的时候，压根没留神我的牢门其实虚掩着没上锁——事情的原委就是这么回事！”
这套说辞并不完全具有说服力，但尼贝听在耳里，反复权衡着利弊局势。总的来说，眼下这般结局反倒更让他如释重负，但看守暗自纳闷那位出手阔绰的尊贵妇人对此会作何感想。她许诺下这般天大的横财、并且预先毫不含糊支付了一万法郎现钱，绝不可能是为了让居尔恩在大雨天的屋顶瓦垄上溜达一圈散散心吧！面对那位大人物，他该如何交代呢？尼贝天真坦白地把自己的焦虑和盘托出告诉了囚犯，居尔恩闻言大笑起来。
“好戏还没开场呢，”他宣称道，“甚至可以说，一切不过才刚刚起步。万一我们不过是想借此考验考验你、摸清你的成色本领呢？把心放宽吧，尼贝。如果居尔恩此刻仍端坐在这座牢房里，那是因为他自有留在这里的深谋远虑。可谁又能预料后头会发生什么呢？”
放风的时辰到了，尼贝重新摆出看守在押解犯人时一贯该有的严酷冷峻做派，领着杀人凶手步下楼梯，走向天井庭院。

在司法宫（Law Courts）的预审法官公署里，菲泽利耶先生正在同朱夫举行闭门密谈，饶有兴致地倾听着这位神探警官向他陈述的心得。
“长官，容我再次强调，在居尔恩寓所起获的那幅军用测绘地图，在我眼里意义非同小可。”
“是吗？”菲泽利耶先生带着一丝怀疑的神色应道。
“不妨容我细细向您道来，”朱夫接着说道，“大约一年前，当我在法国南部的洛特省调查德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人（Marquise de Langrune）在博利厄城堡遇害的命案时，我曾拾获过一小块地图残片，上面标注的正是当时我立足的那片区域。我把那块残片呈递给了负责办理该案预审的普雷勒先生（M. de Presles）。

===Original===

de Presles, the magistrate who was conducting the enquiry. He attached no importance to it, and I myself could not see at the time that it gave us any new evidence.”
“Quite so,” said M. Fuselier. “There is nothing particularly remarkable in finding a map, or a piece of a map, showing a district, in the district itself.”
“Those are M. de Presles’ very words to me,” said Juve with a smile. “And I will give you the same answer I gave him, namely, that if some day we could find the other portion of the map which completed the first piece we found, and could identify the owner of the two portions, there would then be a formal basis on which to proceed to base an argument.”
“Proceed to base it,” M. Fuselier suggested.
“That’s very easy,” said Juve. “The fragment of map numbered 1, found at Beaulieu, belongs to X. I do not know who X is; but in Paris, in Gurn’s rooms, I find the fragment of map numbered 2, which belongs to Gurn. If it turns out, as I expect, that the two fragments of map, when placed together, form a single and complete whole, I shall conclude logically that X, who was the owner of fragment number 1, is the same as the owner of fragment number 2, to wit, Gurn.”
“How are you going to find out?” enquired M. Fuselier.
“It is in order to find it out that we have sent for Dollon,” Juve replied. “He was steward to the late Marquise de Langrune, and has all the circumstantial evidence relating to that case. If he has still got the fragment of map, it will be simplicity itself to prove what I have suggested, and perhaps to make the identification I suggest.”
“Yes,” said M. Fuselier, “but if you do succeed, will it be of really great importance in your opinion? Will you be able to infer from that one fact that Gurn and the man who murdered the Marquise de Langrune are one and the same person? Is not that going rather far? Especially as, if I remember rightly, it was proved that the murderer in that case was the son of a M. Rambert, and this young Rambert committed suicide after the crime?”
Juve evaded the issue.
“Well, we shall see,” was all he said.

The magistrate’s clerk came into the room and unceremoniously interrupted the conversation.
“It has gone two, sir,” he said.

===Chinese===

当时普雷勒法官并未将其放在心上，而我自己当时也看不出这能为案件提供任何突破性铁证。”
“这合情合理，”菲泽利耶先生说，“在一处特定区域的土地上拾获一张或者半张标注该区域的地图，本就谈不上什么值得大惊小怪的稀罕事。”
“普雷勒先生当年对我说的恰恰就是这番原话，”朱夫微笑着说，“而我如今给您的答复，也正是当年呈给他的答复：假若有朝一日，咱们能搜寻到与当初拾获的那块残片严丝合缝匹配拼合的另一半地图，并且能够查明这两张残片的归属物主，那么咱们就拥有了借此展开逻辑推论的坚实法理基石。”
“那就请展开你的推论吧，”菲泽利耶先生示意道。
“其实再简单不过了，”朱夫说，“在博利厄拾获的1号地图残片属于某甲（X）。我不知道某甲究竟何许人也；但在巴黎，在居尔恩的套房里，我起获了属于居尔恩本人的2号残缺地图。假若事实果真如我所料，两块残片拼在一起恰好构成一副完整无缺的地图，那么从逻辑上讲我就可以断言：拥有1号残片的某甲，与拥有2号残缺地图的居尔恩，本就是同一人！”
“那你打算如何验证核实呢？”菲泽利耶先生探问道。
“正是为了核实这一点，咱们才大费周折传唤多隆（Dollon）进京，”朱夫回答道，“他是已故德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人的老总管（steward），手里保管着那桩命案涉及的全部物证卷宗。只要他手里还保存着当年那块地图残片，要证实我的推论简直易如反掌，而要顺理成章验明我推测的身份更是不在话下。”
“不错，”菲泽利耶先生沉吟道，“可即便你当真如愿以偿，依你之见这当真具有举足轻重的重大分量吗？单凭这一项孤证，你难道就能断言居尔恩与残杀德·朗格吕纳侯爵夫人的凶手是同一个人吗？这推论未免扯得有些太远了吧？尤其是，如果我没记错的话，当年卡奥尔法庭早已判定真凶是一位朗贝尔先生的公子，而且那个小朗贝尔在犯下滔天凶案后便畏罪投河自尽了不是吗？”
朱夫巧妙地避开了这个尖锐的话题。
“咳，咱们且拭目以待吧，”他仅仅淡淡地吐出了这么一句。

法官的书记官推门步入公署，毫不客气地打断了两人的私下交谈。
“大人，已经过了下午两点钟了，”他说。

===Original===

“There are some prisoners to examine, and a whole lot of witnesses,” and he placed two bulky bundles of papers before the magistrate and waited for a sign to call the various persons, free or otherwise, whom the magistrate had to see.
The first bundle caught Juve’s attention. It was endorsed “Royal Palace Hotel Case.”
“Anything new about the robbery from Mme. Van den Rosen and Princess Sonia Danidoff?” he enquired, and as the magistrate shook his head, he added, “Are you going to examine Muller now?”
“Yes,” said the magistrate; “at once.”
“And after that you are to examine Gurn, aren’t you, in connection with the Beltham case?”
“Quite so.”
“I wish you would oblige me by confronting the two men here, in my presence.”
M. Fuselier looked up in surprise: he could not see what connection there could be between the two utterly dissimilar cases. What object could Juve have in wanting the man who had murdered Lord Beltham to be confronted with the unimportant little hotel servant who had really been arrested rather as a concession to public opinion than because he was actually deemed capable of burglary or attempted burglary? Might not Juve, with his known mania for associating all crimes with each other, be going just a little too far in the present instance?
“You have got some idea in the back of your head?” said M. Fuselier.
“I’ve got a﻿—a scar in the palm of my hand,” Juve answered with a smile, and as the magistrate confessed that he failed to understand, Juve enlightened him. “We know that the man who did that robbery at the Royal Palace Hotel burned his hand badly when he was cutting the electric wires in the Princess’s bathroom. Well, a few weeks ago, while I was on the look out for someone with a scar from such a wound, I was told of a man who was prowling about the slums. I had the fellow followed up, and the very night the hunt began I was going to arrest him, when, a good deal to my surprise, I discovered that he was no other than Gurn. He escaped me that time, but when he was caught later on I found that he has an unmistakable scar inside the palm of his right hand; it is fading now, for the burn was only superficial, but it is there. Now do you see my idea?”
“Yes, I do,” the magistrate exclaimed, “and I am all the more glad to hear of it, since I am to have both the men here now. Shall I have Muller in first?”
Juve assented.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“有几个犯人正候着提审，外头还排了一大群证人呢。”说着，他把两大沓厚厚的公文案卷呈递在法官面前，静候法官示意传唤相关人等。
头一沓案卷立刻吸引了朱夫的目光，封皮上赫然标注着“皇家宫殿大饭店案”。
“关于范·登·罗森夫人和索尼娅·达尼多夫公主（Princess Sonia Danidoff）遭劫的案子，有什么新进展吗？”他探问道。见法官摇头，他又补充问：“您待会儿打算提审米勒（Muller）吗？”
“是的，”法官回答，“马上就提审。”
“在那之后，您紧接着就要就贝尔特姆案提审居尔恩，对不对？”
“正是。”
“能否劳驾您行个方便：当着我的面，让这两个人在公署里当堂对质。”
菲泽利耶法官惊愕地抬起头：他完全想不通这两桩风马牛不相及的案子之间到底能有什么瓜葛。朱夫到底图什么，非要把杀害贝尔特姆勋爵的重犯，同那个微不足道的饭店下人安排对质？要明白，抓捕那个侍者不过是平息汹汹民怨的妥协姿态，压根没人相信他当真有能耐破门行窃。莫非朱夫那众所周知的偏执狂——非要把全天下所有凶案全都硬扯在一起的狂想症——在这桩案子上又走火入魔有些过头了？
“你脑子里又在打什么鬼主意？”菲泽利耶法官发问道。
“我脑子里不过是在惦记着……某个手掌心里的伤疤罢了，”朱夫微笑着回答。见法官坦言一头雾水，朱夫便出言点破玄机：“咱们都清楚，在皇家宫殿大饭店（Royal Palace Hotel）作案的窃贼，在切断公主浴室里的电线时严重灼伤了手掌。几个星期前，正当我四处搜寻手上带有此类灼伤疤痕的嫌犯时，有人向我通风报信，称贫民窟陋巷里有个形迹可疑的男人在游荡。我立刻派人咬住那个家伙，就在搜捕行动展开的当晚，我本打算将他一把擒获，却大为震惊地发现那个人居然正是居尔恩！那一次让他侥幸脱逃，可当他后来落网被捕时，我发现他的右手掌心里赫然印着一道清晰无误的伤疤；尽管那伤痕如今正渐渐淡化——因为当时不过是皮外灼伤——但痕迹确凿无疑。眼下您明白我的意图了吧？”
“原来如此，我全明白了！”法官击节赞叹道，“听你这么一说我可太高兴了，反正这两个人眼下都在这儿。要不要先传唤米勒进来？”
朱夫点头表示赞同。……

“So you still refuse to confess?” said the magistrate at last. “You still maintain that your﻿—extraordinary﻿—order to let the red-haired waiter out, was given in good faith?”
“Yes, yes, yes, sir,” the night watchman answered. “That very evening a new servant had joined the staff. I had not even set eyes on him. When I saw this﻿—stranger﻿—, I took him to be the servant who had been engaged the day before, and I told them to open the door for him. That is the real truth.”
“And that is all?”
“That is positively all.”
“We are only charging you with complicity,” the magistrate went on, “for the man who touched the electric wires burned his hand; that is a strong point in your favour. And you also say that if the thief were put before you, you could recognise him?”
“Yes,” said the man confidently.
“Good!” said M. Fuselier, and he signed to his clerk to call in another personage.
The clerk understood, and Gurn was brought in between two municipal guards, and was followed by the young licentiate in law, Maître Roger de Seras, who represented his leader at most of these preliminary examinations. As Gurn came in, with the light from the window falling full on his face, M. Fuselier gave a curt order.
“Muller, turn round and look at this man!”
Muller obeyed, and surveyed with some bewilderment, and without the least comprehension, the bold head and the well-built, muscular frame of Lord Beltham’s murderer. Gurn did not flinch.
“Do you recognise that man?” the magistrate demanded.
Muller ransacked his brains and looked again at Gurn, then shook his head.
“No, sir.”
“Gurn, open your right hand,” the magistrate ordered. “Show it,” and he turned again to Muller. “The man before you seems to have been burned in the palm of the hand, as that scar shows. Can you not remember having seen that man at the Royal Palace Hotel?”
Muller looked steadily at Gurn.
“On my honour, sir, although it would be to my interest to recognise him, I am bound to acknowledge that I really and truly don’t.”
M. Fuselier had a brief conversation aside with Juve, and then, the detective appearing to agree with him, turned once more to the night watchman.
“Muller,” he said, “the court is pleased with your frankness.
